---
name: AEC Integrations
description: Editorial directory and review platform for AEC software integrations. Light-only (AECI-226; dark reintroduction dropped, not roadmapped), Tailwind v4 + Spartan brain.
colors:
  # Light theme, the only theme (dark token set removed in AECI-226 and not
  # roadmapped). These are the :root colour tokens in apps/web/src/styles.css:
  # 18 base tokens and 20 added by AECI-1747, 38 in all. Each opaque value
  # renders the hex in styles.css; design-tokens.hex.guard.spec.ts pins that.
  surface-base: 'oklch(100% 0 0)'
  surface-raised: 'oklch(98.43% 0 0)'
  surface-sunken: 'oklch(96.74% 0.0013 286.38)'
  surface-muted: 'oklch(96.74% 0.0013 286.38)'
  border-default: 'oklch(91.97% 0.0036 286.32)'
  border-strong: 'oklch(87.20% 0.0055 286.32)'
  border-input: 'oklch(63.63% 0.0133 286.02)'
  text-primary: 'oklch(14.48% 0 0)'
  text-secondary: 'oklch(44.19% 0.0146 285.79)'
  text-tertiary: 'oklch(55.17% 0.0138 285.94)'
  accent-primary: 'oklch(32.24% 0.0397 166.73)'
  accent-primary-hover: 'oklch(43.47% 0.0641 160.30)'
  accent-primary-soft: 'oklch(95.35% 0.0066 160.07)'
  accent-secondary: 'oklch(74.64% 0.1164 49.55)'
  accent-secondary-deep: 'oklch(51.86% 0.1245 45.13)'
  accent-rating: 'oklch(75.16% 0.1469 83.99)'
  accent-warm: 'oklch(96.13% 0.0111 89.72)'
  status-error: 'oklch(50.13% 0.1783 28.70)'
  # Added by AECI-1758. #8C1D18, the danger button's hover fill only.
  status-error-hover: 'oklch(42.00% 0.1473 28.14)'
  # Added by AECI-1747. Hex in order: #26473A, #15291F, #84848D, #C5D3CA,
  # #B9C7BF, #E7E1D2, #3B6E55, #DADADC, #B5B5B5, #C6C6CA.
  surface-hero-panel: 'oklch(36.82% 0.0453 166.67)'
  surface-footer: 'oklch(26.02% 0.0324 161.25)'
  border-field: 'oklch(61.63% 0.0134 286.00)'
  text-on-brand-secondary: 'oklch(85.36% 0.0195 157.80)'
  text-on-footer-secondary: 'oklch(81.66% 0.0189 161.01)'
  surface-on-brand-hover: 'oklch(91.04% 0.0211 88.73)'
  surface-hero-ribbon: 'oklch(49.48% 0.0689 161.11)'
  surface-sunken-hover: 'oklch(88.89% 0.0027 286.34)'
  border-underline: 'oklch(77.31% 0 0)'
  border-empty: 'oklch(82.77% 0.0055 286.28)'
  # Bone again, #F5F2EA. In styles.css text-on-brand and focus-ring-on-brand
  # are var(--accent-warm), so a copy cannot drift.
  text-on-brand: 'oklch(96.13% 0.0111 89.72)'
  focus-ring-on-brand: 'oklch(96.13% 0.0111 89.72)'
  # Alpha tokens, composited in sRGB over the ground. Bone at 10, 16, 14, 32
  # and 50 percent, Forest hover at 75 and 45 percent, white at 94 percent.
  surface-on-brand-wash: 'oklch(96.13% 0.0111 89.72 / 10%)'
  surface-on-brand-wash-hover: 'oklch(96.13% 0.0111 89.72 / 16%)'
  border-on-brand-subtle: 'oklch(96.13% 0.0111 89.72 / 14%)'
  border-on-brand: 'oklch(96.13% 0.0111 89.72 / 32%)'
  border-on-brand-strong: 'oklch(96.13% 0.0111 89.72 / 50%)'
  surface-hero-glow: 'oklch(43.47% 0.0641 160.30 / 75%)'
  surface-hero-glow-soft: 'oklch(43.47% 0.0641 160.30 / 45%)'
  surface-sticky: 'oklch(100% 0 0 / 94%)'
typography:
  # The limited ramp (clashes 9 and 17, PLAN.md §4.2): eleven sizes since ruling
  # A3 added the 48px score figure, twelve with AECI-1835's wide statement, weights 400
  # and 600. Every value is real CSS. The five fluid sizes are the clamp()
  # expressions in apps/web/src/styles.css (AECI-1748): rem bounds and a
  # rem-plus-vw middle term. AECI-1748 confirmed the weights, line heights and
  # tracking measured from mock-up 6.
  display:
    # The Display clamp the About and Home heroes and the vendor masthead
    # declare (clash 7 rules only on detail pages). Not a ramp token. It does
    # not render today: those heroes put it on the h1 element, and the unlayered
    # h1 rule wins, so they show the h1 size (--t-fig, 28 to 40px, weight 600).
    # AECI-1847 moves the size onto an inner span.
    fontFamily: '"Source Serif 4", Georgia, "Times New Roman", serif'
    fontSize: 'clamp(2.25rem, 4.5vw + 1rem, 4rem)'
    fontWeight: 400
    lineHeight: 1.05
    letterSpacing: '-0.01em'
  display-detail:
    # --t-display, 40 to 72px: the product name on a detail page (clash 7). The
    # page adopts it when it is rebuilt (AECI-1778).
    fontFamily: '"Source Serif 4", Georgia, "Times New Roman", serif'
    fontSize: 'clamp(2.5rem, 2.4vw + 2.375rem, 4.5rem)'
    fontWeight: 400
    lineHeight: 1
    letterSpacing: '-0.02em'
  statement:
    # --t-statement, 34 to 56px.
    fontFamily: '"Source Serif 4", Georgia, "Times New Roman", serif'
    fontSize: 'clamp(2.125rem, 1.8vw + 1.875rem, 3.5rem)'
    fontWeight: 400
    lineHeight: 1.1
    letterSpacing: '-0.02em'
  statement-wide:
    # --t-statement-wide, 34 to 51px: the empty pair page statement (AECI-1835),
    # at Source Serif 4's opsz 60 display cut.
    fontFamily: '"Source Serif 4", Georgia, "Times New Roman", serif'
    fontSize: 'clamp(2.125rem, 3vw + 0.75rem, 3.1875rem)'
    fontWeight: 400
    lineHeight: 1.1
    letterSpacing: '-0.02em'
  figure:
    # --t-fig, 28 to 40px.
    fontFamily: '"Source Serif 4", Georgia, "Times New Roman", serif'
    fontSize: 'clamp(1.75rem, 2.5vw + 0.75rem, 2.5rem)'
    fontWeight: 600
    lineHeight: 1
    letterSpacing: '-0.01em'
  figure-score:
    # --t-48, a fixed 48px (ruling A3, 2026-11-16). The score figure.
    fontFamily: '"Source Serif 4", Georgia, "Times New Roman", serif'
    fontSize: '3rem'
    fontWeight: 600
    lineHeight: 1
    letterSpacing: '-0.01em'
  section:
    # --t-36, 28 to 36px.
    fontFamily: '"Source Serif 4", Georgia, "Times New Roman", serif'
    fontSize: 'clamp(1.75rem, 1vw + 1.35rem, 2.25rem)'
    fontWeight: 600
    lineHeight: 1.1
    letterSpacing: '-0.02em'
  headline:
    # --t-fig, the global h1 rule. Same pixels as before the ramp.
    fontFamily: '"Source Serif 4", Georgia, "Times New Roman", serif'
    fontSize: 'clamp(1.75rem, 2.5vw + 0.75rem, 2.5rem)'
    fontWeight: 600
    lineHeight: 1.15
    letterSpacing: '-0.005em'
  title:
    fontFamily: '"Source Serif 4", Georgia, "Times New Roman", serif'
    fontSize: '1.5rem'
    fontWeight: 600
    lineHeight: 1.25
    letterSpacing: 'normal'
  card-title:
    # --t-20. The mock-up's line-height is 1.1.
    fontFamily: '"Source Serif 4", Georgia, "Times New Roman", serif'
    fontSize: '1.25rem'
    fontWeight: 600
    lineHeight: 1.1
  lede:
    # --t-20, in the body sans.
    fontFamily: '"Atkinson Hyperlegible Next", system-ui, -apple-system, "Segoe UI", sans-serif'
    fontSize: '1.25rem'
    fontWeight: 400
    lineHeight: 1.5
  body:
    fontFamily: '"Atkinson Hyperlegible Next", system-ui, -apple-system, "Segoe UI", sans-serif'
    fontSize: '1rem'
    fontWeight: 400
    lineHeight: 1.6
    letterSpacing: 'normal'
  caption:
    fontFamily: '"Atkinson Hyperlegible Next", system-ui, -apple-system, "Segoe UI", sans-serif'
    fontSize: '0.875rem'
    fontWeight: 400
    lineHeight: 1.5
    letterSpacing: 'normal'
  field-caption:
    # --t-12 with a 20px line height (start).
    fontFamily: '"Atkinson Hyperlegible Next", system-ui, -apple-system, "Segoe UI", sans-serif'
    fontSize: '0.75rem'
    fontWeight: 400
    lineHeight: '1.25rem'
  label:
    fontFamily: '"Atkinson Hyperlegible Next", system-ui, -apple-system, "Segoe UI", sans-serif'
    fontSize: '0.8125rem'
    fontWeight: 600
    lineHeight: 1.4
    letterSpacing: '0.01em'
  overline:
    fontFamily: '"Atkinson Hyperlegible Next", system-ui, -apple-system, "Segoe UI", sans-serif'
    fontSize: '0.75rem'
    fontWeight: 600
    lineHeight: 1.4
    letterSpacing: '0.08em'
    textTransform: 'uppercase'
rounded:
  # The new scale (clash 12, PLAN.md section 4.3). The keys are the role names
  # and equal the utility suffix and the CSS token: mark is rounded-mark and
  # --radius-mark. Do not write rounded-sm, md, lg or xl on a redesigned
  # component. Those are Spartan's scale and render 4.8, 6.4, 8 and 11.2px.
  none: '0'
  # 8px small marks (was 0.25rem).
  mark: '0.5rem'
  # 12px controls: buttons, inputs (was 0.5rem).
  control: '0.75rem'
  # 16px, measured on nested panels in the mock-ups. Not ruled. It sits between
  # control and card so the scale ascends. See "Open values" in section 4.
  panel: '1rem'
  # 24px cards (was 0.75rem).
  card: '1.5rem'
  # Chips. Never a primary action.
  pill: '9999px'
spacing:
  # The 4-point scale (clash 17): 4, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 48, 64, 96px. The keys
  # are Tailwind's step numbers, so spacing.6 is p-6 (24px). The scale is steps
  # 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16 and 24. AECI-1750 adds no spacing tokens.
  '0': '0'
  '1': '0.25rem'
  '2': '0.5rem'
  '3': '0.75rem'
  '4': '1rem'
  '6': '1.5rem'
  '8': '2rem'
  '12': '3rem'
  '16': '4rem'
  '24': '6rem'
components:
  # The redesign components (clashes 19 to 25, 39 to 41 and 43). Sizes and
  # edges are starting values measured from mock-up 6, default state, in
  # docs/design/mockups/redesign-2026-11/mockups/6-product-detail.html. The code
  # issues confirm them. Section 5 "Starting values from the mock-ups
  # (components)" gives the file and line for each value.
  button-primary:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.accent-primary}'
    textColor: '{colors.surface-base}'
    fontSize: '0.875rem'
    fontWeight: 600
    rounded: '{rounded.control}'
    minHeight: '44px'
    padding: '{spacing.0} {spacing.6}'
  button-primary-hover:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.accent-primary-hover}'
  # The main button on a Forest ground (clash 3). Bone fill, Forest text.
  button-on-brand:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.accent-warm}'
    textColor: '{colors.accent-primary}'
    fontSize: '0.875rem'
    fontWeight: 600
    rounded: '{rounded.control}'
    minHeight: '44px'
    padding: '{spacing.0} {spacing.6}'
  button-secondary:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.surface-raised}'
    textColor: '{colors.text-primary}'
    fontSize: '0.875rem'
    fontWeight: 600
    rounded: '{rounded.control}'
    minHeight: '44px'
    padding: '{spacing.0} {spacing.6}'
    border: '1px solid {colors.border-strong}'
  button-secondary-hover:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.surface-sunken}'
    textColor: '{colors.accent-primary}'
  # Ghost takes the compact side padding of the mock-up's small button. The height is still 44px.
  button-ghost:
    backgroundColor: 'transparent'
    textColor: '{colors.text-primary}'
    fontSize: '0.875rem'
    fontWeight: 600
    rounded: '{rounded.control}'
    minHeight: '44px'
    padding: '{spacing.0} {spacing.4}'
  # A destructive action, always with a text label (AECI-1758). White text 6.54:1, 9.11:1 on hover.
  button-danger:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.status-error}'
    textColor: '{colors.surface-base}'
    fontSize: '0.875rem'
    fontWeight: 600
    rounded: '{rounded.control}'
    minHeight: '44px'
    padding: '{spacing.0} {spacing.6}'
  button-danger-hover:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.status-error-hover}'
  # --shadow-card does not exist in styles.css yet. Section 4 defines it and
  # AECI-1750 adds it. The mock-up card fill is white.
  card:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.surface-base}'
    textColor: '{colors.text-primary}'
    rounded: '{rounded.card}'
    padding: '{spacing.6}'
    shadow: 'var(--shadow-card)'
  input:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.surface-base}'
    textColor: '{colors.text-primary}'
    typography: '{typography.body}'
    rounded: '{rounded.control}'
    minHeight: '44px'
    padding: '{spacing.0} {spacing.4}'
    border: '1px solid {colors.border-field}'
  # Focus is an outline, because forced-colours mode drops box-shadow.
  input-focus:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.surface-base}'
    textColor: '{colors.text-primary}'
    border: '1px solid {colors.accent-primary}'
    outline: '3px solid {colors.accent-primary}'
    outlineOffset: '3px'
  # The error edge is 2px in the error colour. An icon and a message always come with it.
  input-error:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.surface-base}'
    textColor: '{colors.text-primary}'
    border: '2px solid {colors.status-error}'
  # The one public pill chip (clash 22). Padding runs top, right, bottom, left.
  chip:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.surface-raised}'
    textColor: '{colors.text-primary}'
    fontSize: '0.8125rem'
    fontWeight: 400
    rounded: '{rounded.pill}'
    padding: '{spacing.1} {spacing.3} {spacing.1} {spacing.2}'
    border: '1px solid {colors.border-default}'
  chip-selected:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.accent-primary-soft}'
    textColor: '{colors.accent-primary}'
    border: '1px solid {colors.accent-primary}'
  chip-dense:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.surface-sunken}'
    textColor: '{colors.text-primary}'
    fontSize: '0.8125rem'
    fontWeight: 600
    rounded: '{rounded.pill}'
    padding: '{spacing.1} {spacing.3} {spacing.1} {spacing.2}'
    border: '1px solid transparent'
  badge-pending:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.surface-sunken}'
    textColor: '{colors.text-secondary}'
    typography: '{typography.label}'
    rounded: '{rounded.pill}'
    padding: '{spacing.1} {spacing.3}'
  # The public bar meter (clash 19). One hue, no gradient, the number printed beside it.
  meter-track:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.surface-sunken}'
    rounded: '{rounded.pill}'
    height: '6px'
  meter-fill:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.accent-primary}'
    rounded: '{rounded.pill}'
  score-display:
    backgroundColor: 'transparent'
    textColor: '{colors.accent-primary}'
    typography: '{typography.figure-score}'
    padding: '{spacing.0}'
  detail-layout:
    # The band shell (clash 16). Phone values first, then md and up, as
    # browse-layout does. Band padding and page gutter, from the mock-ups.
    # md_padding takes the band padding from md up, but its 48px gutter only
    # from lg up. Between md and lg the gutter is 32px. See "The detail page band shell".
    backgroundColor: '{colors.surface-base}'
    textColor: '{colors.text-primary}'
    padding: '{spacing.16} {spacing.6}'
    md_padding: '{spacing.24} {spacing.12}'
    sectionDivider: '1px solid {colors.border-default}'
  browse-layout:
    # Mock-up 10 and ruling 3 (2026-11-16, AECI-1793): the 1328px page wrap
    # (BAND_WRAP_CLASS), gutters 24, 32 and 48px. The header is 24px over 32px,
    # 32px over 40px from md, with a full-width hairline below. The body is 32px
    # over 64px, 40px over 96px from md. Padding lists top and bottom, then gutter.
    backgroundColor: '{colors.surface-base}'
    textColor: '{colors.text-primary}'
    padding: '{spacing.8} {spacing.6}'
    md_padding: '{spacing.10} {spacing.8}'
    sectionDivider: '1px solid {colors.border-default}'
  # Colour references below use the AECI-1747 token keys (surface-footer,
  # surface-hero-panel, border-field and the on-brand washes and borders).
  # The public shell (clashes 26 to 32 and 42, PLAN.md sections 4.7 to 4.10).
  # Starting values from mock-up 6, default state, in
  # docs/design/mockups/redesign-2026-11/mockups/6-product-detail.html. Section 5
  # "Navigation" gives the file line for each and the open values. Colours named
  # as var() are the AECI-1747 tokens, which are not in the colors group above
  # until that issue lands.
  header:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.accent-primary}'
    textColor: '{colors.accent-warm}'
    height: '64px'
    md_height: '76px'
    padding: '{spacing.0} {spacing.6}'
    md_padding: '{spacing.0} {spacing.8}'
    lg_padding: '{spacing.0} {spacing.12}'
  # Sign in hover (the Bone main button on Forest). Forest text is 9.46:1 on it.
  button-on-brand-hover:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.surface-on-brand-hover}'
    textColor: '{colors.accent-primary}'
  # A nav link on Forest. The hover wash is Bone at 10%. Text stays Bone.
  header-link:
    textColor: '{colors.accent-warm}'
    typography: '{typography.body}'
    rounded: '{rounded.pill}'
    padding: '{spacing.3} {spacing.4}'
  header-link-hover:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.surface-on-brand-wash}'
  # Active route: weight 600 and aria-current.
  header-link-active:
    fontWeight: 600
  # Panel-open cue under Browse only.
  active-bar:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.accent-secondary}'
    height: '3px'
    rounded: '3px 3px 0 0'
  # The search field needs its own edge. The wash alone is 1.33:1 on Forest.
  header-search:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.surface-on-brand-wash}'
    textColor: '{colors.text-on-brand-secondary}'
    typography: '{typography.body}'
    rounded: '{rounded.pill}'
    minHeight: '44px'
    padding: '{spacing.0} {spacing.4}'
    border: '1px solid {colors.border-on-brand-strong}'
  header-search-hover:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.surface-on-brand-wash-hover}'
    textColor: '{colors.accent-warm}'
  nav-toggle:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.surface-on-brand-wash}'
    textColor: '{colors.accent-warm}'
    rounded: '{rounded.control}'
    width: '44px'
    height: '44px'
  # The Browse panel and the mobile panel share this white ground.
  menu-panel:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.surface-base}'
    textColor: '{colors.text-primary}'
    rounded: '0 0 {rounded.card} {rounded.card}'
    padding: '{spacing.12} {spacing.12} {spacing.8}'
    shadow: 'var(--shadow-menu)'
  # The stuck bar is solid. No blur (clash 13). Once stuck it takes a hairline edge.
  section-nav:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.surface-base}'
    textColor: '{colors.text-secondary}'
    typography: '{typography.body}'
    minHeight: '64px'
    md_minHeight: '72px'
    borderBottom: '1px solid {colors.border-default}'
  section-nav-current:
    textColor: '{colors.text-primary}'
    fontWeight: 600
  section-nav-count:
    textColor: '{colors.text-secondary}'
    fontSize: '0.8125rem'
    fontWeight: 400
  # The footer ground. Ink is Bone. Labels take var(--text-on-footer-secondary).
  footer:
    backgroundColor: '{colors.surface-footer}'
    textColor: '{colors.accent-warm}'
    padding: '{spacing.16} {spacing.0} {spacing.8}'
---

# Design System: AEC Integrations

## 1. Overview

**Creative North Star: "The specifier's reference."**

AECi reads as an editorial industry reference — a published artifact closer in spirit to a printed product specification or a specialist trade journal than to a SaaS landing page. The reader is an AEC professional making a serious decision; the surface should feel built for that job, not built for a marketing demo. Restraint, density, generous whitespace, sentence case, and a serif headline that borrows posture from `ENR` or `Architectural Record` — never from a startup landing page.

The system rejects the AI-startup visual cohort entirely: no purple-to-blue gradients, no glassmorphism, no hero-metric template, no Inter / DM Sans / Plus Jakarta Sans / Geist / Mona Sans / Space Grotesk / IBM Plex Sans / Outfit / Fraunces / Newsreader / Playfair Display / Cormorant / DM Serif / Instrument Serif / Syne. It also rejects the horizontal-SaaS-directory cohort (G2, Capterra, GetApp): no equal-weight feature grids, no logo clouds as proof, no orange-and-cream marketplace cream. Forest, Bone, and Clay carry brand identity as accents; surfaces stay neutral and let content lead.

**Key Characteristics:**

- Editorial restraint over commercial enthusiasm. Sentence case everywhere (one named exception: the overline role, §3; one ruled label, "Claim Vendor", under the Sentence-Case Rule).
- Pair a refined serif (Source Serif 4) with an a11y-first sans (Atkinson Hyperlegible Next). The pairing itself is the brand statement. A limited ramp of eleven sizes keeps it calm. Weights are 400 and 600, with the exceptions §3 names (clashes 9 and 17).
- Forest is the anchor accent and, in three chrome bands and one feature card, a ground: the header, the hero of a detail page, the footer and a card (clash 1). The reading area between them is white. Bone is a warm-tinted accent surface, one band per page (clash 2), and it is the ink on Forest. Never a page background. Clay is rare (≤5% per screen, decorative/fill only, Clay deep carries meaning-bearing clay). It never fills a button and never carries text (clashes 3 and 4).
- Borders separate controls. One card shadow lifts the cards that float on the page ground, and one menu shadow lifts menus. The modal shadow is the only other shadow. The focus ring is an outline (clash 11, §4). Cards are 24px, controls 12px and chips are pills (clash 12).
- One custom icon set at 1.75 stroke, drawn for the site and served by one component (clash 34, §5 "Icons"). No emoji.
- Motion gives feedback and never decorates: three durations, one easing, no decorative loop (clash 36, §5 "Motion").
- Light only: a single light theme, no toggle and no system-preference detection (AECI-226). The dark reintroduction was dropped — dark is not roadmapped (`STAGE_2_SPEC.md` §9); design once, for light.
- Scope: ruling R2 (2026-11-14) builds the public pages first. Shared components (the header, the footer, the button and the input) are one component on every surface. Portal and admin page bodies wait until Chris has verified the public pages (§5 Components, "Scope").

## 2. Colors

A neutral surface palette with three brand accents (Forest, Clay, Bone) and two near-monochrome text scales. The reading area is white. Forest may fill three chrome bands: the header, the hero of a detail page and the footer (see "Forest grounds" below). Frontmatter values are OKLCH for perceptual uniformity (and to enforce Impeccable's color doctrine); each color's documented hex equivalent is the intended sRGB value used by `apps/web/src/styles.css` and `docs/BRAND_GUIDELINES.md`. Every opaque token renders its documented hex exactly; `design-tokens.hex.guard.spec.ts` pins it.

The redesign rulings of 2026-11-13 (`docs/design/mockups/redesign-2026-11/records/design-rulings.md`) changed this section. A clash number beside a rule means that ruling set it. Where a ruling left a value open, AECI-1747 settled it under "Settled values". Light theme only still holds: no dark theme, no `dark:` variants.

### Primary

- **Forest** (`#1E3A2F` / `oklch(32.24% 0.0397 166.73)`): the primary brand color. CTAs, links, headings, the connector mark, primary badges. Hover state is **Forest Hover** (`#2E5C45` / `oklch(43.47% 0.0641 160.30)`) — measurably brighter, never a synthetic opacity reduction. Forest is also a ground: it may fill the header, the hero of a detail page and the footer (clash 1), and it is the fill of a pressed toggle (clash 5).
- **Forest soft** (`#ECF1EE` / `oklch(95.35% 0.0066 160.07)`): the Forest _wash_ — fills for selected facets, selected filter chips (clash 5), verified-soft and info states. It is never a page band (clash 2). It is how Forest registers at rest without recoloring text. A wash, not a boundary (1.14:1 against white): always paired with a border or another selected-state affordance. Forest text on it measures 10.80:1.

### Secondary

- **Clay** (`#E89668` / `oklch(74.64% 0.1164 49.55)`): warm secondary accent — **decorative and fill only**. Strokes and accents are its job: the connector mark, the hero ribbon, accent strokes. It never fills a button (clash 3) and never carries text (clash 4). Existing badge fills stay, carrying `text-primary` at 8.48:1 — never white text, which is 2.33:1. It measures ~2.3:1 on white, below even the 3:1 large-text/non-text floor, so it can never carry text or meaning-bearing graphics — see the Clay restriction below.
- **Clay deep** (`#A14D22` / `oklch(51.86% 0.1245 45.13)`): the text-capable member of the Clay family — clay-colored text and icons. 5.83:1 on white, 5.59:1 on raised, 5.21:1 on Bone. Doubles as the warning hue. It stays the onboarding series colour in rating displays (clash 4). The token is `--accent-secondary-deep`, and all four final mock-ups use it. It is not the colour of kicker icons, facet-heading icons or the focus ring on public pages (clash 4).

### Rating

- **Goldenrod** (`#DAA520` / `oklch(75.16% 0.1469 83.99)`): the gold-star fill for rating glyphs (`<aec-review-stars>` and the review-form rating listboxes) — token `--accent-rating`. The conventional gold star, tuned warm to sit with the Clay/Bone family rather than a neon yellow. It measures 2.24:1 on white, _below_ the 3:1 meaning-bearing-graphic floor — permitted because the star glyphs are **decorative** (`aria-hidden`): the rating is carried by the `aria-label` (and, in displays, the adjacent numeral), never by glyph color alone. In read-only displays (`<aec-review-stars>`, vendor-detail) empty stars use **Border strong** `#D4D4D8` as a faint track so the gold reads against it. The **interactive review-form picker** instead uses **Text tertiary** `#71717A` for unselected stars: it has no adjacent numeral and renders fully unselected on first load, so a faint `#D4D4D8` track would leave the whole control near-invisible until hovered. (The dedicated Goldenrod gives ratings their own recognizable color and frees Clay deep to mean "text-capable clay / warning.")

### Tertiary

- **Bone** (`#F5F2EA` / `oklch(96.13% 0.0111 89.72)`): warm-tinted _accent surface_. One tinted band per page, for its key section (clash 2). **Not a page background.** On a Forest ground Bone is also the ink: text and the fill of the main button (clashes 3 and 4).

### Neutral

- **Surface base** (`#FFFFFF` / `oklch(100% 0 0)`): default page background.
- **Surface raised** (`#FAFAFA` / `oklch(98.43% 0 0)`): cards, panels.
- **Surface sunken** (`#F4F4F5` / `oklch(96.74% 0.0013 286.38)`): inset wells, code blocks, secondary states.
- **Surface muted** (`#F4F4F5` / `oklch(96.74% 0.0013 286.38)`): interactive row hover / `focus-within` highlight on index tables. Shares the surface-sunken value by design — the two never co-occur inside a row, and a hover wants the same one-step lift sunken gives on white.
- **Border default** (`#E4E4E7` / `oklch(91.97% 0.0036 286.32)`): standard separators. A 1px hairline on the redesigned public components (mock-up 6, line 130, and mock-up 7, line 1133). Some shipped surfaces still draw 0.5px and change as each is rebuilt.
- **Border strong** (`#D4D4D8` / `oklch(87.20% 0.0055 286.32)`): emphasized borders (focus, featured states).
- **Field edge** (`#84848D` / `oklch(61.63% 0.0134 286.00)`, `--border-field`): the redesign's border for every form control (PLAN.md §4.1). 3.71:1 on white, 3.55:1 on raised, 3.37:1 on sunken, 3.31:1 on Bone. Every form control takes it since AECI-1764. It fails on the hero panel (2.77:1) and on Forest hover (2.07:1). It holds on Forest itself (3.33:1) and on the footer ground (4.14:1). It is 2.65:1 on `surface-sunken-hover`, so a control never sits on that fill.
- **Border input** (`#8A8A93` / `oklch(63.63% 0.0133 286.02)`): a plain 3:1 line, 3.42:1 on white, 3.28:1 on raised, 3.11:1 on sunken, so it meets WCAG 1.4.11's 3:1 for user-interface components. `border-default` measures about 1.25:1 and stays the separator token. **Since AECI-1764 it is no longer a control edge** (that is Field edge, above). It is the plain 3:1 line for three non-control uses: the scroll-box scrollbar, the dashed "not measured" chart outline and the "suspected automation" chart fill (`admin/overview/traffic-classes.ts`).
- **Text primary** (`#0A0A0A` / `oklch(14.48% 0 0)`): body and headings. Near-black, not pure black — gentler against bright surfaces, lower halation.
- **Text secondary** (`#52525B` / `oklch(44.19% 0.0146 285.79)`): supporting prose, captions.
- **Text tertiary** (`#71717A` / `oklch(55.17% 0.0138 285.94)`): hints, placeholders, metadata. Re-pointed from `#A1A1AA` in AECI-230 — the old value measured 2.56:1 on white, failing even the 3:1 large-text floor. Now 4.83:1 on white, 4.63:1 on raised. **Never on sunken/muted surfaces** (4.40:1 there) — step up to text secondary.

### Status

- **Error** (`#B3261E` / `oklch(50.13% 0.1783 28.70)`): form/validation error text and icons, **and the `conflict` agreement badge** (6.54:1 on white). Always paired with an inline message or icon — color is never the sole signal. Success states use Forest; warning states use Clay deep. No additional status hues exist.
  - **The scope widened in AECI-605** from form/validation only. `conflict` — two vendors describing the same data flow differently — is the **one non-form state permitted to be red**, and the only red state in the agreement set (`STAGE_2_ATTESTATIONS_SPEC.md` §4.3). It is red because it is genuinely actionable for the reader, not because anything is broken: the copy names the disagreement ("Vendors disagree") rather than faulting either product. It carries an `✕` glyph alongside the hue so it survives greyscale and colour-vision deficiency. Do not extend Error to any further state without a spec decision — `unverified` and `single_source` are deliberately neutral, and making an unconfirmed claim look like a defect is the failure mode this system is built to avoid.
  - **Admin claim account conflict (AECI-1420, 2026-10-19).** A second sanctioned red, on admin surfaces only. When a claimant's address is a site admin or holds a seat on another vendor, `/admin/claims` and `/admin/claims/:id` show a box with the Error border (`.aec-notice-error`), a `--surface-raised` surface and an icon, and the queue disables Grant. It is red because the grant will be refused. Spec: `STAGE_2_VENDOR_PORTAL_SPEC.md` §5. No tinted Error surface token exists, so none was added.
  - **Admin email-domain signal (2026-11-02).** A third sanctioned red, on admin surfaces only. `<aec-domain-match-signal>` (`admin/claims/domain-match-signal.ts`), shared by `/admin/claims` and `/admin/claims/:id`, shows "Domain mismatch" as bold Error text with a red X. It is red because it is the fact an operator must act on before granting. "Domain matches" is bold primary text with a Forest check. "Manual review" and "Domain check pending" are bold primary text with no icon. All four are `text-xs`, and the words carry the meaning, so the icons are `aria-hidden`. Spec: `STAGE_2_VENDOR_PORTAL_SPEC.md` §5.

### Forest grounds (clashes 1 to 6)

Forest and its companions fill three chrome bands. Every pairing on them states its contrast floor. Ratios below are WCAG 2 relative-luminance ratios computed from the hex values, to two decimals.

| Role                                | Hex       | Where it may be used                                                                      | Ink on it                   |
| ----------------------------------- | --------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------- |
| Forest                              | `#1E3A2F` | Header, hero of a detail page, footer, one feature card, primary buttons, pressed toggles | Bone 11.03:1, white 12.34:1 |
| Forest hover                        | `#2E5C45` | Hover states, the soft single-colour glow in the hero (existing `--accent-primary-hover`) | Bone 6.87:1                 |
| Hero panel (`--surface-hero-panel`) | `#26473A` | Panels sitting on the Forest hero                                                         | Bone 9.18:1                 |
| Footer ground (`--surface-footer`)  | `#15291F` | Footer only                                                                               | Bone 13.71:1                |
| Bone                                | `#F5F2EA` | One band per page, ink on Forest, the main button on Forest                               | Forest 11.03:1              |

Floors that hold on every ground in the table:

- Text needs 4.5:1. Large text and meaning-bearing graphics need 3:1.
- The hero panel against Forest is 1.20:1, so a panel edge is a surface cue, not a boundary. A panel that holds a control needs its own 3:1 edge. Field edge on the hero panel is 2.77:1, so a control placed on a hero panel needs a filled background to keep a 3:1 boundary.
- Clay on Forest measures 5.29:1. Clay is a stroke or accent there and still carries no text (clash 4).
- The `--text-tertiary` restrictions stand. It is never on Forest, hero panel, footer ground, Bone, sunken or muted surfaces.
- Any text colour on a Forest ground other than Bone, `--text-on-brand-secondary` and `--text-on-footer-secondary` is not allowed. The measured pairings are under "Settled values".

The rules the rulings set:

- **Forest ground (clash 1, new).** Forest may fill the header, the hero of a detail page and the footer. The reading area stays white. This rewrites the Surfaces-Are-Neutral Rule (see Named Rules).
- **A Forest feature card (clash 1, new).** The clash 1 record is titled "Forest as a full-bleed ground for the header, hero, footer and feature cards". Its new design reads "`.know` (page 8) and `.cm-card` are Forest cards" (`records/design-clashes.md`, lines 9 and 11). Chris ruled NEW, so the new design wins. Ruling R4 dropped `.cm-card`, the Browse menu's card. What remains is the "Know of one?" card in mock-up 8 (`.know`, `8-product-none.html`, line 1100), built on the zero-integration product page by AECI-1720 restore C (§5 "The zero-integration state"). It is a card, not a band: 24px radius, 32px padding, the card shadow, a Forest fill, Bone ink and the Bone main button (`btn-hero`, `--cta-bg: #F5F2EA`, mock-up 6, lines 12 and 90). The mock-ups draw at most one per page. So the limits are: a card and never a full-bleed band, Bone ink, the Bone main button, and one per page. Its secondary text in the mock-up is `#C5D3CA`, which §2 has not measured ("Any text colour on a Forest ground other than Bone is not measured here"). The shipped plan panel (`aec-plan-cta-panel`) is such a card (§5 "Plans calls to action"). **The plan call to action is the page's one Forest feature card** (AECI-1766), so a page that carries it carries no other. AECI-1802 rebuilt it on the home card's recipe: `aec-card`, Forest fill, Bone ink (`--text-on-brand`), `shadow-card`, `rounded-card`, 32px padding (48px from `lg` when split), the Bone main button and a Bone focus ring on its contact link. On `/vendor-program` it is why "How listings are sorted", the no pay-for-placement argument, is a white card and not a Forest one. **The home page has no trust pillars band.** `home/home-trust-pillars.ts` has been on `/about` only since AECI-273. Its full-bleed mid-page Forest ground is gone since AECI-1800. Chris ruled on 2026-11-17 that the three commitments are `/about`'s one Forest feature card: a card on a white band, never a band, built with the home card's recipe below. On the home, the one Forest feature card is "What we do instead" in `home/home-why.ts` (mock-up 9, question 1, approved by Chris; built by AECI-1792). It follows the limits above: a card, Bone ink, `--text-on-brand-secondary` for its secondary text, the Bone main button, one per page. ADR 0059 says "Forest may fill the header, the detail page hero and the footer" and omits the feature card.
- **One Bone band (clash 2, blend).** The body is white. One Bone band per page. No Forest-soft bands, no deep-green bands, no alternating bands.
- **Buttons on Forest (clash 3, blend).** Clay never fills a button. On Forest the main button is Bone with Forest text. Everywhere else the main button stays Forest with white text.
- **Clay and text (clash 4, blend).** Clay does not carry text. Product names highlighted on Forest are Bone. Clay deep stays the onboarding series colour. Kicker icons, chip fills and the focus ring do not take Clay.
- **Selected states (clash 5, blend).** A pressed toggle or segmented control is solid Forest with white text (12.34:1). A selected filter chip keeps the Forest-soft wash, with a border or other selected-state cue. The view toggle is Forest, never near-black.
- **Tokens only (clash 6, blend).** Every colour is a named OKLCH token in `apps/web/src/styles.css`. The new surfaces (hero panel, footer ground) are tokens, and so is every alpha the mock-ups mixed with `color-mix()`. The mock-up's other loose values are dropped, including its extra band, soft-accent, empty-star, venn and meter tokens. No `color-mix()` in components, no tinted shadows, no hex in components, and no Tailwind opacity modifier on a colour utility (`text-(--surface-base)/70`): it mints an alpha nobody measured and blends it in OKLab. The lint (root `eslint.color-patterns.mjs`, and `apps/web/scripts/check-source-constraints.mjs` for `.html` and `.css`) catches colour literals and opacity modifiers. A `color-mix()` over two tokens is review-only.
- **Field edge (PLAN.md §4.1 and clash 23, not clash 6).** `#84848D` is the border of every form control. It is the token `--border-field`. AECI-1764 applied it through `.aec-field-control`.

Scope: ruling R2 (2026-11-14) builds the public pages first. Shared components (the header, the footer, the button and the input) are one component on every surface (Chris, 2026-11-14, see §5 Components, "Scope"). Portal and admin page bodies wait until Chris has verified the public pages, and take the new tokens and nothing more until then.

### Settled values (AECI-1747)

These were open after the rulings. The tokens are in `apps/web/src/styles.css` with their ratios beside them. Ratios are WCAG 2, to two decimals. Text needs 4.5:1, large text and boundaries 3:1.

- **Token names.** Hero panel `--surface-hero-panel`, footer ground `--surface-footer`, field edge `--border-field`, Bone ink on Forest `--text-on-brand` (an alias of `--accent-warm`).
- **Field edge and Border input.** `--border-field` is the edge of every form control. `--border-input` (`#8A8A93`) is no longer a control edge. It stays as the plain 3:1 line for the scroll-box scrollbar, the dashed "not measured" chart outline and the "suspected automation" chart fill, which still read it. Retire it when those three move.
- **Other inks on Forest.** `--text-on-brand-secondary` (`#C5D3CA`): 7.96:1 on Forest, 6.62 on the hero panel, 9.89 on the footer ground, 4.96 on Forest hover. `--text-on-footer-secondary` (`#B9C7BF`): 8.76:1 on the footer ground, 7.04 on Forest, 5.86 on the hero panel. It is not allowed on Forest hover (4.39:1). Tertiary text is never allowed on a Forest ground.
- **Focus ring on Forest.** Bone, `--focus-ring-on-brand` (an alias of `--accent-warm`): 11.03:1 on Forest, 6.87 on Forest hover, 9.18 on the hero panel, 13.71 on the footer ground.
- **The four drifted tokens.** Forest, Forest hover, Clay and Bone now render the hex this file documents. The OKLCH values changed, the hex did not.
- **Mock-up values kept as tokens.** `--surface-on-brand-hover` (`#E7E1D2`, the one hover for the Bone main button on Forest, Forest text 9.46:1), `--surface-hero-ribbon` (`#3B6E55`, 2.08:1 on Forest, decorative only), `--surface-sunken-hover` (`#DADADC`), `--border-underline` (`#B5B5B5`, 2.05:1 on white) and `--border-empty` (`#C6C6CA`, 1.70:1 on white). The last two are never the only cue.
- **Alphas on Forest.** Bone at 10% `--surface-on-brand-wash` (fills), 16% `--surface-on-brand-wash-hover`, 14% `--border-on-brand-subtle` (hairlines), 32% `--border-on-brand`. Bone at 50%, `--border-on-brand-strong`, is the only on-Forest alpha that is a 3:1 edge: 4.01:1 on Forest, 3.61 on the hero panel, 4.53 on the footer ground. Forest hover at 75% and 45% are `--surface-hero-glow` and `--surface-hero-glow-soft`. White at 94% is `--surface-sticky`. White-ground hairlines use `--border-default`, and the selected-count wash uses `--accent-primary-soft`.
- **Mock-up tokens dropped.** `hero-accent-hover`, `hero-accent-ink`, `star-empty`, `venn-a`, `venn-b`, `accent-soft`, `meter-a`, `meter-b`, `logo-clay`, the Forest 16% focus halo of `.field:focus` (the focus ring replaces it) and Forest at 40%, which only the `html.r-btn-orig` restore toggle uses.

### Dark theme — dropped from the roadmap (not shipped)

The dark palette was removed from the active design system in **AECI-226**: AECi ships light only (see the "Light only" rule below). The Stage 2 dark-theme reintroduction was subsequently **dropped** (epic AECI-517 canceled; `STAGE_2_SPEC.md` §9) — dark is **not roadmapped**. The brand-approved dark Forest / Clay / Bone variants stay documented in `docs/BRAND_GUIDELINES.md` §3, and the prior OKLCH dark token set is preserved in git history, so _should_ a later stage ever revisit dark it would be a token-block + toggle change — every component consumes the semantic tokens (`--surface-*`, `--text-*`, `--accent-*`), not a per-component rework — but nothing is planned.

### Named Rules

**The Surfaces-Are-Neutral Rule** (rewritten by clashes 1 and 2, see ADR 0059). The reading area is neutral. The `<body>` is `#FFFFFF`. Forest may fill the header, the hero of a detail page, the footer and a Forest feature card, and nothing else. Bone is _not_ a page background. It is one warm-tinted band per page. Forest soft is never a band. The old form of this rule banned every brand colour as a ground. That ban is lifted for those three Forest bands and the feature card only.

**The Forest-Anchor Rule.** Forest is the primary brand accent and the anchor of the system. Every CTA, every link, every heading color, every primary badge fill: Forest. No alternative primary color exists — proposals for "a second primary" are rejected. One change (clash 3): on a Forest ground a Forest button vanishes, so the main button there is the inverse, Bone with Forest text. It is still the one primary action, and it is not Clay.

**The Clay-Restriction Rule.** Clay is the rarest color in the system. ≤5% of any screen. Clay `#E89668` is **decorative and fill only**: the connector mark, accent strokes, and badge fills carrying `text-primary` (8.48:1) — never white text (2.33:1). It measures ~2.3:1 on white, which fails not just AA body text but the 3:1 floor for large text and meaning-bearing graphics — the former "large-text allowed" clause was mathematically false and was struck in AECI-230. Anything clay-colored that _carries meaning_ — text, icons — uses **Clay deep** `#A14D22` (5.83:1). (Star-rating glyphs are _not_ clay: they use the dedicated **Goldenrod** `--accent-rating` — see the Rating palette above.) Clay never fills a CTA: the Forest-Anchor Rule owns every CTA, including the Bone-on-Forest inverse (clash 3). Clay does not carry text on any ground, so highlighted names on Forest are Bone (clash 4). Kept unchanged by the rulings: the 5% cap and the Clay deep rule for anything meaning-bearing. Keeping Clay rare preserves its meaning as the high-emphasis accent. (The brand policy would carry forward to Dark Clay if dark is ever revisited; dark theme is not currently roadmapped — the Stage 2 reintroduction was dropped.)

**The No-Pure-Black-Or-White Rule.** `#000` and `#fff` never appear in this system. The text-primary token is `#0A0A0A` (a near-black) on a `#FFFFFF` surface base. Pure-black-on-pure-white is harsher than the near-tones and creates unnecessary halation. (The same principle would govern the dark variants documented in `BRAND_GUIDELINES.md` §3 if dark is ever revisited; it is not currently roadmapped.)

**The Anchor-Site Rule.** When a surface uses a Mobbin reference site as its theme, components for that surface come from the _same_ Mobbin site. Pulling components from a second site is a deliberate exception, not a default — and the originating theme site remains the visual anchor for composition, hierarchy, density, and atmosphere. This protects editorial coherence: AECi reads as one publication, not a mashup of unrelated apps. Record the anchor site with the surface (Linear issue or commit message) so future iterations stay aligned. Access Mobbin via the `mcp__mobbin__*` MCP server — see `CLAUDE.md` §"MCP usage rules" for auth flow and the matching design-checklist step.

**The redesign's anchor (clash 44, current).** Chris ruled CURRENT on clash 44 and left no note: the rule stands, and every surface records an anchor. The redesign's mock-ups are the anchor for the new language: `docs/design/mockups/redesign-2026-11/mockups/`, with `PLAN.md` §4 as the written form. Mock-ups 5 to 8 are the empty pair page, the product page, the product with many integrations and the product with none. Mock-ups 9 to 12 are the home, the browse lists, the vendor page and the pair page with integrations. Chris approved 9 to 12 on 2026-11-16, and their notes in `records/` hold his rulings. Use Mobbin only for components the mock-ups do not cover (`PLAN.md` §9.1). The mock-ups draw the header and the footer, so they are the anchor for site chrome too. The pages no mock-up covers (the content pages, the help center and the flows) follow the built `/about` page and these components. That was the build's choice on 2026-11-17, not a ruling. The earlier Faire anchor (`docs/design/home-direction.md`) is retired for the home. A precedent for an in-repo mock-up as the anchor is the Free plan surfaces (§5 "Vendor portal (Stage 2)").

## 3. Typography

**Display Font:** Source Serif 4 (with a size-matched Georgia face, then Georgia, "Times New Roman", serif fallback)
**Body Font:** Atkinson Hyperlegible Next (with a size-matched Arial face, then system-ui, -apple-system, "Segoe UI", sans-serif fallback)
**Label Font:** Atkinson Hyperlegible Next (same family as body, distinct role via size + weight)

**How the fonts load (AECI-1892).** Both fonts are self-hosted under `/fonts/` (`apps/web/public/fonts/`, with their SIL Open Font License files), declared in `apps/web/src/index.html`, with `font-display: swap` and no preload (a preload delayed first paint). Until they arrive, text paints in a local font scaled to the web font's width and line box, so lines break in the same places and the swap moves nothing. One exception: a width in `ch` units. `ch` is the width of the "0" in the font in use, and Atkinson's "0" is wide, so a `max-w-[70ch]` column is 726px with the web font and 617px with the Arial fallback. The long-form pages that still use a `ch` measure (`/about`, contact, roadmap, methodology) re-wrap their first paragraphs at the swap at desktop widths: layout shift 0.021 on `/about` at 1280px, measured 2026-11-17. A measure in `rem` removes it. Since AECI-1804 `/legal/*` and `/accessibility` use the docs article's `42rem`: `/legal/terms` at 1280px went from 0.072 to 0.012 with the fonts delayed 1.5s, measured 2026-11-17. Source Serif 4 narrows as it grows (its optical-size axis), so the serif fallback comes in size bands, one face per band. Each serif role names its band in `styles.css` ("Fallback faces by role"). A role at the browser's default optical size follows its `clamp()` across bands: `--t-fig` moves from the 32 face to the 40 face at 1024px. A role pinned at opsz 60 (below, "How glyphs are drawn") is as wide as at 60px at every size, so it takes the opsz 60 face at every width: Section (`--t-36`), the score figure (`--t-48`), Statement and Display. That face is within 0.3% of the web font's width from 28 to 72px, in both weights (measured 2026-11-18). A new serif size role needs a band there too. Uppercase overlines use their own sans face. Rules: do not load a font from another origin, do not move an `@font-face` into `styles.css` (the critical-CSS pass drops it), and give a changed font file a new file name. `e2e/font-loading.spec.ts` guards the first two and the one-year cache header that makes the third necessary. `src/font-files.guard.spec.ts` guards the third: it pins each file's bytes, so a file changed under its old name fails the unit tests.

**How glyphs are drawn (AECI-1927).** The approved mock-ups draw text lighter than the browser's defaults. Three settings do it, and `styles.css` sets each once, for the whole site:

- **Grayscale smoothing on every ground.** `body` sets `-webkit-font-smoothing: antialiased`, as all eight mock-ups (5 to 12) do. On macOS the default thickens every stroke. This setting stops that, most visibly for Bone text on Forest. `body` also sets `-moz-osx-font-smoothing: grayscale`, so Firefox on macOS draws the same as Safari and Chrome. The mock-ups left it out by omission, not by intent. Windows ignores both.
- **The font's kerning and ligatures.** `body` sets `text-rendering: optimizeLegibility`. Mock-ups 5 to 8 set it and 9 to 12 do not. That is a tie. The build follows 5 to 8, as AECI-1927 asked.
- **Optical size by role.** Source Serif 4 carries an optical-size axis, opsz 8 to 60, in the self-hosted files. By default the browser picks the cut from the px size, so a 28px heading draws the heavier opsz 28 cut. The display-scale roles pin opsz 60, the display cut, with finer strokes: Section (`--t-36`, and `md:text-t-36` from `md`), the score figure (`--t-48`), Statement and Display. The roles below them keep the default: Headline and count figures (`--t-fig`), Title (`--t-24`) and Card title (`--t-20`). The rule sits on the size utilities and on the classes that set a size themselves (`.aec-docs-title`, `.aec-plan-price`), under "Optical size by role" in `styles.css`. A heading element's own size rule beats a size utility on it, so `h1`, `h2` and `h3` keep the default unless an inner span carries a pinned size. The sans has no opsz axis and ignores the rule.

How the mock-ups split. Mock-ups 5 to 8 (the product and empty pair pages) pin opsz 60 on their statement, product name, score figure and section headings. Mock-ups 9 to 12 (home, browse, vendor, pair) set no optical size. The two agree at desktop for Display and Statement, where the default is 56 to 60 anyway. They differ for the 28 to 36px section heading and for every display-scale role on a phone. The build pins those roles, because 5 to 8 are the only mock-ups that state a value. So home, browse, vendor and the pair page draw their section headings slightly finer than mock-ups 9 to 12. Exceptions the role rule does not copy: the product page's About first sentence (mock-ups 6 to 8 pin it at opsz 36, the build keeps the `--t-24` default), and the 36px initial tile in the product hero, which takes opsz 60 from its size class.

Do not set `font-variation-settings`, `font-optical-sizing`, font smoothing or `text-rendering` in a component. Change the role rule instead. `design-tokens.guard.spec.ts` fails on any of them outside `styles.css`, and pins each role's value and its opsz 60 fallback. Contrast does not change with stroke weight: the lowest serif text on the public pages measured 9.18:1, on the Forest hero panel (2026-11-18).

The redesign rulings of 2026-11-13 changed this section (clashes 7 to 10 and 17). A clash number beside a rule means that ruling set it. The fonts did not change. The scope sentence under "Forest grounds" in §2 applies to this section too: ruling R2 builds the public pages first. Shared components (the header, the footer, the button and the input) are one component on every surface. Portal and admin page bodies wait until Chris has verified the public pages (§5 Components, "Scope"). Values the rulings left open are listed under "Open values (set by AECI-1748 and AECI-1749)".

**Character:** Source Serif 4 carries the editorial / industry-publication posture of a printed reference — calmer and more grounded than the SaaS-default sans cohort, but without the stylized affectation of the reflex-reject serifs (Fraunces, Playfair Display, Cormorant, DM Serif, Instrument Serif). Atkinson Hyperlegible Next is an a11y-first sans designed by the Braille Institute for low-vision readers; pairing it with a serif display face makes the trust/transparency principle visible in the typography itself. The pairing is the brand statement: a system that treats the reader's vision as a constraint to engineer for, not an afterthought to "support."

> **Why Next (AECI-230):** the classic Atkinson Hyperlegible ships only 400/700, so this system's 500 (chips) and 600 (labels) weights could not actually render — CSS font matching silently resolved 500 to 400. Atkinson Hyperlegible Next is the Braille Institute's expanded release (loaded as a 400–700 variable font), making every specified weight real. Same lineage, same a11y story.

### The type ramp (clashes 9 and 17)

Chris's note on clash 17: "I do believe we have too many font sizes and variants, see what it looks like to limit them." The plan's answer is one limited ramp. The audit of the mock-ups went from 36 distinct sizes to 10, and from 9 weights to 5 (`PLAN.md` §4.2). Clash 9 was ruled as a blend. Once AECI-1748 and AECI-1749 land, every size on a public page is one of these tokens. There were ten. Ruling A3 (2026-11-16) added an eleventh, `--t-48`, for the score figure. AECI-1835 added a twelfth, `--t-statement-wide`, so the empty pair page statement breaks as mock-up 5 draws it. It waits on Chris's ruling.

| Token                | Size              | rem             | Family | Use                           |
| -------------------- | ----------------- | --------------- | ------ | ----------------------------- |
| `--t-12`             | 12px              | 0.75            | Sans   | Overlines, captions           |
| `--t-13`             | 13px              | 0.8125          | Sans   | Chips, counts, small labels   |
| `--t-14`             | 14px              | 0.875           | Sans   | Buttons, secondary text       |
| `--t-16`             | 16px              | 1               | Sans   | Body, nav links               |
| `--t-20`             | 20px              | 1.25            | Both   | Lede text, card titles        |
| `--t-24`             | 24px              | 1.5             | Serif  | Sub-headings                  |
| `--t-36`             | 28 to 36px, fluid | 1.75 to 2.25    | Serif  | Section headings              |
| `--t-fig`            | 28 to 40px, fluid | 1.75 to 2.5     | Serif  | Count figures                 |
| `--t-48`             | 48px, fixed       | 3               | Serif  | Score figures (ruling A3)     |
| `--t-statement`      | 34 to 56px, fluid | 2.125 to 3.5    | Serif  | The pair page statement       |
| `--t-statement-wide` | 34 to 51px, fluid | 2.125 to 3.1875 | Serif  | The empty pair page statement |
| `--t-display`        | 40 to 72px, fluid | 2.5 to 4.5      | Serif  | The product name              |

rem is px divided by 16. Once AECI-1748 and AECI-1749 land, no other size is allowed on a public page. The mock-ups hold 19px and 22px serif and 12.5px sans only under their `html.r-type-orig` restore toggle, not in the default state. AECI-1749 snaps any that reach the build to the ramp.

- **Weights are 400 and 600** (clash 9). Bold 700 stays only for inline emphasis, as before. Weight 500 survives on exactly two chips that copy the live site: the maintenance marker (`.mchip`) and the agreement badge (`.agree`). Both are 12px at 500, matching §5 Badges "The public chip". They stay at 12px, which is already today's smallest text size. It is not a general weight. Since AECI-1666 the maintenance marker is plain text, not a chip, so the agreement badge is the one chip left at 500.
- **What may sit at `--t-12`.** Overlines, the two 500 chips above, field captions (12px, 20px line height), and the Browse menu's term counts (desktop panel and mobile menu, 2026-11-23: at 13px they crowded the 16px term names). Other counts sit at `--t-13` (`PLAN.md`).
- **Tailwind mapping (AECI-1748).** Every `text-*` size utility resolves to a ramp token. `styles.css` overrides Tailwind's `--text-*` theme variables, so the call sites did not change. Each step keeps Tailwind's own line height, so no multi-line text lost leading. The mapping changes as little as it can: five steps keep their pixels. This applies on every surface, the vendor portal and admin included.

  | Utility                  | Before      | Token         | After, 320 / 768 / 1280 / 1920px wide | Line height    |
  | ------------------------ | ----------- | ------------- | ------------------------------------- | -------------- |
  | `text-xs`                | 12px        | `--t-12`      | 12px, unchanged                       | 16px (1.333)   |
  | `text-sm`                | 14px        | `--t-14`      | 14px, unchanged                       | 20px (1.429)   |
  | `text-base`              | 16px        | `--t-16`      | 16px, unchanged                       | 24px (1.5)     |
  | `text-lg`                | 18px        | `--t-20`      | 20px                                  | 1.556, so 31px |
  | `text-xl`                | 20px        | `--t-20`      | 20px, unchanged                       | 28px (1.4)     |
  | `text-2xl`               | 24px        | `--t-24`      | 24px, unchanged                       | 32px (1.333)   |
  | `text-3xl`               | 30px        | `--t-36`      | 28 / 29.3 / 34.4 / 36px               | 1.2            |
  | `text-4xl`               | 36px        | `--t-36`      | 28 / 29.3 / 34.4 / 36px               | 1.111          |
  | `text-5xl`               | 48px        | `--t-fig`     | 28 / 31.2 / 40 / 40px                 | 1              |
  | `text-6xl` to `text-9xl` | 60 to 128px | `--t-display` | 45.7 / 56.4 / 68.7 / 72px             | 1              |
  - **`text-lg` lands on `--t-20`, not `--t-16`.** Most `text-lg` call sites are serif names and titles. Dropping them to 16px would break the Serif-Floor Rule, and `--t-20` is the card-title step. The sans ledes at `text-lg` carry `leading-relaxed`, so they keep 1.625.
  - **`text-3xl` and `text-4xl` both land on `--t-36`.** It is the nearest step at every width, and both are used as section headings and figures. The common `text-3xl md:text-4xl` pair now renders one fluid size.
  - **`text-5xl` lands on `--t-fig`.** Its only live use off a heading is the vendor portal's glance figure, which is a Figure. At `--t-statement` it rendered 56px at 1440px, larger than the portal's h1.
  - **`text-6xl` to `text-9xl` are unused.** They land on `--t-display` so no utility can render off the ramp.
  - **The ramp itself is exposed as `text-t-12` to `text-t-display`**, each with its role's line height: 20px for `--t-12` (the field caption), 1.4 for `--t-13`, 1.5 for `--t-14`, 1.6 for `--t-16`, 1.5 for `--t-20`, 1.25 for `--t-24`, 1.1 for `--t-36` and `--t-statement`, and 1 for `--t-fig`, `--t-48` and `--t-display`. `text-t-48` is the score figure. New code uses these names. A role that needs another leading at the same size adds a `leading-*` utility.
  - **A `text-*` utility on an `h1`, `h2` or `h3` element is still dead.** See the Unlayered-Heading Rule. Most `text-3xl` to `text-5xl` call sites sit on headings, so the remap does not move them.

- **Serif never sits on a sans-only step.** `--t-12` to `--t-16` are sans. Serif starts at `--t-20`.

### Hierarchy

- **Display** (Source Serif 4, 400): clash 7 (new) allows it on detail pages, where it is the product name at `--t-display`, 40 to 72px fluid. Start values from the mock-up: line-height 1, tracking -0.02em. **What renders (AECI-1847, decision of 2026-11-22: no hero changed size).** Display sits on three heroes, each on an inner `<span>`: the home hero (`--t-display` at every width, line-height 1.05), the product name and the vendor name (`aec-hero-band`: `--t-display` from `md`, `--t-statement` below `md`, `--t-fig` below 360px). The pair page statement is Statement and the empty pair page statement is the wide Statement (`--t-statement-wide`). Every other page `h1`, About included, renders the global `h1` size (Headline, `--t-fig`, 28 to 40px, weight 600). The About hero no longer carries a size utility. `e2e/hero-band.spec.ts` reads each of these sizes at 320, 768, 1280 and 1920px. Used at most once per page. Statement is the pair page's Display use and counts toward that once. An H1 at this size follows the Unlayered-Heading Rule: put the size on an inner `<span>`.
- **Statement** (Source Serif 4, 400, `--t-statement`, 34 to 56px fluid, line-height 1.1, tracking -0.02em): the pair page statement, its H1 (clash 7, new). It is the pair page's one display-scale line.
- **Figure** (Source Serif 4, 600, `--t-fig`, 28 to 40px fluid, line-height 1, tracking -0.01em): score and count figures (clashes 8 and 9). **One large figure per band** (clash 8, blend). The figure is Forest and sits at the Score Display role (§5 → Score Display). Every other number in the band drops to inline text. The hero-metric template stays banned (§6).
- **Section** (Source Serif 4, 600, `--t-36`, 28 to 36px fluid, line-height 1.1, tracking -0.02em): section headings, the H2 on a detail page. Clash 9's blend added a larger section-heading role to the system. This is it.
- **Headline** (Source Serif 4, 600, `--t-fig`, 28 to 40px fluid, line-height 1.15, tracking -0.005em): page H1 on content pages that are not detail pages. AECI-1748 put the global `h1` rule on `--t-fig`. Its clamp is the pre-ramp h1 value, `clamp(1.75rem, 2.5vw + 0.75rem, 2.5rem)`, so no h1 moved.
- **Title** (Source Serif 4, 600, `--t-24`, 1.5rem / 24px, line-height 1.25): sub-headings and section H2 within long-form content.
- **Card title** (Source Serif 4, 600, `--t-20`, 1.25rem / 20px, line-height 1.1): the name on a card (`.card .nm` in the mock-up).
- **Lede** (Atkinson Hyperlegible Next, 400, `--t-20`, 1.25rem / 20px, line-height 1.5): lede text under a heading (clash 9, blend). The mock-ups set it in the body sans. The blend named a lede role of about 1.1875rem. The ramp snaps it to 20px.
- **Body** (Atkinson Hyperlegible Next, 400, `--t-16`, 1rem / 16px, line-height 1.6): default body and reading text, and nav links. Measure (65 to 75ch) caps prose blocks, never the page shell; see the Page-Width Rule below. **Use 700 (Bold) only for inline emphasis** — never for headings (those are Source Serif 4).
- **Caption** (Atkinson Hyperlegible Next, 400, `--t-14`, 0.875rem / 14px, line-height 1.5): metadata, hints, timestamps, helper text. Defined as a role so components stop improvising this size. `PLAN.md` also lists "captions" at `--t-12`. Field captions take `--t-12` (below). Which other captions do is open.
- **Label** (Atkinson Hyperlegible Next, 600, `--t-13`, 0.8125rem / 13px, line-height 1.4, tracking +0.01em): dense chips, counts, table headers, status badges (verified / pending), form field labels. Sentence case (not uppercase — see the rule below). Moved 700 → 600 in AECI-230: with a real 600 cut available, the calmer weight is the premium read at 13px. The taxonomy chip is weight 400 in the mock-up, so the 500 this section used to state for it no longer holds. §5 → Tags / taxonomy chips now records 400. Button text is `--t-14` in the ramp. The button size and weight belong to the button ruling (clash 20), which §5 owns.
- **Overline** (Atkinson Hyperlegible Next, 600, `--t-12`, 0.75rem / 12px, line-height 1.4, tracking +0.08em, **uppercase**; clash 10, current): eyebrows, kickers, and sidebar microheadings ("Vendor", "Categories", "Filters", "At a glance") — the print-kicker role and the _only_ sanctioned uppercase in the system (see the Sentence-Case Rule). Clash 10 kept this rule and changed the mock-up. Every kicker and microheading, including the Browse menu's, uses `.aec-overline`. The mock-up's sentence-case 14px kickers, its 12.5 to 13px bold microheadings and its 11px menu overline are dropped. So are the Clay-deep kicker icons (clash 4). Shipped as the `.aec-overline` class in `styles.css`; apply it on whatever element carries the right semantics (often `h2`/`h3` — the class beats the global heading serif rule, which is the point). Default color text secondary on a light ground. Forest for emphasis. The overline colour on a Forest ground is open (set by AECI-1747).

Floors that hold whatever the ramp: the smallest text is 12px, body is 16px, prose measure is 65 to 75ch, and all text meets AA contrast (§2). No token goes below `--t-12`.

**Fluid tokens use rem.** Each fluid bound is in rem, and the middle term is rem plus vw. Each doubles by 300% zoom at every window width (WCAG 1.4.4; 300% is Safari's limit). Do not copy the mock-ups' `clamp(40px, 5vw, 72px)` style values. Px bounds and a viewport-only middle term ignore the reader's font size and zoom.

### Settled values (AECI-1748)

AECI-1748 confirmed the weights, line heights and tracking stated above. They are the values measured from mock-up 6: Display 400, 1, -0.02em; Statement 400, 1.1, -0.02em; Section 600, 1.1, -0.02em; Figure 600, 1, -0.01em; Lede sans 400, 1.5; field caption 12px on a 20px line. It settled these too:

- **The fluid clamps.** All four use rem bounds and a rem-plus-vw middle term, and all reach their upper bound by 1440px, the mock-up canvas. `--t-36` leaves its lower bound at 640px. `--t-fig` keeps the pre-ramp h1 clamp, which runs from 640px to 1120px and equals the mock-up's `2.5vw + 12px`. `--t-statement` and `--t-display` need a larger rem share to double by 300% zoom: the rem term is at least half the upper bound, and the vw term is the upper bound minus the rem term, divided by 1440. `--t-statement-wide` (AECI-1835) keeps mock-up 5's 3vw slope and its 34px phone size, and doubles by 300% zoom through its 51px cap instead: a floor F doubles at every width while the size never passes 1.5F, so 34px allows 51px at most, against the mock-up's 56px at 1440px. It draws the opsz 60 cut through the shared "Optical size by role" list (AECI-1927), as the mock-up's `.statement` does, with the opsz 60 fallback face at every width.

  | Token                | `clamp()`                                   | 320px | 768px | 1280px | 1920px | Doubles at zoom: 1280 / 1920px window, worst width |
  | -------------------- | ------------------------------------------- | ----- | ----- | ------ | ------ | -------------------------------------------------- |
  | `--t-36`             | `clamp(1.75rem, 1vw + 1.35rem, 2.25rem)`    | 28    | 29.3  | 34.4   | 36     | 246% / 244%, 257%                                  |
  | `--t-fig`            | `clamp(1.75rem, 2.5vw + 0.75rem, 2.5rem)`   | 28    | 31.2  | 40     | 40     | 286% / 267%, 286%                                  |
  | `--t-statement`      | `clamp(2.125rem, 1.8vw + 1.875rem, 3.5rem)` | 35.8  | 43.8  | 53     | 56     | 277% / 258%, 287%                                  |
  | `--t-display`        | `clamp(2.5rem, 2.4vw + 2.375rem, 4.5rem)`   | 45.7  | 56.4  | 68.7   | 72     | 281% / 258%, 290%                                  |
  | `--t-statement-wide` | `clamp(2.125rem, 3vw + 0.75rem, 3.1875rem)` | 34    | 35.0  | 50.4   | 51     | 297% / 300%, 300%                                  |

  Sizes are CSS px at 100% zoom. Browser zoom shrinks the CSS viewport and grows the rem, so a fluid size grows less than the zoom factor until it reaches its lower bound. Each token doubles by 300% zoom at every width, which is Safari's zoom limit. `design-tokens.guard.spec.ts` checks every window from 320px to 4000px. The last column gives the zoom where each token doubles, and the worst case over all widths. WCAG 1.4.4 asks that text can reach 200%. It does not require that at exactly 200% zoom.

- **The score figure step (ruling A3, Chris, 2026-11-16).** `--t-48` is a fixed `3rem`, not a clamp. A fixed rem follows zoom one for one, so it doubles by 200% zoom at every width and passes the 300% guard with no sweep. A clamp with a 48px top would shrink the score on a phone, and the score is the number the reader came for. 48px is a single short number ("4.3") and fits a 320px column. No Tailwind `text-*` utility maps to it. `aec-figure` takes it through `size="score"`.
- **The Tailwind mapping.** The table is under "The type ramp" above.
- **The Headline token** is `--t-fig`.
- **The global heading rules** sit on ramp tokens at the same pixels as before: `h1` at `--t-fig`, `h2` at `--t-24`, `h3` at `--t-20`. They stay unlayered. See the Unlayered-Heading Rule.
- **The `/docs` headings snapped to the ramp.** The page title went from 30px to `--t-36`, the article h2 from 22px to `--t-24` and the article h3 from 18px to `--t-20`.
- **One prose style (AECI-1804).** `.aec-prose` in `styles.css` styles every Markdown body: the docs articles, `/legal/*`, `/accessibility` and `/methodology`. Body is the Body role (`--t-16`, line height 1.6). `h2` is `--t-24` at 1.25 and `h3` is `--t-20` at 1.35, both serif. `h4` to `h6` are the body face at `--t-16` and 600. The docs article's denser variant was retired. `src/prose.guard.spec.ts` fails on any prose size that is not a ramp token, inline code's `0.875em` excepted.
- **Weight 500 left the public pages** except the maintenance marker and the agreement badge. AECI-1748 moved the public and shared templates' `font-medium` to `font-semibold`, keeping the emphasis the author meant. An in-text link takes no weight class at all, because it inherits the sentence's type (the Link Treatment Rule). It left 500 on surfaces another issue rebuilds: chips (AECI-1763), buttons and toggles (AECI-1758 to AECI-1762), tabs (the section nav moved to 400 and 600 with AECI-1768), and the header and menus (AECI-1771, AECI-1774, AECI-1775). The vendor portal and admin were not edited.

### Stray sizes (settled by AECI-1749)

- Every size in public and shared code now reads a ramp token. Sizes under 12px went up to `--t-12`. `0.8125rem` became `--t-13`. The `.aec-plan-price` 32px figure now reads `--t-36` (28 to 36px). `.aec-prose code` stays `0.875em`: it is relative on purpose, so inline code scales with the heading or sentence around it.
- Still off the ramp, because they sit in `admin/` and `vendor/`, which AECI-1749 does not hand-edit: arbitrary `text-[0.6875rem]`, `text-[0.625rem]` and `text-[0.65rem]` utilities in admin templates and charts, one in `vendor/components/vendor-checklist.ts`, and the `rem` sizes in `vendor/integration-detail/integration-detail-styles.ts`. The same goes for the count badge in `layout/nav-menu.ts`, owned by another agent.

### Named Rules

**The Sentence-Case Rule.** Headings, buttons, labels, navigation, table headers, page titles, section titles — all sentence case. Title Case reads as marketing copy; sentence case reads as editorial copy. This rule has exactly **one named exception: the overline role** (above) — uppercase via CSS `text-transform`, never in source strings. Eyebrows, kickers, and sidebar microheadings are overlines; _nothing else_ is uppercase. **One ruled casing exception (Chris, 2026-11-18, AECI-1925):** the claim button on vendor and product pages reads "Claim Vendor", with a capital V, in his casing. Sentence case would print "Claim vendor". The company name follows as visually hidden text, so the accessible name reads "Claim Vendor {company}". No other label takes this casing. Table headers and breadcrumbs are explicitly **not** overlines — they are sentence case (the uppercase table-header treatment that contradicted this rule was struck in AECI-230).

**The Serif-Floor Rule.** Source Serif 4 never renders below 1.125rem / 18px. Anything smaller is by definition a label, overline, or caption — Atkinson territory. (The global `h1,h2,h3` serif rule in `styles.css` makes small serif easy to leak; the `.aec-overline` class on small headings is the standing fix — AECI-230.) Still holds under the ramp (clashes 9 and 17), and the real floor is now 20px: the smallest serif step is `--t-20`, and `--t-12` to `--t-16` are sans. AECI-1748 moved the `/docs` article h3 from 18px to `--t-20`.

AECI-1928 (2026-11-18) moved the last four leaks to the body face, at the same size and weight: the browse card rating number (14px, `reviews/rating-summary.ts`), the 32px initial tile's letter (14px, `shared/logo-or-initial`), the product hero panel's "Integrations by partner category" (16px, a span inside an `h2`, in the category hub that AECI-2018 later removed) and the Disclosure group card's count (12px, inside its `h3`). There is no named exception. Mock-ups 10 to 12 draw the 32px tile's initial in the body face (`font: 600 var(--t-14)/1 var(--font-body)`), so the tile follows them. The 48px, 64px and hero tiles keep the serif, at 20px and up. The mock-ups draw those in the body face too: whether they follow is open (Chris). Two guards keep the rule. `src/design-tokens.guard.spec.ts` fails on a class string that pairs `font-display` with a size below 20px. `e2e/serif-floor.spec.ts` reads the rendered public pages at 1440 and 390px and fails on any visible serif text below 20px, which also catches a small span inheriting the serif from an `h1` to `h3`. To set small text inside a heading, give the span `font-body`.

**The Unlayered-Heading Rule.** The `h1, h2, h3` block in `styles.css` (family, weight, **and size**) sits **outside any `@layer`**, and unlayered declarations beat every layered one regardless of specificity. Tailwind v4 ships its utilities in `@layer utilities`, so **a `text-*` utility written directly on an `h1`/`h2`/`h3` is silently dead** — the element keeps the global size (h1 `--t-fig`, 28 to 40px; h2 `--t-24`; h3 `--t-20`) and the author's intent never renders. Two consequences:

- **To size a heading, put the `text-*` utility on an inner `<span>`**, not on the heading element. The span inherits the display face from the heading and takes its own size. (`product-powered-hub.ts` does this for its card titles.) The same holds for weight (`font-*`), leading (`leading-*`) and tracking (`tracking-*`): the block sets all five properties, so each of those utilities is dead on the element too.
- **No dead utility is left on a public heading (AECI-1847).** AECI-1847 removed the size, weight, leading and tracking utilities from twelve public headings: the `h2` of the home feedback dialog, the claim dialog, list-your-product, the request drawer, the review form's sign-in dialog and the `/account` delete dialog, and the claim dialog flow's six step `h3`s. Each renders exactly what it did before (computed size, weight, line height and letter spacing measured identical at 320, 768, 1280 and 1920px). `src/heading-utilities.guard.spec.ts` fails on a new one. It reads about 200 public headings and fails if it reads 150 or fewer. It reads the static `class` and every `[class]` binding made of string literals, names, member reads (`headerClass.title`) and `+` concatenations, following a constant into the file it is imported from. A binding it cannot read, such as a `computed()` call or a ternary, fails the test until it is named in the spec's `UNREADABLE` list with a reason. That list is empty today, so every public heading is inside the guard. The preview pages are outside it. Admin (`admin/`) and the vendor portal (`vendor/`) are inside it with no exceptions: the redesign slices emptied their shrink-only allowlist and the cleanup slice deleted it (AECI-2083, AECI-1729).
- **Don't "fix" it by moving the block into `@layer base` without an audit.** Doing so silently resizes every heading that carries a losing utility. Public code carries none since AECI-1847, and admin and the vendor portal (the invite page `/vendor/invite/:token` included) carry none since the AECI-1729 slices: 131 headings in `admin/` and 58 in `vendor/` were counted on 2026-11-24, and the heading guard now holds both folders to zero. Moving the block is still not a drive-by: it changes the cascade for every heading at once. The block being unlayered is a bug, but unwinding it is its own scoped piece of work with per-surface decisions, not a drive-by.

**The Page-Width Rule (AECI-987).** Page width and reading measure are two different constraints. The page takes the standard 7xl canvas (§5 → Layout shells); measure (`max-w-prose`, `max-w-[42rem]`, 65 to 75ch) goes on the prose blocks inside it, and form controls cap at `max-w-2xl`. The exceptions are the product page's band shell, which takes a 1328px wrap with 48px gutters (§4 "The detail page band shell", clash 16), and the site header and footer, which take the same wrap (ruling A2, 2026-11-16), and the browse shell, `BrowseLayout` (ruling 3, 2026-11-16, AECI-1793). A routed page never puts a reading-width class on its outermost container: at 1280px that leaves a narrow column with a blank right half. Examples:

- _Editorial_ (`/about`, `/methodology`, and since AECI-1804 `/legal/*` and `/accessibility`): the 1328px page wrap (`BAND_WRAP_CLASS`) since AECI-1800, the prose capped at measure. The legal pages and the accessibility statement put the hero on `aec-bone-band` and cap both the hero and the article at `42rem`, the docs article measure, in rem so the font swap does not re-wrap them. `/about` puts each heading left of its prose from `lg`, so no row has a blank right half. `/methodology` is one Markdown article, so it keeps a single 70ch column. **Since the AECI-1720 layout pass, `/methodology` and `/legal/*` fill the rest of the wrap by layout, never by a wider measure:** from `lg` the article sits in the first column at its own measure (70ch, 42rem) and the "On this page" list (`aec-page-toc`, §5 "Product docs") sticks in the second (`PROSE_PAGE_GRID_CLASS`, 64px gap, 96px from `xl`). Below `lg` the list is not rendered. The headings carry slug ids for it (`aec-prose-blocks`, the docs article's own rendering), so the words and their order are unchanged. The legal PDF is built from the Markdown, not the page, so it does not change. `/accessibility` is hidden until AECI-1687 and keeps its single column. AECI-1801 moved `/updates`, `/roadmap` and `/contact` onto the same recipe. `/roadmap`'s one section is its heading over its two lines at 70ch: a heading-left split left the short heading about 200px from its text (AECI-1720 layout pass). `/contact` keeps its cards, now the shared `aecCard`, in a two-column grid across the wrap.
- _Account_ (`/account`): 7xl canvas, a sticky section nav from `lg`, panels across the content column, inputs capped at `max-w-2xl`. Since AECI-1806 each panel (the onboarding prompt, Profile, Your reviews, Delete account) is the shared `aecCard` with 24px padding, 32px from `md`, a form card (`phoneFlush`, §5 "Cards") since the AECI-1720 layout pass, and the display name is on the field recipe with its `.aec-field-error` message. The overline and the `h1` follow the not-found page. `/welcome` keeps its geometry, and its form is the same card.
- _Form_ (`/products/:slug/review`, the claim and correction pages, `/welcome`): 7xl canvas with the form + context rail (§5 → Layout shells).
- _Focused flow_ (`/auth/login`): the one exception. A single short task with nothing to put beside it stays a narrow centered column. The focused flows are `/auth/login`, `/claims/confirm`, `/notifications/mute`, `/unsubscribe` and `/vendor/invite/:token`. The 404 page left this list in the AECI-1720 layout pass: its five recovery tiles wrapped every description in a 768px column, so it now sits on the 1328px page wrap with its prose at `max-w-2xl` and the tiles across the wrap in the shared three-column `aec-card-grid` (three, then two, each description on one line at 1440px). Since AECI-1808 the three token pages (`/unsubscribe`, `/notifications/mute`, `/claims/confirm`) follow what was the 404 shell: a `max-w-2xl` column on white with 64px above (96px from `md`), the overline in `text-secondary`, then one shared `aecCard` (24px padding, 32px from `sm`, 40px from `md`) that holds the `h1`, the body and the one action. The `h1` is the global Headline size. There is no Bone band. The claim receipt's "What happens next" box is a nested card. Since AECI-1805 `/auth/login` is the same recipe at `max-w-md`, every state (the form, "Check your email", "Restoring your session") is inside one shared `aecCard` (32px padding from `sm`). Since the AECI-1720 layout pass it is a form card (`phoneFlush`, §5 "Cards"): below `sm` the page gutter is 24px and the card drops its frame and spans it, so the email field is as wide as it was before the card (257px at 320, 327px at 390, measured with a 15px scrollbar). The Google button has 12px padding and a 6px gap, with a label that never wraps. So at 320px "Continue with Google" stays on one line beside its mark in any fallback font (measured: Verdana, the widest common sans, fits with 12px to spare). The brand mark is a `rounded-mark` tile with no shadow and a Clay fill from `--accent-secondary`. The callback notices are `rounded-control` sunken boxes. The Terms line keeps its words and sits under both buttons, with its two links in the in-text link recipe.
