The Google button and its "G" mark are unchanged: the mark's four fills are the only colour literals left in `login.html`.

Inside the docs shell the canvas is the shell's content column: the docs home and section index widen into it, and articles keep 42rem.

**The Two-Family Rule.** Source Serif 4 for display, statement, figure, section, headline, title and card title. Atkinson Hyperlegible Next for lede, body, label, overline, and caption. No third typeface enters the system. Monospace appears only when rendering literal code (in `<code>` and `<pre>`) and uses the system monospace stack — it is not a brand face.

**The Reflex-Reject Rule.** This system explicitly does not use, and will reject any proposal to introduce: Inter, DM Sans, Plus Jakarta Sans, Geist, Mona Sans, Space Grotesk, IBM Plex Sans, Outfit, Roboto, Open Sans, Arial, Fraunces, Newsreader, Lora, Crimson, Crimson Pro, Crimson Text, Playfair Display, Cormorant, Cormorant Garamond, DM Serif Display, DM Serif Text, Instrument Sans, Instrument Serif, Syne. The chosen pair (Source Serif 4 + Atkinson Hyperlegible) is the answer; the reflex list is the question that has already been refused.

## 4. Elevation

The redesign rulings of 2026-11-13 changed this section (clashes 11 to 18). A clash number beside a rule means that ruling set it. Where the record has Chris's own words, they are quoted. Where it says only NEW, BLEND or CURRENT, the rule follows the clash recommendation and `PLAN.md` §4.3. The scope sentence under "Forest grounds" in §2 applies to this section too: ruling R2 builds the public pages first. Shared components (the header, the footer, the button and the input) are one component on every surface. Portal and admin page bodies wait until Chris has verified the public pages (§5 Components, "Scope"). Values the rulings left open are listed under "Open values (set by AECI-1750, AECI-1765 and AECI-1766)". ADR 0059 records which written rules this section rewrites.

Borders separate controls. One shadow lifts the cards that float on the page ground, and one shadow lifts menus. The kept modal shadow is the only other shadow. The focus ring is an outline.

The system is still flat by default. Depth is conveyed through color (Bone callouts, sunken inset wells), spacing (generous whitespace around elevated content), border weight on controls, and one card shadow on top-level cards. Cards, menus and dialogs are the only surfaces that rise off the page, and a focus ring marks the element the keyboard is on.

### Shadow Vocabulary

The recipes are written here once. Every other section and every component points to a token name, never to a copy of the values. The tokens are in `apps/web/src/styles.css` (AECI-1750 and AECI-1770). `--shadow-card` is on the shared card recipe (`aecCard`, AECI-1765, §5 "Cards"). `--shadow-menu` and `--shadow-modal` are on the shared overlay classes (AECI-1770, §5 "Overlays").

| Token                             | Recipe                                                                  | Where                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Clash |
| --------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----- |
| `--shadow-card` (`shadow-card`)   | `0 1px 3px rgb(0 0 0 / 0.06), 0 16px 36px -16px rgb(0 0 0 / 0.24)`      | Top-level floating cards: the product card grid, the search hit cards, the integration group card, the facts card (`PLAN.md` §4.3 calls them "the facts card and entity cards") and the Free and Managed plan cards (ruling A7) | 11    |
| `--shadow-menu` (`shadow-menu`)   | `0 8px 24px -4px rgb(0 0 0 / 0.12), 0 2px 8px -1px rgb(0 0 0 / 0.06)`   | Menu panes, autocomplete dropdowns, tooltips with body content, the Browse menu panel                                                                                                                                           | 11    |
| `--shadow-modal` (`shadow-modal`) | `0 16px 48px -8px rgb(0 0 0 / 0.18), 0 4px 16px -2px rgb(0 0 0 / 0.10)` | Dialogs and drawers. Pairs with the 50% modal scrim (`--scrim-modal`)                                                                                                                                                           | none  |

- **Card shadow (clash 11, blend).** One recipe, pure black, for a card that stands on the page ground. A card nested inside another card takes no shadow of its own (see "Cards may nest" below). Chris's ruling was BLEND with no note, so the recipe is the clash recommendation and the plan's value.
- **"Entity cards (index rows)" take no shadow.** `PLAN.md` says "entity cards", but §5 already uses that name for index rows, which are table rows. They keep the hairline and take no shadow. The cards that take `--shadow-card` are the components in the table.
- **Which surfaces take the card shadow (rulings A7 and A8, Chris, 2026-11-16).** Each takes it through `aecCard`, never a copy of the recipe.

  | Surface                                                       | Card shadow                                                                                                         |
  | ------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
  | Product card grid, search hit cards, integration group card   | Yes, built (AECI-1765)                                                                                              |
  | Facts card                                                    | Yes, built (AECI-1779, `products/product-facts-card.ts`)                                                            |
  | Product page extensions tiles                                 | Yes, built (ruling 35, `products/product-extensions.ts`)                                                            |
  | Free and Managed plan cards (`shared/plan-cta/plan-cards.ts`) | Yes, built (A7). The Managed card keeps its 2px Forest border, and its "Managed" name and price also mark it        |
  | Review cards and score cards                                  | Yes, built (AECI-1784, `products/product-reviews.html`). The reply and update panels inside a review card take none |
  | Table wrappers                                                | Never                                                                                                               |
  | The plans rail card (`plan-cta.ts`, `.aec-plan-card`)         | Not ruled. It keeps its 8px hairline frame                                                                          |

  **No wide lead card (A8).** The reviews, extensions and similar-products grids have no wide first item. Every item is one cell. `/products` keeps its lead card (ruling 2 of 2026-11-16, `records/10-directory-notes.md`).

- **Menu shadow.** This is the existing Dropdown / Popover recipe, unchanged. Only its name is new.
- **Both are pure black, measured.** The values are `--sh-card` and `--sh-pop` on line 15 of mock-up 6 (the same on mock-ups 5, 7 and 8). Pure black is what `PLAN.md` §4.3 rules ("One card shadow, in pure black"). It is also the form of the modal shadow and of the CDK backdrop, so the three agree. `styles.css` is the token definition site and is exempt from the colour rules, so lint is not the reason. The guard spec pins both strings exactly.
- **Forced-colors mode drops `box-shadow`.** A card that relies on `--shadow-card` has no edge there. The card recipe gives it a transparent 1px border, which the system colours (AECI-1765). Verified in Chromium with `forcedColors: 'active'`: `box-shadow` computes to `none` and the border computes to a system colour. `e2e/card.spec.ts` pins it.
- **Modal / Dialog** (`--shadow-modal`): for overlay surfaces (dialogs, drawers, command palettes). Pairs with the 50% modal scrim. No ruling touched the value, so it is unchanged. AECI-1770 gave it a name and replaced one of the six inline copies: the request drawer's. Five remain, in the four admin dialogs and `vendor-product-facet-editor.ts:383`, and each moves by adding `aec-dialog`. Prettier writes the second alpha as `0.1`, and the guard spec pins that spelling.
- **Focus ring** (`outline: 3px solid var(--accent-primary)` at `outline-offset: 3px`, mock-up 6, line 29): the keyboard-focus indicator. It is an outline and not a `box-shadow`, because forced-colours mode drops shadows and keeps outlines. The shipped ring is an outline too (`apps/web/src/styles.css`, the `:focus-visible` rule). Never `outline: none` without a replacement. On a Forest ground, the hero panel and the footer ground the ring is Bone (`--focus-ring-on-brand`, AECI-1747, 11.03:1 on Forest). A Forest ring there is invisible, and on the hero panel it is 1.20:1. §6 "Floors" lists this floor. The global `:focus-visible` colour rule sits in `@layer base` (AECI-1845). So a control on Forest takes the Bone ring from the plain utility `focus-visible:outline-(--focus-ring-on-brand)`, at 3px with a 3px offset. It needs no component CSS and no `!`. The shipped default ring is still 2px at a 2px offset (`focus-visible:outline-2 focus-visible:outline-offset-2`), not the mock-up's 3px. That changes only when each surface is rebuilt.
- **Buttons, chips, inputs and tabs keep borders and take no shadow** (clash 11). Hover lift on cards is clash 37 and belongs to §5 "Motion", not to this section.
- **A shadow is never the only cue for a boundary that needs 3:1.** A card is not a control, so its shadow edge may stand alone. A control placed on or inside a card keeps its own 3:1 edge (§2 "Field edge"). The mock-up's other shadow variables (`--sh-mark`, `--sh-ctl`, `--sh-btn`, `--sh-bar`) are not in the plan's table and are not part of the language.

> **`rgb(0 0 0 / α)` is the one color function lint allows** (AECI-597). The AECI-597 guard bans `rgb()`, `hsl()` and `oklch()` everywhere in `apps/web`, with a carve-out for all-zero RGB channels (`COLOR_FUNCTION` in `eslint.color-patterns.mjs`) precisely so the shadow recipes keep working, including the underscore spelling Tailwind arbitrary values use, `shadow-[0_16px_48px_-8px_rgb(0_0_0/0.18)]`. A tinted shadow will fail `pnpm lint`. The mock-ups' green-tinted shadows (clash 11) are dropped for that reason. If one is ever wanted, change it here first and widen the carve-out in `eslint.color-patterns.mjs` in the same PR.

### Radius (clash 12)

Chris's ruling was BLEND with no note. The blend is the clash recommendation: add a 24px card radius and a 12px control radius, let public chips be pills, and keep primary actions off the pill.

| Role                      | Radius                                                                  | Frontmatter key   | CSS token and utility                 | Clashes |
| ------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------- | ------------------------------------- | ------- |
| Cards                     | 24px                                                                    | `rounded.card`    | `--radius-card`, `rounded-card`       | 12      |
| Controls: buttons, inputs | 12px, with the header search pill (clash 29) as the one named exception | `rounded.control` | `--radius-control`, `rounded-control` | 12, 20  |
| Chips                     | Full pill                                                               | `rounded.pill`    | `--radius-pill`, `rounded-pill`       | 12, 22  |
| Small marks               | 8px                                                                     | `rounded.mark`    | `--radius-mark`, `rounded-mark`       | 12      |
| Nested panels (open)      | 16px                                                                    | `rounded.panel`   | `--radius-panel`, `rounded-panel`     | 12      |

On a redesigned public component use the role utilities `rounded-mark|control|panel|card|pill`. `rounded-sm|md|lg|xl` still render Spartan's 4.8, 6.4, 8 and 11.2px and are for surfaces not yet rebuilt. The frontmatter key equals the utility suffix, so `{rounded.control}` is `rounded-control`.

- **No pill buttons** (clash 12, a rule that stands). "Sign in" is a rounded rectangle at 12px. A chip may be a pill. A button, a link styled as a button and a primary action may not.
- **The old scale stays (AECI-1750, rulings of 2026-11-14).** `--radius` is `0.5rem`, so Spartan derives 4.8, 6.4, 8 and 11.2px for `rounded-sm`, `rounded-md`, `rounded-lg` and `rounded-xl`. AECI-1750 did not remap those four utilities. This departs from the issue text ("map `--radius-sm`, `--radius-md`, `--radius-lg` and `--radius-xl` onto them so existing usages land on the new scale"). The reasons:
  - **Order of work.** Chris ruled that public pages are built first, that shared components (header, footer, button, input) are one component on every surface, and that the vendor portal and admin page bodies are restyled only after he has verified the public pages: "We will do admin pages i just want a good natural stopping point where i can verify the work." The four utilities appear 1,176 times in `apps/web/src` (admin 548, vendor portal 253, shared components 76, public 247, previews 40, mostly in the `rounded-(--radius-md)` form). A remap would reshape about 800 portal and admin page-body corners at once, before that checkpoint.
  - **The scale would stop ascending.** Mapping `lg` to 24px leaves `rounded-lg` larger than `rounded-xl`. Spartan's `2xl`, `3xl` and `4xl` stay derived from `--radius`, so they would be smaller than `lg`.
  - **Names.** A role name cannot be confused with Tailwind's meaning of `md` or `lg`. That confusion is the trap in the frontmatter below: `rounded.md` once meant 12px while `rounded-md` renders 6.4px.
- **Code tokens by role.** Each is declared on `:root` and exposed in `@theme inline`, so the utility exists. No component uses one yet.
- **Portal and admin carry the old scale until their slices (AECI-2083).** `src/radius-utilities.guard.spec.ts` holds every `rounded-sm|md|lg|xl`, `rounded-(--radius-sm|md|lg)` and arbitrary `rounded-[…]` under `vendor/` and `admin/`, on any side or corner, to a shrink-only count per file (`src/guard-allowlists/radius-utilities.txt`). A new file starts at zero. The 2px arbitrary corners in `admin/charts/` are exempt.
- **Chip shape and the header's pills.** The chip vocabulary is clash 22 and §5 owns it. The header search and nav hover shapes belong to the header, and §5 Navigation owns them.

### Spacing (clash 17)

Chris's note on clash 17 was about type: "I do believe we have too many font sizes and variants, see what it looks like to limit them." The spacing blend is the clash recommendation: snap to the scale and the look survives.

- **The scale is 4, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 48, 64 and 96px** (clash 17). It is Tailwind's default spacing, steps 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16 and 24. AECI-1750 adds no spacing tokens, and `styles.css` declares none. It sweeps nothing.
- **Snap, do not invent.** The clash recommendation names the mock-up's strays: 22 goes to 24, 26 and 28 go to 24 or 32, and 104 goes to 96. Padding, margin and gap sit on the scale. A control's height is not spacing, and clash 20 sets it.
- **Tailwind steps off the scale** include 5, 7, 9, 10 and 11 (20, 28, 36, 40 and 44px). The half steps 0.5, 1.5, 2.5 and 3.5 (2, 6, 10 and 14px) are off the scale too.
- **Half steps snap.** Clash 17 says snap and names no optical-alignment exception, so a half step is not allowed because it looks right inside a control. A real exception needs a ruling from Chris.
- **Who snaps what.** Each redesigned public component uses the role radius utilities and on-scale spacing when its own issue rebuilds it. The remaining public and shared-shell uses are swept by AECI-1844. The vendor portal and admin page bodies wait for Chris's checkpoint above. Counts at AECI-1750 (utilities with a spacing prefix, specs excluded): integer steps off the scale 503, half steps 869.
- **The AECI-1844 sweep (2026-11-22).** On public pages and the shared shell, radius moved by role. Inline links, option rows, skeletons and the 24px chevron targets take `rounded-mark`. Notices, dashed empty states and the "Built within" tile take `rounded-panel`. Inputs and choice rows take `rounded-control`. Spacing snapped: 2px went to 4px, 6px to 8px, 20px to 24px, 40px to 48px and 56px to 64px. Two exceptions run the other way: the `/search` tabs and the product combobox's empty line went from 10px to 8px. A 16px icon beside a 20px line is centred with `h-5 items-center`, not a 2px nudge. It left these, each held by a rule above or below: the standalone link and citation recipes, the Clay bar's 3px corners, the header row links' 10px padding and the 64px header, the segmented toggle's 3px inset, the dense chip's 2px padding, the 24px partner-link target (`-my-0.5 py-0.5`), the integration card recipe, `FORM_CARD_GAP_CLASS`, the focused-flow card's 40px, the Google button's 6px gap, the header search's 80px end padding, the hero band's 128px and the band wrap's gutter `calc()`. It also left the footer column gap, because "about 604px" (§5 "The footer") was measured with it. It left the consent banner's 20px, because 24px wrapped its last word onto a line of its own at 1440px. And it left mock-up 5's Venn ovals at 56px, because 64px made them visibly heavier. The search fields' icon clearance (`ps-9`, `ps-11`) and the badges' padding stay, because the scale has no step that fits. The 6px focus-ring room on the Integrations chip scroller stays too. The xs standalone links (`view-public-link`, the group card's "View product", the pair page's links) wait for AECI-1758. (`view-public-link` took the role's 14px at 600 with AECI-1809.) The Managed plan card is `p-[23px]`, the 24px of the Free card less its extra 1px border. The sweep left the signed-in pages (`/account`, `/welcome`, `/auth/terms`, the review form and `/claims/confirm`) and `logo-input`, which renders only in the portal and admin. Remaining on public and shell files outside those pages: 15 radius, 19 integer and 32 half-step uses, all of the kinds listed here.

| Surface                     | Integer off-scale (5, 7, 9, 10, 11, 14, 20, `[...]`) | Half steps |
| --------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------- | ---------- |
| Public                      | 151                                                  | 157        |
| Shared components and shell | 30                                                   | 53         |
| Vendor portal               | 109                                                  | 161        |
| Admin                       | 169                                                  | 485        |
| Previews                    | 44                                                   | 13         |

### Layout: bands, grids and nesting

#### The detail page band shell (clash 16, new)

Chris ruled NEW: the new design wins. The product page drops the two-column layout with a metadata sidebar. This describes the bands. The pixel values are starting values in the table below. AECI-1778 rebuilds `DetailLayout` as a band shell. Only `product-detail.ts` and `vendor-detail.ts` use `DetailLayout` today. The vendor page took the band shell in AECI-1797 (mock-up 11, rulings of 2026-11-16). See "The vendor page on the band shell" below. The pair page with integrations does not use `DetailLayout`.

From top to bottom, a product page is:

1. **The Forest header** (§2 "Forest grounds", clash 1).
2. **The Forest hero band**, with the soft single-colour glow (clash 13, below). It holds the breadcrumb, the name, the ratings, the actions and the hero visual.
3. **The facts card.** One elevated card (`--shadow-card`) that overlaps the bottom of the hero and carries the key facts in columns. It replaces the metadata sidebar.
4. **The sticky section nav.** It sits on a solid surface once it sticks. No frosted blur (clash 13).
5. **Full-width bands**, one per section. The first band is the one Bone band (clash 2). It runs from behind the lower half of the facts card, through the section nav at rest, and through the About band. The rest are white, and a hairline `border-default` separates two white bands.
6. **The mailing-list band and the Forest footer.** On the band shell the mailing-list band is white, so it never adds a second Bone band.

- **The page wrap** is 1328px with 48px gutters (`PLAN.md` §4.3, no clash). That leaves a 1232px content column. Gutters step down at narrower widths (starting values below). The breakpoints are Tailwind's defaults (see "Breakpoints" below).
- **No sidebar on the product page.** Resources, vendor facts and the claim and correction entries move into the facts card. The old rules for where the sidebar undocks, its docked and undocked formats and its `space-y-6` rhythm are retired. The vendor page left them in AECI-1797.
- **The 608px table check is replaced.** A body table is checked against the content column, which is the viewport minus both gutters. That is 960px at a 1024px viewport and 327px at 375px. The old 608px figure and the 38rem floor came from the sidebar split and no longer apply. The mock-up's comparison table asks for `min-width: 1240px`, which is wider than the 1232px column (`PLAN.md` §10.2 lists the table view as clipped at 1440px). The table view must scroll inside its own wrapper or fit.
- **The sticky nav keeps its own slot**, bare as a direct child of the page container, because sticky is bounded by the parent's box. It marks one link `aria-current="location"`, and SSR renders the first link for every visitor. Both rules stand from the old text. The rulings did not touch them.
- **DOM order is the single-column reading order** at every width. The bands work without the layout.
- **The empty pair page** (mock-up 5, AECI-1827, restyled to the mock-up by AECI-1835) has no facts card and does not use `DetailLayout`. The page renders as the mock-up's `body` does through the shared rule on `body` (grayscale smoothing and `text-rendering: optimizeLegibility`, AECI-1927); it sets nothing of its own. The hero is the shared `aec-hero-band` with `titleSize="statement-wide"`, `panel="bare"`, `panelFrom="wide"` and `bottom="band"`, so the 540px diagram sits beside the text from 1040px, as mock-up 5 has it, both columns top-aligned, and under it, at the start, below. The kicker row holds the "Not on record" pill (a Bone wash pill with the `unlink` glyph in a round pip), with no "Integration" overline (ruling 98). AECI-1666 retired the maintenance chip that sat beside it. Its words open "About this listing", which ends the page body's last band, after the API note. The h1 is the legal statement at `--t-statement-wide` (34px on a phone, 47.4px at 1180, 51px from 1280) in the opsz 60 display cut, balanced, with the names at weight 600 and unbroken from `md`. The caveat follows at `--t-20` (`--t-16` below `md`) in `--text-on-brand-secondary`, its first sentence strong in Bone. While the alternatives are on the page the caveat ends with mock-up 5's "Below are the closest pairs we do have, what you would be moving, and where to look yourself." Under it, the correction entry: an inline strip on `--surface-hero-panel` with a flag tile, "Know of one?", its line, and an `on-brand` "It does exist" button that opens the existing correction request (ruling R6) in the request drawer the page hosts, with both products recorded (AECI-1828, `pair_slug`), its body prefilled with "An integration between {A} and {B}. Where I saw it: " for the reader to change. The diagram (`PairEmptyStage`): two white cards, the first tilted -2.2deg to the start, the second 1.8deg to the end, each with a Clay "Product one" or "Product two" tag, its 64px mark, its name as a link, "by {vendor}" and its first two categories with "+N more". Between them, two `--surface-hero-ribbon` ribbons with a Clay line stop at a Bone medallion with the `unlink` glyph and a dashed Clay halo, over the mock-up's rings, labelled "No integration on record" (§11.4). No arrowheads and no data-type beads. The Clay line draws once on first paint, over `--duration-slow` times four, and reduced motion shows it drawn. Below `md` the cards stack with the label between two Clay ticks. An unclaimed card shows its initial tile in the 64px slot and the Unclaimed note under the byline, with the 32px sourced logo beside the note only when one is on record. Below the hero, in mock-up 5's order: "What you would be moving" over "The data each product already exchanges" (AECI-1830), the page's one Bone band, three groups (only the first product with its mark, both with no mark, only the second with its mark) on the mock-up's two overlapping ovals from `md`: `--venn-a` and `--venn-b` mixed from tokens on white (the hero ribbon green at 20%, #D8E3DD, and Clay at 36%, #F7D9C9), the second multiplied over the first, and below `md` each group a card in its oval's fill. Every text on the ovals is `--text-primary`, never secondary: primary is 11.22:1 on the overlap at worst, where secondary would be 4.38:1. Each data object is a toggle pill that narrows the matches. The status line under them reads "Showing every match. Pick the data you need to narrow the list.", and while data is picked "Showing only matches that move {objects}." with Clear and See matches, announced politely. Then "Closest matches on record" over "Keep one product and swap the other" (AECI-1829) and the intro "These products are similar to {swap} and have an integration with {kept} on record. Open one to see what moves between them.", a white band, top to bottom (AECI-1835, the owner's second review). The lane switch, directly under the intro: two options named for the lanes, "{A} integrations" and "{B} integrations", in the segmented recipe of `listing-view-toggle.ts` (the field edge on the group, the pressed option solid Forest with a tick in place of its mark) at 16px in a 44px row, so it reads as the heading of what follows. Below `sm` the options stack, so a long product name wraps inside its option. A visually hidden `h3` repeats the selected lane's name for the outline and for screen readers; the switch itself is a labelled group of pressed buttons, because a heading cannot hold controls. Under it, the filter panel (`INTEGRATION_FILTER_PANEL_CLASS`, one Bone panel, no toggles): the search (product and vendor names, never in a form, never reordering), then the "Similar to {swap} means it shares" label and the category chips, wrapping from the start. Then the list row, immediately above the cards, as on the product page's Integrations section: the announced count, "Showing {n} of {total}", and "Clear filters" while anything filters (it clears the search, this lane's categories and the picked data, then moves focus to the search) at the start, and the shared Cards and Table toggle (`aec-listing-view-toggle`) at the end. Then the "How these are ordered" disclosure stating the real order, then the cards or the table, each designed empty state, and "See all". The cards follow "The integration card" (§5): one column on a phone and two from `md`. A third column would need every card at least 400px wide, a 1,248px list, and the page wrap caps the list at 1,232px, so the list never takes three. One match takes the whole row. The direction line is the shared "Sends to {match}", "Receives from {match}" or "Syncs both ways" (`contextDirectionLabel`, the match as the partner), and the table names the two scores as the ledger does. Then two white bands, each the mock-up's small label over its large heading. "Side by side" over "How the two products compare": the ledger card the full row (integrations on record, reviews, and the two scores named "Product quality" and "Onboarding experience" as on the product page (AECI-1720 restore C), each count with its own value and a meter scaled to the larger of the two, each score on its stars out of 5, the integrations figure the facts card's own, "None on record yet" at zero, the review rows only when either side has a review), then "Who each product is for" as its own full-row card. A term one product carries is a chip with that product's tile. A term both carry is one `wash` chip that leads with the word "Both" in place of the two tiles, with no legend line; a screen reader hears the term, then "appears on both products". "Look it up yourself" over "Each vendor's own pages": one card per product with mock-up 5's head: Forest with Bone text for a claimed product (we hold no vendor brand colours) and the sunken neutral head for an unclaimed one (ruling R7), "Published by {vendor}" only while every link the card shows is the vendor's own value ("by {vendor}" when AECi corrected one or the card has none). It lists only what we have: its API documentation, its integrations directory, a value under review, and its own list on AEC Integrations. No line says something is missing. A card with none of these is left out, and a lone card takes the row. Then the API note. With the alternatives on the page the mailing list is left out, as mock-up 5 has none, so "What you would be moving" is the one Bone band. If the alternatives read fails, the mailing list returns as that band. Products run in URL order, context first. Its claim rows are one column, each the full lane wide (mock-up 12, `.rows`): a lane on its own no longer splits its rows in two columns from `sm`, which left each row half the lane (562px of 1,166px at 1440px). Two lanes still sit side by side from `lg` (AECI-1720 restore C). The lane's direction glyph is `aria-hidden`, because the lane heading beside it says the same words.
- **Bone fills are not bands.** Clash 2 is about full-width tinted bands as the page ground. A Bone or tinted fill inside a card is a nested fill, so the Bone stat panel in the facts card and the reviewer-update panel in a review card do not count against "one Bone band per page".
- **The legal notice band is shell chrome.** It does not count as the page's one Bone band. (Orchestrator reading, 2026-11-14, Chris to confirm.)
- **Floor: the facts card never overlaps past the hero.** The overlap never exceeds the hero's bottom padding at any width. At 128px of padding and a 96px overlap the clearance is 32px. Below `md` the padding is 96px and the overlap 64px, so the clearance is also 32px (AECI-1778; the mock-up's 96px overlap there left none).
- **Floor: bands clear the sticky bar.** Each band's scroll margin is at least the sticky bar's height, and a control reached by keyboard is never hidden under the bar (WCAG 2.4.11). The bar is 72px plus a 1px hairline, and today's sections use `scroll-mt-20` (80px), which clears it by 7px. A band's controls are covered by the focus rule under §5 "The section nav".
- **Floor: hero contrast is measured at the glow's peak.** Hero text is measured against Forest hover, where Bone is 6.87:1 and a Clay stroke is 3.29:1.

**Settled by AECI-1778 (built).** `DetailLayout` takes `shell="bands"` on the product page. The vendor page takes it too since AECI-1797. No page uses the default `shell="columns"` any more.

- **Order.** Hero, facts card, section nav, About band, then the rest of the body. The breadcrumb sits inside the hero, so the `breadcrumbs` slot is not rendered on the band shell.
- **The facts overlap is 64px below `md` and 96px from `md`.** The hero's bottom padding is 96px and 128px, so the hero's last control keeps 32px above the card at every width. Measured at 320, 390, 768, 1024, 1280 and 1440px. The mock-up's 96px below `md` left zero room, and the card touched the buttons.
- **The overlap needs a block formatting context.** The Bone ground under the card is `flow-root`. Without it the card's negative margin collapses through the ground and drags the Bone over the hero.
- **The Bone band runs through the nav at rest.** The product page passes `rest="clear"` to the section nav. At rest the bar has no fill and no hairline, so the Bone ground runs from behind the lower card, through the bar, into the About band. Stuck, the bar is solid white with its hairline. Only colours change and the 1px border stays, so the swap moves nothing (CLS 0, pinned in `e2e/section-nav.spec.ts`). The band shell's container is Bone and the body sits on its own white band, so the clear bar always shows Bone. On Bone the links are `--text-secondary`, 6.91:1, and the Forest focus ring is 11.03:1, both measured in the browser. The server never renders the bar stuck, so cached HTML is one look for every visitor.
- **The About band hides when the page has no About.** The page projects `body-lead` only when the description, its under-review marker or "Who it is for" shows (AECI-1780).
- **The nav bar spans the page and its links sit on the band wrap.** The layout gives the nav host the wrap's gutters as padding: 24px, 32px, then `max(48px, half the space beyond 1328px plus 48px)` from `lg`.
- **The body is one white band in the wrap**, with 64px of padding above and below (96px from `md`). Since the AECI-1720 restore (owner, 2026-11-17) each body section is its own band, as mock-up 6's `.band.plain`: 64px of space above and below it (96px from `md`), with a 1px `--border-default` hairline between neighbours (`divide-y` on the body slot). The first and last sections take the body band's own outer padding. Usefulness mounts only when it has points, so a hidden section never takes a band.
- **The mailing-list band is white** on the band shell (`ground="white"`), with a hairline above. Other pages keep the Bone band.

**Settled by AECI-1779 (built).** The facts card is `aec-product-facts-card` (`products/product-facts-card.ts`), one `aecCard` with `overflow-hidden` so the Bone stat panel clips to the card's 24px corner.

- **Top row, in reading order:** the Bone stat panel, Vendor (with "Built within" under it), Categories, then Resources and the two buttons. Since the AECI-1720 restore the buttons have no "Actions" heading, as in mock-up 6 `.f-acts`. "Claim Vendor" stays first (ruled 2026-11-16; the label since AECI-1925, with the company name visually hidden after it), with the seal glyph, and "Suggest a correction" carries the flag. "Learn more about claiming" is `--t-13` in `--text-secondary` with an `--border-underline` underline, as in-text links carry no weight. The vendor page keeps its "Actions" heading for now, a divergence for Chris to rule on.
- **Category chips** carry the 14px tag glyph (mock-up 6 `.tagchip`).
- **At zero the stat panel adds "Know of one?"**, a `--t-13` link to `#integrations`, where the zero state's "Know of one?" group sits (mock-up 8). It promises nothing. It shows on the Integrations section's own zero test: no integrations on record and no §13.7 reach line. With a reach line that section has no "Know of one?" group, so the card has no link. Columns from `xl` take the mock-up's ratios, `0.95fr 1fr 1fr 1.15fr`. With no Categories group the row is `0.95fr 1fr 1.15fr`, so no column stands empty. Two columns from `md`, where an odd last column spans both. One column below `md`.
- **Column rules.** From `xl` each column after the stat panel has a 1px `--border-default` line on its start edge, inset 24px top and bottom. It is a pseudo-element fill. It was built while a border-colour utility lost to the unlayered `*` rule, which AECI-2088 fixed. Below `xl` there is no rule, as in the mock-up. Each column has 24px padding.
- **The stat panel** is `aec-figure` on `integration_count`, the same number the product cards print, with the cards' "integration" or "integrations" noun. A zero uses the figure's legal-reviewed zero mode. A stored zero never sits over listed rows: the column is recomputed after each write and can lag, so when it reads 0 and the page lists rows, the card prints the number of rows the Integrations and "Integrations it powers" sections list instead. A non-zero count is printed as stored, so it can exceed the rows the Integrations section lists: a connector's count includes the pairs it powers, and the Via lanes collapse several edges into one row. **The Integrations section heading carries no count** (Chris, 2026-11-16). It reads "Integrations", because this card already shows the count and on a connector product the two numbers differ. The section's "Showing n of m" line, the group counts and the chip counts are unchanged. "Integrations it powers (N)" keeps its count for now. The numeral is not `aria-hidden` when it is not zero, so a screen reader hears "14", then "integrations", once.
- **The reach line stays out of the card.** `STAGE_1_5_SPEC.md` §13.7 places "N more pairs reachable via connectors" under the Integrations section, and the payload contract says `reachable_pair_count` feeds that one sentence. Mock-up 6 puts it in the stat panel. Moving it needs a spec change first.
- **Vendor** is a borderless row link with a hover fill (`--surface-sunken`), the 32px mark, the name in Source Serif at `--t-20` and the size and ownership line at `--t-13`.
- **Resources** are row links: a 32px Forest-soft tile with the glyph, the label at `--t-16` weight 600, and the new-tab cue at the end.
- **Group headings** are the overline with a 16px glyph: building, tag and book in the card; users, hard hat and gantt in "Who it is for".
- **Audiences, Trades and Project phases are not in the card.** AECI-1780 moved them into the About band. The card carries no caption or correction line: since AECI-1666 they are in "About this listing".

**Settled by AECI-1780 (built), restored by AECI-1720 (owner direction, 2026-11-17).** The About band and "How teams use it" follow mock-up 6, with the mock-up's labels, product-named headings and neutral intros. Lines that make a claim or a promise wait for the owner.

- **About** is the Bone lead band. From `lg` it is two columns, `1.25fr 1fr` with a 48px gap: the description on the left and "Who it is for" on the right. Below `lg` they stack with a 32px gap. With no description and no under-review marker for it, the band is one column: "Who it is for" takes the full width, 24px under the heading (ruling 36, 2026-11-16). The layout reads product data only, so the server and the browser agree. The band renders when the description, its under-review marker or "Who it is for" shows, and the section nav lists About on the same test. The left column is `aec-product-about` (`products/product-about.ts`). The overline label "About" sits 12px above the heading and is `aria-hidden`, because the heading repeats it. The heading reads "About {product}", in the Section role (`--t-36`, Source Serif 600, line height 1.1, tracking -0.02em) on an inner `span`. The description sits 24px under the heading and keeps the stored paragraph breaks: a blank line starts a paragraph, a single line break is a `<br>`, and every line is interpolated text, never HTML. The first paragraph is the serif lead at `--t-24`, line height 1.4, in `--text-primary`. Later paragraphs are `--t-16` in `--text-secondary`, 16px apart. No caption sits under the description: its "Maintained by" line is in "About this listing" (AECI-1666).
- **"Who it is for"** is `aec-product-who-it-is-for` (`products/product-who-it-is-for.ts`): a white `aecCard` on the Bone band, 24px padding and 32px from `md`, with an `h3` at `--t-24` and the line "The teams, project phases and trades it is listed for." under it at `--t-14` in `--text-secondary`. Each chip carries its group's 14px glyph (users, hard hat, gantt) through the chip's `icon` input, passed by `aec-taxonomy-badge`'s optional `icon`. Audiences, Trades and Project phases follow in that order, each an `h4` overline with its 16px glyph over its `aec-taxonomy-badge` chips. Terms keep the API order, so phases read in lifecycle order. A group renders only when it has a term. A group a removal emptied disappears, heading and all, and "About this listing" lists the removal (AECI-1666). The card carries no caption or correction line: the Managed caption and each correction are in that panel.
- **The Managed caption is in "About this listing" (AECI-1666).** The Managed group is the three facets and the two Resources links. It took one line beside each part until AECI-1666. Now the panel holds one line, "Audiences, trades, phases and resources: Maintained by…", while any part of the group shows. That keeps the Terms' promise that the page marks each field the vendor maintains.
- **Mock-up wording, built (owner, 2026-11-17).** The labels above the headings, the product-named headings ("About {product}", "How teams use {product}") and the "Who it is for" line are built. The usefulness intro "What it is used for, by the people who use it and the stage of the project they are in." is not built: "by the people who use it" can read as "written by users", and the section is written by the vendor or by AECi, as "About this listing" says (`STAGE_2_5_SPEC.md` §12). It waits for the owner. A neutral alternative was proposed, "What it is used for, by team and by project phase.", and is not built either. Each label is the overline at `--t-12`, 12px above its heading, and `aria-hidden` because the heading repeats it. The section carries no caption, at its foot or beside the heading as the mock-up draws it. Its "How teams use it: Maintained by…" line is in "About this listing" (AECI-1666, `STAGE_2_VENDOR_PORTAL_SPEC.md` §4.5.8).
- **"How teams use it"** is the first section of the white body band. Its head is the label and the `h2` "How teams use {product}" at `--t-36`, with no intro line. The rows sit 32px below the heading. One row per facet, "By audience" then "By phase". Each row's `h3` is a 20px Forest glyph and the facet name in Source Serif at `--t-20`, 16px above its cards at every width. It is not a side column: mock-up 6's 170px `.use-lab` column left a blank strip and narrowed the cards, so it was dropped on 2026-11-24 to give the cards the full row. Each group is a white `aecCard` with 24px padding: the group name at `--t-20` serif, then its points, each after a 16px Forest tick, at `--t-16` in `--text-primary`. Cards run three across from `lg`, two from `md`, one below, and never more columns than the facet has cards, so one or two groups fill the row with no empty columns. The group stays a `dl` with one `dt`.

#### The vendor page on the band shell (AECI-1797, built)

Mock-up 11 and Chris's rulings of 2026-11-16 (`records/11-vendor-and-pair-notes.md`). Wording is unchanged except one approved caption.

- **Hero.** `aec-hero-band` with the breadcrumb and the "Vendor" overline, then the name at Display. The maintenance chip is retired (AECI-1666). Its words open "About this listing", the last band of the page. No picture on the right (ruling 2). A claimed vendor's logo is the 104px hero mark. An unclaimed vendor has no large mark. Its small mark sits beside the Unclaimed note under the name, and the logo component decides what it shows, never above 32px (ruling 6). The logo correction line is in "About this listing" (AECI-1666).
- **The description is the hero lede** (ruling 3). `--t-20` in `--text-on-brand-secondary`, at most 40rem wide. Below 640px it steps down one ramp role to `--t-16` at its 1.6 line height (ruling 77, 2026-11-16), so a long description does not make the hero a wall. It is never truncated or clamped. There is no "About" heading. A hidden description shows its under-review line there.
- **Under-review lines** sit under the lede, above the actions. Each names its field. Below `sm` each holds two lines' height, so the web font swap moves nothing. The correction lines that sat here are in "About this listing" since AECI-1666, after Products.
- **Actions.** "Visit website" is the `on-brand` button. The social links are `on-brand-secondary` icon buttons: a Bone wash and a Bone edge (ruling 13). They share one wrapping row at natural widths, so the band's equal-column action grid does not stretch them.
- **Facts card.** One `aecCard` with three cells: the Bone "At a glance" cell (Products as the figure at `--t-fig`, Integrations as an inline pair), Company facts, then Actions. From `md` the stat and facts cells sit side by side with Actions under both. From `xl` there are three columns, `0.8fr 2fr 0.9fr`, and the facts run as one list of label and value side by side. A vendor with no company facts has two cells, side by side from `md`. Cells are separated by a default hairline. The licence note sits under the card on the Bone ground.
- **One Bone band.** The facts ground is the page's one Bone band (ruling 12). The body band is white, and the mailing list is `ground="white"`.
- **No section nav** (ruling 13). The page has two sections.
- **Products.** The table stays (ruling 4), with the heading in the Section role and the caption "A to Z by name." under it, the order the API returns (`toVendorDetail`). The empty state keeps its sentence and shows no caption. The rating column reads "Product quality", as on the browse lists, with the same message id (ruling 5 said "Overall"; AECI-1720 restore C gave the score one name on every public page). Each row's trailing product link is a 44px square with the control radius (mock-up 11, `.go a`), so a row is 69px tall, as the mock-up draws it, and the target is 44px.

#### Hero glow, no blur, no noise (clash 13)

Chris ruled BLEND on the second pass, 2026-11-13: "Keep the soft single-colour glow in the hero. Drop the frosted-glass blur on the sticky bar. Drop the noise texture."

- The hero keeps one soft glow in a single colour, Forest hover (§2). The mock-up draws it as two radial gradients of that one colour. No second hue.
- The sticky section nav is a solid surface with a hairline `border-default` edge. Chris's words name the sticky bar. The wider ban on blur elsewhere rests on the existing §6 Don't on glassmorphism (blur, glass cards, glow borders).
- No noise texture. Bars and meters have no gradient fill (clash 19, which §5 owns).
- The rulings allow the hero glow. They do not allow other gradients, and glassmorphism stays banned (§6).

#### Cards may nest (clash 14, new)

Chris ruled NEW: the new design wins, and the old "Don't nest cards inside cards" is rewritten, and §6 now says so.

- A card may hold another card, panel or tile. The mock-ups nest one level. The rulings say nothing about two.
- **Only the outer card carries the shadow.** A nested panel is a tint or a hairline on the card's own surface. It takes no shadow of its own, so a stack shows one elevation.
- A nested panel keeps the readability floors. Text on it meets AA, and `--text-tertiary` is never used on a Bone, sunken or muted surface (§2).
- **Measured nests in the mock-ups:** rating boxes inside entity cards, score tiles, the reviewer update and the vendor reply inside a review card, the reach box inside the integrations fill, and the Bone stat panel inside the facts card. A Bone or tinted fill inside a card is a nested fill, not a band (see the band shell above).
- **Vendor replies** (rewrites the placement rule in §5 Auth & Reviews, clash 14). A reply now sits inside its review card as a nested panel, under the review text. It no longer sits below the card as an indented sibling block. It has a small leading mark, the label "Response from {vendor}" and the date. The review stays an `article` named by its title.

#### Coloured side stripes are allowed (clash 15, new)

Chris ruled NEW. The old "Side-stripe borders are banned" is rewritten, and §6 now says so. The clash recommendation had been a label colour or a small dot. Chris chose the stripe.

- A card or tile may carry a coloured stripe on its leading edge. The mock-ups use it to code the two review scores: Forest for product quality and Clay deep for onboarding experience.
- **A stripe is never the only cue.** Each stripe sits beside a text label ("Score one", "Score two" or the criterion name). The label stands alone, so the coding survives greyscale and colour-vision deficiency.
- **Stripe colours are existing tokens.** The score stripes use Forest and Clay deep. Clay deep is the meaning-bearing Clay (§2, 5.83:1 on white). Clay itself stays decorative.
- Gradient text stays banned, and so does a gradient stripe.

#### Card grids cap at three columns (clash 18, blend)

Chris ruled BLEND with no note. The blend is the clash recommendation: keep cards, cap at three columns, and break each grid with a group header or a wider lead card, as the grouped mock-up (7) does.

- **Three columns at most**, on every card grid. The mock-up steps from 3 columns to 2 and then to 1 as the viewport narrows (starting values below). The mock-ups' four-up grids go. The integration card grid stops at two: its roomier card needs 400px (§5 "The integration card").
- **Break the rhythm on purpose.** A group header (a hairline rule, the group name and its count) breaks a grouped grid. A wider lead card breaks an ungrouped one.
- **Ruling R3 (2026-11-14) dropped the wide lead card** on the product page integrations list. Chris replied "A", accepting all six recommendations. Row 6 reads "Score figures move back up. The wide lead card goes." The integrations list breaks with group headers instead. R3 does not say whether the wide first items in the mock-up's reviews, extensions and similar-products grids go.
- The wide lead card in §5 "Product card grid" is not rewritten here. It belongs to the card work (AECI-1765).

#### Breakpoints (clash 18 and the Same-Behavior Rule)

Breakpoints are Tailwind's defaults: `sm` 640px, `md` 768px, `lg` 1024px, `xl` 1280px and `2xl` 1536px. `styles.css` sets no custom breakpoints, and a card grid reflows at the same widths on every page. The mock-ups' 760, 1040 and 1260px map to `md`, `lg` and `xl`. The mock-up's "and below" becomes "below" the Tailwind width, so 1040px and below is below `lg`. Nothing is set at `2xl` yet.

### Open values (set by AECI-1750, AECI-1765, AECI-1766, AECI-1778 and AECI-1790)

- **Nested panel radius (AECI-1750, still open for Chris).** The mock-ups use 16px on nested panels and 12px on score tiles. Neither value is in the ruled scale of 8, 12, 24 and pill. The 12px tile is `--radius-control`. The 16px value has a token, `--radius-panel`, so a page issue can use it without an arbitrary value. Whether 16px joins the scale or snaps to 12 or 24 is Chris's call, and deleting the token is one line.
- **Radius of a logo tile by size (AECI-1750, see §5 "Logos").** The mock-up's mark is `border-radius: 22%`, with 8px at 22 to 28px and 24px at 104px. Only "small marks 8px" is ruled. AECI-1769 put the 32px and 64px marks on `rounded-mark` (8px), so the scale never inverts. The large-mark radius (64px, and the 104px hero mark) is still open for Chris.
- ~~**The `radius-xl` mapping (AECI-1750).**~~ Settled: there is no mapping. `rounded-xl` stays Spartan's 11.2px (5 uses: 2 on public pages, 3 in previews), and the 16px nested panel is `rounded-panel`. The sm, md and lg utilities stay as they are for the same reason.
- ~~**Sweep of off-scale spacing in the code (AECI-1750).**~~ Settled: no sweep in AECI-1750. Each redesigned public component snaps when its issue rebuilds it, and a follow-up issue sweeps the rest of the public and shell uses. See "Spacing" above.
- ~~**The card border (AECI-1765).**~~ Settled: no hairline beside the shadow. The card keeps a 1px border that is `transparent` (the unlayered `.aec-card` rule), so the box does not change size and forced-colors mode paints the edge. Measured on a white card on the white page ground at 1440px: the shadow alone gives the side edge 247 against 255 (1.07:1) and the bottom edge 238 (1.14:1). That is faint by design. A card is not a control, so no 3:1 floor applies (§4 "Shadow Vocabulary"). A hairline would double the edge the mock-up draws with the shadow alone. Chris may overrule: it is one colour change in `.aec-card`.
- ~~**Which surfaces take the card shadow (AECI-1765).**~~ Settled by rulings A7 and A8 (Chris, 2026-11-16). The table is under "Shadow Vocabulary" above: the plan cards take it now, review and score cards when their own issues build them, table wrappers never.
- ~~**The nested panel fill (AECI-1765).**~~ Settled: `--accent-primary-soft` (the same hex as the mock-up's `--band-cool`), with `rounded-panel` and no shadow, as `<div aecCard nested>`. Text on it: `--text-primary` 17.33:1, `--text-secondary` 6.77:1, Forest 10.80:1. `--text-tertiary` is 4.23:1 and stays banned there, as on `--surface-sunken` (4.40:1). Chosen over `--surface-sunken` because it separates from the white card a little more (1.14:1 against 1.10:1) and is the mock-up's own colour. Nothing nests a card yet. AECI-1788 (reviews) is the first caller.
- ~~**Wide first items in the reviews, extensions and similar-products grids (AECI-1765).**~~ Settled by ruling A8 (2026-11-16): no wide lead card in those grids. `/products` keeps its lead card (ruling 2 of 2026-11-16, `records/10-directory-notes.md`). See "Shadow Vocabulary".
- **Page wrap for the other shells (AECI-1790, the browse mock-up).** The wrap is 1328px with 24, 32 and 48px gutters on the band shell, the header and the footer (ruling A2). ~~The Browse shell~~ Settled for the Browse shell by ruling 3 of 2026-11-16: `BrowseLayout` sits on `BAND_WRAP_CLASS` (AECI-1793), so `/products`, the term pages and `/search` line up with the header and footer. The form shells still use the 7xl canvas (§5 "Layout shells"). Ruling A2 moves each page body when its own issue rebuilds it, not before.
- ~~**The glow (AECI-1766).**~~ Settled, built as `aec-hero-band` (`shared/hero-band/hero-band.ts`). The glow is two plain `radial-gradient()`s over the existing alpha tokens, `--surface-hero-glow` (Forest hover at 75%) and `--surface-hero-glow-soft` (45%), so no `color-mix()` is needed. The first is `900px 560px` at `78%` and the second is `600px 400px` at `6% 100%`. Both fade to `transparent` at 70%. In `radial-gradient(900px 560px ...)` the second length is the VERTICAL radius, so the first glow reaches 0.7 x 560 = 392px from its centre. **The centre is held at least 392px down (`max(42%, 392px)`), so the glow is fully transparent on the band's top row and the Forest header above meets the band on one flat ground, with no hairline and no colour step.** A test renders the band directly under the real header and samples the pixel rows at 1440, 1024 and 390px: the header's last row and the band's top row are both `rgb(30, 58, 47)`, within 1 per channel. **This sits lower than mock-up 6, which centres the glow at 42% of the hero.** At the mock-up's height that put the glow on the top row, a visible step under a Forest header. Flagged for Chris: if he wants the mock-up's glow, the header must carry it too. The rule is unlayered in `styles.css` (`.aec-hero-band`). **Contrast on the glow.** The §4 floor ("Floor: hero contrast is measured at the glow's peak") measures at the full Forest hover, which is stricter than the glow: Bone is 6.87:1 and `--text-on-brand-secondary` is 4.96:1. Measured against the lightest point of the glow itself, the 75% centre blended in sRGB, Bone is 7.75:1 and secondary is 5.59:1. Where the two glows overlap, which the radii allow only at the worst case, Bone is 7.34:1 and secondary is 5.29:1. All clear the 4.5:1 floor. `styles.css` quotes the same figures. The hero panel is opaque, so its ratios stand (Bone 9.18:1, secondary 6.62:1). Under forced colours the glow is dropped, and the band keeps a 1px `CanvasText` edge above and below and a 1px edge on the panel. The mock-up's 128px bottom and 24px top hero padding, and the 1328px wrap with 24, 32 and 48px gutters, are kept. The Bone focus ring is 11.03:1 on flat Forest, 7.75:1 on the glow's lightest point and 7.34:1 where both glows overlap, all above the 3:1 floor for a focus indicator. The band has no entrance animation.
- ~~**Page wrap of the header and footer against the bands (AECI-1766).**~~ Settled by ruling A2 (Chris, 2026-11-16). The header, its Browse and mobile menu panels, and the footer now sit on `BAND_WRAP_CLASS` (`shared/band-wrap.ts`), the same 1328px wrap with 24, 32 and 48px gutters as the bands. Page bodies do not move now. Each moves when its own issue rebuilds it. Until then an un-rebuilt page body on the 7xl canvas sits 8 to 16px off the shell's edge. Measured left content edges in CSS px, with Chromium's 15px classic scrollbar gutter, on 2026-11-16:

  | Window | Header row and wordmark | Footer columns and copyright | Hero and Bone bands (`/_demo/spartan`) | `/about` body (7xl, unchanged) |
  | ------ | ----------------------- | ---------------------------- | -------------------------------------- | ------------------------------ |
  | 1440px | 97                      | 97                           | 97                                     | 105 (8px right)                |
  | 1280px | 48                      | 48                           | 48                                     | 32 (16px left)                 |
  | 1024px | 48                      | 48                           | 48                                     | 32 (16px left)                 |
  | 768px  | 32                      | 32                           | 32                                     | 32                             |
  | 390px  | 24                      | 24                           | 24                                     | 24                             |

  Without a scrollbar the 1440px row is 104 against 112. The `/about` column records the page before AECI-1800, which moved its body onto the wrap, so it now lines up with the header. The header stays 64px below `md` and 76px from `md`. The Browse panel's first column and the mobile menu's content sit 1px further in, inside the panel's 1px side edge, as before. Below 320px the mobile menu keeps its 16px gutter.

- ~~**Connector products on the band shell (AECI-1778).**~~ Settled for now: nothing moves. "Built within" stays in the facts card, under Vendor. "Integrations it powers" stays in the body, before or after Integrations by the §13.6 role rule. The reach line ("Connects N products in the AEC Integrations catalog") sits in the hero under the name. AECI-1788 (2026-11-17) restyled "Integrations it powers" in the Integrations section's language and moved nothing: see §5 "Open values (components)". "Built within" is drawn by the facts card, which AECI-1788 did not touch.

### Named Rules

**The Borders-Not-Shadows Rule** (rewritten by clash 11, see ADR 0059). Buttons, chips, badges, inputs and tabs use borders to separate from their surface (1px default on the redesigned components, `border-strong` for emphasis, 2px for featured and error states, clash 23). Some shipped surfaces still draw 0.5px and change as each is rebuilt. Box-shadows on those elements are forbidden. Cards are no longer on that list. A top-level card takes the one card shadow, and a menu takes the one menu shadow. The old form of this rule forbade shadows on cards, and that ban is lifted for `--shadow-card` only. The failure the rule was written against, a rounded rectangle with a generic drop shadow, still applies to any shadow that is not one of the recipes above.

**The Arrow Rule** (AECI-919, rewritten by clash 33). Chris ruled NEW on clash 33 and left no note: the new design wins. **An arrow may mean "go here" as well as data-flow direction.** The mock-ups use an arrow for "It does exist", "See all categories", the read-more circle on a card and the jump after "reviews". The old rule banned all of that. The failure it was written against is real, so two limits stay.

- **The direction vocabulary is unchanged.** The three directional glyphs, `→` outbound, `←` inbound and `⇄` both, mean data-flow direction. They come only from a direction helper, and every one of them is framed to the **context product** (`STAGE_1_5_SPEC.md` §3.2). There are two emitters: `directionGlyph()` (`products/pair-direction-labels.ts`) and `contextDirectionLabel()` (`search/mechanism-labels.ts`). On the connector page, which is read from neither product, the words name both products (`pairFlowSentence`, same file) and the glyph keeps the hub's or endpoint A's frame. The `/search` integration card does the same, framed on the record's source, which is the sender on a one-way record (AECI-1939). The powered hub's flat rows call `directionGlyph()` and keep a `·` placeholder for an unknown direction at the call site (AECI-1123 folded their hand-rolled copy in).
- **A "go here" arrow is a drawn icon, never one of those text characters.** It is an arrow or a chevron from the icon set (§5 "Icons"). It is `aria-hidden` and mirrors in right-to-left text. A drawn icon cannot be confused with the direction set, and a text `→` can.

| Job                                                                                               | Use                                                                                  | Never                           |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------- |
| Separating a pair of things (the pair-page rail, any "A and B" header)                            | a hairline rule (`h-12 w-px bg-(--border-default)`), or nothing                      | `⇄`, `↔`, `+`                   |
| "Activating this goes somewhere" (a row's click affordance, a card's read-more, a "see all" link) | an arrow or chevron icon from the set, `aria-hidden`, mirrored in right-to-left text | the text characters `→` and `←` |

`+` is not an escape hatch for the first row: it is already spent on the version-diff `added` marker (see the Diff markers component). The rule exists because the pair page violated it twice at once. The rail rendered a hardcoded `⇄` at `text-3xl`, louder than the `⇄` a bidirectional mechanism earned below it. The product-detail integrations row rendered a nav `→` in the same row as a direction `→`. Both are collisions of **meaning**, not of style, so a reader who learns the vocabulary in one place is actively misled in the other. The clash record names the same collision on the mock-up's integration cards, which carry a read-more arrow beside a real direction arrow ("Revu sends"). Whether a card may carry both is open (AECI-1782 and AECI-1765).

A **display name is not exempt.** A mechanism heading, a card title or a badge label that carries `A → B` asserts a direction, and on a context-framed surface it asserts an absolute one that will contradict the relative arrow beside it. The pair page's card `h2` is the shipped case: the API strips a directional pair title rather than render it (`toMechanismHeading`, `apps/api/src/lib/drizzle-helpers.ts`).

**The Link Treatment Rule** (AECI-980). **A link's styling states its ROLE, and every new tab is both drawn and announced.** Before AECI-980 the app spelled one job four ways, spelled the new-tab cue three ways, and spelled `rel` four ways. None of that was a decision; it was six years of local choices.

There are five roles. Pick by what the link does, never by where it sits.

| Role                    | Recipe                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Where                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ----------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Primary action**      | the primary button (§5 Buttons): `accent-primary` fill, white text, 44px high, 12px radius, `--t-14` at weight 600, `no-underline`. On a Forest ground the on-brand button, Bone fill with Forest text                                                                                                      | **One per surface.** Today that is "Visit website" on product and vendor detail.                                                                                                                                                       |
| **Standalone link**     | `--t-14` at weight 600, `text-(--text-secondary)`, `underline decoration-(--border-strong) underline-offset-4`, `min-h-11` with no side padding, `rounded-mark`, `hover:text-(--text-primary)`                                                                                                              | Every "go look at a page" link. `aec-view-public-link` is the reference spelling.                                                                                                                                                      |
| **Citation**            | the standalone recipe minus the padding, on `rounded-(--radius-sm)`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | A source hanging off a figure or a stat. The home band's "Source" is the only one.                                                                                                                                                     |
| **Admin metadata chip** | `rounded-full border`, `px-3 py-1`, `text-xs font-medium`, `no-underline`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | **Admin console only**, and only inside a `flex flex-wrap` chip row that also holds non-link chips. The pill is load-bearing there: flattening it would break the row's grammar.                                                       |
| **In-text link**        | `text-(--accent-primary)`, `underline underline-offset-2` (permanent, not hover-only), `hover:text-(--accent-primary-hover)`, `focus-visible:outline-2 focus-visible:outline-offset-2 focus-visible:outline-(--accent-primary)`. No padding, no size or weight of its own: it inherits the sentence's type. | A link that is a word or phrase inside running text: a notice, a help line, an empty state. `vendor-contest-protest.ts` (`linkClass`) is the reference spelling. The portal's product-not-found notice uses it (AECI-1102, AECI-1129). |

Clash 21 (CURRENT, no note from Chris) keeps this rule. The mock-up's bold Forest links without an underline (`.f-learn a`, `.scope a`, `.inline-link`, "See all categories", `.card .go`, `.see-all a`) are dropped. "See all" and similar links use the standalone recipe. A link inside a sentence uses the in-text recipe. One filled button stays per surface. §5 Buttons has the button recipe. The standalone link's old `text-xs font-medium` is retired, because 12px is not a link size on the ramp (§3) and weight 500 belongs to two chips only. The new size and weight are the mock-up's own standalone link (`.know-link`, mock-up 8, line 1088: 14px at 600, text secondary, underlined with a `border-strong` line at a 4px offset). They are starting values for AECI-1758 to confirm.

**Accent is spent on the primary action and nowhere else.** A second accent-coloured link on a surface competes with the one thing the reader is meant to do. AECI-980 pulled three links off accent for this: the pair page's "View listing" and "Documentation", and the integration group card's "View product".

**The in-text link is the one exception, and it earns it.** Inside a sentence the link must be told apart from the words around it. The underline is the non-colour cue axe's `link-in-text-block` rule requires, and the accent is the colour cue. Neither alone is enough (AECI-1102). The exception covers a link inside running text only. A link that stands on its own line is a standalone link, whatever surface it sits on. The underline is permanent because the reader must find the link before hovering. The hover colour is restated because a `text-*` utility overrides the base layer's `a:hover`, so without it the link gives no hover feedback.

**When a link opens a new tab.** Two cases, and only two.

1. **The destination is not ours.** Any third-party site. We do not control what it does with the reader's session or their back button.
2. **The reader is mid-task on an editing surface.** The vendor portal and the admin console hold unsaved form state, `apps/web` has no `CanDeactivate` guard and no `beforeunload` handler, and a same-tab navigation silently discards an edit. The link is a lookup, not a destination.

Everything else stays in the tab. A link from a read-only public page to another public page is a destination, and opening it in a new tab steals the reader's back button for nothing.

**The vendor portal's "Learn more" links into the vendor guide** (AECI-1253) are case 2: standalone links, new tab, `rel="noopener"`, one component (`vendor/components/vendor-docs-link.ts`). The visible text is "Learn more" and a visually hidden suffix names the page. `STAGE_2_VENDOR_PORTAL_SPEC.md` §6.20 has the list.

**One page-scoped exception: `/vendor-program`** (Chris, 2026-10-12). It is a landing page whose job is a claim, so its links into the docs (`/docs/*`) open a new tab with the full treatment below, and the claim stays one tab away. Its conversion links ("Claim your listing", "Questions? Contact us", "Go to your portal") stay in the tab. `VENDOR_PROGRAM_PAGE_SPEC.md` §3 holds the rule. It does not extend to any other public page, `/docs/vendors/plans` included.

**One surface-scoped exception: the claim confirmation.** After a claim is sent, the request form's "How claims work" link (`requests/request-form-body.html`) opens `/docs/vendors/claiming-your-listing` in a new tab with the full treatment, so the confirmation and its "What happens next" steps stay on screen. It applies on the claim page and in the request drawer alike, and it no longer closes the drawer. No other link in the request form changes.

**Case 2 has one supersession: if the destination is one of our own request forms, open the in-place drawer instead** (`aecRequestTrigger` + `aec-request-drawer`, AECI-128). Not navigating beats navigating elsewhere: the edit is safe, and the vendor keeps the thing they are correcting on screen behind the panel. The anchor keeps its `href` and the full new-tab treatment, because the no-JS and modified-click paths do navigate. AECI-967 did this at the vendor portal's three correction links; `STAGE_2_VENDOR_PORTAL_SPEC.md` §6.9 carries the detail.

**Every new-tab link carries `aec-new-tab-icon`** (`apps/web/src/app/shared/new-tab-icon/new-tab-icon.ts`). It is the ONLY place the `aec-icon` `external` glyph (an up-right arrow) is drawn, and it carries the `sr-only` "(opens in a new tab)" note in the same breath — because the two halves kept shipping apart. Seven links disclosed the new tab to a screen reader and showed a sighted reader nothing; eight drew a `↗` text character instead of the glyph. The note sits **inside** the anchor, not beside it: a sibling span is not read in a VoiceOver rotor or an `NVDA+F7` links list, so a disclosure parked outside reaches browse mode and nowhere else.

The text `↗` is retired. It is not a direction glyph, but it sits one character from the vocabulary the Arrow Rule above reserves, and on the pair page it rendered in the same card as a real `→`. A drawn icon cannot be confused with the direction set.

**A caller-supplied `aria-label` overrides the note**, because an `aria-label` replaces the anchor's contents for assistive tech. So any link that passes one must state the new tab in that name itself, and the name must begin with the visible text (WCAG 2.5.3 Label in Name).

**The one exception is a brand-glyph icon button** — the vendor hero's social links. A 9x9 icon button has no room for a second mark, and adding one would read as a sixth platform. Those disclose the new tab in the accessible name only, which is also their only name.

**`rel` has exactly two recipes.** Nothing else.

| Destination                                                       | `rel`               |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------- |
| Our own pages (portal or admin linking out to the public catalog) | `noopener`          |
| Anything external                                                 | `noopener nofollow` |

`noopener` is never optional: without it the new browsing context gets a handle on this one. `nofollow` on the external recipe is a deliberate SEO position, and it was previously half-applied — "Visit website" passed link equity to vendor sites while the pair page's "View listing" did not, which meant the same decision was made twice, differently, by accident. Vendors pay for plans, so a followed link from a paid listing would read to Google as a paid link.

**`noreferrer` is deliberately absent (AECI-1640).** AECI-980 added it, and it hid AECi from every destination: vendors saw our clicks as direct traffic and could not check Vendor Performance click counts against their own analytics. It protected almost nothing, because the site-wide `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin` (`apps/web/src/server/seo-headers.ts`) already sends only our origin on a cross-site click, never the path or query. Do not add it back to the external recipe. `<img referrerpolicy="no-referrer">` on avatars is a separate concern and stays.

## 5. Components

Components are bound to tokens via the front-matter `{...}` references. Concrete behavior, states, and Spartan brain primitive bindings below.

> **Behavior providers (ADR 0010, Accepted).** Component _behavior_ here is headless. **Spartan brain** (ADR 0005) covers the overlay primitives — buttons, popovers, dialogs. **Angular Aria** (`@angular/aria`, stable in v22) is the provider for _new_ interactive and form-control patterns: select, combobox, listbox, radio, accordion, tree, grid, menu, toolbar, tabs. Both bind to the tokens below identically, via Tailwind **`aria-*:` variant utilities** (`aria-selected:`, `aria-expanded:`, `aria-checked:`) plus the **`data-[active=true]:`** variant Aria sets on the active option — token-bound, no TS state mirror. (Three adopter-facing deviations: Aria@22 ships no `radio`/`select`, so listbox/combobox stand in; discrete-choice Aria controls bridge into Signal Forms via `[(value)]`+`(valueChange)`, not `[formField]`; and **the accordion is not used for SSR-indexable content** — its panel renders through `afterRenderEffect`, which is client-only, so the panel ships empty to crawlers. See the Disclosure group card below, and the ADR.) The behavior provider is invisible to the visual system — the **Anchor-Site Rule** governs composition, hierarchy, density, and atmosphere, not which library supplies keyboard/ARIA logic, so two providers is not a mashup. See `docs/adr/0010-angular-aria-alongside-spartan.md`.

The redesign rulings of 2026-11-13 and 2026-11-14 changed the components in this section. They are clashes 19 to 25, 39 to 41 and 43, and Chris's rulings R1, R2, R3 and R5. A clash number beside a rule means that ruling set it. Where the record has Chris's own words, they are quoted. Where it says only NEW, BLEND or CURRENT, the rule follows the clash recommendation and `PLAN.md` §4.4. The record is `docs/design/mockups/redesign-2026-11/records/design-rulings.md`. ADR 0059 records only the rewrites its table lists, which here are the shadow rule, the radius rule, nested cards, side stripes, the icon rule (clash 34) and smooth scrolling (clash 38). Values the rulings left open are listed under "Open values (components)", each with its code issue.

**Scope (R2, 2026-11-14).** Chris: "Just do public pages for now". Ruling R2 builds the public pages first. Shared components (the header, the footer, the button and the input) are one component on every surface. Portal and admin page bodies wait until Chris has verified the public pages, and take the new tokens and nothing more until then. R2 is a staging point and not a permanent limit. Page bodies are restyled later, and nothing ships to production half built. The deferred portal and admin migrations are AECI-1756, AECI-1757, AECI-1761 and AECI-1762.

**The portal and console shared pieces (AECI-1729).** The portal and admin page bodies are now restyled, and they build from two folders on top of the site-wide primitives (`aecButton`, `aec-icon`, `aec-chip`, `aecCard` and the field recipe in `shared/form-field/`). The vendor portal's pieces live in `apps/web/src/app/vendor/shared/`: the page wrap (`portal-layout.ts`), the one Bone notice (`portal-notice.ts`), the form with its sticky save bar (`portal-form.ts`, `keep-clear-of-save-bar.ts`) and the portal's segmented toggles (`portal-segmented.ts`). The operator console's pieces live in `apps/web/src/app/admin/console/`: the table recipe, panel, label and caption (`console-table.ts`), the segmented filter (`console-filter.ts`), the detail-page looks such as facts, notices, neutral boxes and status lines (`console-detail.ts`), the status-to-tone map and the console chip tones (`status-tones.ts`, `chip-tones.ts`), the inline control (`inline-control.ts`), the danger form (`danger-action.ts`) and the row disclosure (`disclosure-button.ts`). A screen binds these and keeps no copy of its own.

**Ruling on the shared button and input (Chris, by Slack, 2026-11-14).** `BUTTON_PRIMARY_CLASS`, `BUTTON_SECONDARY_CLASS`, `FIELD_INPUT_CLASS` and `.aec-field-control` are shared with the portal and admin. The question was whether they inherit the new button and input or the classes are forked. Chris: "A. We will do admin pages i just want a good natural stopping point where i can verify the work". Option A was: the portal and admin inherit the new shared button and input. One component everywhere. AECI-1813 sweeps any layout that breaks. AECI-1758 and AECI-1764 are unblocked. The portal and admin keep their current icons and Tailwind's 150ms default until their own migrations run (§5 "Icons" and "Motion").

This section describes components, not page layouts. The home, the browse lists, the vendor page and the pair page with integrations have their own mock-ups (9 to 12, approved 2026-11-16) and their own entries below.

**The Same-Behavior Rule.** A pattern behaves the same way on every page that has it. Listing paging is infinite scroll through `<aec-pagination-footer>`, except that the list of integrations pages by group (R5 below). (The operator console's feeds and lookup screens keep Previous/Next by an explicit ruling, `docs/ADMIN_PANEL_SPEC.md` §5.7.) Filters live in `<aec-facet-panel>`, except for the list of integrations (R5 below).
