Sort sits in the toolbar row and offers the same orders, except that the list of integrations has no sort control (R5 below). Filters, sort and view live in the URL, and changing one keeps the reader's scroll position. Empty, loading and error states read alike. A visitor who learns a pattern on `/products` must not relearn it on `/search` or a browse page. Anyone building or reviewing a page that departs from an existing pattern stops and tells Chris before shipping, with what differs and which page sets the pattern. He decides whether to align it or keep the difference. A kept difference is recorded in that component's entry here, with the reason. This rule exists because `/search` shipped a Previous/Next paginator while every other listing infinite-scrolled, and nobody raised it.

**A ruled difference on the product page's Integrations panel (AECI-2021, ruled 2026-11-23).** Chris ruled that the Integrations section's filter panel shows a control only when it can change the list. A chip shows only while its count over the unfiltered rows is below the row total, and a pressed chip always stays. The search box needs 5 rows, and the Cards/Table toggle 2. A panel with no control left does not render. This applies to the product page's Integrations panel only. "Integrations it powers" on a connector page keeps its search box and toggle on every list, so the two panels on one connector page can now differ. That difference is ruled, not an oversight. The section entry in §5 has the detail.

**Departures ruled under R5 (clash 45, 2026-11-14).** The new integrations list departs from five shipped patterns. Chris, by Slack: "A" (AECI-1736). Each kept departure becomes the rule for every page with that pattern. Planning that change on the other pages is not done in this file. Each page's code issue does it. Who adopts what, and when:

- **The product page's integrations list adopts all three kept departures now** (AECI-1782 and AECI-1783).
- **The vendor page and the pair page with integrations have no list of a product's integrations**, so the departures do not reach them. The vendor page keeps its products table (ruling 4 of 2026-11-16, `records/11-vendor-and-pair-notes.md`). The pair page shows the pair's own mechanisms and data flows.
- **`/products`, `/search` and the taxonomy browse pages keep the facet panel, the sort and the pagination footer.** Ruling 1 of 2026-11-16 (`records/10-directory-notes.md`) declined grouping, the jump rail and "Show more" there.
- **The Disclosure group card** (`IntegrationGroupCard`) is now only the wrapper of a Via lane on the product page and of a hub in "Integrations it powers". The heading-row List filter is retired (AECI-1788). The connector products' "Integrations it powers" section adopted the filter panel and the cards in AECI-1788. It keeps its group cards, one per hub, as the Via lanes keep theirs.

| Departure                                              | Ruling   | The rule                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| ------------------------------------------------------ | -------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Integration filters in their own panel                 | Kept     | A list of integrations puts its filters in one panel of its own, not in `<aec-facet-panel>`. The panel holds the name filter, direction, data objects and partner category. On the product page's Integrations section each shows only when it can change the list (AECI-2021).                        |
| A long list groups by category with a sticky jump rail | Kept     | Rows group by partner category under a group header. A sticky rail jumps between groups. The rail and the filter chips show the same count for the same category.                                                                                                                                      |
| "Show more" per group plus a load bar                  | Kept     | Each group opens a short peek and a "Show more" button reveals the rest. "Show more" and "Show fewer" are both the bordered secondary button. The "Load more" bar shows how far the reader is (the meter below). Every row stays in the server HTML. "Show more" reveals rows and does not fetch them. |
| Illustrated empty states                               | Rejected | Empty states stay text. They read alike on every page.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| A sort control on the integrations list                | Rejected | No sort control. The order is fixed, see R1 below.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |

Ruling R3 (AECI-1734, 2026-11-14, "A") sets the default for the long list. Decision 20 reads "The 58-integration list is grouped by category by default". The size at which a list counts as long, and the one-list mode in pages of 12, belong to AECI-1783.

**Order (R1, 2026-11-14).** Chris, by Slack, on AECI-1732: "Maintain orders as they are. If its a new feature a to z". Existing orders stay. Every new on-page list is A to Z by name, with the id as the tie-break, through the collation rule in `CLAUDE.md`. The via-lane groups and the powered-hub groups keep their count order. The pair page list keeps integrations first, then evidenced pairs, and since AECI-2043 Sync Service cards come last, A to Z by the offering vendor. In the grouped list, the groups run A to Z by category name with the id as the tie-break (AECI-1783). "Confirmed by both companies" is a label only. No filter and no sort reads it. The mock-up's "Most evidence first" sort cannot ship (`PLAN.md` §7.3).

### Buttons

The redesign rewrites this subsection (clashes 20, 21 and 37, recorded in `design-rulings.md`, and clash 12's radius, see ADR 0059). AECI-1758 built one `aecButton` directive for `<button>` and `<a>`, in `apps/web/src/app/shared/button/button.ts`. A native `<button>` needs no headless behaviour, so `aecButton` does not wrap Spartan's `BrnButton`. The two can sit on one element.

**How to use it.** `<button aecButton type="submit">` is a primary button. `<a aecButton variant="secondary" routerLink="…">` is a link that looks like a secondary button and stays a link. The inputs are `variant` (`primary`, `on-brand`, `secondary`, `ghost` or `danger`), `compact`, `iconOnly` and `disabled`. Layout classes such as `w-full` and `sm:me-auto` still go on the element. In new code they go in one class binding (`[class]="'w-full'"`), not a static `class` (see **At hydration** below). Height, radius, fill, edge and text colour never do. Code that binds `[class]` uses `buttonClass(variant, options)` from the same file. `BUTTON_PRIMARY_CLASS` and `BUTTON_SECONDARY_CLASS` are that recipe, so their callers render the new button. The edge colour lives on the unlayered `.aec-btn` and `.aec-btn-secondary` rules in `styles.css`. A `border-(--x)` utility on a button does nothing, because an unlayered class beats every utility. **At hydration**, a button with a static `class` beside the directive (the 404 "Go home" link's `mt-12`) drops to that class alone until Angular's update pass restores the recipe. The update pass runs before any frame, so no reader sees it. A style read in that gap does see it, and then the fill fades in: that is what made the AECI-1922 e2e flaky (AECI-1937, measured). New code gives a button one class binding and no static `class` beside it (`ANGULAR_STYLE_GUIDE.md` §10).

Chris's note on clash 20 was "I kind of like same size buttons". The ruling is BLEND: one height, a 12px radius, a bordered secondary and no shadow.

- **Portal and admin buttons move to `aecButton` slice by slice (AECI-2083).** `src/native-button.guard.spec.ts` holds every `<button>` without `aecButton` under `vendor/` and `admin/` to a shrink-only count per file (`src/guard-allowlists/native-button.txt`). A new file starts at zero. A slice that keeps a native button (a tab, a switch, a disclosure) says why in its issue and leaves the count on its line.
- **One height: 44px** (clash 20). Every button of every variant has it. The survey found no shared button and five heights in use (32, 36, 40, 44 and 48px). The mock-up's `.btn` sets `min-height: 44px`, and its small button keeps the same 44px with less side padding (mock-up 6, lines 88 and 98). The height is a minimum, so a label that wraps grows the button. An icon-only button is 44 by 44px. That clears the 24 by 24px target floor (WCAG 2.5.8) by 20px, so no button needs its own target-size check. A segmented control is one frame of 44px holding 36px segments (mock-up 6, lines 255 and 256). Each segment is above the floor.
- **Shape: 12px** (`rounded-control`, clash 12). Never a pill. "Sign in" and every primary action is a rounded rectangle. A chip may be a pill. A button, a link styled as a button and a primary action may not.
- **Label:** 14px (`--t-14`) at weight 600, sentence case (mock-up 6, line 88). This is not the 13px `label` role. §3 leaves the button size to this subsection.
- **Padding:** 24px at the side (`spacing.6`) and 16px on the compact variant (`spacing.4`). Ghost always takes 16px. The icon and the label sit 8px apart (`spacing.2`). Every variant has a 1px edge, transparent unless it is secondary, so all variants measure the same. Forced-colours mode paints the transparent edge as a real one.
- **Primary** (`button-primary` and `button-primary-hover`): Forest fill, white text at 12.34:1. Hover brightens to Forest hover. Never a pill, never Clay.
- **On a Forest ground** (`button-on-brand`, clash 3): Bone fill with Forest text, 11.03:1 (§2). It is the one main action in the Forest hero. Hover fills with `--surface-on-brand-hover` (`button-on-brand-hover`), where Forest text is 9.46:1. Its focus ring is Bone. Clay never fills a button.
- **Secondary** (`button-secondary` and `button-secondary-hover`): `surface-raised` fill, `text-primary` text and a 1px `border-strong` edge. "Secondary buttons are bordered." Hover sinks the fill to `surface-sunken` and the text to Forest (mock-up 6, lines 96 and 97). `border-strong` measures 1.48:1 on white, 1.42:1 on raised and 1.32:1 on Bone. WCAG 1.4.11 does not require a boundary on a button when its text label identifies it, so the mock-up's edge stays. The 3:1 boundary rule applies to form controls whose boundary is the only thing identifying them: inputs, selects, checkboxes and switches (see Inputs below). A text-labelled button is identified by its label.
- **Ghost** (`button-ghost`): transparent fill, `text-primary` text, no visible border, 44px high. Used for tertiary actions in dense surfaces. Hover shifts the text to Forest.
- **Danger** (`button-danger` and `button-danger-hover`, AECI-1758): `status-error` fill with white text at 6.54:1. Hover darkens the fill to `--status-error-hover` (`#8C1D18`), 9.11:1. It always carries a text label, so colour is never the only sign. It is for a destructive action and counts as the surface's filled button.
- **Danger is for a confirm that takes something away from someone, or penalises them, and cannot be fully given back (ruled 2026-11-25, AECI-2124).** A confirm that gives something back is primary even when nothing undoes it: "Lift and restore every seat" returns every seat's access and takes nothing away.
- **The admin "Set end date" confirm stays primary (ruled 2026-11-25, recorded on AECI-1729).** It sets a future end and removes nothing at the time of the click.
- **An action that Restore fully undoes is not a danger action (AECI-1811).** The admin "Hide the value" and "Hide from the public site" confirms are primary, because unhiding puts everything back. Retire is danger in the portal and in admin: it closes other companies' open requests and row threads, and Restore reopens none (`STAGE_2_VENDOR_PORTAL_SPEC.md` §4.6.1, §4.6.4).
- **The operator console follows the same rule (AECI-2112).** On `/admin/vendors/:id`, "Hide from the public site" and "Hide the value" confirm with a primary button, and their Bone warning notices stay as prominent as before. "Retire integration", "Confirm removal" on a seat and "Remove the logo" on an objection have no full undo and are danger.
- **The vendor page's second half follows it too (AECI-2124).** Danger: "Suspend and send the notice" and "Start stage 2 and send the notice" (each penalises the vendor and sends a notice), "Return the records" (it discards the vendor's open product proposals), "Terminate and refund" (it ends every subscription, bans every seat and moves money), "Confirm revoke" and the bulk "Revoke N subscriptions" (the subscription ends now, its product drops to Free and every seat is emailed), and "Confirm withdraw" on a grant (nothing restores a withdrawn grant). Primary: "Lift and restore every seat", "Log this cost" and "Void this entry" (a cost can be voided, and a void gives back what it would have deducted), "Add the seat", "Grant the subscriptions", the ban-term grant, "Save end date", "Save reference" and the legal name and billing email Save. Every opener is a compact secondary button.
- **The second action on a Forest ground** (`button-on-brand-secondary`, AECI-1766): `aecButton variant="on-brand-secondary"`. Fill `--surface-on-brand-wash` (Bone at 10%), hover `--surface-on-brand-wash-hover` (Bone at 16%), label `--text-on-brand`, a 1px edge `--border-on-brand-strong` (Bone at 50%, the one on-Forest alpha that is a 3:1 control edge), a Bone focus ring at 3px and 3px, 44px high, 12px radius, disabled at 60% as the other variants. It replaces the mock-up's `color-mix()` ghost (clash 6). Clay never fills a button. Ghost on Forest is still not built: its resting label is 1.60:1 on Forest. Measured, with the wash composited over each ground:

| Ground                                 | Label on wash | Label on hover wash | Edge against the ground |
| -------------------------------------- | ------------- | ------------------- | ----------------------- |
| Forest                                 | 8.28:1        | 6.94:1              | 4.01:1                  |
| Hero panel                             | 6.99:1        | 5.93:1              | 3.59:1                  |
| Footer ground                          | 10.30:1       | 8.52:1              | 4.51:1                  |
| Forest hover, the strictest glow point | 5.41:1        | 4.71:1              | 3.00:1                  |

- **One filled button per surface** (clash 21, the Link Treatment Rule stands). The filled primary, or the on-brand button on Forest, is the surface's one main action. Everything else is secondary, ghost or a link. The clash record found three filled primaries in view on page 6 ("Visit website", the sticky bar's "Write a review" and the review panel's "Write a review"). Only one of them stays filled. The product page is built with "Visit website" as that one, and both "Write a review" placements secondary (AECI-1760). This is pending Chris's ruling.
- **No shadow** on any button, resting or hovering (clash 11). Press movement is clash 37 and §5 "Motion" owns it. The mock-up presses with `translateY(1px) scale(.98)` (line 89). The old line "no transform, no scale" is rewritten by clash 37, and the movement is off under reduced motion.
- **States:** `:focus-visible` draws a 3px outline at a 3px offset in Forest. On a Forest ground, the hero panel and the footer ground it is Bone, set by AECI-1747 as `--focus-ring-on-brand`. A Forest ring on the hero panel is 1.20:1 and must not be used (mock-up 6, line 29). It is an outline and not a box-shadow, because forced-colours mode drops shadows. Disabled reduces opacity to 0.6 and disables pointer events. It does not change colour. Disabled text is exempt from the contrast minimum (WCAG 1.4.3), and the measured values are in the table below.
- **Disabled is the `disabled` input.** On a `<button>` it sets the native `disabled` attribute. A link has no native disabled state, so on an `<a>` it sets `aria-disabled="true"` and `tabindex="-1"`, and a click listener cancels the click. Enter on a link fires a click, so Enter is cancelled too. The listener is added in code and only while the link is disabled, so an enabled link keeps no listener of its own (the anchor rule in `ANGULAR_STYLE_GUIDE.md`). Re-enabling restores the link's own `tabindex`. A caller that sets `aria-disabled` by hand gets the dimmed look only. The link still takes focus and still navigates. The input is the supported way.
- **Type:** a `<button aecButton>` with no `type` attribute gets `type="button"`, so it never submits a form by accident. Write `type="submit"` on a submit button. An explicit type is kept.
- **Press:** `motion-safe:` `translateY(1px) scale(.98)` over `--duration-fast` with `--ease-standard`. Colour changes run on the same step.
- **Icon-only:** `iconOnly` draws a 44 by 44px square with no side padding. The caller gives it `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or visually hidden text. A decorative `<aec-icon>` is not a name. Dev mode logs an error when the name is missing.
- **Scope:** one button on every surface. The portal and admin inherit it (Chris, 2026-11-14, see the section introduction). The page restyle is public pages only (R2). AECI-1813 sweeps any portal or admin layout it breaks.

**Buttons still outside the component (hand-off, 2026-11-14).** The folder lists for the public pages are in AECI-1759 and AECI-1760. These places are named so nobody misses them:

- `preview/unified-home/unified-home-preview.ts` lines 624 to 626 (`primaryBtn`, `linkBtn`). A preview route. It stays as it is.
- `vendor/vendor-invite-page.ts` no longer has its `btn` constant. Its button is `aecButton`. The integration detail page's `BTN_PRIMARY`, `BTN_SECONDARY`, `BTN_DANGER`, `ICON_BUTTON` and `ROW_ACTION` constants are gone: AECI-1811 part 2 moved every user onto `aecButton`.
- `vendor/components/vendor-removal-election.ts` and `vendor/components/vendor-license-actions.ts` no longer read `BUTTON_SECONDARY_CLASS`. Their buttons are `aecButton`.

**Portal button heights (AECI-2113 survey, 2026-11-27).** No portal action button is under 44px. Every one is `aecButton`, and none adds a height class. The portal controls under 44px are the kinds this section exempts: the 36px segments inside the 44px segmented frame, the email switch, the tip triggers, and the 24px row-action links. The row-action links (`rowActionClass` and `quietActionClass` in `vendor-integration-create.ts`, `detailLinkClass` in `vendor-owned-integrations.ts`) stay text links beside the 44px buttons in their rows. Chris kept them as links on 2026-11-27.

**Migrated (AECI-1759).** The buttons and button-styled links in `layout`, `shared`, `home`, `products`, `vendors` and `not-found` are `aecButton`: the plan cards and plan calls to action, the claim dialog footer and close button, the mailing-list "Subscribe", "Load more", the feedback form and its dialog close, the home call-to-action pair, "Visit website", the vendor social icon links, "Claim" and "Suggest a correction", and the 404 "Go home". These stay outside the component on purpose:

- The Cards/Table view toggle and the other pressed toggles (the pair page's Basic/Detailed toggle, the taxonomy sort toggle). They follow the pressed-toggle rule.
- Tabs, in-text links ("Clear filters", "Show latest", the correction links) and chips.
- Select and combobox triggers (AECI-1764).
- The header icon controls (AECI-1771) and the footer links.
- Tiny inline icon triggers: the info hint, relative time, claim provenance and the combobox clear button. A 44px target would break the line they sit in.

AECI-1775 moved the two mobile menu account buttons to `aecButton`. "Sign in" is primary at full width (`layout/nav-menu.ts`). "Account" is secondary at full width (`layout/nav-menu-account.ts`). They sit on the menu's white panel, so neither takes an on-Forest variant.

`pagination-footer`'s button branch is the admin one. It now renders the shared secondary look, so it is visible on `/search` and on `/admin/vendors`.

- **A busy state does not set `aria-disabled`.** `aecButton` dims anything with `aria-disabled` to 60%, so a pending action guards its handler and marks the form `aria-busy` (the home hero does this while it checks a pair).
- **A disabled link uses the `disabled` input.** Spartan's `brnButton` marks a disabled link with `data-disabled`, which the `aecButton` look does not read. No public page uses `brnButton` on a link.
- **Flagged for Chris (orchestrator's decisions).** The mailing-list "Subscribed" state stays a disabled secondary (the live region carries the success). On the plans page the hero call to action and the plan cards are both filled today, unchanged by AECI-1759. On the product page "Visit website" stays filled.

**Migrated (AECI-1760).** The buttons and button-styled links in `about`, `methodology`, `updates`, `roadmap`, `vendor-program`, `contact`, `docs`, `legal`, `accessibility`, `auth`, `account`, `welcome`, `requests`, `reviews`, `claims`, `unsubscribe`, `notifications`, `waitlist`, `analytics` (the consent banner) and `core/app-update` are `aecButton`. The local `BTN` constants in `unsubscribe`, `updates`, `nudge-mute` and `claim-confirm` are gone, and `welcome`, `account` and the review-update panel no longer read `BUTTON_PRIMARY_CLASS` or `BUTTON_SECONDARY_CLASS`. Those two constants stay in `form-field-classes.ts`, but no portal or admin template reads them any more (checked by AECI-2113, 2026-11-27). Callers in these folders no longer append `disabled:opacity-50`. These stay outside the component on purpose: the star-rating radios and the "would you recommend" options in the review form, and its role select trigger.

- **Dismiss and close buttons are ghost icon-only.** The request drawer close, the waitlist welcome dismiss, the legal notice dismiss and the update banner dismiss were 32 to 36px. They are now 44px. The docs menu toggle (a disclosure with `aria-expanded`) is secondary icon-only.
- **Flagged for Chris (AECI-1760).** (1) "Write a review" is secondary in both product page placements. `ReviewCta` now defaults to `secondary`. A caller with no other filled button can pass `variant="primary"`. (2) The delete-account confirmation moved from a near-black fill to the `danger` variant. (3) The consent banner keeps "Decline" as secondary and "Accept" as primary, as on the live site before this change. Whether the two should carry equal weight is Chris's call. (4) `/contact` has one filled link per card and `/account` has one filled button per section ("Answer now", the profile "Save", "Save answers"). Each card or section is read as its own surface.

**Measured contrast (AECI-1758).** WCAG 2 ratios from the token hexes. The secondary edge is not a required boundary, see Secondary above.

| Variant   | Rest                    | Hover                        | Focus ring                                                | Disabled (0.6) |
| --------- | ----------------------- | ---------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------- | -------------- |
| Primary   | white on Forest 12.34:1 | white on Forest hover 7.68:1 | Forest on white 12.34:1                                   | 3.68:1         |
| On-brand  | Forest on Bone 11.03:1  | Forest on `#E7E1D2` 9.46:1   | Bone on Forest 11.03:1, hero panel 9.18:1, footer 13.71:1 | 5.04:1         |
| Secondary | ink on raised 18.97:1   | Forest on sunken 11.23:1     | Forest on white 12.34:1                                   | 5.12:1         |
| Ghost     | ink on white 19.80:1    | Forest on white 12.34:1      | Forest on white 12.34:1                                   | 5.25:1         |
| Danger    | white on error 6.54:1   | white on `#8C1D18` 9.11:1    | Forest on white 12.34:1                                   | 3.04:1         |

### Cards

Used for vendor profiles, integration cards, the `/search` result tiles (see Search & discovery below — they are the canonical instantiation of this primitive), and content modules. Distinct from the **Entity cards (index rows)** below, which despite the shared "card" name render as `<tr>` table rows and do _not_ use this primitive. The redesign rewrites this subsection (clashes 11 and 12, see ADR 0059, and clash 18, recorded in `design-rulings.md`). AECI-1765 built the card surface: `aecCard` in `apps/web/src/app/shared/card/card.ts`, with the unlayered `.aec-card`, `.aec-card-stripe` and `.aec-card-grid` rules in `styles.css`.

- **Corner style:** `rounded-card` (24px, clash 12).
- **Background:** `surface-base`, which is white (mock-up 6, line 293). The old text said `surface-raised`.
- **Shadow:** `--shadow-card` (§4 "Shadow Vocabulary", clash 11). The old "Shadow Strategy: None" is rewritten. Only the components named in that table take it. A card nested in a card takes none (clash 14).
- **Border:** no visible hairline. A 1px `transparent` border (`.aec-card`) keeps the box size and gives forced-colors mode an edge (settled, §4 "Open values").
- **The recipe:** `<section aecCard class="p-6">` is the frame (radius, fill, edge, shadow). Padding and layout stay on the element. `interactive` adds `relative`, the lift and a 3px focus ring at a 3px offset around the whole card (`has-focus-visible`, so a link inside it draws none of its own). `tone="warm"` swaps the fill to Bone for a featured card. `nested` is the panel form: `rounded-panel`, `--accent-primary-soft`, no shadow. `stripe="forest|clay"` adds the 7px leading-edge score stripe (clash 15), beside a text label. `phoneFlush` makes it a **form card** (AECI-1720 layout pass): below `sm` the card drops its radius, edge and shadow and pulls out over the 24px page gutter (`max-sm:-mx-6`), so its own 24px padding sits where the gutter was and its fields are as wide as a form with no card. Nesting a card in the gutter cost a field 48px at 390px and left a placeholder cut off. From `sm` it is the normal card. It needs a 24px gutter below `sm` and `p-6` below `sm`. Every card on a form page takes it, so a page has one look on a phone: the claim and correction pages and their receipt, every card on the review form (its "Thank you", "not signed in" and "already reviewed" states included), `/welcome`, every `/account` panel ("Your reviews" and "Delete account" included), sign-in and the listing request page. Below `sm` a form card stands 24px under the text above it, not 40px (`FORM_CARD_GAP_CLASS`), because its own top padding is already blank space there. The claim dialog is a full-screen sheet below `sm` with 24px padding and no card, so it was never narrowed (342px at 390, as on `main`). `aec-card-grid` is the three-column cap: one column, two from `md`, three from `lg`, 24px gap.
- **Hydration:** at hydration, a card with a static `class` beside the directive drops to that class alone until Angular's update pass restores the recipe. The update pass runs before any frame, so no reader sees it (AECI-1937, measured). New code gives a card one class binding and no static `class` beside it (`ANGULAR_STYLE_GUIDE.md` §10).
- **Card title:** the Card-title role (`text-t-20`) with `leading-tight` (25px line). A heading carries the size on an inner `span` or block link (the Unlayered-Heading Rule). `text-lg` left a 31px line.
- **Internal padding:** `spacing.6` (24px, mock-up 6, line 293). Dense list contexts may use `spacing.4` (16px).
- **Hover, when interactive:** the card lifts. The mock-up moves it `translateY(-4px)` on hover and `-1px` on press (lines 294 and 295). Clash 37 sets this and §5 "Motion" owns the timing. The classes are `motion-safe:group-hover/card:-translate-y-1` and `motion-safe:group-active/card:-translate-y-px` over `duration-base` and `ease-standard`, so reduced motion removes the move. **The hover sits on a static wrapper named `group/card`** (the grid `<li>`, or the component host), and the card lifts through it. A card that lifted on its own `:hover` would rise out from under a pointer near its bottom edge, drop, and flicker. Every interactive card sits in such a wrapper, and `e2e/card.spec.ts` holds the pointer 2px above the bottom edge to check it. The title turns Forest on hover. That colour change is the hover cue that remains under reduced motion (flagged for Chris to confirm). A card that is not interactive never lifts. It replaces "raises to 1px `border-strong`, no shadow, no scale".
- **Grids** cap at three columns with a 24px gap (clash 18, §4).
- **A card that is one link** names the product or vendor and nothing more. The whole card text is not the link name (`PLAN.md` §10.2). **One pattern, the stretched title link:** the link sits on the title and is stretched over the card with `after:absolute after:inset-0 after:rounded-card` on a `relative` card (the recipe adds `relative`). The link name is the title alone, and the vendor, rating, integration count and eyebrow read as plain text beside it. There is no `aria-label` on the link, because it would replace the whole name and hide those facts from a screen reader. The card stays clickable everywhere, middle-click and modified clicks work, and the anchor carries no listener. The product card grid, the three search hit cards and every later card that is one link use it. Before AECI-1765 the whole card was one `<a>`, so its name was the whole text (`D Recently added Dynamo for Revit Autodesk 3.3 11 reviews Not yet connected`). Now it is `Dynamo for Revit`.

### Disclosure group card

`apps/web/src/app/products/integration-group-card.ts` (AECI-841). One bordered card per group, used by
both product-detail integration sections. The header bar is the disclosure control; the body is the
group's rows.

- **Frame:** `aecCard` with `overflow-hidden`: `rounded-card` (24px), `--shadow-card`, no hairline. The §4 table names this card. The old 0.5px `border-default` and "no shadow" are rewritten by clashes 11 and 12.
- **Header:** `surface-sunken` fill with a `border-default` bottom rule. Contents in order: a chevron
  (the `chevron-down` icon, rotated 180° when open), the subject's logo, the subject name at
  `--t-20` (the card title role) on an inner `<span>` (the Serif-Floor Rule — the size cannot go on the `<h3>`, see the
  Unlayered-Heading Rule), and the group size at `--t-13` in text secondary, in the body face so it does not inherit the `<h3>`'s serif (AECI-1928). The code renders it at `text-xs`, 12px. The code says `text-t-20 leading-tight`: 20px on a 25px line.
- **Behavior is hand-rolled, not Angular Aria.** A `<button aria-expanded aria-controls>` inside the
  `<h3>`, over a `role="region"` panel hidden with the `hidden` attribute. Aria's accordion renders
  its panel through a client-only `afterRenderEffect`, which would ship an empty card to crawlers on
  an SSR page — see ADR 0010's deviation (c). **The panel content stays in the DOM when collapsed.**
- **A link never nests inside the header button.** When the group's subject has its own page, that
  link sits beside the button in the same header bar as a compact "View product" anchor.
- **That anchor stays in the tab.** It is the **standalone-link role** under the Link Treatment
  Rule above, and an ordinary in-app link: `routerLink`, no `target`, no `rel`, no
  `aec-new-tab-icon`. A product page linking to another product page is a destination, so neither
  new-tab case applies, and Back restores the reader's scroll position. (It opened a new tab until
  AECI-1125, as "a lookup, not a destination". That was the rule's only exception on a public page,
  and it is gone.) **The accessible name names the product and begins with the visible text**
  ("View product: Agave ERP Sync"). The visible "View product" repeats on every card, so the name
  has to say which product, and starting with the visible text keeps WCAG 2.5.3 Label in Name so
  speech input can target it. The link keeps a 28px target, over the 24px floor (AECI-1079).
- **Open by default.** Collapsing is a reader action; nothing is hidden from a crawler or a no-JS
  reader on first paint.
- **Clash 24 (blend) and the mock-up.** The integrations section is built from shipped parts: this card, the table, the row and the List filter (the List filter since superseded by AECI-1788: both sections take the filter panel recipe in `integration-filter-recipe.ts`). Every row stays in the server-rendered HTML. The mock-up draws one card per partner product in a three-column grid, not one disclosure card per counterpart. AECI-1782 settled it: the per-partner card is its own card (`product-integration-card.ts`), and this card stays as the lane wrapper when a page has a Via lane. The listing sort select is not one of the parts, because R5 rejects a sort control on the integrations list.

**Vendor portal variant (AECI-999) — superseded 2026-09-28 by the integration detail page, AECI-1156.**
The portal's Integrations tab used to nest the same pattern three deep: `vendor-counterpart-group.ts`
(`<h2>`), `vendor-integration-card.ts` (`<h3>`) and the data-flow lane, each retired. What replaced it
is described under "Vendor portal (Stage 2)" → "Integration detail page" below: the list still groups
by counterpart, but a row is a link to its own page rather than a disclosure trigger, and nothing on
this surface nests three deep any more.

### List filter (retired)

`integration-list-filter.ts` (AECI-841, AECI-848) was the name filter in an integrations section's heading row. It was deleted in AECI-1788 (2026-11-17). No page rendered it any more: the product page's `#integrations` was rebuilt to mock-up 6, and `#powered-integrations` follows the same recipe through `integration-filter-recipe.ts`. The `role="status"` line stays in the DOM from first paint when the panel renders, empty while nothing filters, so its first change is announced. Since AECI-2021, `#integrations` shows controls only when they can change the list; `#powered-integrations` retains its filter on every nonempty list.

### Entity cards (index rows)

Listing rows render through `ProductCard` (Phase 2 Spec §11.2, `apps/web/src/app/products/product-card.ts`). Despite the "card" name it renders as a **table row**: it uses an attribute selector on `<tr>` (`tr[aec-product-card]`) so the rendered DOM stays a valid `<tbody>` child — a custom element placed directly inside `<tbody>` is foster-parented out by the HTML tree builder (the same pattern Angular CDK uses for `tr[cdk-row]`). AECI-190 split the products catalog into two views: this `<tr>` component is the **table view** (shared by the `/products` table and the taxonomy browse-page tables), while a separate `ProductCardGrid` (below) is the **card-grid view** and the default for `/products`.

> **Formerly three.** §11.2 also specified `VendorCard` (`tr[aec-vendor-card]`) and `IntegrationCard` (`tr[aec-integration-card]`), the row primitives of the `/vendors` and `/integrations` index pages. AECI-165 removed both index pages (orphaned from the nav after AECI-160; the URLs now 301 to `/products`), leaving the two components with no consumer. They were deleted in AECI-657 along with `IndexLayout`. `/vendors/:slug` detail pages are unaffected — they never used `VendorCard`.

Row behavior:

- **No card shadow, no card radius** (clashes 11 and 12). `PLAN.md` §4.3 and §4.4 say "entity cards", and in this file that name means these table rows. They keep the hairline and take no `--shadow-card`. The card shadow applies only to the components §4 "Shadow Vocabulary" names.
- **Host:** `text-primary`; `hover` and `focus-within` raise the row fill to `surface-muted`. No shadow, no scale.
- **Cells:** `spacing.4` horizontal / `spacing.3` vertical padding. Numeric cells (counts, founded year) are `text-end` and `tabular-nums`. Linked cells shift to `accent-primary` on hover with a `focus-visible` ring.
- **Empty states:** a missing optional field renders an en-dash (`–`), never a bare blank, and color is never the sole signal. Since AECI-1793 the dash is `aria-hidden` and the words ("No vendor listed", "No ratings yet") are visually hidden text beside it, because an `aria-label` on a plain `<span>` is not reliably read.

Cell content:

- **ProductCard** (`tr[aec-product-card]`) — a monogram (`LogoOrInitial`, 32px) beside the name (→ `/products/:slug`), vendor (→ `/vendors/:slug`; nullable per AECI-115), the overall rating as a `RatingSummary` (`variant="cell"` — gold star + average + review count, or an en-dash below the §5.5 ≥5-review gate; `hidden md:table-cell` so it collapses with the vendor column on mobile, where the card-grid view carries the rating), and the integration count as an `IntegrationStat` (graceful "Not yet connected" at zero). AECI-190 folded this richer treatment in so the `/products` table view and the taxonomy browse-page tables share one row.
- **ProductTable** (`aec-product-table`, `products/product-table.ts`, AECI-1793) — the one table around those rows on `/products`, the four term pages and the `/search` products tab. Before it each page carried its own header. Hairlines only: a rule above the header, one under it and one under each row. Header cells are at the Label role, 13px at 600. The rating column is headed **"Product quality"**, the name the product page gives the same score. Ruling 4 of 2026-11-16 renamed it "Rating" to "Overall" so it would match the product page. AECI-1720 restore C moved the product page to mock-up 6's names, "Product quality" and "Onboarding experience", so the column follows it and one name shows on every public page (same message id). The integration count sits on one line, so every row is the same height. Below `md` the stacked vendor link has a 24px line box, so the name and vendor targets each meet 24px.

### Search & discovery (Phase 3)

The Phase 3 search surface (`/search`, the listing-page filters, and the header autocomplete) adds one page shell and several small headless-behavior components. Search itself runs **browser-side against Algolia** with the search-only key (Spec §7.5) — the admin key is never shipped (see the CSP / key handling note in §6). Every component renders correctly via tokens and i18n-wraps (or is passed) every visible string. Components live in `apps/web/src/app/search/` (search experience) and `apps/web/src/app/shared/facets/` (listing-page filters).

- **Search page shell** (`<app-search-page>`, `search/search-page.ts`) — the `/search` experience: a `role="search"` query box (seeded from `?q=`), the **entity tablist**, and an empty state, all inside the shared **`BrowseLayout`** (the `/products` shell: breadcrumb, `minmax(0,1fr)`/`minmax(0,3fr)` rail and results columns, `min-w-0` on both). The tablist sits on the header's bottom rule. Since AECI-1796 the header uses the shared browse header parts (`layouts/browse-header.ts`): the `h1` 16px under the breadcrumb at the Headline role. The query box sits 24px under the `h1`, up to 42rem wide, on the field recipe (`FIELD_INPUT_CLASS`: the 3:1 field edge, 44px, 12px radius, 16px text) with a decorative 20px search glyph 16px in and 48px start padding. The tabs sit 24px under the box. Each tab is 44px tall at 14px. The selected tab is primary ink at weight 600 over the 2px Forest underline, the other secondary at 400, so colour is not the only cue (ruling 9). The count beside each label stays at 400. The Vendors table draws the shared product table's frame and header cells (`TABLE_HEADER_CELL_CLASS`), and a missing headquarters is an `aria-hidden` dash with visually hidden "No headquarters listed". The empty state's "browse by category" is the in-text link, underlined at rest. The facet rail is the shared **`<aec-facet-panel>`**, so `/search` gets the same mobile "Filters" disclosure, active-count badge and "Clear filters" action as `/products`. Results use the `/products` grid breakpoints (`sm:` 2 columns, `lg:` 3), the sort control sits in the results column's toolbar row at the 44px height of `aec-listing-toolbar`, and the mailing-list band closes the page. Results infinite-scroll like `/products`: the same `<aec-pagination-footer>` ("Showing X of Z", a scroll sentinel that loads the next page, a "Load more" button as the keyboard floor), fed by `connectInfiniteHits`, which accumulates pages and resets to page 1 on any query, filter or sort change. The grid dims only during that reset, never while loading more. While a search for a changed query, filter or sort is in flight, a "Load more" press or sentinel report is held, not sent. It runs once that search lands and its page 1 is in, so a page 2 can never replace the new page 1, and infinite scroll never stalls on a report that arrived too early (AECI-1906). Search used a Previous/Next paginator until 2026-10-26; it was removed under the Same-Behavior Rule. The footer renders a `<button>` here, not an anchor, because `/search` has no SSR'd `?page=2` (it is `noindex` and fetched browser-side). The toolbar carries the shared `<aec-listing-view-toggle>` (`?view=`, remembered through `createListingView()` as on `/products`). The table view reuses the `/products` row, `tr[aec-product-card]`, fed from the Algolia record. The Vendors tab gets `tr[aec-search-vendor-row]`, the same row rhythm with Name, Headquarters and Integrations. Filters, tab and sort ride the URL (`search-url-state.ts`), so reload, Back and a shared link keep them; a filter change keeps the scroll position, as on `/products`. A failed search shows the `/products` "Couldn't load" message when no results are on screen (`role="alert"`). "Clear filters" clears both tabs, as `/products` clears everything; it shows when either tab has a filter. Uses `surface-base` / `text-primary`; the query input follows the **Inputs / Fields** style. Unlike the other shells this page is **non-cacheable** (`private, no-store` — it is deliberately absent from `ROUTE_CACHE_PATTERNS`) and **`noindex`** (`MetaService.setSearchMeta`, §4.6 — search results aren't canonical content). **Graceful degradation:** when the public Algolia config is absent (local dev / an unprovisioned env / CI) the shell renders a `role="status"` "temporarily unavailable" notice and never constructs the controller — this is also the path the bound e2e exercises. Search/connector logic lives in `search-controller.ts` (the `instantsearch.js` + connectors → signals deviation, ADR 0014); the shell only binds the resulting signals.

- **Entity tabs** — the products / vendors / integrations switcher (`role="tablist"` with `role="tab"` / `role="tabpanel"`), implementing the APG tabs pattern: roving `tabindex` (active = 0), `aria-selected` / `aria-controls` / `aria-labelledby`, and ←/→/Home/End key handling that moves and activates. Active tab carries a 2px `accent-primary` bottom border (the **Navigation** active-route treatment); inactive tabs are `text-secondary` with a transparent border. Per-tab hit counts render as `tabular-nums` `text-secondary`. Switching tabs is pure show/hide of already-materialized signal slices — it never re-queries.

- **Search hit cards** (`<aec-search-product-card>` / `<aec-search-vendor-card>` / `<aec-search-integration-card>`) — grid hit tiles for the `/search` results, the **canonical instantiation of the Cards primitive** (the Cards entry above: `rounded-card` at 24px, `--shadow-card`, `spacing.6` padding. The old 0.5px border, 1px `border-strong` hover and "no shadow" are rewritten by clashes 11 and 12). Distinct from the **Entity cards (index rows)** above: those are `<tr>` table rows for the index/browse pages; these are tiles bound to the **denormalized Algolia record** (Spec §7.1), so there is no follow-up fetch. **The product and vendor tiles are the `/products` card-grid tile** (`product-card-grid.ts`), field for field: the card's title is the one link, stretched over the card; `aec-logo-or-initial` (the record's `logo_url`) and the `aec-integration-stat` badge share the top row; the name (the card title role, `--t-20`, truncated) and vendor sit beneath, with "Independent" when there is no vendor; products end with the inline rating. The vendor tile puts headquarters where the vendor line goes. Neither shows a description, taxonomy chips, product count or founded year, because the `/products` tile does not. The product tile carries the tile's connector/hybrid `RoleBadge` row too, from the record's `product_role` (AECI-1086); a record indexed before that field existed shows no chip. The integration tile takes its mechanism chip from the shared `mechanism-labels`. Its direction follows the record (AECI-1939), which is oriented so the source sends on a one-way record: the headline draws `→` from `directionGlyph()` on a one-way record, `⇄` on a bidirectional one and a neutral `·` when the direction is unknown, and the meta line names both products with `pairFlowSentence()` ("Revit sends to Navisworks"), with no line when unknown. The tile is not surfaced while the Integrations tab is hidden (`STAGE_1_SPEC.md` §7.5).

- **In-page facet widgets** (`search/widgets/`) — four generic, **i18n-free chrome** widgets bound to InstantSearch connector render-state slices; the page passes each one its localized heading and the localized labels for any boolean/"all" values (facet _values_ are data and render verbatim). Each names its group with `<fieldset>` + `<legend>` and wraps every control in its `<label>`, and renders nothing when its slice is empty (keeps the rail clean for an empty index):
  - **Rows (AECI-1793, ruling 9 of 2026-11-16).** Every filter row is a 36px `<label>` with a 20px checkbox or radio (44px until 2026-11-23, which spaced the list too loosely; 36px still clears the 24px target floor), the term on one line and the count on the end edge at 13px. The legend is the overline role. "Show more (N)" and "Clear filters" are the standalone link role with no padding and a 44px target. One set of class constants, `shared/facets/filter-row.ts`, draws them on `/products` and `/search`.
  - **`<aec-search-refinement-list>`** — checkbox group (multi-select) over string facets (`accent-(--accent-primary)` checkboxes). Shows the first 10 values plus any checked value further down (on `/search` a checked value Algolia left out of its answer is added back at 0, AECI-1899), then a "Show more (N)" text button (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`) that reveals the rest in place and becomes "Show fewer" (2026-10-26). The `/products` sidebar renders through it too, so both pages collapse alike. `/search` asks Algolia for up to 100 values per facet, its default cap; it used to ask for 12 and silently dropped the rest.
  - **`<aec-search-numeric-menu>`** — radio group over count buckets (`0 / 1–10 / 11–50 / 51+`, §7.2); radios share a page-supplied tab-unique `name` so two same-attribute menus never collapse into one group.
  - **`<aec-search-range-input>`** — a min/max number-input pair (vendor `founded_year`); index-wide bounds become placeholders + native `min`/`max`; each field has an sr-only `<label>`. Since AECI-1796 the legend is the overline role, as on every filter group, and each bound is the shared field (`FIELD_INPUT_CLASS`): the 3:1 field edge, 44px, 12px radius, 16px text. The two fields share the rail width around the dash.

- **Listing-page facet sidebar** (`<aec-facet-sidebar>`, `shared/facets/facet-sidebar.ts`, AECI-143) — the filter rail for `/products` and the taxonomy browse pages (fills the `BrowseLayout` `filters` slot). Deliberately **NOT Algolia-backed**: it reads scoped counts from `GET /api/products/facets` via `httpResource` (captured in the SSR transfer cache), so the host pages **stay edge-cacheable** — their cache key includes `category_id` / `audience_id` / `phase_id` via `LISTING_CACHE_KEY_PARAMS`. **Single-select per dimension** (the API takes one `{kind}_id` per dimension): clicking the active term clears it, another replaces it, `page` resets to 1. Browse pages pass `lockedKind` + `lockedId` to scope to (and hide) their own taxonomy. Renders its term lists through the shared `<aec-search-refinement-list>` widget, plus a "Clear filters" link when any filter is active. The mobile disclosure around the groups (the `< md` "Filters" trigger with its count badge, collapsed by default) is the shared **`<aec-facet-panel>`** (`shared/facets/facet-panel.ts`), which `/search` uses too, so the two rails cannot drift. The trigger is the secondary button, 44px, and reads the active count as visually hidden text (", 2 active"), because the badge is `aria-hidden`. **Order caption (rulings 5 and 6 of 2026-11-16, AECI-1793).** The panel ends with one 13px line that states how the terms are ordered, passed as `orderNote`. `/products` and the term pages say "Phases in project order. Other terms A to Z." It is true because categories and trades are ordered by `display_order`, which runs A to Z. The line names only groups on screen (AECI-1794). With no Phases group shown, it says "Terms A to Z." That is every phase term page, which locks and hides its own group, and any filter state that leaves no phase term. Before the counts load, the line follows the lock alone. The server renders with the counts and the first client render runs before they arrive, so on a filtered URL that leaves no phase term the line reads the long form for about 20ms after hydration, then the short form again. It is not a live region and does not move the layout (measured on AECI-1794). `apps/api/src/lib/taxonomy-a-to-z.spec.ts` holds that. `/search` passes its own line, ruled 2026-11-16 (AECI-1796). It names only the groups on screen that it is true for: "Categories, Audiences, Trades, and Vendor: most matching products first. Phases in project order." The group names are the panel's labels, joined for the locale, and a group with no terms drops out. A checked term always stays on screen, at a count of 0 when the response leaves its facet out, so it can be unchecked (AECI-1899). A count group whose only rows are such checked terms has no count order, so the line does not name it. Those four lists take InstantSearch's default order (checked terms first, then count, then name), except that the name tie-break is `compareText` (`compareFacetValues` in `search-facet-order.ts`), so case never decides a tie. The connector default compared code units and ran "SRS Distribution" before "Spotable". Phases are re-sorted into project order. The count menus and Founded are not named, so the Vendors tab, which has only those, shows no line. `search-facet-caption.component.spec.ts` runs the real connector with shuffled counts and fails if a named group leaves that order. The words are provisional. The browse lists keep the filter panel, the sort control and "Load more". They take no grouping, jump rail or "Show more" per group (ruling 1).

- **Header search autocomplete** (`<aec-search-autocomplete>`, `search/search-autocomplete.ts`, AECI-144) — the search-as-you-type field in the site header (the home hero and its pair finder reuse it). This is the project's **first Angular Aria combobox** adoption (ADR 0010): `ngCombobox` + `ngListbox`/`ngOption` supply the `role=combobox/listbox/option` semantics, `aria-expanded`/`aria-controls`/`aria-activedescendant`, the Arrow/Home/End/Escape model, and the CDK-Overlay-positioned popup. No suggestion is active until the first Down or Up (`listboxEntry`, AECI-1860), so Enter before then goes to `/search?q=`. We supply only token CSS (`group-data-[navigated=true]:data-[active=true]:` highlight on `surface-sunken`, the popup on the shared `.aec-popover` surface since AECI-1796: white, 12px radius, a hairline and the menu shadow). Rows are at least 44px, the name 14px at 600 and the second line 13px secondary. A row carries no logo. A real `<label for>` names the input (never placeholder-as-label). The default placeholder is **"Search directory"** (`@@app.search.autocomplete.placeholder`), one i18n source for the header pill and the mobile menu. The home hero passes its own (`@@home.hero.search.placeholder`). It says "directory" because the suggestions are products and vendors, not integrations (AECI-985). The `/search` box keeps its own placeholder, "Search products and vendors", which names its two tabs. It **SSR-renders only the static `<form>`/`<label>`/`<input>`** (visitor-state-neutral, so it is safe inside the cached header) and is hydration-enhanced; when the Algolia config is absent it stays a plain submit-to-`/search` field with no error UI (correct for a header control). The listbox is rendered **only when there is ≥1 hit**, so a zero-hit query shows no empty floating panel (keeps it `role=option`-only), and the combobox collapses whenever it has no row, so `aria-expanded` never claims a listbox that does not exist (AECI-1308); Enter with no selection routes to `/search?q=`, which owns the "no results" empty state. A third mount, the claim dialog's Find it step (`shared/claim-dialog/`, AECI-1293), uses optional hooks the header does not: a visible `label` (`labelVisible`) and a taller `inputHeightClass`. A visible label defaults to the Label role (`FIELD_LABEL_CLASS`, AECI-2003). A caller on a dark ground passes its own `labelClass`. The header pill (AECI-1772) passes its own set: `inputHeightClass` `h-11`, `fieldClass`, `focusRingClass`, `pill`, `shortcutHint` and `keyShortcuts`. The `searchSettled` output reports each settled result set and each clear. It was written for Find your product on `/vendor-program` (AECI-1287), removed 2026-10-12, and nothing listens to it today. The home pair finder (AECI-1555) uses three more optional inputs, all defaulting to the header's behavior: `kinds` filters suggestions (`['product']`), `ownForm = false` drops the wrapping `<form>` so a parent form holds two fields without nesting, and `fillOnSelect` writes the chosen title into the field. The owned `<form role="search">` is named by `searchName`, default **"Site search"** (`@@app.search.autocomplete.landmark`), which the two header mounts (the pill and the mobile menu) keep. Any mount that can share a page with the header passes its own, so a landmarks list never shows two identical "search" entries: the hero passes "Search the directory" and the claim dialog "Find your listing" (AECI-830).

- **Per-tab sort dropdown** (`<aec-listing-sort-select>`, `shared/listing-toolbar/listing-sort-select.ts`, AECI-175 / ADR 0014; shared with the listing toolbar since AECI-1486) — the sort control above the `/search` results, the same one `/products` and the taxonomy pages render (see "Listing toolbar" below). Products offer the five `/products` sorts with the same default, Newest; Vendors offer Name A–Z. There is no Relevance option (ruled 2026-10-26). A **non-editable Angular Aria combobox + listbox-in-overlay**, the same discrete-choice pattern as the review-form role picker (ADR 0010): `ngCombobox` + `[(expanded)]` trigger, with a `cdkConnectedOverlay` (`usePopover:'inline'`) supplying the floating layer since `ComboboxPopup` renders in-flow. A real label names the trigger (`aria-labelledby`), the active sort shows in the trigger with a ✓ on the chosen option, and labels are `$localize`d. On `/search` it is backed by Algolia **replica indexes** (`SEARCH_RANKING.md` §5a); the active sort mirrors to `?sort=`. There it mounts browser-side once results load (it reads the controller's sort options), so the degraded/SSR shell shows no sort control.

### Extensions: "Built within" (AECI-710)

Two components in `apps/web/src/app/products/product-extensions.ts` render the `product_extensions` relation on product detail (`STAGE_1_5_SPEC.md` §13.3b). Anchor site: **Faire**, the catalog's anchor. Its "Shop more from {brand}" parent strip is the pattern for the sidebar card, and its brand tile row for the body grid.

- **Resources** (AECI-1309, `product-detail.ts`): a "Resources" overline in the metadata sidebar, after the taxonomy groups and before Actions, with up to two inline Forest text links, "Integrations page" and "API documentation". Each opens a new tab with `aec-new-tab-icon` and `rel="noopener nofollow"`, like "Visit website". The group renders only when the product has at least one of the two URLs, on any plan. _(2026-11-14: this describes the shipped page. The redesigned product page has no metadata sidebar (§4, clash 16), and AECI-1778 decides where Resources moves. Nothing here is changed.)_
- **`ProductBuiltWithin`** (`section[aec-product-built-within]`): a "Built within" overline and one link card per host, in the metadata sidebar directly under Vendor. Same card shape and surfaces as the vendor card, because it is the same kind of fact. _(2026-11-14: this describes the shipped page. The redesigned product page has no metadata sidebar (§4, clash 16), and AECI-1778 decides where Built within moves. Nothing here is changed.)_
- **`ProductExtensionsSection`** (`section[aec-product-extensions-section]`, `#extensions`): the `aria-hidden` overline label "Extensions" (AECI-1720 restore), an `h2` "Extensions built within {product}" at `--t-36` on an inner `span`, one sentence in the reader's terms 16px under it, then a grid of link tiles. Each tile follows mock-up 6 `.xt`: the 32px logo (the tile-mark size of the logo rule, not the mock-up's 56px), the name at `--t-20` serif, "by {vendor}" at `--t-13`, the shared card's rating line (shown only at five reviews or more, so the mock-up's "Not yet rated · 2 reviews" is not shown), a dense "Runs inside {product}" chip with the puzzle glyph, and a 32px round arrow on `--surface-sunken` at the end. Each tile is one white `aecCard` that is itself the link, with the card shadow, 24px padding and the card lift on hover (ruling 35, 2026-11-16, AECI-1784). The grid is the shared card grid (`.aec-card-grid`) with a set `data-columns`: one tile takes the full row, two share it equally from `md`, three or more take the three-column grid. Every row is as tall as the tallest tile, so no tile is larger than its siblings. The tiles keep the API's order, A to Z by name then id.
- **The extension's empty Integrations state** (`ProductIntegrationsSection`, `hosts` input) opens with one plain sentence, "{product} runs inside {host}; see Built within.", above the usual empty copy. Unlinked, and only when the section has no rows.
- **Never integration vocabulary.** No table, no lane, no `IntegrationGroupCard`, no direction, **no chevron** and **no count** in the heading. The missing chevron is what keeps a tile from reading as an integration row, so do not add one. One verb, _built within_, names the relation in both directions.

### Product card grid (AECI-190)

`ProductCardGrid` (`aec-product-card-grid`, `apps/web/src/app/products/`) is the **default view** of every product listing — `/products` and, since AECI-657, the four taxonomy browse pages — a buyer-facing catalog grid (anchor site: Faire). The table view above stays available on both via the toolbar toggle (`?view=table`). This is the card-grid variant the note above anticipated.

**Listing toolbar** (`<aec-listing-toolbar>`, `apps/web/src/app/shared/listing-toolbar/`) — the control strip above every product listing: the sort control (`<aec-listing-sort-select>`) on the start edge, a cards/table segmented toggle on the end edge. Extracted from the `/products` template by AECI-657, which is when the taxonomy browse pages got one; before that they had no sort control at all, against `STAGE_1_SPEC.md` §4.5. Presentation-only — the host owns `?sort=` / `?view=` in the URL (both fork the edge cache key), and the shared `productSortOptions()` supplies the option set to both surfaces so they cannot drift. The toggle buttons carry `aria-pressed`; the "Sort" label names the combobox trigger through `aria-labelledby`. Both halves are their own components, `<aec-listing-sort-select>` (`listing-sort-select.ts`) and `<aec-listing-view-toggle>` (`listing-view-toggle.ts`), so `/search` renders the identical two controls in its own toolbar row. The sort control SSRs here, so its ids come from a root-scoped counter (`ListingSortIds`) that restarts for every render, never a module-level counter, which would keep climbing across requests in a Worker isolate and break the label association after hydration. Two details that look cosmetic but are load-bearing: the component sets `host: { class: 'block' }`, because a custom element is `display: inline` by default and the hosts' `space-y-6` on the grid slot silently collapses against an inline box (the first card sat flush against the toolbar until it was fixed); and both controls measure 44px on the 3:1 field edge (AECI-1793). The sort trigger is the field recipe, at least 176px wide. The view toggle group is `.aec-field-control` with a 3px inset around 36px buttons (1 + 3 + 36 + 3 + 1). Change one side and change the other. **The pressed view** is solid Forest, and its glyph becomes a tick, so the state is not colour alone (clash 5). In forced-colours mode it takes `Highlight` (`.aec-view-toggle-btn` in `styles.css`). The product page's integrations section uses the same toggle.

Until 2026-10-26 this was a **native `<select>`**, kept so sorting worked with no JS, while `/search` used the Aria combobox. Chris ruled for one control on every listing (AECI-1486), so the toolbar now renders the `/search` combobox. The cost is accepted: with no JS the trigger does not open, though a `?sort=` in the URL still renders that order.

- **Layout:** responsive `grid` — 1 / 2 / 3 columns by `aec-card-grid` (one column, two from `md`, three from `lg`, 24px gap), which caps at three (clash 18). The lead spans two columns from `md` (`md:col-span-2`). It must not span at `sm`, because the grid is one column below `md` and a span there makes an uneven extra column. The grid is deliberately _broken_ (per the anti-reference against identical SaaS grids): the lead product gets a wide featured card spanning two columns on a warm **Bone** (`accent-warm`) band. The host enables the lead only on page 1 at the newest sort, so its "Recently added" eyebrow stays truthful; otherwise the grid is uniform. **The lead card stays** (ruling 2 of 2026-11-16, mock-up 10). AECI-1793 set it to 32px padding, the name at 24px, the eyebrow at 13px and the count on one line. Below five reviews no card carries a rating line (ruling 4).
- **Column cap (AECI-1785).** `columns` sets the column count and makes every row the same height: `1` keeps one column at every width, `2` keeps two at `lg`, `3` keeps the shared steps. It sets `data-columns` on the list, and the rules sit beside `.aec-card-grid` in `styles.css`, because a column utility loses to that unlayered rule. Only the product page's similar products sets it. `/products` and the term pages leave it unset and are unchanged.
- **Tile:** one card whose title is the link to `/products/:slug` (the stretched title link, §5 "Cards"), so the role renders as a **non-link** chip (`RoleBadge`, never an `<a>`-based badge — nested anchors are invalid + an axe failure). The tile carries no category: a product can sit in several, and none is its "main" one. Monogram (`LogoOrInitial`), name (Source Serif), vendor, the overall rating as a `RatingSummary` (`variant="inline"` — the line is **omitted entirely** below the §5.5 gate, so the card reflows with no orphaned label; `RatingSummary` is non-link, safe inside the whole-card anchor), and the integration count as an `IntegrationStat` badge. The tile takes the Cards entry above: 24px radius and `--shadow-card`. Hover lifts per clash 37. The old "borders not shadows" and border-raising hover are rewritten by clashes 11 and 37. **Tile height (checked by AECI-1720 restore C).** A tile with a mark, a name and a vendor is 145px at 1440px, mock-up 10's 147px. A row is as tall as its tallest tile, as in the mock-up (`.grid > li { display: flex }`), so a tile beside one with a "Connector" chip or a rating line is about 192px with blank space under its vendor. The mock-up's sample page has no such neighbour in view, which is the whole 40px. Nothing is changed: the chip and the rating line stay on their own lines, as mock-up 10 draws them, and the long-name truncation is untouched.

### Integration stat

`IntegrationStat` (`aec-integration-stat`) renders a product's `integration_count` as a deliberate metric — the directory's whole thesis is "which tools connect to what" — in two weights: `inline` (the number and its noun on one line, so table rows keep one height; table cells and the wide lead card) and `badge` (a quiet chip with a "link" icon on the card grid). AECI-1793 retired `headline`, the large Forest figure on the lead card, for the one-line `inline` (mock-up 10). It pluralizes the noun (1 → "integration"). The glyph is `aria-hidden` — the number + noun carry the meaning. Clash 25 rewrites the zero rule for the large figure.

- **Inline and badge at zero** render "Not yet connected" in `text-secondary` at 13px, never a bare `0`. On the badge it takes the chip's 32px line, so a card's top row keeps its height. Neither the ruling nor the mock-ups change them, and `product-card.component.spec.ts` pins the card. Whether they follow the large figure is open (AECI-1767). A `connector` card, where the caller passes the role and kind (the product grid and the search card), reads "No integrations delivered yet" on a Sync Service and "No integrations powered yet" otherwise (`connectorZeroStatLabel`, QA pass on AECI-2035): a connector's job is to connect others, so "Not yet connected" was false of it.
- **The large figure may read 0** (clash 25, NEW). Chris: "I like the new design". The figure is Source Serif 600 in Forest at `--t-fig` (§3 Figure). A "0" is allowed only with honest supporting text in the same block. The text says what the number counts, what zero means and how to correct it (a "Know of one?" link). `PLAN.md` §5.2, "Copy fixed by legal review for this state", fixes the words. The heading is "We have no integrations for {product} on record". The caveat is "Not on record means we have not found one. It is not proof that none exists." Use them exactly. Mock-up 8 draws the shape at lines 1405 to 1410, but its short form, "integrations on record" and "Not found yet. Not proof that none exists.", is unreviewed copy and is not approved. A zero with no supporting text is never allowed.
- **Say it once.** The numeral is `aria-hidden`. `aec-figure` (AECI-1767) owns the zero state: `zero="integrations-not-on-record"` plus the product name renders the legal heading and caveat from `$localize` strings (`@@shared.figure.zero.integrations.heading` and `.caveat`), so no caller passes its own words. It adds no visually hidden "0 " (mock-up 8's "0 integrations on record" form is the unreviewed short copy). A zero with no zero mode logs an error in dev mode and renders the caller's label and text in production, never nothing.
- **One large figure per band** (clash 8). The zero counts as that figure.
- **The glance band** in the vendor portal is not a public page. R2 keeps its sentence-at-zero rule (see "Vendor portal (Stage 2)").
- **Public demand counts** (clash 43, BLEND). "Know of one?" ships first, because it is a correction prompt with no number. No public count of look-ups or demand ships until its rule is written. **A look-up count or an "I need this too" count does not ship before the Privacy Policy text is live** (AECI-1825, ruling R7 on AECI-1738). Settled: the source is pair page views over 90 days with automated visits removed (`PLAN.md` §6.1). The wording is "Looked up {n} times in the last 90 days. Visits we identified as automated are not counted." The count is hidden below 10, and "I need this too" shows its count only above the same floor of 10 (R7, 2026-11-14, Chris "A"). The repeat-click guard is server-side and keyed on a hashed IP and day. Still unwritten: the rule for the count itself, and the code issue that owns it.

### Role chip

One non-link chip for the card grid. It is a public chip (see Badges: "The public chip"), so it is a pill and shares the Tags / Taxonomy-chip surface (bordered, `surface-raised`, `text-secondary`). _(Clash 22 rewrites the AECI-841 metrics: `px-2.5 py-1` and 29px are replaced by the public chip recipe.)_

- **RoleBadge** (`aec-role-badge`) — the product's `product_role`, shown **only** for `connector` / `hybrid`; the default `application` renders nothing, so the chip earns attention by appearing selectively. The grid gates the whole chip row on the role, so an application's tile carries no empty row. The `/search` product card (`<aec-search-product-card>`) uses the same chip and the same gated row, from the Algolia record's `product_role` (AECI-1086).

### Home (Phase 4)

The home page (`/`, `apps/web/src/app/home/`) assembles the §4.1 surface: four bands in `home.ts`, whose doc comment holds the order (epic AECI-1554). Since AECI-1792 it is built on mock-up 9 (`docs/design/mockups/redesign-2026-11/mockups/9-home.html`), and Chris approved the six recommendations in `records/9-home-notes.md`. The mock-ups are the anchor (clash 44). The Faire anchor (`docs/design/home-direction.md`) retired for the home. Grounds: the hero is the page's one Bone band (`aec-bone-band`, clash 2). The other three bands are white, split by a hairline `border-t`, with 96px of padding above and below, 64px below `md`. Every band sits on the 1328px wrap (`BAND_WRAP_CLASS`). Cards are `aecCard`, except the one Forest feature card. Every string is i18n-wrapped. Stats data is SSR-resolved from `GET /api/stats/home` (`home-stats.resolver.ts`); browse counts come from the **live** `GET /api/taxonomy` (`home-browse.resolver.ts`), never the stats_cache.

- **Home hero** (`<aec-home-hero>`, `home/home-hero.ts`, AECI-1555, AECI-1792) — two columns at `lg` (the text, then a 416px column), one below, inside the Bone band. Left: the overline, the page's only `<h1>` at **Display** (`--t-display`, 40 to 72px, weight 400, tracking -0.02em, on an inner `span`, mock-up 9 question 2), the lede at `--t-20`, and the reused `<aec-search-autocomplete>` on `.aec-field-control` with a leading search glyph. Then the **"Most viewed" row** (mock-up 9 question 3): up to five product links from `popular_products`, the most-viewed products over 30 days among views the traffic classifier judged a person or could not decide about, recomputed nightly (AECI-1572, AECI-1586), labelled "Most viewed, last 30 days:". When that list is empty the row shows the five largest categories, labelled "Largest categories:". The fallback is the head of the browse block's category list, so both lists come from resolver data and ship in the SSR HTML. The row's height is reserved, 48px from `sm` and 72px below, so the page does not move after load (AECI-1862: CLS 0.27 to 0.01 at 1440px). Right: the **pair finder**, a white `aecCard` ("Do these two connect?") with two product-only pickers on `.aec-field-control` and a Forest submit. It routes to the product-pair page, a product page, or `/search`. Without JS it is a real `GET /search` form.
- **Two search fields from `lg`** (mock-up 9 question 7). The header search pill and the hero field both show on the home. The header behaves the same on every page (the Same-Behavior Rule), and the hero field is the page's own tool.
- **Credibility row** (`<aec-home-credibility-strip>`, `home/home-credibility-strip.ts`) — the Bone band's last row, under a `--border-strong` hairline (`.aec-rule-strong`). Coverage counts in the body face at 600 and 16px in Forest (the Serif-Floor Rule), each suppressed at 0, a tabular-nums "+{n} in the last 30 days" note after the integrations count (`integrations_added_30d`, shown only above 0), and "Independent. Rankings are algorithmic." with a balance-scale glyph (never a check or shield).
- **Browse block** (`<aec-home-browse-block>`, `home/home-browse-block.ts`) — one `<h2>` (`--t-36`, on an inner span) over four **browse grid** rows (`<app-browse-grid>`, `home/browse-grid.ts`): category, trade (published trades only, linking to `/trades/:slug`), project phase, audience (a curated role list). Each row has a 240px label column: the `<h3>`, "View all", and a caption that states the row's order (mock-up 9 question 5): "Most products first", "In project order" or "Roles chosen by AEC Integrations". The wording follows the Browse menu's footer line. Each row opens with a one-sentence lede. One chip style: the `TaxonomyBadge` count-chip, a pill, count shown only above 0.
- **Argument band** (`<aec-home-why>`, `home/home-why.ts`, AECI-1558, AECI-1792) — heading left and prose right from `lg` (5:7), then three cited figures in a hairline row at `--t-24`, Source Serif 600, Forest (mock-up 9 question 4). They read as sub-headings, so the band keeps clash 8's one large figure at most. Each citation is printed under its figure, ending in a uniquely named "Source" link (AECI-830). Then "What we do instead", **the page's one Forest feature card** (mock-up 9 question 1): `.aec-card` edge, `rounded-card`, `shadow-card`, Forest fill, Bone ink, 32px padding and 48px from `lg`. Its three points sit in columns split by `.aec-on-brand-rule` hairlines, each with a 44px tile on `--surface-on-brand-wash`, and the Bone `aecButton variant="on-brand"` "How we rank" sits top right from `md` and last below it. No reviews claim (AECI-1557).
- **Closing CTA** (`<aec-home-closing-cta>`, `home/home-closing-cta.ts`, AECI-1564, AECI-1792) — one white `aecCard` on a white band, two panes at 2:1 from `md`: the vendor call to action (Forest link to `/vendor-program`) and "Suggest a tool", which `@defer`-loads `home-feedback-dialog.ts` + `home-feedback-form.ts`.
- **Trust pillars** (`home/home-trust-pillars.ts`) — not on the home. Mounted on `/about` only. Since AECI-1800 it is a bare `<section>` on a white band: the 5:7 head row of `home-why.ts` (overline and a Section-role `<h2>` left, the lede right), then the three commitments as the three points of the page's one Forest feature card (Chris, ruling 2026-11-17), then the closing line on white. The card reuses the classes of the home's "What we do instead" card: `aec-card`, Forest fill, Bone ink, `shadow-card`, `rounded-card`, 32px padding (48px from `lg`), and columns from `md` split by `aec-on-brand-rule` hairlines. Each point is an `<h3>` in Bone over its body in `--text-on-brand-secondary`. The pillars have no link, so the card has no button and no focus stop. The Clay top stripe and the hover lift are gone.

### Unclaimed note

**Unclaimed note** (`<aec-unclaimed-note>`, `shared/unclaimed-note/unclaimed-note.ts`, AECI-1618, ADR 0051): the sentence "Unclaimed · not affiliated with or endorsed by this vendor", word for word as counsel gave it. It is plain muted text in `text-secondary`, 13px. It is never a chip, a badge or a header, and no public label says Claimed.

- **Placement.** An unclaimed vendor, product or pair page keeps its subject's logo, smaller. The 64px header logo gives way to a 32px logo under the `h1`, with the note directly beside it, so a screen reader hears the two together. On the pair page the two subjects are centred, and below `sm` the note sits under the logo instead of beside it, because beside it the sentence would wrap to a sliver. It shows even when the listing has no logo.
- **Elsewhere.** Lists, cards, rows and search show the initial tile for an unclaimed listing, never the logo and never the note. The API returns `logo_url: null` there. `aec-logo-or-initial` decides from its required `claimStatus` (AECI-1769, AECI-1851, §5 "Logos and initial tiles"). Search records follow the same rule: the index builders null `logo_url` for an unclaimed listing, and the daily `algolia_unclaimed_logo` check reports a record that still carries one (AECI-1867).
- **Cached pages.** `claim_status` is listing state, not visitor state, so the note is safe on cached SSR routes.
- **No tag in the sticky bar (AECI-2012).** Ruling 41 tried a one-word "Unclaimed" chip after the name in the product page's stuck section bar (AECI-1931). Chris took it out on 2026-11-23: "let's remove it from the sticky bar for now, but still show." The bar shows our wordmark and the name only, on every listing. An unclaimed product still shows this note in its hero, under the name. Counsel called the tag optional (Q61), so its removal needed no counsel question.
- **On the Forest hero (AECI-1778).** `tone="on-brand"` sets `--text-on-brand-secondary`, 7.96:1 on Forest and 4.96:1 at the glow's peak. The words never change. On the product hero the 104px mark is the initial tile, and the 32px sourced logo with the note sits under the vendor line.

### Company facts

**Company facts panel** (`<aec-company-facts>`, `vendors/company-facts.ts`, AECI-1310): the Company facts cell of the vendor page's facts card (AECI-1797), an overline over a definition list. Each row is the label and the value side by side, `--t-14`, the labels in one subgrid column so every value starts on the same line (2026-11-23). The source notes sit behind a 44px "i" beside the overline ("Sources for these company facts"). The "i" opens the browser's own popover (`popovertarget`), not Spartan's, so the notes are in the server HTML and open with JavaScript off ("Open by default"). The panel is a `role="dialog"` named "Sources". Click, Enter or Space opens it. Escape or a click outside closes it. Tab moves from the "i" into it, and closing returns focus to the "i". With CSS anchor positioning it hangs under the "i" and stays inside the screen edges. Its height is capped at the room left below the "i" (`position-area`), so it scrolls inside itself on a short screen, and it flips above once less than 96px of room is left. The browser's own popover height (`fit-content`) does not shrink to that room, which is why the panel sets `height: auto`. Without anchor positioning (Firefox, 2026-11) it opens centred on the screen, at most 80% of the screen tall. It lists each shown fact with its note in `text-xs text-secondary`: "Set by AEC Integrations on {date}" with a citation-role "Source" link, or "Set by the vendor". An under-review row keeps its marker in the list and is left out of the pop-out. With no sourced row there is no "i". A fact with no value is not a row, and with no facts the panel renders nothing. It replaces the hero's HQ and Founded chips. The product page's vendor card carries one summary line in `text-xs`.

**Company facts note** (`<aec-company-facts-note>`, `shared/company-facts-note/company-facts-note.ts`, AECI-1310): the Open Government Licence attribution, one caption line in `text-secondary`. It is a blanket note under the vendor page's facts card and at the foot of `/search`, on both tabs. Its licence name is the link and opens in a new tab.

### Auth & Reviews (Phase 5)

Phase 5 adds the authenticated surfaces — sign-in, review submission + display, account, and the admin moderation queue (`apps/web/src/app/{auth,reviews,account,admin}/`, plus the reviews section on the product page). They were built through the v0 → Angular workflow and reuse the established catalog token + type vocabulary so the signed-in experience reads as the **same publication** as the public directory (the Anchor-Site Rule).
