Every component is token-only (zero hardcoded color), i18n-wraps every visible string, and ships **light-only** (AECI-226 — no `dark:` variants). New form controls follow **ADR 0009** (Signal Forms) and the **Angular Aria** ADR (0010, Accepted — overlay primitives stay Spartan); the cacheable product page stays visitor-state-neutral (see the cache-neutrality constraint in CLAUDE.md).

- **Login** (`<aec-login-page>`, `auth/login.ts`) — the `/auth/login` page: a magic-link email field (a real `<label for>`, never placeholder-as-label) and a Google OAuth button, driven by Signal Forms with a validated `return` path. Non-cacheable, `noindex`. Degrades gracefully when Supabase is unconfigured (the field still renders; no console error). A centred header carries the favicon italic-i mark (decorative, `aria-hidden`) over "Sign in to AEC Integrations". Since AECI-1805 every state sits inside one shared `aecCard` in a `max-w-md` focused-flow column (§3 "The Page-Width Rule"). A "Last used" pill (after Resend's sign-in page) sits on the top-right edge of the method this browser started last. The method lives in `localStorage` (`auth/last-sign-in-method.ts`), is read in `afterNextRender` so the SSR HTML stays visitor-neutral, and reaches screen readers through the button's `aria-describedby`.
- **Terms tick box** (`<aec-terms-accept-page>`, `auth/terms-accept.ts`) — the `/auth/terms` page a reader sees once, at the first sign-in after a Terms version that reduces their rights. It reuses the Login header: the centred italic-i mark, a heading, a one-line lede and the same `max-w-md` column and shared card (a form card below `sm`), with no small label above. After hydration it adds "What changes on <date>" with the published summary bullets and links to the new version and the one that applies now. One unticked box and "Accept and continue" follow, then a "Not now" link that goes on and posts nothing. The analytics consent banner is held off this page so there is one "Accept" (`bannerHeldOn`). Anchor reference: Kraken "Accept terms" on Mobbin (https://mobbin.com/screens/beb75876-149a-4300-9ede-39d7b6e63562). Mobbin has no Resend terms screen, so the layout follows `/auth/login`.

- **Review form** (`<aec-review-form>`, `reviews/review-form.ts`) — the `/products/:slug/review` submission form and the project's **first Angular Aria form** (satisfies AECI-133). The two 1–5 **star-rating controls** (overall + onboarding) are horizontal Aria **listboxes** (`role=listbox`/`option`, roving tabindex, `aria-selected` highlight); role-at-company is a non-editable Aria **combobox**; optional fields (years-using, would-recommend) are held as signals and merged at submit. Signal Forms own validation (`SubmitReviewSchema` from `@aeci/shared`); a confirmation replaces the form on success, errors are retryable inline. The headline (5–100) and body (50 min) length floors are stated in persistent help text under each control per the §5 → Inputs named rule, not held back until the error fires. **Layout (AECI-987):** the form + context rail (§5 → Layout shells). The rail names the product and its vendor (logo, name, vendor off the resolver's `ProductDetail`; an unclaimed product shows its initial tile there, AECI-1769), says a person checks every review before it is published, and links the reviewing guide. The sign-in notice, already-reviewed and success states keep the same canvas and rail, in columns 1 to 7. **New language (AECI-1807).** The overline and the `h1` follow `/welcome`: the overline is `.aec-overline` and the `h1` carries no size class, so the global Headline rule sets it. The form is the shared `aecCard`, its fields padded inside it (24px, 32px from `sm`) and the submit in the form footer (`FORM_FOOTER_CLASS`). The sign-in notice, the already-reviewed notice and the confirmation are each one `aecCard`. The controls take the shared field recipe, and the years field keeps its 112px width. The star options are 44px squares with the 12px control radius and the 3px ring. "Would you recommend it?" is the segmented control: a 44px framed row of 36px segments, the chosen one solid Forest with a tick. The rail's guide link is the in-text link look with a 44px target. No label, help text, error, score name or request body changed.

- **Star display** (`<aec-review-stars>`, `reviews/review-stars.ts`) — the **read-only** rating display used by the summary averages (decimals, e.g. 4.3) and per-review rows. Glyphs are decorative (`aria-hidden`); the precise value is announced via `aria-label`, so shape/color is never the sole signal.

- **Rating summary** (`<aec-rating-summary>`, `reviews/rating-summary.ts`) — the **compact, editorial** rating for product **cards and table rows** (the list-surface counterpart to the five-star `ReviewStars`, which stays on the detail page). Deliberately numeral-forward — a single gold star (`--accent-rating`, `aria-hidden`), the average (Source Serif, `tabular-nums`), and a review-count caption — so a catalog reads like a publication, not a G2/Capterra ratings wall (a PRODUCT.md anti-reference). It **owns the §5.5 visibility gate** (`RATING_VISIBILITY_MIN_REVIEWS`) so it behaves identically bound to a `ProductListItem` or a denormalized `AlgoliaProductRecord`: shown only at ≥5 reviews and a non-null average. Two variants for the two empty-state needs — `inline` (grid + search cards) renders **nothing** when gated and collapses the host to `display:none` so it leaves no flex/gap; `cell` (dense table) renders the `–` en-dash empty state (the same convention as the vendor / category cells). The value is announced once via `role="img"` + `aria-label`, never by glyph color alone. Closes the 2026-06-12 trust-audit P0 ("zero social-proof on cards"); the existing "Highest rated" / "Most reviewed" sorts now have a visible counterpart.

- **Product reviews section** (`<aec-product-reviews>`, `products/product-reviews.ts`) — the reviews block on the product detail page. It sits on white: the page's one Bone band is the About band. Enforces the **≥5 summary gate** (§5.5): `reviewCount === 0` → a "Be the first to review" empty state; `0 < count < 5` → the list + the threshold note, no averages; `count ≥ 5` → the list + the score band. **The score band (AECI-1784, rulings A3 and A4)** is two `aecCard` score cards, Product quality with the Forest stripe and Onboarding experience with the Clay deep stripe (mock-up 6's names, AECI-1720 restore C), each with its own 48px `aec-figure` and stars, never merged. A third card holds the range marker and the review count (§5 Score Display). From `md` the two score cards share a row and the marker spans it. From `xl` the three sit in one row at `1fr 1fr 1.2fr`. "Load more" paginates client-side. **Each review is an `article` named by its title, and the article is the card** (`aecCard`, 24px radius, card shadow). Its first block is the review as written: the Card-title heading, the date, two rating tiles (12px radius, `surface-sunken`, a 3px Forest or Clay deep leading stripe beside the "Product quality" or "Onboarding experience" name, the stars and the number), the body at `--t-16`, and pill chips for role, years of use and "would recommend", each with a decorative glyph. No company or firm shows (Privacy Policy). **Row sharing (ruling 34, Chris, 2026-11-16).** One or two reviews share the row equally, so no blank column is left: one review is full width at every width, and two are halves from `md`. Three or more take the grid: one column below `md`, two from `md`, three from `xl`. Every card in a row is the same width, so no review is featured, and the order stays the API's. A full-width card caps its text, not itself: the rating tiles, the review body, the update and the reply text are `max-w-prose`. **Reviewer updates** (AECI-1613) follow as a Bone nested panel. **Vendor replies** (AECI-1178, rewritten by clash 14) follow as a nested panel inside the card, under the review text. The old rule put them below the card as an indented sibling block (`ms-6`, `sm:ms-10`) on `--surface-sunken` with a full `border-default` border, to avoid a card in a card. Clash 14 lifted that ban (§4 "Cards may nest"). The panel has a small leading mark, the label "Response from {vendor}" and the date, and it takes no shadow of its own. Its fill is Forest soft, the `aecCard nested` panel (settled by AECI-1784). Reuse it for any answer that belongs to a parent item but is written by someone else. **Mock-up 6's head and list row (AECI-1720, Chris 2026-11-17: the mock-up's small label over a large heading is preferred).** The kicker "Reviews" (`.aec-overline`, a `<p>`) sits over the `<h2>`. With reviews the heading reads "Two ratings, kept apart" with the intro "Every reviewer rates the product itself and, separately, what it took to get it running. A great tool with a hard rollout shows up as exactly that." With none it stays "Reviews" and has no intro. The heading is `--t-36` from `md` and `--t-24` below. The review button stays at the right of the head on every page, so it has one place whether or not the score cards show. The mock-up's "Ratings show once a product has five reviews." and "Reviews are checked before they appear." are promises that wait for Chris and are not built. Each score figure prints a small "/5" (`aec-figure` `suffix`). Under its stars each score card ends in one line, pinned to its foot (`mt-auto`), so a card as tall as the scale card is not half empty: "The score for the product itself." (Overall) and "The score for getting set up and started." (Onboarding). These are the reviewer guide's definitions (`/docs/reviewers/writing-a-review`). The mock-up's lines ("How well it does the job once it is in place.", "How the first weeks went…") add a time scope and an adoption test the form never asks about, so they are not used. The names stay "Overall" and "Onboarding". Over the list, an `<h3>` "Newest first" (the API's order) and "Showing {n} of {N}". So a review title is an `<h4>`, and its update and reply labels `<h5>`. Years of use read "Using it 1 year" and "Using it {n} years" (two message ids; an ICU plural in the template printed its text twice on hydration). An update's new ratings print their number beside the stars. **A lone last card (AECI-1720).** From `xl`, a count that three columns would leave with one card alone on its last row and two columns fill exactly (4, 10, 16, 22) keeps two columns. A count that leaves one card alone in both (7, 13, 19) keeps three: no equal-width grid avoids it, and widening the card would feature it. The mock-up's vendor mark in the reply label is not shown: the reply payload carries no logo.

- **Review CTA** (`<aec-review-cta>`, `reviews/review-cta.ts`) — the **cache-neutral** call-to-action embedded in the (cacheable) product page. SSR / pre-hydration renders a neutral "Write a review"; **client-side hydration** (`afterNextRender`) reconciles to `anon` ("Sign in to review" → `/auth/login?return=…`) or `authed` ("Submit a review"), so the cached HTML never carries session state.

- **Account** (`<aec-account-page>`, `account/account.ts`) — the `/account` page (non-cacheable): a read-only email, an editable display name (Signal Forms `PATCH`), and a **delete-account** flow gated behind a Spartan dialog confirmation that calls the GDPR `DELETE /api/account` (anonymizes the user's reviews). Sign-out is menu-only (AECI-986): the page manages profile, review history, and deletion; session exit lives in the account menus (§Navigation → The Overflow Rule). **Layout (AECI-987):** the 7xl canvas. From `lg` a sticky section nav (Profile, About you, Your reviews, Delete account) takes columns 1 to 3 and the sections take 4 to 12, in the `ACCOUNT_ONBOARDING_SPEC.md` §7.2 order. The nav links are plain anchors with absolute hrefs (`/account#profile`), never a bare `#id` (the `<base href>` trap); each target carries `scroll-mt-8`. Inputs cap at `max-w-2xl` inside the Profile panel. "Your reviews" is a divided list across the full column, with the date and status badge at each row's end. No side-by-side forms: Profile and About you differ in height and would leave a blank half.

- **Admin shell** (`<aec-admin-shell>`, `admin/admin-shell.ts`) — the `/admin` layout + role gate. A non-admin resolver result renders the global 404 surface (the admin area is never _revealed_ to non-admins); an admin sees the header, nav, and **live queue badges** seeded from `GET /api/admin/summary` and decremented in-place by each queue via `AdminSummaryStore` (no round-trip). Since AECI-922 that is one badge per Operations queue — pending reviews, open correction requests, open vendor claims — with their **sum** on the closed Operations category trigger and on the header account menu; a zero renders no badge at all (`ADMIN_PANEL_SPEC.md` §5.0c). The Re-index queue badges its own entry only and is in neither sum.

- **Moderation queue** (`<aec-review-queue>`, `admin/reviews/review-queue.ts`) — the `/admin/reviews` child route. Lists pending reviews with product, reviewer email, queue age, body, and toxicity score; **client-side sortable** (default: toxicity high→low, nulls last — worst content first). One-click **approve**; **reject** opens a Signal-Forms reason field (required, Aria textbox). On success the row leaves, a live region announces it, and the shell badge ticks down. A toxicity score ≥ 70 gets a warning highlight — flag, never auto-block.

### Requests & Moderation (Phase 6)

Phase 6 extends the admin area with **vendor-request moderation** (`/admin/requests`) and **reviewer ban management** (`/admin/reviewers`) (`apps/web/src/app/admin/{requests,reviewers}/`), plus the repeat-offender → ban flow grafted onto the Phase 5 moderation queue. Both new pages are children of the existing `<aec-admin-shell>` (same role gate + live badge), reuse the established catalog token + type vocabulary so the moderation surfaces read as the **same publication** as the public directory (the Anchor-Site Rule), are token-only (zero hardcoded color), i18n-wrap every visible string, and ship **light-only** (AECI-226 — no `dark:` variants). Spartan stays for the one overlay primitive (the ban dialog).

- **Public request form** (`<aec-request-form-body>`, `requests/request-form-body.html`) — the shared body behind both the routed `/{products,vendors}/:slug/{claim,correction}` page and the in-place `RequestDrawer`. One Signal Forms model; the active `kind` picks the shared Zod schema (`ClaimFormSchema` / `CorrectionFormSchema`). Both free-text bodies carry a **20-character floor**, so both state it in persistent help text under the textarea along with what to actually write — the claim's "Anything we should know?" reads as an optional afterthought otherwise, and its `body` column is NOT NULL (see the §5 → Inputs named rule). In the **drawer** variant the panel is a clipped flex column with **one** scroll port: the eyebrow/title/close/subtitle block is pinned above it and the submit control is pinned below it in a bordered `--surface-raised` bar, so only the fields scroll and "Send claim"/"Send correction" is never scrolled out of reach. The bar full-bleeds past the port's horizontal padding and owns the panel's bottom padding (the port has none), which is what keeps content from showing beneath it. The routed **page** variant keeps normal flow — it has no scroll port of its own and the browser window is the scroller. **The context rail lives on the routed page only** (`requests/request-form.html`, AECI-987): the form + context rail pattern, naming the company and the product page a claim came from (the claim resolver's data), the claim or correction next steps, and a docs link. It leaves once the request is sent, because the confirmation carries its own next steps. The body owns the page header, so below `lg` the rail follows the form. The drawer has no rail. **New language (AECI-1807).** On the routed page the overline and the `h1` follow `/welcome`, the form is the shared `aecCard` with its fields padded inside it and the submit in the form footer, and the confirmation is one `aecCard` whose "What happens next" is a nested card. The drawer keeps a bare form and its pinned bar, because the panel is already the surface. Both take the shared field recipe (`FIELD_INPUT_CLASS`, `FIELD_ERROR_CLASS`). The submit now follows "Submit stays enabled", except that a claim's submit stays disabled until the Vendor Terms box is ticked (`VENDOR_SIGNUP_PROCESS.md` §4), as on the claim dialog. The rail's docs link is the in-text link look with a 44px target. No label, help text, error, terms line or request body changed.

- **Request queue** (`<aec-request-queue>`, `admin/requests/request-queue.ts`) — the `/admin/requests` child route. **Corrections only since AECI-922** (`ADMIN_PANEL_SPEC.md` §5.0c): the request pins `kind: 'correction'`, the heading reads "Correction requests", and vendor **claims** live on `/admin/claims`. Lists corrections as cards with a status badge, the linked target product/vendor, submitter, and submitted-age. **One** client-side filter (status: open / resolved / rejected), an `aria-pressed` toggle-button group. Each row surfaces the **possible-duplicate** flag computed at submit (an open sibling request for the same target) — informational, never auto-deciding; the admin resolves every request by hand. The **domain-match** chip left with the kind filter: it compared a _claimant's_ email domain to the vendor's, and a correction identifies nobody. The linked **Linear issue** opens out. **Resolve** is one-click; **reject** opens an optional reason field. On success the row leaves, the Operations nav badge ticks down, and a `role=status` live region announces it; a capped-note shows when the server holds more rows than the page loaded.

- **Reviewer bans** (`<aec-reviewer-bans>`, `admin/reviewers/reviewer-bans.ts`) — the `/admin/reviewers` child route: the list of **currently-banned** reviewers (newest ban first) and the home for **unbanning** (`PATCH /api/admin/reviewers/:id` `{action:'unban'}`; the in-flight row's button disables, a live region announces the result, an already-unbanned row degrades gracefully). The ban _action_ itself is raised from the moderation queue's repeat-offender flow (below), not here.

- **Repeat-offender prompt + ban dialog** (in `<aec-review-queue>`, `admin/reviews/review-queue.ts`) — when an admin rejects a review and that pushes the reviewer past the rejection threshold, the `PATCH /api/admin/reviews/:id` response carries a `repeat_offender` payload that raises a dismissible prompt. Confirming opens a **Spartan ban dialog** (`BrnDialog`) with a required reason; the dialog is driven **imperatively** from the event handler (`openBan()`), never from an `effect()` (a `BrnDialog.open()` inside an effect throws NG0602). Banning calls `PATCH /api/admin/reviewers/:id` `{action:'ban', reason}`. Since AECI-1949 the dialog reads the person's pending counts from `GET /api/admin/users/:id` when it opens. When there is anything pending it shows "Also reject N pending items", checked by default, and sends `reject_pending` with the ban. `<aec-ban-pending-choice>` (`admin/reviewers/ban-pending-choice.ts`) renders that box here and on `/admin/users/:id`, so the two ban forms behave alike. On `409 BAN_PENDING_CHANGED` the dialog refetches the counts, shows an inline message and stays open.

### Removal election and removals section (AECI-1618)

Two small surfaces, both built from existing parts. **Anchor reference:** Base44, "Delete your account" (Mobbin screen `6abe261c-e5ce-4cad-933f-a80daae66e7a`), for the irreversible-action dialog. Components for the surface come from the same site, per the Anchor-Site Rule.

- **The removal election** (`<aec-vendor-removal-election>`, `vendor/components/vendor-removal-election.ts`) sits on the vendor plan panel's `ended` state, owner seats only. It is a quiet block, not a banner: a small overline heading, one sentence with the closing date, and a secondary button, "Remove my text". The button opens a confirmation dialog from the click handler, never from `effect()`. The dialog lists the exact fields it will blank, says plainly that this cannot be undone, and says what stays. Its confirm button uses the primary style, and the warning is in the words, not the colour. After an election the row reads "You removed your text on {date}." A failed read says so and offers a retry, because a missing control would hide a right the vendor has.
- **The removals section** (`<aec-brand-asset-removals>`, `admin/vendors/brand-asset-removals.ts`) sits above the `/admin/vendors` list, because that list scroll-pages and anything under it is out of reach. Since AECI-2112 it is a flat console panel (`CONSOLE_PANEL_CLASS`) headed "Pending brand-asset removals" (`h3`) over the console table recipe. It has no paging, because the API returns the full set and the set is small. This is a deliberate difference from the list below it, not a lapse in the Same-Behavior Rule. Hold and Lift hold are secondary buttons that open a detail row under the company, with the Bone notice and a reason field that is recorded in the audit trail. A failed load offers a retry. `/preview/admin-brand-asset-removals` renders it over a fake API.

### Operator console (Phase 8.3)

Phase 8.3 (`docs/ADMIN_PANEL_SPEC.md`, epic AECI-572) turns the moderation area into the **operator console**: `<aec-admin-shell>`'s `h1` becomes "Admin", its nav groups into **Insights / Catalog / Operations**, and `/admin` opens on the Overview. **No new Mobbin anchor was picked, deliberately** (spec §9.10): the console inherits the Phase 5/6 admin queues' visual language and the home stats cards' card vocabulary — bordered `--surface-raised`, border not shadow, Forest figures, `tabular-nums`, Bone/Clay-deep for anything cautionary. One publication, one voice (Anchor-Site Rule). Token-only, i18n throughout, light-only.

- **Overview** (`<aec-admin-overview>`, `admin/overview/`) — the `/admin/overview` child route and the 05:00 analytics digest as a live page. A traffic-classes card leads (AECI-1346), then four `<aec-stat-tile>`s plus a catalog-totals card, a 30-day chart of requests by traffic class, ranked top-sources / top-products lists, and a six-item status strip (the sixth, copyright restores, AECI-1944). A **Recompute** button re-reads the bundle with `?recompute=1` to fill the two network-dependent status items, announcing via a polite live region.

- **Resolution honesty is a visual rule here, not just a data one.** Every tile's caption states the window it covers, and the unique-visitors definition renders _next to the number_ rather than in a tooltip. The response's caveats render through `<aec-admin-notes>` — a Note/Caveat chip plus localized prose keyed off the API's machine-readable `code`, placed where the reader meets the figures it qualifies: above them on Overview and Catalog, and as a closing "About these numbers" section on the screens that read top-down. **One component renders it everywhere** (AECI-835), so a caveat's wording cannot differ by screen. Unmeasured values read "Not measured", never `0`.

- **Charts are hand-rolled SVG** (`admin/charts/`, spec §8 / §13 D3): `<aec-sparkline>` and `<aec-stacked-bar-chart>`, geometry from pure functions so they are SSR-safe, sized by `viewBox` rather than measurement, series in Forest and Clay-deep (distinct in hue **and** lightness) by default. A chart is never the only representation of a number: the stacked bar carries a visible legend and a visually-hidden `<table>` of the full series, and a sparkline only ever accompanies a figure already rendered as text. An empty series renders nothing rather than a flat line implying a measured zero.

- **Three column marks on `<aec-stacked-bar-chart>`, each legend-and-table only when present** (AECI-877, AECI-1346). **Degraded**: dimmed and hatched, for a day measured without its inputs. **Not measured**: a dashed `--border-input` outline in place of bars, for a day with no measurement at all; never a zero-height column, and a window of nothing but outlines still draws. **Boundary**: a dashed `--text-secondary` rule in the gap before a column, for a change in how the data was counted, with one legend entry per distinct change. They compose on one column. A caller passing none draws exactly as before. A chart with more than two series passes `seriesFills`; the traffic classes use `--text-secondary`, `--border-input`, Forest and Clay-deep, each at least 3:1 on the surface and each touching pair distinct in hue or lightness (`admin/overview/traffic-classes.ts` carries the measured ratios).

- **Operator lists are tables, not cards** (AECI-694). The rule is the shape of the data, not the surface: when every field is short and every row has the same fields, the operator is comparing a column down the page and a card makes them read a paragraph per row. `/admin/vendors` and `/admin/users` were cards and are now tables; the moderation queues were cards because a review's body is long free text. The 2026-11-24 ruling superseded that: since AECI-2102 the four moderation queues (reviews, review updates, review replies, change requests) are console tables, with the long text and the decision in each row's expanded detail. Their filter rows are the console's segmented filter (`CONSOLE_FILTER`, below). The console's table markup is one pattern, first written in `admin/system/system-status.html`: `overflow-x-auto` wrapper, `min-w-[…]` on the table, a visually-hidden `<caption>` naming it, `th[scope=col]` in the head, `th[scope=row]` on each row's identity cell, `text-end tabular-nums` on counts, and the Entity-cards en-dash-with-`aria-label` for a genuinely absent value. **Table headers are sentence case**, per the Sentence-Case Rule, which explicitly strikes the overline treatment for them.
- **Claims, requests and row threads follow the same recipe** (AECI-2111). `/admin/claims` and the three sections of `/admin/requests` (corrections, product requests, early-unassign requests) are console tables with an expanded row holding the record and its decision forms. `/admin/threads` is a plain console table. The two detail pages, `/admin/claims/:id` and `/admin/threads/:id`, are labelled facts over flat panels. The claim detail keeps its sections in one column, in their old order, because the claim screens' wording and order are fixed. The claim note stays a textarea form: the inline control is single-line. Shared looks the table recipe does not name (the in-text link, the neutral flag box, the detail facts grid) live in `admin/console/console-detail.ts` since AECI-1729.
- **The reporting and catalog screens follow it too** (AECI-2125). Activity, Traffic, Audience, Subscribers, Catalog, Connectors (list and detail) and the Re-index queue sit on flat console panels and console tables. Their window, traffic-type, timezone, membership and priority controls are the segmented filter. The Audience feedback inbox is a console table, not a list of cards. Every 320px reflow keeps the page still: tables scroll in their region, and a breakdown bar's label truncates inside its cell. Charts keep their data and scales. A line or stacked chart that draws a series on palette slot 3, 4 or 5 (under 3:1, the relief rule in `styles.css`) shows its legend with a visible value beside each series, so colour never carries a value alone. Button weights follow the ruling under Buttons: openers are secondary ("Done" on the Re-index queue was a filled primary), and confirms that can be undone (the lane handover and reclaim, saving a mapping, re-index Done) are primary. No screen in this set has a danger confirm.

- **Users, audit, email, legal and DMCA follow the same recipe** (AECI-2128). `/admin/users`, `/admin/audit`, `/admin/email`, `/admin/legal` and `/admin/dmca` are console tables, flat panels and the shared field recipe, with every word, confirm step and gate as before. `/admin/users/:id` is labelled facts over flat panels. The shared audit trail opens a row's field changes with a leading ghost toggle that keeps its visible words, "Show changes" and "Hide changes" (ruled on PR #1495). It is the disclosure's chevron with the words beside it, and it is described by its row's header so each toggle can be told apart. Since AECI-1729 it is the shared `AdminDisclosureButton` in its visible-label mode (`withLabel` with the words projected, `describedBy` set to the row header), so the trail keeps no button of its own. The detail row is always rendered and hidden until opened, so `aria-controls` always resolves. The diff table inside it is built only when the row opens. The date presets on `/admin/audit`, the trail scope on `/admin/users/:id` and the email window are the segmented filter. The danger look is for a confirm that takes something away from someone, or penalises them, and cannot be fully given back. Here that is "Confirm ban", "Publish version" and "Release the hold". "Send the notice again" only re-queues the notice for companies not yet delivered and takes nothing from anyone, so it is primary. Placing a hold, pausing or resuming an email switch and saving a DMCA log entry are primary too.
- **The console table recipe and the chip tones (AECI-1813, mock-up 14's legend, ruled 2026-11-24).** The recipe is one set of class constants, `CONSOLE_TABLE` in `admin/console/console-table.ts`: a labelled scroll region (`role="region"`, `tabindex="0"`, no shadow, radius or fill), a visually hidden caption, header cells at the Label role with a hairline above and a strong rule below and never sticky, hairline rows with no zebra and the muted fill on hover and focus-within, the identity cell a weight-600 `th scope="row"`, end-aligned tabular numbers, compact 8px cells, one leading 44px disclosure per expandable row (`AdminDisclosureButton`), an expanded row and its detail row on the raised fill under a strong rule with the detail pinned to the region's start edge, the Forest-soft selected row, and an empty state that is one line with no table. A screen converts a table by binding the constants. Panels are flat and bordered at `rounded-panel` with no shadow (`CONSOLE_PANEL_CLASS`). Statuses are `aec-chip density="dense"` on one of seven tones (`ConsoleChipTone`: count, neutral, waiting, positive, quiet, attention, critical), each AA on its own fill and never colour alone. The five tones the public ladder lacks are drawn by `admin/console/chip-tones.ts`, a stylesheet only the console and its `/preview/admin-*` routes load, so the public bundle carries none of them. `ADMIN_STATUS_TONES` in `admin/console/status-tones.ts` is the one status-to-tone map. The inline control form (`AdminInlineControl`) and the danger pattern (`AdminDangerAction`: a secondary opener, an optional Bone warning box, a reason that is required unless the form says otherwise, a danger confirm and a ghost Cancel) sit beside them. `/preview/admin-console-legend` renders all of it. A list's Status, Sort by or Show filter is the segmented filter (`CONSOLE_FILTER` in `admin/console/console-filter.ts`, AECI-2102): a labelled group of native toggle buttons with `aria-pressed`, the pressed one on the Forest fill with a tick so the state is never colour alone, mapped to Highlight in forced colours.
- **The danger form is the pattern for inline destructive actions (AECI-2104).** The contract is in `docs/ADMIN_PANEL_SPEC.md` §5.0d and the usage notes at the top of `admin/console/danger-action.ts`. Eight rules for the look. **One danger button per form, and only on the press that does the damage.** In a two-step form, step one's "Continue" is secondary and the danger button appears on step two. **The reading order is fixed:** the optional warning box, optional read-only facts, the reason's label, its help, the reason, its error, an optional internal note, then the buttons. **Step two is a Bone box** (`--accent-warm` fill, `--border-strong` edge, `rounded-control`) under the reason, holding a weight-600 title, the body, the danger confirm, a secondary "Edit the reason" and the ghost Cancel. **A locked reason stays in place** on the sunken fill (`--surface-sunken`), read-only, so the operator sees exactly what will be sent. **Focus on step two lands on the box, not the button.** The box is named by its title and described by its body, so a screen reader hears the consequence first and a repeated Enter cannot fire the action. **Busy holds the form open.** The confirm reads its busy label ("Removing…") and a visually hidden polite status says it once. The opener, Cancel, Escape and "Edit the reason" do nothing and the form carries `aria-busy`, per the busy rule under Buttons. **An error is one alert element**, a string or inline content with a link, focused on failure. **The warning box is optional** only where today's form has none (the review queues' Reject). A blocked opener keeps its focus and says why through `aria-disabled` and its description. The legend shows each state open.
- **The shell (AECI-1813).** The console sits on the 1328px page wrap (`BAND_WRAP_CLASS`), 48px above and 96px below. The section row's items are 16px at 400, the current one Forest at 600 over the Forest underline. Operations opens as two labelled columns, "Queues" and "Accounts and site", on the shared popover. The row's dropdowns close after the Browse menu's 260ms grace, and opening one closes the others at once. The breadcrumb is the shared `aec-breadcrumb`. Below `lg` the account trigger and its badge are in the mobile menu, as on the public header.

- **A sortable header selects a sort on the first click and REVERSES it on the second.** `<th aec-sort-header>` (`shared/sort-header/sort-header.ts`) renders a button plus a ↑ or ↓ and sets `aria-sort` to the direction currently in effect for that key. Clicking an inactive header adopts that column's **natural** direction — one copy, in `ADMIN_VENDOR_SORT_DEFAULT_ORDER` / `ADMIN_USER_SORT_DEFAULT_ORDER` (`packages/shared`), which the API's `apps/api/src/lib/sort.ts` also resolves its ORDER BY from, so the arrow cannot disagree with the list. Clicking the active header sends `?order=asc|desc`. _(It originally did not flip, because no list endpoint took an `order` parameter; that held over a two-key API and became a defect at seven — an arrow on a clickable header reads as a toggle, and a second click that silently no-ops is the worse lie. `ADMIN_PANEL_SPEC.md` §5.7 carries the full reasoning.)_ The corollary is unchanged and matters more: **a column the API cannot order by gets no control at all**, and stays plain `<th>` text with no hover state. A header that looks clickable and reorders the 25 rows on the current page presents a page as a ranking, which is worse than no control. (`/admin/users` Last sign-in was the standing example until the API began ranking the whole filtered set before it chooses the page, `ADMIN_PANEL_SPEC.md` §5.8.)

- **Relative time is for ledgers, and it always carries the exact instant** (AECI-694). `<aec-relative-time>` (`shared/relative-time/`) renders a compact span ("4h", "2d") beside an info control **whose accessible name IS the full `medium`/UTC datetime** — not an `aria-describedby` pointing at the panel, so a screen-reader or keyboard user never depends on a transient overlay to learn when something happened. Sighted users get the panel on hover, focus or click. It is used **only** in the audit trail, where "how long ago" is the question being asked; every other timestamp in the console is absolute `medium`/UTC, because "Profile created" is a date, not a staleness signal. Two implementation notes worth keeping: the panel is a `cdkConnectedOverlay` rather than the cheap CSS tooltip (`home/home-why.ts`) because every table it appears in sits inside `overflow-x-auto`, which clips an in-flow panel; and `now` is read once at construction, matching the console's no-live-updates rule, so the stamp is as fresh as the fetch that produced the row.

- **Explanatory copy that does not deserve a paragraph is an `<aec-info-hint>`** (`shared/info-hint/`, AECI-915) — a 16px "i" button next to the thing it explains, with the text in a `cdkConnectedOverlay` on hover, focus and click. It generalises the control `<aec-relative-time>` introduced above and keeps that component's binding rule: **the accessible name of the button IS the text**, and the panel is `aria-hidden`. Not a `title` attribute (not reliably keyboard-reachable, inconsistent in screen readers, unstylable) and not an `aria-describedby` pointing at the panel (which makes assistive tech depend on a transient overlay and double-announces). Reach for it when the copy is a sentence or two of _optional_ orientation; if a reader needs it to complete the task, it is body text, not a hint. First consumers: the vendor portal's taxonomy cards, at two levels — the facet hint beside the heading and each term's description beside its row. **Never nest one inside a heading**: an accessible name is computed from descendants, so a hint inside an `<h3>` makes the whole paragraph part of that heading's name and wrecks the screen-reader heading list. Put it in a wrapper row _beside_ the heading. Nothing lints this and axe cannot see it.

- **Console detail screens carry a breadcrumb, not a back link** (AECI-777, spec §5.0b). `<aec-admin-breadcrumb>` renders once in the shell, under the category row, as `Admin › Operations › Vendors › Acme Corp`. It replaces the four hand-rolled "Back to …" links the parameterised routes each carried, which had drifted into four class strings and two wrapper shapes for one job. Three rules. **It follows the public site's breadcrumb treatment** rather than inventing a console one, per `docs/ADMIN_PANEL_SPEC.md` §9 item 10 — the same `<nav aria-label="Breadcrumb">` + `<ol>`, the same `›` separator marked `aria-hidden`, the same `aria-current="page"` on the last crumb, sentence case (the Sentence-Case Rule names breadcrumbs explicitly as _not_ overlines, so the uppercase variant on `/taxonomy` is the outlier, not the model). **Only ancestors are crumbs** — never siblings — so it is a location readout and not a second navigation surface; the category crumb is plain text because there is no route behind it, and neither is the page you are on. **It contains no heading**, because the shell owns the only `h1` and each screen the only `h2`; a heading in the trail would sit between them and break heading order, which is the same conclusion the nav's group labels reached twice already. Its consequence for the screens: with the trail carrying the way back, a detail `h2` names **which** vendor / account / claim / catalogue rather than the entity type, showing the same fallback word the trail does until the fetch resolves.

- **An audit action is rendered in English, with the raw token beneath it.** `describeAuditAction()` maps the vocabulary `apps/api` actually writes; an action this build has never heard of humanises its token rather than rendering blank, which is mandatory because `audit_log.action` is deliberately an open string and the table is excluded from the retention prune. Keeping the token visible is not redundancy: the description is what makes the ledger readable to someone who has not memorised the vocabulary, and the token is what makes a row greppable against a log line.

### Vendor portal (Stage 2)

The signed-in vendor's portal (`apps/web/src/app/vendor/`): the vendor-level row
(Vendor Overview / Profile / Products / Messages / Seats) or, once a product is open,
the product-level row (Profile / the four facet tabs / Integrations / Reviews, plus Catalogue on a `connector`-role product; Reviews since AECI-1179), plus the AECI-606
Integrations section. Gated by
`vendorMeResolver`, `noindex`, non-cacheable.

**Every section has its own address: `/vendor/:vendorSlug/<section>`** (`STAGE_2_VENDOR_PORTAL_SPEC.md` §6.2). The sections are `routerLink` anchors over a `<router-outlet/>`, not buttons over an in-page switch, so a section is linkable, bookmarkable, and reachable with Back — and `aria-current="page"` is driven by `routerLinkActive` rather than by hand. The vendor slug leads because the address should say which company is being edited; bare `/vendor` resolves the caller's own vendor and redirects. The nav's first item reads **"Vendor Overview"**, not "Overview": it sits inside a page whose `h1` is the company name, and it has to stay self-describing in a screen-reader's link list and a history entry.

**One header, and it follows the context** (`vendor/vendor-dashboard-tabbed.ts`,
§6.11). A breadcrumb sits where the eyebrow was: `Vendor portal › Company` on vendor pages,
`Vendor portal › Company › Products › Product` once a product is open. The `h1`, the "View
public page" link and the tab row all switch to the open product, and a quiet "← Back
to Company" link sits at the end of the breadcrumb line. Never stack a second heading
or a second nav row under the first: two headers of different sizes left it unclear
which one owned the page, and a segmented product row beneath an underlined vendor row
read as a button group rather than navigation.

**The shell in the new language (AECI-1809, mock-up 13, ruled 2026-11-24).** Anchor: mock-up 13 (`docs/design/mockups/redesign-2026-11/mockups/13-vendor-portal.html`), so no Mobbin reference. Order: site header, portal header, banner slot, tab row, page. All of it sits on the site's page wrap (`PORTAL_WRAP_CLASS`, `vendor/shared/portal-layout.ts`), so a section never sets its own width. The header is plain white, with no Forest band. It holds the shared `aec-breadcrumb` (hover underlines in ink, as on public pages), then one row: a 48px initial tile or logo (`aec-logo-or-initial` at `md`), the `h1` at its global size (`--t-fig`, 28 to 40px), the open product's plan badge and "View public page" (the standalone link role, 14px at 600). Content panels are the shared white card (`aecCard`, card shadow, 24px padding). Notices are not cards. **A section header's description runs the full width of the content below it** (Chris, 2026-11-26): the paragraph under a portal section or form heading (a tab's `h2`, a card's `h3`, a form's title block) takes no `max-w-*` cap, so a short wrapped intro never pushes the page down. The cap stays on notice copy (`PORTAL_NOTICE_BODY_CLASS`), confirmation boxes, empty states, field hints and body text inside rows. The page bodies keep their old look until their own slices (AECI-1810 to AECI-1812) rebuild them. AECI-1810's first part rebuilt the Vendor Overview, the Products list, "Add a product" and each product's overview (checklist, plan panel and removal election). Its second part rebuilt the company Profile, each product's Profile, the logo field and dialog, and the four facet editors.

**The tab row is a horizontal row under the title**
(`vendor/vendor-portal-nav.ts`, §6.4) — a 14rem side rail spent a seventh of a wide
page on five short links, and the editors it fronts are what want the width. Labels
are 16px in secondary ink at 400. The current tab is Forest at 600 with a 2px Forest
underline on the row's hairline, drawn by the link's own `::after` and keyed off
`aria-current` (AECI-1809). Each label reserves its 600 width with a hidden bold copy,
so tabs never shift. The focus ring is drawn inside the tab. The header gives up
its own rule so the row reads as attached to the panel it switches. Narrow viewports
**scroll the row sideways** rather than wrapping it — a wrapped tab row breaks its
own underline across two lines — and `overflow-y-hidden` prevents a second scrollbar
axis. The scrollbar is hidden; a 40px fade at an edge with more tabs past it says the
row scrolls, and a tab reached by Tab scrolls clear of the fades. There is exactly one row at every width, never a `md:hidden` duplicate. It is
deliberately **not sticky**: `section-nav.ts` is sticky because it is an in-page jump
nav on a long scroll, and a router nav has no such coupling. The same component draws
the product row, so the two can never drift apart visually.

> **The portal row no longer uses `.aec-nav-tab` (AECI-1809).** Its underline is a drawn bar, not a border, so it needs no edge class. The `/search` tabs and the docs guide toggle still use `.aec-nav-tab`. AECI-2088 moved the global `border-color` rule into `@layer base` on 2026-11-24, so border-colour utilities (`border-(--x)`, `divide-(--x)`, `border-transparent`) now apply. The unlayered edge classes (`.aec-nav-tab`, `.aec-notice-*`, `.aec-chip`, `.aec-field-control`, `.aec-btn`, `.aec-card`) still beat a utility on the same element. `apps/web/src/border-color-layer.guard.spec.ts` fails if the rule leaves the layer again.

**The portal's one notice** (`aecPortalNotice`, `vendor/shared/portal-notice.ts`, AECI-1809). Every portal banner is the same calm Bone notice: the `--accent-warm` fill, a 1px `--border-strong` edge, `rounded-panel`, 24px padding and 24px below it. The heading is the banner's `h2` at `--t-20` serif (`.aec-portal-notice h2` in `styles.css`, because a size utility on a heading is dead). Body copy is 14px, capped near 32rem. No status colour, no alert role, no dismiss, no shadow. Plan ended, product ended, listing hidden, the optional acceptance prompt and its confirmation use it.

**The portal form layout** (`vendor/shared/portal-form.ts`, AECI-1809, for the later slices): two columns of fields from 768px, overline fieldset legends with a hairline between fieldsets, and a save bar that sticks to the foot of the form card, the status at the start and the primary button at the end, full width below 640px. Fields keep `FIELD_*_CLASS`. `PORTAL_FORM_CLASS` on the form gives every control a bottom scroll margin taller than the bar, so a focused field never sits under it (WCAG 2.4.11). The status line is plain text, and the same sentence goes to the portal's one live region through `VendorPortalAnnouncer`. The demo is `/preview/portal-form`.

**Escape is Cancel in a portal nested form** (`aecCloseOnEscape`, `vendor/shared/close-on-escape.ts`, AECI-2133, ruled 2026-11-25). Every inline form with a Cancel button closes on Escape exactly as Cancel closes it, and focus returns to the form's opener. This matches the admin console, where Escape and Cancel are one act. Escape does nothing while the form sends, during an input-method composition, or when a control in the form already spent it (an open list, picker or tooltip). It never also closes a parent row or panel. A form inside a dialog leaves Escape to the dialog.

- **Products is a plain link to a product list** (`sections/vendor-product-list-page.ts`, §6.11). The list is one shared white card (`aecCard`, AECI-1810) of whole-row links divided by hairlines, not the admin table recipe: a small logo, the name, and a dense "Primary" chip. Below about 400px the score and the plan badge wrap under the name, so a long name never breaks mid-word. Primary first, then by name. Since AECI-1218 each row ends with the checklist score ("Checklist" over "2 of 3") and the plan badge in a fixed-width column, so Free and Managed line up down a long list. It replaced a search-box dropdown in the nav, which had to go once "Products" became a breadcrumb destination.
- **A product page shows one product** (`sections/vendor-products-page.ts`). The form opens with its read-only name block, and a URL naming a product the vendor does not own says so, with a link back to the list, rather than quietly rendering a different one.
- **The Profile forms and the facet editor are portal form cards** (AECI-1810 part 2, mock-up 13's "form" page; `vendor-profile-form.ts`, `vendor-product-form.ts`, `vendor-product-facet-editor.ts`). Each form is the shared white card with the portal form layout: fields two to a row from 768px in their old order (reading across a row gives the single-column sequence), the description, the logo and each company-fact group spanning both columns, and the sticky save bar at the card's foot. The company form's "Profile details" legend is a visible overline now. The notes that sit outside the fields (the name-on-record hint, "changed somewhere else", the read-only seat note, the product's Managed-only notice and the facet's plan reason) are sunken panels above the card, so no card nests in another. Labels are the Label role and controls the field recipe; a read-only control (a plan lock, an AECi lock or a seat without the capability) takes the sunken fill through a `read-only:` variant. An AECi lock's reason has an `aec-icon` lock glyph beside it. Ownership, Company size and Funding stage are 44px pills in an Angular Aria listbox (one tab stop, arrow keys within): the selected pill is Forest with white text at 600 and a tick, so the state is never colour alone. The facet editor's terms are one list divided by hairlines that runs to the card's edges, with the ticked terms repeated above it as dense neutral `aec-chip`s beside the "x of 10 selected" count, which names the chip list. The point lists' move and remove controls are 44px icon-only secondary `aecButton`s that wrap under the point below about 420px. The logo row's label is the Label role ("Logo", no longer capitals), Edit is a secondary button, and its confirmation is plain text announced through the portal's live region. The logo dialog keeps its file-rules sentence, because that sentence opens with "Use one of the two options below" and would not be true under the field.
- **The locked and hidden field notes cap at `24rem`** (AECI-2123; `vendor/components/vendor-locked-note.ts` and `vendor-hidden-note.ts`). They are 12px text under a field on the profile, product, facet and integration pages. At `max-w-prose` (65ch) a line ran 90 to 93 characters, because Atkinson's `ch` is a wide digit. At `24rem` it runs 66 to 72, inside the 65 to 75 measure (Chrome with the self-hosted font, 2026-11-27). A rem measure also does not re-wrap at the font swap.

- **Each taxonomy facet is its own tab, edited inline** (AECI-994, `STAGE_2_VENDOR_PORTAL_SPEC.md` §6.12). Categories, Trades, Audiences and Phases each render the full vocabulary as a checklist, a `<label>` around a real checkbox with the term description beside it, and one page Save. Under a ticked Audience or Phase sits a bullet list (`vendor/components/vendor-bullet-list-editor.ts`): an input per point, a plus button that adds at the bottom, and 44px icon-only secondary `aecButton`s to move up, move down and remove (32px bordered buttons until AECI-1810). Reordering is buttons rather than drag so it needs no second interaction model. Unticking a term with points confirms in a `BrnDialog` that lists what will be lost. The history below is superseded.
- **(Superseded) Taxonomy reads as a summary and writes in a modal** (AECI-915). The four facets shipped as fieldsets of `aria-pressed` toggle chips — 107 chips on one screen — which made "what is this tagged as" a visual diff and left the per-term descriptions nowhere to go. Now each facet is a card in a **two-column grid**: name, a right-justified pencil, and one row per _assigned_ term. This is the exception the "Don't use modals as the first thought" rule contemplates — the alternatives were tried and shipped, and the read case and the write case genuinely want different densities. The picker (`vendor/components/vendor-taxonomy-facet-dialog.ts`) is a `BrnDialog` opened imperatively from the click handler (never an `effect()`, NG0602), and every row is a `<label>` wrapping a real `<input type="checkbox">` so the whole row is the click target and the keyboard and ARIA behaviour is native rather than re-implemented over `role="checkbox"`. **Its Save persists**, because there is no `CanDeactivate` guard on this app and a Save that only staged would lose work silently; a failure keeps the modal open with the draft and puts the error inside it.

- **The Overview is a landing page, not an inventory** (AECI-983, `STAGE_2_VENDOR_PORTAL_SPEC.md` §6.10). Three blocks: a glance band, the "Getting started" checklist, and a "What needs you" list of whole-row links. **Since AECI-1810 (mock-up 13, ruled 2026-11-24) the page is two columns from 1024px**: the list on the start side, the figures and the checklist in a 400px end column (432px from 1280px). The end column comes first in the DOM, so below 1024px the order is figures, checklist, list, as before. The rows share ONE white card divided by hairlines, under their "Needs you now" and "Worth doing" overlines, with a hairline between the groups. Each row is a whole-row link: an `aec-icon` (warning, clock, pencil or users), the title (Forest on hover) and one line, a dense `aec-chip` and an `aria-hidden` chevron that mirrors in RTL. The chip tones are `error` for a conflict, `strong` for work with a clock on it and `sunken` for the rest, the same three steps the old portal pill classes drew. The `strong` chip carries the decorative "!" (`exclamation`) mark, as the Integrations list's attention chip does (AECI-1811), so the two pages read the same. Row hover moves the surface (`hover:bg-(--surface-sunken)`). The all-clear and the paused statement are white cards too.
- **The glance band reuses the former home stats-card vocabulary, with two departures** (`vendor/components/vendor-glance-band.ts`). The home cards were deleted in AECI-1554; the vocabulary stands here: two tiles on the shared white card (`aecCard`, AECI-1810), side by side from 360px, a `font-display text-5xl` figure, a quiet 13px `--text-secondary` line, a 14px weight-600 label, and every label row `sm:min-h-11` (`min-h-16` on a phone) so the figures align even when a label wraps. The zero sentence is 20px, so "None open" fits the narrow column. **A zero is never a bare 0**: it renders a sentence ("No conflicts", "None open"). _(2026-11-14, R2: the portal takes the new tokens and nothing more. Clash 8's one large figure per band and clash 25's large zero are public-page rules, so the three figures and the sentence at zero stay here. AECI-1748 maps `text-5xl` to `--t-fig`, so the figures render 28 to 40px.)_ **Departure one: a non-zero conflict figure is `--status-error`**, not Forest. `conflict` is the one sanctioned red, and a conflict count that reads like good news defeats the tile. At zero there is no figure, so red never appears for good news. **Departure two (since AECI-1810): a link tile lifts** as an interactive card, and its label turns Forest and underlines. The `group/card` wrapper holds the hover. **Only a tile with something to do is a link.** At zero, In conflict and Suggestions are plain cards with a 32px Forest check badge. With work waiting, the tile is a link with a badge beside the figure: a red X for conflicts, a Forest exclamation mark for open suggestions, all on `aec-icon`. The badges are `aria-hidden`; the figure and the link's `aria-label` carry the state.
- **The Views period toggle is an `aria-pressed` button set, not Angular Aria.** The tile was removed on 2026-10-13 (Chris). The glance band shows two tiles until AECI-941 rebuilds Views with a real figure and this toggle. Aria@22 ships no radio group (ADR 0010), and the admin date-range control (`admin/traffic/traffic.html`, since AECI-2125 the console's segmented filter) already made this call, so the vendor surface follows the shipped precedent: a `fieldset` with an sr-only legend, a sunken segmented track, and the pressed segment filled Forest, with its focus ring set to Forest so it does not vanish. The toggle left with the tile, and the cleanup slice (AECI-1729) deleted its unlayered pressed-state rule from `styles.css`. A rebuilt toggle uses the portal segmented piece (`vendor/shared/portal-segmented.ts`). Visible "1d", accessible name "1d, last day" (WCAG 2.5.3).
- **The connector seat's Catalogue tab (labeled "Catalog" since AECI-1262) is a row card, not a table** (AECI-1083, `STAGE_2_VENDOR_PORTAL_SPEC.md` §6.16). It shows only on a `connector`-role product whose catalogue AECi has handed to the vendor (AECI-2206), last in the product row. The listings share one white card (`aecCard`), divided by hairlines, one row each: the name in the body face at 16px (on a span, because the `h3` element rule is unlayered serif), a "View listing" link, then each match as a phrase with a dense `aec-chip` "Counts toward reach". The admin triage table needs 64rem, so the portal does not borrow it. Edit opens the form inline under its row, a bordered panel capped at `max-w-3xl` with the portal form layout; one form at a time, focus moves in on open and back to Edit on close. Since AECI-1811 every button is `aecButton`, so a disabled one dims to 60% like every other button (it used to swap to the sunken surface). The two read-only states ("We maintain this catalog", "no catalog yet") are the portal notice. Text blocks are capped at 52ch, because `npx impeccable detect` measured ~96 characters a line at 65ch in Atkinson and ~87 at 52ch. The intro under the heading runs the full width (the header rule above).
- **(Superseded by AECI-1218) The plan panel had a compact strip for the quiet state.** Plans moved to the product level (`STAGE_2_PAID_TIERS_SPEC.md` §13.1 decision 2), so the full panel lives on each product's overview. The vendor overview first carried a one-line plan summary; that is gone too. The vendor checklist took the full width until AECI-1810 put it in the right column from 1024px, under the figures (see "The Overview is a landing page" above). The compact strip and the "Account access" heading are gone.
- **The Free plan surfaces** (AECI-1218, `STAGE_2_VENDOR_PORTAL_SPEC.md` §6.18). **Anchor: the in-repo mockup** (`docs/design/mockups/free-plan-portal/`) **and DoorDash Merchant's "Get ready to go live" setup list on Mobbin** (numbered steps, one action each, the optional step marked in words).
  - **Checklist card** (`vendor/components/vendor-checklist.ts`): the shared white card (AECI-1810), the heading at the 20px card-title size (on a span inside the `h2`) with an "x of y done" score at the right, a segmented bar (`aria-hidden`; the text carries the score), then a divided `<ol>` of steps. Each step has a 28px round marker: the number, a Forest-filled check when done, or a lock when the step is outside the product's plan. An sr-only "Done" / "Not done" / "Not done, optional" rides in the title, so state is never colour alone. Optional steps carry a dense "Optional" chip. One action per step, under the step's text: a compact secondary `aecButton` link, "Looks right" (`vendor-looks-right.ts`, a compact secondary button), or a dense "Available on Managed" chip.
  - **Plan badge** (`vendor-plan-badge.ts`): the word "Free" (outlined) or "Managed" (sunken fill, strong border). Text, never colour alone. Portal only. No public page carries a plan or account badge (AECI-1264).
  - **Product plan panel** (`vendor-plan-panel.ts`): "Free includes" / "Managed adds" lists under `aec-overline` microheadings (a size utility cannot beat the unlayered `h3` rule), a plain price line (no "Draft price" tag since the 2026-10-08 ruling; it follows the per-vendor override, `STAGE_2_PAID_TIERS_SPEC.md` §13.13), one secondary `aecButton` call to action, and decision 10's line under a hairline. Since AECI-1810 the panel is the shared white card, and the warm Bone card in `expiring` and `overdue`. It sits in a 400px column beside the product checklist (432px from 1280px). No box inside the card: `npx impeccable detect` flags a bordered price box as a nested card.
  - **Plan-ended banner** (`vendor-plan-ended-banner.ts`): above the tab row on every portal page, the portal notice (AECI-1809), two columns ("Still works" / "Now read-only"), the Forest primary button. Calm: no status colour, no alert role, no dismiss button.
  - **Product-ended banner** (`vendor-product-ended-banner.ts`, AECI-1408): the plan-ended banner's sibling for one product, under the product header on every product tab. The portal notice: the heading and one dated line on the start edge, the Forest "Go to Billing" button on the end for an owner seat, wrapping below on narrow screens. No lists, because the plan panel on the product's overview already names what Managed adds. Hidden whenever the vendor-wide banner shows. Anchor: the vendor-wide banner itself (Anchor-Site Rule), so no new Mobbin reference.
  - **Legal notice band** (`legal-notice-band.ts`, AECI-1592): the site-wide notice of a material legal change, in the app shell under the header on every page, while its 30-day window is open. One full-width Bone band with a strong bottom border: the bold dated sentence, the one-line summary and the "Read the new version" link on the start edge, an icon dismiss button on the end. Calm like its anchor: no status colour, no alert role, no live region. Unlike its anchor it can be dismissed, because it runs on every page for a month. Anchor: the product-ended banner (Anchor-Site Rule), so no new Mobbin reference.
  - **Locked field**: the control stays `readonly` on the sunken surface, and a lock glyph plus a one-line reason sits between the label and the control, tied with `aria-describedby`. One notice above the form names every locked field once.

**It is not a static page — it live-updates while it is open** (AECI-516, shipped 2026-08-19; `docs/STAGE_2_REALTIME_SPEC.md`, transport decision ADR 0023). A poll loop (`vendor-live-sync.ts`) reads a per-vendor freshness cursor — every 20 s focused, 60 s unfocused, **paused with no timer when the tab is hidden** — and asks the shared store (`vendor-portal-store.ts`) to refetch only the sections that actually moved, so a claim approved or a plan activated by an admin lands without a reload. There is no socket. Two visual consequences are binding: **a background refresh must never reflow the control under the pointer or steal focus** (staleness is the lesser harm), and **a section holding unsaved edits is never overwritten** — it defers and offers a quiet "Updated elsewhere — reload this section" affordance instead. Toggle-shaped writes (Yes / No, and pressing either again to clear it) render optimistically and **roll back with a visible error**; form-shaped writes stay pessimistic, because "Saved" before it saved is a worse lie than a short wait.

**No new Mobbin anchor was picked, deliberately** (since AECI-1809 the shell's anchor is mock-up 13, above) — the same call the operator console made above (`ADMIN_PANEL_SPEC.md` §9.10), and recorded here because the Anchor-Site Rule's "record the anchor site with the surface" had never been satisfied for `/vendor`. The portal inherits the Phase 5/6 admin-queue and Phase 8.3 console vocabulary: bordered `--surface-raised` cards, border not shadow, the eyebrow-then-heading header, Forest figures, `tabular-nums`. The §6.11 breadcrumb reuses the public product page's breadcrumb, so it does not introduce a second anchor. It is an internal, signed-in surface reading the same catalog the public directory renders, so a second reference site would make AECi read as two products. One publication, one voice (Anchor-Site Rule). Token-only, i18n throughout, light-only.

- **Integrations tab: a list of links, not a list of cards** (`<aec-vendor-integrations-section>`, `vendor/components/`, reshaped 2026-09-28 by AECI-1149). Rows still group by the counterpart product, but each row is now a link to that integration's own page: the counterpart's mark and name, one line saying what is shared, and a status pill. Nothing on the tab expands in place any more.
  - **In the new language (AECI-1811, mock-up 13).** The rows share one white card divided by hairlines, like the overview's "What needs you" rows. The status pill is a dense `aec-chip` in the tones the portal pill already had: Forest wash with a tick for up to date, the one red with an X for a disagreement, a strong edge for the rest, and a "!" mark for the states that need someone. The status filter keeps its `aria-pressed` toggles and their URL state (ruled), drawn as the site's segmented control: the 44px framed track, 36px segments, pressed Forest with a tick. This is the ruled difference from public pages: the portal filters with pressed toggles on the segmented track, and public pages filter with filter chips (`aec-chip [selected]`). The track wraps, because the set grows with the statuses on record. Search and "Integrates with" are the shared field recipe. "Integrations your company offers", the Connectors list and "Products you asked us to add" are one card each, divided by hairlines.
  - **The create and owner edit forms** use the portal form layout (`vendor/shared/portal-form.ts`): two columns from 768px, overline legends, the sticky save bar with its plain status line, and the result announced through `VendorPortalAnnouncer`. "Add an integration" stays an inline disclosure, not a dialog. Its panel is the form card, so it takes `PORTAL_SAVE_BAR_CLASS` whole. The owner edit form (in an owned row) and the catalogue match form (in a listing row) are not the foot of a card, so their bar keeps the sticky foot, hairline and order but not the pull over a card's padding. A refusal is the bar's status line, still `role="alert"`.
  - **By product kind (AECI-2045 QA, 2026-11-24, `STAGE_2_VENDOR_PORTAL_SPEC.md` §6.13b).** A Sync Service's tab leads with the pairs it delivers, under one heading with one summary line, and a pure Sync Service offers one "Add an integration", its own form. A hybrid keeps both sections, each under its own heading, and its two triggers say what each adds. Both triggers are `aecButton variant="secondary"`, so they share one size and one radius (the Same-Behavior Rule). The Sync Service form is on the portal form recipe the ordinary create uses.

- **Daily reminder email switch** (AECI-1204, `vendor/components/vendor-nudge-mute-toggle.ts`, on Messages above the notification list). **Anchor site: Shopify admin** settings cards, the same anchor as the integration detail page below (Mobbin screens `e53a5210-9e2c-405c-ab2d-87de1432dd5e` and `f6ea66fe-de5c-4239-aaf8-30d7bb3f85f2`). The shared white card (`aecCard`, since AECI-1812), title and a two-sentence description on the start edge, the switch on the end, and a status line ("On" or "Off since {date}"). The track is 44 by 24px and the knob 16px, 4px in from either end, so every offset is on the 4-point scale. It is a native `<button role="switch">` with `aria-checked`, because Angular Aria has no switch (ADR 0010). ON means "email me". The off track is `--text-tertiary`, which keeps 3:1 against the card (WCAG 1.4.11). No switch renders until the saved state has loaded: a same-size `--surface-sunken` pill holds the place, so the control never shows a state it does not know. The write is optimistic and rolls back with a visible error.
- **Reviews, Messages, Changes and Seats in the new language (AECI-1812, mock-up 13).** Restyle only: wording, gates and writes are unchanged. Each section heading is a bare `h2` at the global size. Messages is a stack of shared white cards, 32px apart: claim and correction status (hairline rows, the state on a dense `aec-chip`), Change requests ("Received" and "Submitted" as overline groups), the reminder switch, and the notification archive (a `details` card whose drawn chevron turns when open). Each review is one white card. The reviewer's updates sit under a 2px rule. Replies are sunken panels set in from the start edge, and a rejection reason or the published-edit warning is a rule, not a box. Changes keeps its filter toggles (ruled) on the Integrations tab's segmented recipe, with a tick on the pressed one. Its rows share one white card, and the start-of-history note is a sunken hint panel. Seats puts "Wants to join" on a white card. The roster is a flat table in a labelled, focusable scroll region that is `relative`, so the visually hidden names inside the row buttons cannot push the page sideways. It scrolled the page at 390 and 320px before. Pending invites are a hairline list. Contest values wrap inside a `minmax(0, 1fr)` column, which fixed the sideways scroll on Messages at 320px. **Button severity.** The danger look is only ever on a confirm. Seat "Remove" and invite "Revoke" are secondary openers. Each opens an inline confirm in its row, and the confirm is danger, because neither has a full undo (AECI-2129, ruled 2026-11-25). A removed owner comes back from a new invite as a seat that is not an owner. Undoing a revoke sends a new email and spends one of the day's invites. The confirm says what happens in one or two sentences, with "Remove seat" or "Revoke invite" and a secondary "Cancel". Opening it focuses its question. Cancel returns focus to the opener. After the row goes, focus moves to the next row's first live control, else the previous row's, else the Seats heading. A refusal is a `role="alert"` line, and focus returns to the row's Remove or Revoke, never to the alert. The seat confirm sits in its own table row, as wide as the visible scroll region and pinned to its start edge, so it reads in full at 320px. The claim "Decline" still acts in one click. The contest "Withdraw" confirm is danger: its own copy says it cannot be reopened. The review reply "Withdraw" confirm is danger on a published reply and primary on a pending one (ruled for AECI-1812). Withdrawing a published reply clears `published_at` and takes it off the product page, and a resubmit only returns it to pending, where AECi can still reject it (`STAGE_2_VENDOR_PORTAL_SPEC.md` §11c). A pending reply was never public. Openers, Resend and the claim Accept and Decline pair are secondary. Success lines are plain text announced once through `VendorPortalAnnouncer`. The one exception is the seat invite dialog's "Invite sent to" line, which keeps a local `role="status"`: the dialog is modal and the CDK hides the shell's live region while it is open.
- **Billing and Proposed products in the new language (AECI-1812 part 2, mock-up 13 has no Billing page).** Restyle only. No price, plan name, tax, renewal, refund, cancellation or agreement sentence changed, and none moved: the disclosure, the liability cap and the agreement note still sit above "Continue to Stripe", in the same order. Each section heading is a bare `h2` and each sub-heading a bare `h3`. Billing's left column is a stack of white cards: "Subscriptions waiting for you" (each grant split by a hairline), one card for the billing email and the company legal name (split by a hairline), and Pay by card. "At a glance" is a white card in the right column from `lg`. The checkout banner and the grant outcome are calm panels with the panel radius, not cards. The Subscriptions and Agreements tables are flat, on the Seats roster's head and cell recipe (`vendor-billing-styles.ts`), each in a labelled, focusable, `relative` scroll region, so the page no longer scrolls sideways at 390 and 320px (it was 1056px wide). The subscription status is a dense `aec-chip`: Live is `wash`, a will-not-renew or ends-soon row is `strong` with a clock (a public chip never takes Clay), ended is `quiet`, and the badges are `strong`. The Product label on the assign control is the Label role, not the overline. The release dialog and the Add subscriptions dialog are `aec-dialog` surfaces with a 44px ghost close button. Proposed products is a stack of white cards. Its facts take the Label role, its taxonomy values are `aec-chip`s (`neutral`, and `sunken` with "Not added" for a value that names no term), and the accept summary is a Forest-soft panel. **Button severity.** "Continue to Stripe" is the page's one primary. Openers are secondary: "Add subscriptions", "Manage billing in Stripe", "Change", "Assign", and the proposal "Accept" and "Decline". Confirms that the owner can undo or that add something are primary: "Release at renewal" ("Keep at renewal" undoes it), "Send request" (AECi decides, nothing changes yet), "Assign subscription", the Add dialog's "Add subscriptions", a grant's "Accept" and "Accept and add product". "Decline product" is danger: AEC Integrations does not send a declined product again, and nothing reopens it. A refusal inside a control is the field error line, `role="alert"`. The accepted-grant notice lost its `role="status"`: it is plain text and its words go once through `VendorPortalAnnouncer`.
- **Product picker** (AECI-1244, `<aec-product-combobox>`, `shared/product-combobox/product-combobox.ts`). The one way to pick a published product by name. It serves the vendor "Add an integration" counterpart, the vendor catalogue match form and the admin connector mapping edit. **Anchor site: Shopify admin**, its product search dropdown (Mobbin screens `b5b15af1-6439-49ed-98e3-de1377b29f00` and `54fca623-f156-426f-adcc-10b701e2d3b1`). A text box with a leading search glyph and a trailing clear button. It searches as you type after a 250 ms pause, from two letters, and the latest query wins. The popup lists rows A to Z, each a 32 px `LogoOrInitial`, the name and the vendor, with the active row on `--surface-sunken`. Searching, no match and a failed search show as one quiet line in the popup, never an empty listbox. It is an editable Angular Aria combobox over an Aria listbox inside a `cdkConnectedOverlay` (ADR 0010), the `aec-select` wiring. No row is active until the first Down or Up: Down enters at the first row, Up at the last (AECI-1860, `shared/listbox-entry/listbox-entry.ts`). Enter picks the arrowed-to row, picks nothing before that, and never submits the form. The caller owns the label, the hint, the error and the chosen state, which reads as the product plus a Change button. Focus moves to Change after a pick and back to the box after Change. The result count reaches the page's one live region through the `announce` output. The control has no region of its own. There is no Search button: a picker that waits for a button press reads as dated.
- **Integration detail page** (AECI-1149 to AECI-1153, `vendor/integration-detail/`, `STAGE_2_VENDOR_PORTAL_SPEC.md` §6.17). **Anchor site: Shopify admin** (Settings → Checkout; an order detail page), chosen for the same reason as the rest of the portal — non-technical staff use it daily. A title block (the two product marks, the title, the status pill, "View public page"), then a two-column body: a left `<nav>` of section links with scroll-spy (`aria-current="location"` on the item under the sticky bar) and five white `aecCard` sections on the right, in order — Overview, Data that's shared, Integration links, Change requests, Settings. Below `lg` the nav sits above the cards and is not sticky. Once the title block scrolls out of view a slim bar pins to the top of the viewport with the same marks, title, pill and "View public page" link, browser-only (SSR renders no bar).

- **In the new language (AECI-1811 part 2, mock-up 13).** The page carries no component CSS of its own any more: AECI-2088 made border utilities apply, so `ID_STYLES` is only `:host { display: block; }`. The title block, "Things that need you" and the five sections are the shared white card (`aecCard`, `p-6`). "Things that need you" is one list divided by hairlines, a "!" mark on the items that need the caller and a clock on the ones waiting on someone else. The section nav is the portal tab row's language on its side: secondary text at rest, the current section Forest at 600 with a 2px Forest edge on the rail (the tab row draws the same state as an underline), 44px targets, scroll-spy and `aria-current="location"` unchanged. Every status is a dense `aec-chip` through `STATUS_CHIP_TONE` and `STATUS_CHIP_ICON` in `integration-detail-styles.ts`, the maps the integrations list uses too. Forms nested in a row (add a row, the answer form, the request form, the protest form, "Not ours?") are a white bordered panel with the nested save bar (`PORTAL_NESTED_SAVE_BAR_CLASS` in `vendor/shared/portal-form.ts` since AECI-1812, one constant where three copies were). Change requests is one hairline list inside its section card, and its All / Open / Closed toggles sit on the segmented track. Retiring sits in a plain bordered panel with a secondary opener and a danger confirm, because a retire closes other companies' open requests and row threads and Restore reopens none. Deleting a row you added, deleting a message, withdrawing a request and withdrawing a review request cannot be undone, so their confirms are the danger button.

- **Tooltips are built exactly like `shared/info-hint`, everywhere on the page.** Every label, pill and flag carries an `i`-style trigger: a real `<button>` with a `cdkConnectedOverlay` panel that is hoverable (a grace period across the gap), dismissible on Escape, and persistent until the pointer or focus leaves. The full text also sits in the trigger's `aria-describedby`, always in the DOM, so assistive tech never depends on the overlay opening (WCAG 1.4.13). No panel holds a link or a control.

- **Direction is always the caller's own frame.** Rows render "To {other}" / "From {other}" / "Both ways" — the same idea the public pair page uses, reframed per caller. The stored `a_to_b`/`b_to_a` never reaches the browser as-is.

- **The row status pill is its own component, not `<aec-agreement-badge>`.** `Data that's shared` renders a caller-seat status (a dense `aec-chip`: `ok` the Forest wash with a tick, `conflict` the red with an X, the rest a strong edge) rather than reusing the public badge component, because the two name different things — the portal's pill is the _caller's_ position, the public badge is the _agreed_ state. Their wording still cannot disagree: the pill's tooltip quotes the same `STAGE_2_ATTESTATIONS_SPEC.md` §6.2 outcome sentence verbatim, and `conflict` stays the one red state on both surfaces.

- **A disagreement shows both positions in the row, not as an error.** The Change requests section's disagreement item is a title naming the data and each side's stance, the caller's answer and reason beside the other company's, with "They have not given a reason" when there is none. Two companies describing a flow differently is a disagreement to resolve, not a defect in either product — red belongs to the status pill alone.

- **Yes / No are plain, `aria-pressed` toggle buttons on the segmented track** (`SEGMENTED_CLASS`, `segmentClass`), because they are commands that write on activation, not values you pick and submit (ADR 0010 governs the latter). Pressing the pressed button again clears the answer. **The toggles show only on a row the caller has not answered** (AECI-1246): an answered row reads as plain text ("Yes" or "No" with its icon) and a **Change** link, which reveals that row's toggles until a save or Escape, because a stray click on a filled toggle changed answers vendors meant to keep. "The direction is wrong" folds its corrected row into a "You submitted a change" box under the denied row (sunken fill, a 3px Forest inline-start border, no divider above it, so it reads as part of the row and not as another data row), with Change and Cancel. The reason form opens _inline, under the row_, never behind a second disclosure. Native `<select>` elements pick the data type and direction (ADR 0010 deviation (d), unchanged in shape by this page, just relocated) — not an Aria combobox or `<aec-select>`, because each is one value from a closed, plain-text list in an ordinary form.

- **One polite live region on the surface, many assertive ones.** Successful writes announce through the one channel; failures are alert text beside the control that failed.
