The region lives in the dashboard shell, not on this page, fed by `VendorPortalAnnouncer` (`vendor/vendor-announcer.ts`), unchanged by this page's rebuild. **Never add a second persistent one.** A **local** `role="status"` is still allowed only for immediate feedback on an action the user just took, beside the control they took it with — this page has none: even the "row already exists" case on Add a data flow announces through the shared channel rather than a local region. Rule and audit: `STAGE_2_REALTIME_SPEC.md` §6.3 / §6.5.

- **The status pill's tooltip says what happens next**, not a permanent sentence under the row. It lists, in order, the caller's reason, the other company's reason, and the §6.2 outcome sentence verbatim, so a vendor who says no to a false row and sees no visible consequence can still find one without a second click. A "Things that need you" callout above the sections surfaces the same information as one-sentence links for anything that currently needs the caller. Contract: `STAGE_2_ATTESTATIONS_SPEC.md` §6.2, `STAGE_2_VENDOR_PORTAL_SPEC.md` §6.17.2.

- **Copy never says attestation, claim, provenance, data object, contest, counterparty, mechanism or iPaaS** (§6.17.8). It says "Company", never "vendor", and a change is written "from X to Y" in words — never the arrow glyph, which this surface reserves for a data flow's direction (the Arrow Rule, above).

- **Copy carries the trust promise.** Nothing implies answering Yes or No affects ranking or placement; the only search reference is that search refreshes within a day; "Verified" is framed as an account status arranged with AEC Integrations, and the unverified state explains what verification unlocks rather than 403-ing a vendor out of their own data.

### Product docs (Stage 2, AECI-1104; layout AECI-1259)

Three surfaces, home → section → article (`apps/web/src/app/docs/`), all inside one **help-center shell** between the site header and footer. **Anchor site: Devin (DeepWiki)**, Mobbin screen `c3936cdb-13b2-4b54-b2a2-c7ccf723dd1b`, which replaced the Zendesk anchor on 2026-10-09 (AECI-1259). DeepWiki supplies the structure, density and restraint. AECi keeps its own type, tokens and Forest links. It is a public, edge-cached reading surface, so it reuses the `/legal/*` and `/methodology` prose vocabulary rather than the portal's cards. Everything is built from the docs manifest, so a section with no pages shows nowhere (AECI-1248).

AECI-1803 (2026-11-17) restyled the chrome below in the new language. The structure is unchanged. Every docs control is a 44px target at the least, with the 3px Forest ring at a 3px offset. Weights are 400 and 600 only: the `font-medium` that sat outside §3 is gone. Pills and highlights take `rounded-mark` (8px). Sizes are ramp utilities (`text-t-14`, `text-t-12`).

- **Shell** (`docs-shell.ts`, the `/docs` layout route). From `lg`: a 16rem sidebar on `--surface-raised` behind a hairline end border, spanning the shell's full height, and a content column. The shell carries a hairline top border under the site header. A per-guide search box sits under the guide toggle (AECI-1270). There is no "Ask" box.
- **Guide toggle** (AECI-1271): the first thing in the sidebar on every docs page, a `nav` named "Guides" holding two links, "General" (`/docs`) and "For vendors" (`/docs/vendors`). It replaced the vendor guide's "Back to the help center" link. The current guide's link carries `aria-current="true"`, never `"page"`, because a deeper page is not the guide's landing. It is the `.aec-nav-tab` underline on a hairline: 44px-tall `--t-14` `--text-secondary` links at 400, the current one `--text-primary` at 600 over a 2px `--accent-primary` bottom border, as the section nav does. It is navigation, not a segmented control, so it never takes the `aria-pressed` button-group look. Derived from the URL alone, no cookie or storage. Mobbin anchor: Devin (DeepWiki) screen `470ef8ee-da99-4893-9a15-bde5f3744177`, whose sidebar puts its top-level destinations above a hairline and the scoped page tree below. The per-guide docs search box (AECI-1270) sits directly under it.
- **Docs search box** (AECI-1270, `docs-search.ts`): directly under the guide toggle, full sidebar width, searching the current guide only. Its label names the guide: "Search General" or "Search For vendors" (visually hidden `label`, placeholder "Search"). An input on `--surface-base` that takes the Inputs recipe (`FIELD_INPUT_CLASS`, AECI-1803): 44px high, the field edge, 12px radius, 16px text, and a leading 20px `--text-secondary` magnifier with the text starting at 48px, as the `/search` query box does. Client only: the SSR HTML holds a fixed 44px slot (`h-11`) so the tree does not jump, and the slot goes when there is no docs index in the config. Results open in a top-layer popup (an Angular Aria combobox in `cdkConnectedOverlay`): a `--surface-raised` panel with a hairline border and the menu shadow (`--shadow-menu`, §4, AECI-1770), up to 24rem wide. Each row is at least 44px: a `--t-12` `--text-secondary` trail ("Section / Page"), the heading in `--t-14` 600 `--text-primary`, and a two-line `--t-12` snippet. Matched words are `mark`: underlined in the heading, `--text-primary` 600 in the snippet, never a highlighter fill. The active row is the `--surface-sunken` `rounded-mark` pill the tree uses. No row is active until the first Down or Up, as in the product picker (AECI-1860). Enter with no row active opens the first row, the top match the live region has just counted. No hit shows "No matching sections" as plain text, and a polite live region announces the count. With no row, settled or still in flight, the combobox stays collapsed (AECI-1308). Mobbin anchors: DeepWiki's search field (screen `470ef8ee-da99-4893-9a15-bde5f3744177`) and Devin's results list (screen `e37a4e69-9a61-4f36-803d-edbd89c1229d`), whose rows lead with a breadcrumb trail.
- **Sidebar tree** (`nav` named "Help center" on the General guide): "Help center" first, then each General section in manifest order, its pages indented under a `--border-default` start-edge guide line. A single-page section is one item. **On a vendor guide page the tree is the vendor guide's own** (AECI-1265): a `nav` named "Vendor guide", then "Vendor guide" and its pages, overview and claiming first (AECI-1271). The URL alone picks the tree, so the SSR HTML is the same for every visitor. All groups are expanded. Items are 44px-tall `--t-14` `--text-secondary` links, no icons. The rows touch: no gap between rows, and none between a section's last page and the next section (AECI-1720 layout pass). The section names are links to the section index, so they keep the 44px target too. That is the densest the 44px target allows: 44px a row against 33px before AECI-1803. At 390 by 844 the open menu shows 13 of 16 links (12 before the pass, 16 before AECI-1803). On a vendor guide page at 1440 by 900 the sidebar is 904px (942px before the pass, 731px before AECI-1803). Hover darkens the text only, so the current page stays the one filled item. The current page is a `--surface-sunken` `rounded-mark` pill with `--text-primary` 600 text and `aria-current="page"` (`routerLinkActive`, exact match, so a section item is current only on its own index). From `lg` the tree is sticky at the viewport top, at most one viewport tall, and scrolls on its own when it is longer. The site header is not sticky, so nothing sits above it.
- **Top bar** over the content only, with a hairline under it. Left: a breadcrumb derived from the URL _(2026-11-14: clash 31 rules the separator `›`, and AECI-1777 moved this trail onto the shared breadcrumb, §5 Navigation "The breadcrumb")_. "Help center", then the section, on the General guide. On the vendor guide the trail starts at "Vendor guide", which is its one section (AECI-1271). Ancestors are links. The current place is text with `aria-current="page"`. On an article the trail stops at the section, as DeepWiki's does, because the `h1` names the page. Right, articles only, from `sm`: the pre-formatted "Last updated" line in `--text-secondary`.
- **Below `lg`** the tree becomes a panel under the top bar, closed by default. A bordered 44px icon button at the start of the top bar ("Docs menu", `aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`), drawing the registry's `panel-left` glyph, toggles it. Escape and any navigation close it. DOM order is top bar, panel, page at every width, so the SSR HTML is identical for every visitor.
- **Article column** (`docs-page.ts`): a centred 42rem column. A Source Serif `h1` at `--t-36`, 28 to 36px (`.aec-docs-title`, the Headline role brought down from a hero), one muted lede line from the frontmatter description, then `.aec-prose`, the one prose style every Markdown page shares (AECI-1804: the denser `.aec-docs-prose` variant is gone). No overline. Docs `h2`s are `--t-24` and `h3`s `--t-20`, both serif at or above the 20px floor. _(AECI-1748 moved them from 1.875rem, 1.375rem and 1.125rem, which were off the type ramp (§3).)_ Below `sm` the "Last updated" line moves under the lede.
- **Heading ids.** Every body `h2` and `h3` has a stable GitHub-style slug id, unique per page, built at manifest time (`docs-markdown.ts`). The template renders the headings itself, because the `[innerHTML]` sanitizer strips `id`. Headings carry `scroll-margin-top: 1.5rem`.
- **Tables scroll in their own box (AECI-1918).** `renderDocsBody` wraps every prose table in `div.aec-prose-table-scroll`, so a table wider than a phone scrolls sideways inside its box and the page does not (WCAG 1.4.10). It covers the docs articles, the recorded versions, `/legal/*` and `/methodology`. The table keeps `display: table`: set to `block`, some screen readers stop reading it as a table. The wrapper is markup only, so no word changes. While the table overflows, the box is a keyboard stop with `role="region"` and a name from the table's caption, else the nearest heading above it, else the page's `h1` (`shared/prose-blocks/prose-table-scroll.ts`, set after hydration, so the cached HTML stays the same for everyone). It shows the chip's ring, 3px Forest at a 3px offset. A box whose table fits is no stop and no landmark. Measured 2026-11-18 at 320px before the fix: three articles scrolled the page by 2 to 43px. `e2e/docs.spec.ts` lists every page with a table at 320 and 390px.
- **"On this page" rail** (`shared/page-toc/page-toc.ts` with `variant="rail"`, rendered as `<app-docs-toc>`; one component with the list below since the AECI-1720 layout pass), from `xl` only, sticky at the far right of the content. One short dash per `h2`, the closing "Related" excluded, none at all below two headings. The current dash is longer and `--text-primary`. Others are `--text-tertiary`, which holds the 3:1 non-text floor because a closed dash is the link's only visible mark. Each link is at least a 24px target (WCAG 2.2 target size), which sets the dash pitch. Hover or keyboard focus opens it into a bordered `--surface-base` panel listing the headings. The labels are visually hidden, never removed, so the links stay named. Links are plain anchors to the article path plus `#id`, opted in to the smooth in-page jump (AECI-1752). The first dash is current in the SSR HTML; an `IntersectionObserver` moves it only after the first browser render.
- **"On this page" list on reading pages** (the same component, `variant="list"`, AECI-1720 layout pass), on `/methodology` and `/legal/*` from `lg`, sticky in the column beside the article. It is the docs rail with its labels shown, because that column is wide and the docs rail sits beside a sidebar. The links, `data-smooth-scroll`, the host click listener, the first heading current in the SSR HTML and the `IntersectionObserver` are one code path for both looks. It sticks only on a viewport tall enough to show all of it, the rule the product page aside follows (§5 "Layout shells"): from 46rem (736px) tall, and from 54rem (864px) while the consent banner shows. Below that it scrolls with the page, so Tab never lands on a link that is cut off or under the banner (WCAG 2.4.11). The tallest list, the Vendor Terms' 20 links at 1024px wide, is 655px plus the 32px offset. A `nav` named by its visible overline "On this page" (`@@app.pageToc.heading`, `id="page-toc-heading"`, reserved from heading slugs), then one link per `h2`, `--t-14` `--text-secondary` on a hairline start edge, at least a 24px target as on the docs rail, so a 20-heading agreement fits one screen. The current link is `--text-primary` at 600 with a 2px Forest bar on the edge. The weight, not the colour, carries it in forced colours. Nothing below two headings. Settled (Chris, 2026-11-19): the docs rail keeps its dashes and this list keeps its labels.
- **Plans comparison** (`docs-plans-comparison.ts`, `/docs/vendors/plans` only; its two cards are `shared/plan-cta/plan-cards.ts`, shared with `/vendor-program`): the one place the docs use cards. Placed by a `<!-- docs:plans-comparison -->` widget line and rendered outside `.aec-prose`. Two plan cards side by side from `sm` (stacked below), each the shared card (`aecCard`, ruling A7, 2026-11-16): `--surface-base` fill, 24px radius, `--shadow-card` and a transparent 1px edge. Managed carries the 2px `--accent-primary` featured border. The border is not its only mark: the card is named "Managed" and carries the price. Each card: a 20px serif plan name, a one-line summary, a serif price at `--t-36` (28 to 36px) with a `text-sm` unit, one action (Free: a hairline-ringed "Claim your listing" button at the Managed button's size; Managed: the Forest-filled "Claim your listing", the surface's one accent action; both actions open the claim dialog, AECI-1293. E8.1 removed the inquiry line and reply promise, and E8.2 retired "Start with Managed", both 2026-10-19), then a benefit list ("Includes" / "Everything in Free, plus"). Under the cards, the sort rule on a Bone (`--accent-warm`) band. Then a three-column comparison table: hairline row rules, uppercase `text-xs` group rows (`th scope="colgroup"`), a Forest check or a `--text-tertiary` dash per cell with a visually hidden "Included" / "Not included", or a printed value (price, billing). Labels say what the vendor gets, not the portal control's name. Unshipped work never appears in it. Classes are unlayered `.aec-plan-*` in `styles.css`. Mobbin anchor: Mintlify's billing page, screen `c74405c6-a307-4e35-b8eb-21a1129ce5f3`.
- **Plans calls to action** (`shared/plan-cta/plan-cta.ts`, on `/docs/vendors/plans` and `/vendor-program`, AECI-1285, reworded by AECI-1293). One claim workflow (`VENDOR_PROGRAM_PAGE_SPEC.md` §15): every main control opens the claim dialog and reads "Claim your listing", the Managed card, the rail and the phone bar included (E8.2 retired "Start with Managed", 2026-10-19). Never "Get Managed", "Buy" or "Start now": public pages still lead with the claim, and paying appears only on the portal's Billing tab ("Pay by card", AECI-1396). Each control stays a link to `/search` for no-JS and new-tab clicks (`/vendor-program#find-your-product` until that section was removed on 2026-10-12). Claim is the only plan action. Questions go through a text line, "Questions?" plus an underlined "Contact us" link (`aec-plan-contact-link`), a plain link to `/contact` with no topic and no form (E8.1, 2026-10-19; it replaced the plan inquiry dialog). It sits in the closing panel and, on `/vendor-program`, under the FAQ. It is not on the hero. Placements: (1) a row under the intro, the Forest "Claim your listing" at the one button height (44px) on its own; (2) the Managed card's button, full card width at 44px, and the Free card's button at the same height as a secondary button with its hairline edge instead of the Forest fill. Clash 20 retires the `py-3 text-base` and `py-3.5 text-base font-semibold` sizes. From `sm` each card is a row subgrid of the pair, so the two buttons sit on one line although the Managed price row is taller; (3) on the docs Plans page only, from 90rem, a `w-44` `.aec-plan-card` under the rail's dashes (plan, price, a "Claim your listing" button at 44px), sticky with them; (4) below `lg`, a slim bar fixed to the bottom on `--surface-base` behind a hairline, shown only after the intro row scrolls away and hidden while a full call to action is on screen, with `scroll-padding-bottom` and body padding reserved so it never covers a focused link or the page end; (5) the "Getting Managed" panel, the page's closing block: the Forest feature card, a 24px serif headline in Bone (`--text-on-brand`), one terms-and-safety line (the monthly and the annual price), an on-brand "Claim your listing" button (Bone fill, Forest text, clash 3) beside the contact line in Bone, and two numbered next steps that describe the claim dialog (find your listing and send your claim, then a person reviews it and emails the result), shown only while the button opens the dialog. _(AECI-1802: the "Getting Managed" panel is `aec-plan-cta-panel` (`shared/plan-cta/plan-cta.ts`), one shared component. It is the Forest feature card clash 1 allows (§2), a card inside the content column that closes the page, not a full-width band: the shared card frame (`aec-card`, `rounded-card`, `shadow-card`), Forest fill, Bone ink for the heading, terms, steps and contact line, and the Bone main button (`variant="on-brand"`, clash 3). 32px padding, 48px from `lg` when split. Its contact link takes the Bone 3px focus ring. The words, prices, buttons, steps and their order are unchanged. The same card renders on `/docs/vendors/plans`.)_ On `/vendor-program` the panel is its own section (an `h2`). A signed-in vendor seat sees "Go to your portal" wherever a control would open the claim dialog. The docs Plans page has a muted intro line above the hero button, linking to `/vendor-program` with an always-underlined Forest link. Every click fires `plan_cta_clicked` with its placement and page (`docs/ANALYTICS.md` §4).
- **Vendor program page** (`vendor-program/vendor-program.ts`, `/vendor-program`, AECI-1284 to 1286, `VENDOR_PROGRAM_PAGE_SPEC.md` §3). A plain editorial landing page, not a SaaS hero. Mobbin anchor: **Mintlify** (marketing home, screen `4cc8ee93-cf4e-4b9f-a905-8e11b2b9da9e`, for the hero: a left-aligned serif headline, one lede, a filled and an outlined button side by side; the plan cards already follow its billing page). **The rule:** No narrow column with a blank right half: fill desktop width by layout. (Chris, 2026-10-12.) From `lg` every section uses the whole 1328px page wrap (`BAND_WRAP_CLASS`, AECI-1802), by split, grid or side by side, never by stretching prose; there is no rail column (the rail card stays on the docs Plans page only). Layout (AECI-1802, the content-page language of `/about`): the hero on `aec-bone-band`, the page's one Bone band, a 12-column grid from `lg`: the `h1` at the Headline role (the global h1 size), the lede (`max-w-[52ch]`, `--t-20`) and the button in seven columns, no eyebrow (`impeccable detect` bans a kicker above the `h1`), and "How claiming works" in five, the shared card (`aecCard`) holding three Forest-disc numbered steps (a `p` label, not a heading; hidden below `lg`). Then one white band on the page wrap with `h2` sections, their headings at the Section role (`--t-36`) on an inner span, most split 5:7 (the home argument band's split): the heading left, the content right. Section patterns: "What changes when you claim" (second, after the hero) splits five and seven, with two shared cards (`aecCard`) side by side from `sm`, each an overline ("Before you claim", "Once you claim and update it"), the listing's real record line drawn through `aec-maintenance-marker`, the same plain-text line that opens `aec-listing-provenance` (a header chip until AECI-1666) and one line; "What a free claim gets you" and "What Managed adds" are one pattern (Chris, 2026-10-12): each splits four and eight, the heading and lede on the left (Free's seat line, Managed's terms line), and on the right a hairline-ruled two-column list from `md` of the shared item `li[aec-vendor-program-benefit]` (`vendor-program/vendor-program-benefit.ts`). An item is a plain semibold title, never a link, then the body in `--text-secondary`, then, when the line has a docs page, a standalone semibold Forest "Learn more" link with the new-tab cue, whose sr-only suffix names the title. No subgroup headings in either section; "How listings are sorted" is the shared white card, split 5:7 (it left its Bone panel in AECI-1802: one Bone band per page, and the closing panel is the page's one Forest feature card); the "Coming soon" section and its "Not available yet" pill were removed on 2026-10-13 (the page shows only what ships); the FAQ is native `<details>` rows between hairlines, a chevron icon that turns on open (`motion-reduce` off), so every answer is in the SSR HTML. Find your product, the search section that sat third, was removed on 2026-10-12: the claim dialog starts with the same search. The closing Forest feature card splits six and six from `lg` with its actions stacked, then seven and five with the actions in a row from `xl` (its `split` input). Prose measures stay at `52ch` (`text-lg`) and `56ch` (`text-base`) on phones; inside a split's seven columns a paragraph fills its column, which is about 80 characters at 1280px. Atkinson's `ch` is a wide digit, so `65ch` measured about 87 characters a line. The plan cards carry `data-plans-cta="card"` here, so the phone bar also hides over them. Every docs link, including each "Learn more", opens a new tab with `aec-new-tab-icon` (the page-scoped exception in the Link Treatment Rule); inside a sentence the in-text link class keeps the icon inline (`[&_svg]:inline`).
- **Claim dialog** (`shared/claim-dialog/`, AECI-1293, `VENDOR_PROGRAM_PAGE_SPEC.md` §15): one per page (`aec-claim-dialog-host`), `@defer`-loaded on the first claim click, so the cached HTML carries none of it. The one exception is the listing request page (AECI-1474, §2.5): it embeds the flow inline (`ClaimDialogFlow` with `embedded`), not the dialog, so its SSR HTML carries the empty typed-in You step. Mobbin anchor: Mercor's "Complete your profile" modal (screen `a39d82f4-c61a-44cb-be88-f81eec9320a8`): the title and "Step N of 4 · name" with a thin Forest progress bar in the header, a hairline under it, the step body scrolling, a hairline footer with a bordered "Back" left and the Forest primary right. The shell is a BrnDialog over the CDK overlay on `--surface-base`, no shadow, after the home feedback dialog. Type: a 24px serif "Claim your company", which becomes "Claim <Company>" once a found listing names it (AECI-1364), a `text-sm` line ("Claiming is free. We set up your account when we approve your claim."), a 44px close button. From `sm` a centred `w-[min(92vw,36rem)]` panel, `rounded-lg`, hairline border, scrolling inside at `85vh`; below `sm` a full-screen sheet (`100dvh`, no radius) so the footer stays above a phone keyboard. Each step opens on a 20px serif `h3` that takes focus. Step 1 is the site's search box with a visible label and an in-text "Type it in" button; step 2 shows the chosen listing on a `--surface-raised` strip with "Change", or the name and website fields, then the claim form's identity fields (`requests/claimant-fields.ts`); step 3 is three full-width radio rows (`min-h-11`, the checked row on `--accent-primary-soft`); step 4 the "Additional comments" textarea. The receipt reuses the claim confirmation (a Forest check disc, "What happens next" on `--surface-raised`) and its one action is "Done". Errors are `role="alert"` lines under their field, and Continue focuses the first bad field.
- **"How did you find us?" field** (`<aec-found-via-field>`, `shared/found-via/found-via-field.ts`, AECI-1063, `docs/ANALYTICS.md` §11b). One optional question on the claim dialog's comments step, the claim page and drawer, the mailing band, `/updates`, the feedback form and, since AECI-2208, the About you form on `/welcome` and `/account`, as its last question. There it runs with `[liveRegion]="false"` and relays its follow-up announcement to the page's one live region. Mobbin anchor: Grain's "How did you hear about Grain?" select, whose list names ChatGPT beside search and colleagues (screen `ec616781-d530-45c0-ac6b-1ec13cb7bd31`), with the conditional box from User Interviews' "Other" row (screen `47fb20dd-38db-4ec9-9783-17a47e3186e6`). The choice is the stacked `aec-select` with the "Clear answer" row once answered, like the "About you" questions. A follow-up text box (200 characters) appears under it for AI tool, search, vendor, event and other, and its label follows the choice. The field owns one polite `sr-only` live region that says when the box appears. Moving to a choice without a follow-up empties the box. Never required, never blocks a submit. Hosts bind it through `value`/`valueChange` and `detail`/`detailChange` (ADR 0010), not `[formField]`.
- **Listing request page** (`vendor-program/list-your-product.ts`, `/vendor-program/list-your-product`, AECI-1474, `VENDOR_PROGRAM_PAGE_SPEC.md` §2.5). The claim dialog's form out of its dialog, so the dialog's Mobbin anchor carries over. A vendor comes here to fill the form, so the form starts on the first screen (AECI-1720 layout pass: AECI-1802's Bone hero had pushed it from 128px to 494px at 1440; it is at 124px now, at 1440 and at 1180). There is no Bone band. One white band on the 1328px page wrap, 32px above (48px from `md`), split 5:7 from `lg`: on the left the `h1` (the Headline role) and one `max-w-[52ch]` lede at `--t-20`, then, sticky at `top-8` once they have scrolled away, an `h2` "What happens next" at the Section role on an inner span over a four-item `<ol>` in the `/vendor-program` "How claiming works" style: a 28px `--accent-primary` number disc, `aria-hidden`, beside a semibold title and a `--text-secondary` line, `space-y-5`. On the right the form on the shared form card (`aecCard phoneFlush`: 24px radius, `shadow-card`, frameless and gutter-wide below `sm`). The list keeps the left column from reading blank beside the tall form. The card holds a 24px serif `h2` "Request a listing" over the flow's progress bar, `h3` steps and hairline footer. Opened on the typed-in step, the indicator reads "Step 1 of 2" on You and "Step 2 of 2" on Comments. Find, reached by Back, shows none. Embedded, the flow drops its inner scroll and the receipt has no "Done". Focus starts in "Product or company name" without scrolling. Below `lg` they stack: the heading, the list, then the form.
- **Previous / next pager** at the article foot (`nav` named "Previous and next articles"): a hairline, then two text links, "Previous" left and "Next" right, each a muted label over the neighbour's title in Forest. No cards. It walks one section in order and never crosses into the next section.
- **Docs home** (`/docs`, `docs-home.ts`): widens into the shell's content column (`max-w-6xl`, AECI-987). The header keeps 42rem and summaries `max-w-[36rem]`; from `lg` the audience split and "All sections" run two columns, each row carrying its own hairline. It lists General sections only, never the vendor guide (AECI-1265). Vendors reach their guide, claiming included, through the guide toggle (AECI-1271). The `h1` "Help center" and a one-line intro, then the **audience split** as a divided list: readers and reviewers. Each audience is an Atkinson 600 subhead (`.aec-docs-subhead`), a one-line summary and its section links. An audience with no section yet drops out. Below it, "All sections" under an overline: a divided list of each section's linked name, summary and page links. Every link is a 44px target (`inline-flex min-h-11`, AECI-1803), so the page links stack with no extra gap.
- **Section index** (`/docs/<section>`, `docs-section.ts`): widens like the docs home (AECI-987). The header and the intro prose keep 42rem; from `lg` the page list runs two columns. The section label as `h1`, its summary as the lede, then a divided list of the section's pages, each a linked title (a 44px target, AECI-1803) with its one-line description. A section intro, when declared, renders as `.aec-prose`: its lead above the list, its headed parts below with bound heading ids. The vendor guide's landing (`/docs/vendors`) uses it to say who the guide is for, point readers and reviewers back to General, and carry its "Current limits" note.
- **Reading order:** top bar, sidebar, then the page. On an article: the article, the rail, then the pager.
- No screenshots in content at v0 (`STAGE_2_PRODUCT_DOCS_SPEC.md` §4). Light only. Token-only. The docs chrome takes no shadow of its own. The search popup takes the menu shadow, named above. Chrome is i18n-wrapped, and the Markdown body is content. The docs classes live unlayered in `styles.css` after `.aec-prose`, for the reasons the Unlayered-Heading Rule and `.aec-nav-tab` record.

### Dialogs

**The Title-First Dialog Rule** (AECI-1861, ruled 2026-11-20). **Every confirmation dialog opens with focus on its title, so a screen reader reads what it asks before any button.** Spartan's default focuses the first button or field. In the AECI-1687 VoiceOver run the Delete account dialog then read only "Cancel", and the title and the warning were never spoken. So each dialog is a `role="alertdialog"` with `autoFocus: 'first-heading'`. Its `h2` carries `brnDialogTitle`, `tabindex="-1"` and the focus ring, and BrnDialog wires `aria-labelledby` to it. The text that says what the choice does carries `brnDialogDescription`, so `aria-describedby` reads it next. The visitor then tabs to the first button or field. Escape and Cancel still return focus to the trigger. The rule covers the short form dialogs in the admin console and the vendor portal too: Invite a colleague, Add subscriptions and the two logo editors open on their title, not their first field. Today it holds on every dialog in the admin console and the vendor portal. The Delete account dialog (`account/account.html`) gets it with AECI-1687. The review form's sign-in prompt, the home feedback dialog, the request drawer and the claim dialog wait on a ruling (AECI-1995). The claim dialog already opens each step on its `h3`. Open a dialog from a click handler, never from an `effect()` (NG0602). Pinned by `expectTitleFirstDialog` (`core/testing/title-first-dialog.harness.ts`) in each dialog's component spec.

### Row actions menu (AECI-2213)

**One icon button per table row opens that row's actions, so the row stays a row.** The component is `RowActionsMenu` (`apps/web/src/app/vendor/shared/row-actions-menu.ts`). It takes a list of `{ id, label, destructive? }` and emits the picked id. Two menus use it: the Billing tab's per-subscription menu (AECI-1391) and the Sync Service owner's data flows table (`STAGE_2_VENDOR_PORTAL_SPEC.md` §6.15.3). A new per-row menu in the portal reuses it.

- **Trigger.** A ghost icon button (`more-vertical`), labelled "Actions for {thing}", with `aria-haspopup` and `aria-expanded`. It keeps a sunken fill while open. It sits at the end of the row, in a column headed "Actions".
- **Menu.** A `BrnPopover` (CDK overlay, so a scrolling table does not clip it) holding a labelled list of plain buttons, the account menu's item recipe (`layout/user-menu.ts`). No `role="menu"`, no roving tabindex. Tab moves between items inside a focus trap. Escape and an outside click close it. Focus goes back to the trigger.
- **Not Angular Aria, not the CDK menu.** Either one pins a shared module into the initial bundle and breaks the 1.05 MB budget (ADR 0010, amendment 2026-10-16).
- **Items.** Ordered by use. A destructive item goes last and reads in `--status-error`. An item that can only fail is left out, not disabled.
- **Picking.** The trigger takes focus and the menu closes before the host acts, so a dialog opened from the pick hands focus back to the trigger. A destructive pick opens a Title-First confirmation dialog ("Yes, remove it" / "Keep it").
- **Anchor reference.** In-app: the Billing tab's menu and the header account menu. Mobbin was not available when this was built (it asked for a paid plan), so no Mobbin screen is recorded (comment on AECI-2213).
- **Same-Behavior exception (ruled 2026-11-26, AECI-2213 ruling 4).** On the owner's data flows table this menu replaces the Yes and No toggles. The endpoint vendor's "Data that's shared" (§6.17.4) keeps Yes and No, because there a No is a dispute sent with a reason. The two tables therefore differ on purpose.

### Inputs / Fields

Native inputs driven by Signal Forms today (ADR 0009); richer controls use Angular Aria per the provider note above (ADR 0010, Accepted) — `select`/`radio` are realised via combobox/listbox (Aria@22 ships neither), and these discrete-choice controls bridge into Signal Forms via `[(value)]`+`(valueChange)`, not `[formField]`. Styling binds to tokens. Clash 23 (NEW) rewrites the look. The floor stays: every form control has a visible edge at 3:1 (WCAG 1.4.11). AECI-1764 builds it for `FIELD_INPUT_CLASS`, `.aec-field-control` and `aec-select`.

- **Style (clash 23, built by AECI-1764):** 44px high (`min-height: 2.75rem`), `rounded-control` (12px), a 1px solid **field edge** (`--border-field`, §2), a `surface-base` fill, no shadow, and 16px of side padding (`px-4`). Body typography role at 16px (`text-t-16`). The edge measures 3.71:1 on white, 3.55:1 on raised, 3.37:1 on sunken and 3.31:1 on Bone (§2). One recipe covers a text input, a textarea and the `aec-select` trigger: `FIELD_INPUT_CLASS` (`shared/form-field/form-field-classes.ts`) and the trigger carry the `aec-field-control` class. That class is unlayered in `styles.css` and sets the edge colour, the radius, the height floor and the focus and error edges, so a hand-written copy of the class moves with it. Do not reach for `border-(--token)` utilities on a control: the unlayered `.aec-field-control` class beats every utility, so they do nothing. A leading icon moves the text start to 48px (mock-up 6, lines 251 and 254). The leading icon is not built yet.
- **The fill is always `surface-base`.** The mock-up's pre-ruling look was borderless with a shadow, and it is dropped because the edge is what carries the 3:1.
- **A control on Forest or on the hero panel** takes a `surface-base` fill. Field edge on the hero panel is only 2.77:1 (§2). A white fill on Forest is 12.34:1, so the boundary holds. A translucent Bone wash is never a field's only boundary. It measures about 1.29:1 on Forest. The header search belongs to AECI-1744.
- **Placeholders** are `text-tertiary`, which is 4.83:1 on white. They sit on the field's white fill, never on Bone or sunken.
- **Focus (built):** the edge shifts to Forest (`.aec-field-control:focus-visible`), paired with the 3px outline at a 3px offset (mock-up 6, line 29). The outline survives forced-colours mode. The mock-up's `.field:focus` sets `outline: none` and draws a 4px halo from `color-mix()` (line 253). Both are dropped, because `color-mix()` is banned in components (clash 6) and a box-shadow halo vanishes in forced colours. No halo, no underline animation. On a Forest ground, the hero panel and the footer ground the ring is Bone, set by AECI-1747. A Forest ring on the hero panel is 1.20:1 and must not be used.
- **Error (clash 23):** the edge thickens to 2px in `status-error` (`#B3261E`, 6.54:1 on white, see §2 → Status). The mock-up draws 1px plus a 1px ring, which is 2px (line 465). An icon and an inline message come with it. The message (`FIELD_ERROR_CLASS`) is 14px at weight 600 in `status-error`, with the `alert-circle` glyph 8px before it (line 463). The glyph is `.aec-field-error::before`, a CSS mask of the registry drawing, so every form shows it with no template change. A `forced-colors` rule paints it `CanvasText`, because forced colours would otherwise erase a background-painted mask. Turning a field invalid does not move its box: the invalid rule takes the extra 1px of edge out of the padding. A failing field is `aria-invalid="true"` with the message id in `aria-describedby`, and the message is `role="alert"`. Colour is never the sole error signal. The mock-up's Clay-deep edge on a Clay-soft fill is dropped, because §2 reserves red for errors and Clay deep for warnings. The fill stays `surface-base`. The red edge is `.aec-field-control[aria-invalid='true']`, so the attribute that tells a screen reader is the one that paints the border. Set `aria-invalid="true"` (or `AecSelect`'s `invalid` input) and the border follows.
  - **Every field error uses `FIELD_ERROR_CLASS`** (AECI-1984, the Same-Behavior Rule). No template carries a hand copy of the error line.
  - **Choice card:** `.aec-choice-card:has([aria-invalid='true'])` turns the card's border `--status-error`. The `/auth/terms` tick box is the first case.
  - **Bare checkbox or radio:** a native box cannot take a border colour, so `.aec-choice-input[aria-invalid='true']` (`CHOICE_INPUT_CLASS`) draws a 2px `--status-error` outline instead. The Forest focus ring replaces it while the box has keyboard focus. Inside a choice card the box draws no outline, because the card's edge already turns red.
  - A form-level failure ("Something went wrong") is not a field error and keeps its own plain style.
- **Segmented control:** a 44px frame (3px padding, 1px `border-strong`, 12px radius) around 36px segments with an 8px radius and 14px text at 600. The pressed segment is solid Forest with white text (mock-up 6, lines 255 to 258, clash 5). It also takes a tick. The mock-up does not draw that tick. The tick rule and the forced-colours mapping are in Badges, "A selected filter chip".
- **Form footer** (`FORM_FOOTER_CLASS`): a hairline, then the secondary action at the start and the primary at the end. Below `sm` the two stack full width with the primary on top (`flex-col-reverse`). The DOM keeps secondary then primary, so the keyboard order matches the row from `sm` up. The secondary takes `sm:me-auto`. A bare `me-auto` would stop it stretching in the stacked column. First used by `/welcome` (AECI-1296).
- **Submit stays enabled.** A form's primary action is disabled only while the request is in flight. Pressing it with a bad answer shows every error and moves focus to the first bad field. A disabled submit gives no reason, and it also swallows Enter in the form's text fields. `/welcome` and the `/account` "About you" save follow this (AECI-1296), as the claim dialog's Continue already did. Sign-in, the review form and the `/account` display-name save follow it too (AECI-831). The claim and correction forms follow it since AECI-1807, on the routed page and in the drawer. A claim's submit still waits for the Vendor Terms box, as the claim dialog's Continue does. Their submit is also disabled until hydration, as sign-in's is. That gate came first: the SSR form then had no method, so an early press would GET the page with every answer in the query string, and on sign-in would drop `?next=`. Both forms are now `method="dialog"` as well (the next rule), so the native submit does nothing and the gate is the second layer. On the review form the four required controls carry `aria-required="true"`.
- **Forms are inert before hydration (AECI-1919).** Every `<form>` declares its `method`. A form the component submits takes `method="dialog"`: outside a `<dialog>` its native submit does nothing, so a press or Enter on the server-rendered HTML can never GET the page with the answers in the address. The `submit` event still fires, so after hydration the handler runs and "Submit stays enabled" holds. It needs no script, so the CSP and the cached HTML are unchanged. A press made before hydration is replayed into the handler once the app is stable, and it is dropped: the form takes `(aecSubmit)` (`shared/form-field/form-submit.ts`), which ignores a replay, so the visitor presses again on the live page. Browsers older than Safari 15.4 and Firefox 98 do not know `dialog` and treat it as `get`, so on them an early press still reloads the page with the answers in the address. The PostHog URL cleaner strips those `ng.form*` parameters. The search forms built to work without scripts (the header search, `/search`, the pair finders) keep `method="get" action="/search"`: they put only the query in the address, by design. The lint guard `form-declares-method` (`apps/web/scripts/check-source-constraints.mjs`) fails any form that is not `method="dialog"` or `method="get" action="/search"`, and `e2e/forms-before-hydration.spec.ts` checks one route per form component with JavaScript off. A new form component is one line in that spec.
- **What a visitor typed before hydration is kept; consent boxes wait (AECI-1720).** A server-rendered field can be filled before the scripts load. Hydration writes each `[formField]` control's starting value over it, and the Signal Forms listener is added in code, so event replay never hands it the early events. The answers were wiped. An inline recorder in `src/index.html` keeps the last value of each text field, select and radio group, from `input` and `change` events the browser dispatched, whether the visitor, an extension or a script's `click()` caused them. So it holds nothing the same action after hydration would not have put in the form. `AecEarlyInput` (`shared/form-field/early-input.ts`) sits on every native `[formField]` control except checkboxes and puts that value back in the same frame hydration writes the field, so nothing flickers. Once the app is stable the recorder stops: its listeners and values go and its global is deleted. No checkbox is ever carried. A consent box carries `aecConsent` (`shared/form-field/consent-box.ts`) and is `disabled` in the server HTML until the app is live, in the browser's own disabled look, so a visitor cannot tick it early and watch it untick. Its label and links stay readable and clickable. `[aecConsent]="ready"` also waits for the page: `/auth/terms` keeps its box disabled until the summary of changes has loaded or the read has failed. Every component that imports `FormField` imports `AecEarlyInput`, and every checkbox carries `aecConsent` unless it is on that spec's short list of non-consent boxes (filters, row selections, taxonomy choices) (`early-input-coverage.spec.ts`). The recorder and the attribute are static, so the cached HTML stays the same for every visitor. `e2e/forms-early-input.spec.ts` holds the entry bundle, fills and ticks, and checks the hydrated form.
- **Not on the recipe yet.** These controls measured 1.27:1 (`border-default`) before AECI-1764 too, and this work did not make them worse.
  - The home hero search and the two pair pickers moved onto `.aec-field-control` with AECI-1792, and the `/search` query box with AECI-1796, so AECI-1850 is covered for all four. The hero search draws its leading glyph from the home hero, not from the field, so the shared leading icon is still not built.
  - Hand-rolled portal and admin inputs, such as the admin date and text filters: the portal and admin page work, after Chris verifies the public pages. Until then a 44px recipe control can sit beside a 34px hand-rolled input. The company and product Profile forms, the logo dialog and the "How teams use it" point lists moved onto the recipe with AECI-1810.
- **Disabled:** background fades to `surface-sunken`, text-secondary text (`FIELD_INPUT_CLASS` and the `aec-select` trigger, which keys it off `aria-disabled`). Pointer events disabled. **Never via opacity** (AECI-982): `disabled:opacity-50` on the Logo URL field took `#0A0A0A` over white to ~3.7:1 — below the AA 4.5:1 floor — and no token review can pin an opacity's ratio because it composites against whatever sits behind the element. The token pair is measurable: text-secondary on surface-sunken is 7.0:1. Axe cannot catch this class of defect — it exempts disabled controls from color-contrast — so field text treatments are regression-tested as class assertions.
- **Label (AECI-1850):** the Label role (§3): 13px (`text-t-13`, line height 1.4) at weight 600, tracking +0.01em, `text-secondary`, in sentence case. `FIELD_LABEL_CLASS` carries it, and `FIELD_LABEL_TYPE_CLASS` carries the same type with no `block` for an inline label. Every public label imports one of them: the shared forms, the home feedback form, the `/account` profile panel, the `aec-select` label and the pair page version select, whose label was an overline. There is no hand copy left in public code, and `src/field-label.guard.spec.ts` fails on the old uppercase recipe written out there. Before AECI-1850 the label was the uppercase 12px bold overline, which the Sentence-Case Rule reserves for overlines. The label text did not change. The change reaches every form that uses the constants, the vendor portal and admin included. The `aec-select` option-group heading inside the listbox is not a field label and keeps its uppercase style. Hand-rolled portal and admin labels that keep their own uppercase string wait for the portal and admin page work. The sign-in email label, the search box's visible label (`search-autocomplete.ts`, used by the home pair finder and the claim dialog) were 14px hand copies until AECI-2003 moved them onto the Label role. The guard also fails on a public `<label>` static class, or a literal `labelClass` binding, that is weight 600 but not 13px. The portal and admin logo upload label (`shared/logo-input`) is allowlisted until the portal and admin page work.
- **Help text:** the Caption role (`--t-14`, §3), `text-secondary`, directly under the control, always rendered. Optional-field marking (`(optional)` on the label) is unchanged — unmarked still means required.
- **Portal and admin labels (AECI-2083).** `src/field-label.guard.spec.ts` holds them at zero with no allowlist since AECI-2132, when the last offender moved to the Label role and `field-label.txt` was deleted. A `<label>` or `<legend>` that sets its own type without the Label role fails. A label for a checkbox or radio does not count: its text is an option, body text, not the field Label role.

**Named rule — URL and read-only field text stays readable in every state (AECI-982).** A field that displays a URL, a stored reference, or any value the reader may need to copy back out keeps WCAG AA contrast in every state it can render: default, hover, focus, read-only, disabled, invalid, browser-autofilled, and long-overflow. Read-only uses the sunken-surface tell with `--text-primary` text (the §8 promise that a downgraded vendor's data is still there and still readable); disabled uses the recipe above; invalid keeps the field's own text tokens and carries the failure in the `--status-error` border, inline label and icon, never by dimming the value. Browser autofill is pinned by the global `:-webkit-autofill` guard in `styles.css`, which repaints the UA's autofill background with `--surface-base` so the declared text token keeps its measured ratio. The shipped case was the shared Logo URL field (`apps/web/src/app/shared/logo-input/logo-input.ts`), whose disabled dim was the AECI-982 production defect. Any field carrying a rule the user can trip — a minimum length, a range, a format, an expected kind of answer — states it in persistent help text under the control, with the constraint sentence itself set in `<strong class="text-(--text-primary)">` so it survives a skim of the surrounding guidance. A requirement that only appears in the error message after the user has already failed is a defect, not a validation strategy: the user writes an answer, gets rejected, and has to reverse-engineer what was wanted. The rule has an a11y half that is not optional: the hint's `id` is in the field's `aria-describedby` **from first render**, and the error `id` is _appended_ to it when the error fires (`'x-hint x-error'`) rather than replacing it — so the requirement is announced on focus and survives the failure. Because the hint now carries the requirement, the error states the _failure_ tersely ("Add at least 20 characters.") instead of restating the instruction — the two render stacked, and a paraphrase of the hint reads as noise. Shipped on every field in the app with a length floor: the claim body and correction body (20 chars, `requests/request-form-body.html`) and the review headline (5–100) and review body (50, `reviews/review-form.html`).

### Badges

> **No verification iconography on public surfaces.** This rule applies through Stage 2
> and later. No checkmark, shield, tick, or "Verified" fill may imply that AECi endorses
> a vendor, product, or integration. Provenance and account state are carried by visible
> text because text can state the exact claim. The legacy `vendors.verified` mirror is a
> paid-entitlement signal. It rendered as a neutral account-status label until AECI-1264
> removed that label (2026-10-09), and it must not come back as a glyph or a badge. This is also why `home-credibility-strip` uses a balance scale rather than
> the shield-check it originally shipped with.

**The public chip (clash 22, BLEND).** Chris ruled BLEND with no note. The blend is the clash recommendation: one public pill chip with an optional leading icon, one dense variant, and no Clay-soft category chip. This rewrites the AECI-841 "one chip spec" (`px-2.5 py-1`, `0.75rem`, `font-medium`, `rounded-mark`, 29px). The AECI-841 lesson stands: **chip metrics travel with the vocabulary, not the component**. A second set of padding is a second vocabulary. AECI-1763 built `aec-chip` (`shared/chip/chip.ts`, recipe `.aec-chip` in `styles.css`) and moved the badge components onto it. The recipe is unlayered CSS keyed off data attributes. It was built while a border-colour utility never applied, before AECI-2088.
`MaintenanceMarker` carried the old chip until AECI-1666 retired the header chip. It is plain text now, the first line of "About this listing".

- **Recipe** (mock-up 6, `.tagchip` line 130 and `.aud` line 223): a pill (`rounded-pill`), `surface-raised` fill, `text-primary`, a 1px `border-default` edge, 13px (`--t-13`) at weight **400**, sentence case. Padding is 4px 12px 4px 8px (`spacing.1`, `spacing.3`, `spacing.2`). The leading icon is 14px with an 8px gap on the default chip and 4px on dense (lines 131 and 303). The chip is about 31px tall: 13px text at the 1.6 line height it inherits from the body, plus 8px of padding and a 2px edge. That clears the 24px target floor. A chip with no icon takes 4px 12px. A chip with a leading icon takes 8px on that side (AECI-1763).
- **A link chip** (taxonomy) hovers to a `border-strong` edge and Forest text (line 225).
- **A selected filter chip** (clash 5) takes `accent-primary-soft` fill, Forest text and a 1px Forest edge, with `aria-pressed` (lines 265 and 273). **Selection is never colour alone.** A selected filter chip and a pressed segment each show a tick, as the mock-up draws on the filter chips (lines 268 and 269, and `.cat .on` at lines 274 and 275). The mock-up's pressed segment has no tick, so it needs one. The tick marks selection, not verification, so the no-verification-icon rule at the top of this subsection does not apply. In forced-colours mode a pressed or selected state maps to `Highlight` fill and `HighlightText` text, because the wash and the Forest fill both vanish there.
- **A filter chip is a button** (`<aec-chip [selected]>`, a `<button aria-pressed>`; AECI-1763 built the recipe and no public page uses it yet). It is a pill at `min-height: 44px`, 14px at weight 600, with a 1px `border-strong` edge, and may carry a count (lines 260 and 270). It is a chip and not a primary action, so the pill is allowed. The mock-up sets the count inside at 12px. §3 allows `--t-12` only for overlines, the two weight-500 chips and field captions, so the count sits at `--t-13`.
- **The dense variant** is for a chip inside a card or a table row. The mock-up's `.mini` (line 303) is a pill with a `surface-sunken` fill, no visible edge, 13px at weight 600 and a 4px icon gap. The old dense badge (`px-2.5 py-0.5`, `text-xs`, `font-bold`) retires, because weight 700 leaves chips (clash 9) and 12px is not a chip size. Settled by AECI-1763: dense has the public chip's 12px horizontal padding and 2px vertical padding, with a line height of 1.4, so it is 24px tall and a row stays on one line. **Ruled by the orchestrator, for Chris to confirm:** dense chips in index rows use the `neutral` tone (raised fill with an edge), because the sunken fill equals the row-hover fill and would vanish on hover. The mock-up's "sunken, no edge" dense look (a dense chip with no tone) renders nowhere now. The recipe keeps it for a dense chip on a non-hover ground, so it is untested in the product. The mechanism, depth and object-coverage chips in index rows and cards are dense and neutral. Measured: default 30.8px tall, dense 24.2px, the 12px label face 29.2px.
- **Weights:** 400 on the public chip, 600 on the dense chip and the filter chip. **Only the maintenance marker and the agreement badge keep weight 500.** Since AECI-1666 the marker is plain text, so only the agreement badge takes this chip face. Both are 12px at 500 with tracking +0.01em and 4px 10px padding (mock-up 6, lines 503 and 504). They take the chip shape. The mock-up copies the live site and draws them at a 4px radius, which is not on the ruled scale and is not a pill. AECI-1763 confirmed the pill. Both use the `label` face of `aec-chip` (an input no other chip sets).
- **Scope (AECI-1763, AECI-1729).** The chip recipe covers public pages, shared components, the portal and the console. The portal and admin slices moved every hand-rolled pill onto `aec-chip`, and the cleanup slice deleted the old unlayered portal pill rules from `styles.css`.
- **Never a Clay-soft chip.** The mock-up's Clay-soft category chip (`.mini.ct`) is dropped (clashes 4 and 22).
- **Portal badges.** The vendor plan badge and the review status badge sit in the portal. They are components, so they render `aec-chip` (dense) and are pills everywhere they appear (AECI-1763). Since the AECI-1729 slices the portal page bodies and the console render `aec-chip` too.
- **Chips are sentence case**, like everything else. The Sentence-Case Rule's single exception is the overline role, and a chip is not an overline. The product-detail "Not yet rated" chip was uppercase at `0.08em` tracking, an overline in a chip's clothing. Fixed in AECI-841.

What actually renders today:

- **Maintenance marker** (`shared/maintenance-marker`). **Retired as a chip by AECI-1666
  (2026-11-17).** Its wording now opens the "About this listing" panel as plain text, the record
  line (see "Listing provenance panel" below). No page header carries a chip any more. An
  unclaimed listing always reads the AECi form. The history below records the chip as it shipped,
  and its wording rules still govern the record line. Neutral chip — `border-default` /
  `surface-raised` / `text-secondary`, **no dot**, no icon. (It shipped with a 6px decorative
  `text-tertiary` dot; AECI-841 removed it. On a chip with exactly one state a leading dot reads as
  a status light, which is precisely the trust signal this component exists **not** to give — the
  same reason there is no checkmark on it. The agreement badge's dots stay, because there the dot
  is the only thing separating `unverified` from `single_source`.) On product detail, vendor
  detail, and the pair page. It is a **label, not a sentence, so it carries no terminal
  period**, and the date clause is joined with a middot. Four readings, all **live** since
  AECI-616, reworded by AECI-1142. Ruling 2026-09-28 (Chris): the maintenance chip uses exactly two labels everywhere, "AEC Integrations maintained" and "Vendor maintained". The four readings are
  `AEC Integrations maintained` · `AEC Integrations maintained · Reviewed <date>` · `Vendor
maintained` · `Vendor maintained · Updated <date>`. The company is never in the visible label.
  Clash 40 (CURRENT) confirms this. The mock-up's "Maintained by AEC Integrations" pill with a Clay pencil pip is dropped, and so is any icon. Ruling R3 decision 12 (AECI-1734, 2026-11-14, Chris "A"): the "Reviewed" date returns on the chip.
  Where the page can prove who wrote (the vendor page, and the product page's vendor), an sr-only
  suffix names it for screen readers: "Vendor maintained: Procore Technologies keeps this up to
  date". The pair page cannot prove it, because any endpoint vendor or the owner can flip a pair
  row, so it passes no name. The pair page also passes no date: each card's At a glance strip
  states its own "Last checked". The date renders only
  when `last_reviewed_at` is set, and it is `null` on almost every record because **nothing
  was backfilled** — bare attribution is the honest default, not missing data. Never wire the
  date to `updated_at`: it is `$onUpdate` and promote restamps it, so the date would refresh
  itself on every bulk re-promote (60 production products share one `updated_at` day). The
  vendor branch is driven by real vendor work: an attestation, or — since AECI-981 — any
  vendor-authorized catalog save in the portal (a profile edit, a product edit, a version
  write). A save both flips the branch and supplies the date, which is why the vendor
  wording reads `Updated` where the AECi wording reads `Reviewed`: they are the same stored
  column describing two different acts, and swapping the verbs would misattribute one party's
  work to the other. Dates are formatted in **UTC**, not the
  ambient zone — SSR runs UTC and the browser does not, so a zone-local format would trip a
  hydration mismatch either side of midnight.
  - It **coexists** with the agreement pill below rather than replacing it, deliberately. Since
    AECI-1666 the marker's wording is the panel's record line, not a header chip. The
    marker was page-header attribution ("who is on the hook for this page"), the pill is
    per-claim state on the mechanism cards ("do the two vendors agree about this one data
    object"). Two distinct signals share the pair page — marker and agreement chip — and
    collapsing them would lose information. (The account-status label left this page in
    AECI-1131 and was deleted everywhere in AECI-1264; see below.)
- **Agreement pill** (`products/agreement-badge`): same neutral chip tokens. Renders
  `Listed by AEC Integrations` on most claims (AECI-1142; `Unverified · AECi` before it), the
  honest posture, not a warning. The other three states are in the Agreement badge table below.
- **Pending** (`badge-pending`): surface-sunken fill, text-secondary text, a `border-default` edge at 1px (the `sunken` tone of `aec-chip`). Indicates "submitted, not yet reviewed" — never confused with confirmed.
- **No public label says "Claimed"** (clash 39, CURRENT). The wording is counsel's, and changing it is a legal decision, so none of the redesign's labels may say it. The mock-up's hero pill "Listing claimed by Bluebeam", its facts-card caption "Listing claimed by …" and the pair ledger's "Claimed by {vendor}" against "Not claimed" are dropped. The Unclaimed note stays word for word (§5 "Unclaimed note", ADR 0051).
- **Subscription status chip** (`vendor/components/vendor-billing-licenses.ts`, the vendor Billing tab, AECI-1866). *Since AECI-1812 the chip is the shared dense `aec-chip`: Live `wash`, will-not-renew or ends-soon `strong` with a clock (no Clay on a public chip), ended `quiet` (`LICENSE_CHIP_TONE` in `vendor-billing-styles.ts`). The cleanup slice (AECI-1729) deleted the unlayered status-chip rules the record below describes. The original record:* Three tones, existing tokens only, as unlayered status-chip classes in `styles.css` (built while a border-colour utility never won, before AECI-2088): `--live` is the Forest-soft wash with Forest text and a Forest border (10.80:1), the `confirmed` agreement chip's treatment; `--warning` is Clay deep text (5.59:1 on raised) on `surface-raised` with a `border-strong` edge, for a live subscription that ends inside the 45-day renewal window or will not renew; `--ended` is the neutral chip in `text-secondary`. The words always carry the state ("Live", "Ends soon", "Ended, released"), and a will-not-renew row keeps "Live" beside its "Cancels at term end" badge, so colour is never the only signal. Clay deep is text only, so the Clay-Restriction Rule holds. Ended rows sit in a closed native `<details>` under the live table ("Ended subscriptions (n)"), same columns, the whole row in `text-secondary`.
- **No account or plan label on public pages** (AECI-1264, removed 2026-10-09, marketing review B1). `aec-vendor-account-badge` ("Active on AEC Integrations", AECI-965, relabeled AECI-1131) is deleted. "Active" implied a vendor without it was inactive, and it marked publicly who pays. Do not build a replacement public marker of plan or payment. The `vendors.verified` mirror still drives the claim CTA's copy on the vendor and product pages, which is copy, not a badge. Inside the portal the plan reads as the **Plan badge** above.
- **Agreement badge** (`aec-agreement-badge`, AECI-300 / AECI-605): the per-claim state on the product-pair page's data-flow lanes — whether the two vendors agree that a `data_object` flows between their products. Its agreement-specific wording and tonal ladder keep it distinct from the maintenance marker. Four states, and the tonal ladder between them is the point:

  | State           | Treatment                                                            | Label                         |
  | --------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------- |
  | `unverified`    | `border-default` / `surface-raised` / `text-secondary`, tertiary dot | "Listed by AEC Integrations"  |
  | `single_source` | the **same neutral chip**, `text-secondary` dot                      | "Confirmed by {vendor}"       |
  | `confirmed`     | Forest-soft wash + Forest text + Forest border (10.80:1)             | "Confirmed by both companies" |
  | `conflict`      | `--status-error` text + border on `surface-base`, `✕` glyph          | "Companies disagree"          |

  Labels reworded by AECI-1142 to plain buyer words ("company", not "vendor"; no "Unverified",
  no "AECi" abbreviation). The `unverified` accessible name adds "Neither company has confirmed it
  yet", so "Listed" is never heard as an endorsement.

  Three rules hold this together. **`single_source` shares the neutral chip with `unverified` on purpose** — one vendor affirming while the counterparty stays silent must never borrow the affirmative Forest treatment, so the only difference is a slightly stronger dot; the badge names the vendor and its screen-reader text states the other's silence outright. **`confirmed` is the only badge that earns the wash**, and only for two _distinct_ vendors. **`conflict` is the only red**, and it reports a difference between vendors, not a defect in either product. Colour is never the sole signal (WCAG 1.4.1): every state carries a distinct visible label and accessible name, and the dot/glyph is `aria-hidden`. **The full meaning is real text (AECI-1720 restore C).** It sits in a visually hidden span inside the chip, and the visible label is `aria-hidden`, so a screen reader hears the meaning once. The chip carries no `aria-label`: an `aria-label` on a plain span is not reliably read (records/10 §2, records/11 §8, mock-up 12). The label stays a label only, and the hidden text adds no claim: it is the accessible name the chip already had.

  **The row roll-up (AECI-1781, ruled 2026-11-14, Chris "A", `STAGE_2_ATTESTATIONS_SPEC.md` §4.3).** A row with several flows shows one state. The weakest flow decides. Any conflict makes the row `conflict`. A row is `confirmed` only when every flow is confirmed by both companies. A flow that every voting company denied is left out. A row at `single_source` names no vendor and reads "Confirmed by one company". The attributed "Confirmed by {vendor}" form is for a single claim. The state is a label only. Nothing sorts or filters by it.

  Clash 41 (CURRENT, no note from Chris) confirms the four-state ladder. **Only "Confirmed by both companies" is green.** A one-company confirmation looks neutral. The mock-up drew all three confirmed wordings ("Confirmed by both companies", "Confirmed by Sage" and "Confirmed by Bluebeam") as the same bold Forest text with a link icon, and drew "Listed by AEC Integrations" with an info icon and no chip. That is dropped, and the cards render `<aec-agreement-badge>`. "Confirmed by both companies" is a label only, with no filter and no sort (R1). **No summary count of agreement states ships** (`PLAN.md` §7.7 names "a filter or a summary count" as the placement risk, and confirmation is a Managed-only capability). That rules out an agreement-state count in a group header (`.gs`, mock-up 7, line 1137) and the rail's "Evidence for these N" panel.

- **Version diff markers** (AECI-303, `STAGE_2_ATTESTATIONS_SPEC.md` §9): on the product-pair page's claim rows, what the selected product-version pair _changed_. Deliberately **not badges** — a rule and a label, not a chip:

  | State       | Treatment                                                                                           | Label                  |
  | ----------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------- |
  | `unchanged` | **nothing**                                                                                         | —                      |
  | `added`     | `border-s-2` Forest **start rule**, `+` glyph, `.aec-overline` in `text-secondary`                  | "New in {version}"     |
  | `removed`   | `border-s-2` `border-strong` start rule, `−` glyph, name steps to `text-secondary` + `line-through` | "Removed in {version}" |

  Four rules, each of which rules something else out. **Position carries the separation:** the marker sits at the row's _start_, the agreement badge at its _end_ — left is what changed in this version, right is who agrees about it. Two questions, two zones, no competition. **`added` gets the rule, not a wash:** `--accent-primary-soft` is the `confirmed` chip's, and a wash inches from a neutral chip reads as "confirmed" by proximity; a single vertical mark is unmistakably structural, and doubles as a scannable gutter down the lane (borders-not-shadows, and the same spirit as the Score Display's range marker). **`removed` is never `--status-error`:** `conflict` is the only red, and a second red on the same row would collapse "vendors disagree" into "no longer supported". Clay is excluded on both counts — `added` is not a warning, and the Clay-Restriction Rule caps that hue at ≤5% of a screen, which a per-row marker blows instantly. And **`unchanged` renders nothing**, which is both the overwhelming majority state and what keeps the default latest × latest view byte-identical to a pair page with no version data at all.

  `text-secondary` only, never `text-tertiary`: these rows sit on `surface-base` inside a `surface-raised` lane, and the tertiary token is forbidden on sunken/muted surfaces. Logical `border-s-*` keeps the mark on the correct edge under RTL. Every state carries a glyph **and** a visible text label (WCAG 1.4.1), and `removed` adds a decoration change on top.

  **No Mobbin anchor was picked for this surface, deliberately** — the same call the AECI-605 agreement badge recorded. The pair page has three shipped layers and a settled chip/token vocabulary; the diff markers inherit it rather than importing a second site's visual language onto the page that is most editorial. This is the standing precedent for anchorless surfaces (see the Phase 8.3 operator console: "One publication, one voice").

**Superseded treatment:** the earlier Forest-filled `badge-verified` placeholder must not ship.
Vendor agreement is represented by the explicit `AgreementBadge` labels above, including
"Confirmed by both companies" when two distinct vendors affirm the same claim.

### Field caption

`aec-field-caption` (`shared/field-caption/`, AECI-1614, `STAGE_2_VENDOR_PORTAL_SPEC.md` §4.5.8). One quiet line that attributes a group of fields or marks a correction: "Maintained by {vendor} · updated {date}", "Maintained by {vendor} until {date}", "Corrected by AEC Integrations on {date}" then a source link, or "Removed by AEC Integrations on {date}" (AECI-1628). **Since AECI-1666 it renders only inside the listing provenance panel**, one line per group or correction, each prefixed with its label ("Website: Corrected…", "How teams use it: Maintained by…"). No page mounts it under a group, beside a value or on a table row any more. **It is not a badge, and must never become one:** the field-caption role (`--t-12` with a 20px line height, §3), `--text-secondary`, no fill, no border, no chip, no icon. Clash 40 (CURRENT) confirms the wording "Maintained by {Vendor} · updated {date}", always with the date. Anything heavier reads as an endorsement or an ad. `--text-secondary`, not tertiary, because the panel sits on base and raised surfaces. Dates in UTC, `MMMM d, y`. The source link is the Link Treatment Rule's citation role, with the new-tab cue. Mobbin anchor: **v0**'s extension marketplace listing (screen `bcc629fe-8ac0-4b61-919f-4ddf02953ea4`), whose "Published" and "Last released" facts sit in plain muted text, with no chip or status mark. A vendor's own link on a pair card reads "Link from {vendor}" in the same style, and since AECI-1666 it always shows.

### Listing provenance panel

`aec-listing-provenance` (`shared/listing-provenance/`, AECI-1666, `STAGE_2_VENDOR_PORTAL_SPEC.md` §4.5.8). One "About this listing" panel per product, vendor and pair page. It holds every provenance statement the page makes, so counsel's attribution is on the page once rather than on every block. Content, in order:

1. The record line: "AEC Integrations maintained", or "Vendor maintained · Updated {date}". This is the retired header chip's wording as plain text. An unclaimed listing always reads the AECi form.
2. Each group with a caption: "{Group}: Maintained by {Vendor} · updated {date}", or "… until {date}".
3. Each correction, labelled: "{Field}: Corrected by AEC Integrations on {date} · Source", or "{Field}: Removed by AEC Integrations on {date}". Labels come from `shared/field-caption/correction-labels.ts`. The panel lists only what the page renders. A correction that set a value is listed while that value shows. A removal is listed while the slot it emptied is empty. A field the page never renders gets no line, and neither does a curated pair link whose slot a vendor's own link fills. `correctionsInSlots` in `shared/field-caption/field-caption-facts.ts` applies the rule on every page.
4. A count of values under review on the page. It equals the number of `aec-under-review` markers the page renders, through `countUnderReview` in the same file. On the product page that includes each Integrations row's markers (`countLaneUnderReview`), counted over the unfiltered list.
5. On the product page only: "Each integration's page says who maintains it."

Placement. Product and vendor pages: a sidebar card at the end of the `metadata` slot of `aec-detail-layout`, from `lg`. Below `lg`: a native `<details>`, closed by default, at the end of the main column. It is not the Angular Aria accordion, because that ships an empty panel to crawlers (see Behavior providers above). Pair page: after the mechanism cards at every width, a `<details>` below `lg`. With more than one mechanism, each label carries the mechanism name. The empty pair page has no cards, so both copies end its last band, "Look it up yourself", after the API note (`PairEmptySections` projects them). Every listing shows the panel (ruled 2026-11-17). On a listing AECi maintains alone it holds only the record line "AEC Integrations maintained", plus " · Reviewed {date}" when the record has one. It is a pure function of stored facts, with dates in UTC, so cached HTML stays visitor-neutral. **On the band shell (AECI-1720, ADR 0059)** the product and vendor pages have no sidebar, so both copies sit in the last band of the page body: after Reviews and Similar products on a product page, after Products on a vendor page. The card is `aecCard` with `p-6`. From `lg` its overline heading, with the 16px `info` glyph as in the facts card's group headings, sits left and the lines right, the section row's 5:7 split, so a short panel never leaves the right half blank. The disclosure is the same card with the heading in its `<summary>` and the `chevron-down` glyph, which turns on open and holds still under reduced motion. No new token, size or radius.

Two markers stay inline and are not part of the panel: the Unclaimed and not-affiliated line beside the logo (`aec-unclaimed-note`), and "Under review" in a hidden value's slot (`aec-under-review`). A facet or link section that a removal left empty disappears. The removal is listed in the panel. Mobbin anchor: Stripe's customer detail page, whose "Details" sidebar panel lists record facts as quiet label and value lines, screen `d0203ce6-56d8-4a16-b4b6-08cbf7eb4c68`. The detail pages' existing anchor site still governs the page around it.

### Tags / Taxonomy chips

Chip-style links to category / audience / phase browse pages (the `TaxonomyBadge` component). Distinct from the status badges above — these are navigational, not state indicators. The taxonomy chip is the public chip (Badges: "The public chip", clash 22), so this entry only records what is its own. `aec-taxonomy-badge` renders `aec-chip` with `link` (AECI-1763).

> **The non-link card-grid chip** (`RoleBadge`, see "Role chip" above) is the same public chip without the link hover.

- **Shape:** a pill (`rounded-pill`), no longer `rounded-sm`.
- **Surface:** `surface-raised` fill, a 1px solid `border-default` edge raising to `border-strong` on hover. The edge is 1px (mock-up line 130, settled by AECI-1763). A link chip has a 3px focus ring at 3px offset.
- **Typography:** Atkinson Hyperlegible Next at weight **400**, 13px (`--t-13`). The 500 this entry used to state is gone (clash 9). The mock-up sets 400 (line 130). `text-primary` shifts to `accent-primary` on hover.
- **Case:** sentence case, per the Sentence-Case Rule. **Not met by the taxonomy chips today (AECI-1720 restore C, open).** The rule is followed in source strings: every chip whose words are our copy ("Connector", "Not yet rated", the agreement labels) is written in sentence case. No display transform exists. Category, audience, phase, trade and data-object chips print the vocabulary's stored names, and those are Title Case by design (`docs/DATA_OBJECT_VOCABULARY.md` "names are Title Case"; the taxonomy seed likewise). Lowercasing them in the browser would break proper nouns and acronyms (BIM, RFI, ERP, QA/QC, product names), so it is not done. It waits for a vocabulary decision: sentence-case the stored names, or add a curated display name.

### Score Display

The signature data component for review scores. Source Serif 4 numerals in Forest, no chart-junk decoration, no sparkline behind the number — the score _is_ the visual. Clashes 8 and 19 and ruling R3 rewrite it.

- **Numeric value:** the score figure, `--t-48` (§3). Source Serif 4 at weight 600, a fixed 48px, line-height 1, tracking -0.01em, Forest (`accent-primary`). `aec-figure` sets it with `size="score"`. Mock-up 6 draws the figure at `var(--t-fig)` (line 392). Ruling A3 replaces that.
- **Ruling R3 decision 6 (AECI-1734, 2026-11-14, Chris "A"):** "Score figures move back up." Clash 8 had dropped them to 40px, and the score cards looked half empty (`PLAN.md` §10.3). **Settled by ruling A3 (Chris, 2026-11-16): 48px**, the `--t-48` token. Count figures stay at `--t-fig`. The reviews band applies it (AECI-1784): one `aec-figure size="score"` per score card.
- **Two large figures in the reviews band (ruling A4, Chris, 2026-11-16): yes.** It is the one exception to clash 8's one large figure per band. One figure per score, two scores, never merged, averaged or re-weighted. `aec-figure` (§5 Figure component) is the one numeral for both.
- **Label below:** Atkinson Hyperlegible label scale, text-secondary color. Each score card also prints its name ("Score one", "Score two" or the criterion) beside its stripe (§4 clash 15).
- **Stripe:** a 7px leading stripe, Forest for product quality and Clay deep for onboarding experience (mock-up 6, lines 386 and 387). It is never the only cue (§4).
- **Range marker** (optional, sparse): a vertical mark per score on a 1-10 axis, with no fill, no gradient and no animation. The mock-up's "On the same scale" panel draws a 1px line, 2px pins 28px tall and tick labels at `--t-12` (lines 397 to 408). Two pins on one axis are two scores. Each pin carries its printed label and number, so colour never tells them apart. Clash 19 rebuilds the scale to this spec and drops the gradient gap bar. **Built by AECI-1784** as the third card of the reviews band, on a 1 to 5 axis (the rating scale), with no heading of its own. The pins print the score names, "Product quality" and "Onboarding experience" since AECI-1720 restore C, and the averages. The axis is `aria-hidden`, because the two score cards beside it already print both numbers. The "Based on N reviews" line under it stays readable. The Onboarding pin label is Clay deep, 5.83:1 on white.

### Figure component (clashes 8 and 25)

`aec-figure` (`shared/figure/figure.ts`, AECI-1767): one number at `--t-fig` in Source Serif 600, with a `label`, optional `support` text, an optional `suffix` (the small "/5" of mock-up 6) and projected content (a "Know of one?" link). Presentation only. The caller formats: `text` is the printed string ("4.0", "12,345") and `value` is read only to detect zero. The component never rounds or formats.

- **Tones.** `forest` (default) is Forest on a light ground. `on-brand` is for a Forest ground, where Forest ink would vanish: the numeral is `--text-on-brand` (Bone, 11.03:1 on Forest, 9.18:1 on the hero panel) and the label and support are `--text-on-brand-secondary` (7.96:1 on Forest, 6.62:1 on the hero panel). AECI-1778's hero uses it.
- **The zero state is owned by the component.** `zero="integrations-not-on-record"` and `product` render the heading "We have no integrations for {product} on record" and the caveat "Not on record means we have not found one. It is not proof that none exists." (`PLAN.md` §5.2). The numeral is `aria-hidden`. Projected content follows. A zero with no zero mode, or no product, logs an error in dev mode and renders the caller's `label` and `text` in production.
- **Size.** `size` is `fig` (default, `--t-fig`, 28 to 40px) or `score` (`--t-48`, a fixed 48px, ruling A3). A score figure passes `size="score"`. Every other figure keeps the default. The specimen on `/_demo/spartan` shows both, with the reviews band at 48px.
- The facts card (AECI-1779) and the reviews band (AECI-1784) use it. `IntegrationStat` is unchanged.

### Meter (clash 19)

Clash 19 was ruled BLEND with no note. The blend is the clash recommendation: allow one public bar-meter pattern. The old "multi-series data visualisation is operator console only" stands for charts. A meter is not a chart. AECI-1767 built `aec-meter` (`shared/meter/meter.ts`, with its CSS in the component's `styles` so no page pays for it until it uses the meter). No page uses it yet. The page issues adopt it.

- **One hue.** Forest fill on a `surface-sunken` track. No gradient. The mock-up's ledger meters with gradients are dropped.
- **The number is always printed** beside the bar as text, never only in the graphic. The bar is a second way to read it.
