The track and fill are not carrying the meaning, so the 3:1 graphics floor is met by the number. **Settled (AECI-1767), the plain pattern.** The label and the number are real text in the accessibility tree. The bar is `aria-hidden`. There is no `role="meter"` and no `aria-value*`, because JAWS on Edge and VoiceOver on iOS ignore `role="meter"`, NVDA on Firefox reads only its name and TalkBack gives no value, and the ARIA-AT project has no data for the pattern. Three inputs set the name: `label` (the meter's own visible name), `labelledBy` (an element id for a caller whose own heading names the score: the meter renders no label and becomes a labelled group) and `decorative` (hides the whole meter from assistive tech, for a block that already prints the number, such as the reviews band). A meter with none of them logs an error in dev mode. The component never rounds or computes: the caller passes the value and the printed text. The bar clamps to the range and a value outside it logs an error in dev mode. There is no transition, so nothing animates under reduced motion. Forced colours: a `Canvas` track with a `CanvasText` edge and a `Highlight` fill. Measured: the Forest fill on the `surface-sunken` track is 11.23:1 and Clay deep on it is 5.31:1, both above the 3:1 floor. The specimen is `/_demo/spartan`.
- **Size:** 6px high with a full-pill end. The dimensions come from mock-up 7 only (lines 1129, 1130, 1149 and 1150). The evidence panel that the mock-up's meter sits in is dropped (see Agreement badge). Mock-up 8's category bars are the chart R3 decision 19 cut.
- **Clay deep** appears only when a bar stands for the onboarding score, beside a visible name (clash 4): the meter's own `label`, or the caller's heading through `labelledBy`.
- **The load bar** ("Showing 24 of 58") is the same meter with `layout="stacked"`: the bar over its text, at most 260px wide, with a "Load more" secondary button under it (mock-up 7, lines 1148 to 1152). The listing's "Showing n of m" text needs a polite live region (`aria-live="polite"`) owned by the caller, because the count changes as the list grows. The meter does not own one. AECI-1783 adds it.
- **Admin charts** keep their own rules below. The meter does not touch them.

### Open values (components)

Each value is set by the code issue named. A value the mock-ups set is not listed here, it is in the table above.

- **Ghost and secondary buttons on a Forest ground (no code issue).** AECI-1758 did not build them, see §5 Buttons. The mock-up builds its ghost button with `color-mix()`, which clash 6 bans. AECI-1758 settled the danger colours and the on-brand hover (`--surface-on-brand-hover`, from AECI-1747).
- **The field edge token name (AECI-1747 and AECI-1764).** Settled by AECI-1764. `--border-field` replaced `--border-input` as the control edge. `--border-input` stays for the three non-control uses in §2.
- **The error icon and message wiring (AECI-1764).** Settled. The 2px edge, the message and the icon are built. The icon is a CSS mask of the `alert-circle` drawing (`.aec-field-error::before`), with a `forced-colors` rule that paints it `CanvasText`.
- ~~**Which more surfaces take the card shadow, including the plan cards (AECI-1765).**~~ Settled by ruling A7. The table is in §4 "Shadow Vocabulary".
- ~~**The lead cards in the reviews, extensions and similar-products grids (AECI-1765).**~~ Settled by ruling A8: none. `/products` keeps its lead card (ruling 2 of 2026-11-16, `records/10-directory-notes.md`).
- ~~**The reviews-band exception to clash 8 (AECI-1767).**~~ Settled by ruling A4: two figures, yes.
- ~~**How far above 40px the score figure goes (AECI-1767, with AECI-1748).**~~ Settled by ruling A3: 48px, `--t-48`.
- **Whether the inline and badge weights of the integration stat follow the large zero (AECI-1767).** Kept as shipped: they read "Not yet connected". Asked of Chris on AECI-1767.
- **Which filled button stays on the product page (AECI-1778).** AECI-1768 left "Write a review" off the sticky bar, so the bar adds no filled button (clash 21).
- ~~**The logo stack on the "Show more" button (AECI-1783).**~~ Settled by AECI-1783. It names the first five hidden partners of the group, in the group's row order, as 32px `sm` marks overlapping by 8px. It is `aria-hidden`, because the button's text names the count and the category. An unclaimed product shows its initial tile there, as everywhere else. Its items are `ProductLink`s, so the API's withheld logo already gives the tile (AECI-1769). The button is the bordered secondary button, not the mock-up's Bone fill.
- **The integrations section (AECI-1782).** Settled in part. The per-partner card is a new card (`product-integration-card.ts`), not the Disclosure group card. The group card stays as the lane wrapper on pages with a Via lane, and its cards then take the nested surface (clash 14). The copy left open here was approved by Chris on 2026-11-16 and built in AECI-1783. The section head, the filter panel, the list row and the card body follow mock-up 6 since AECI-1720 (Chris, 2026-11-17: he prefers the mock-up's small label over a large section heading; the earlier "not adopted" note for this section is withdrawn):
  - **Section head (AECI-1720).** The kicker "Integrations on record" (`.aec-overline`, a `<p>`, never a heading, 12px under it) over the `<h2>` "Integrations" at `--t-36` from `md` and `--t-24` below, tracking -0.02em. The heading keeps no count (ruled 2026-11-16). The mock-up's "{n} of {N} integrations shown" badge is not built. It is the section's row count, and that can differ from the facts card's figure (a stored count that lags, a connector's powered edges), which is why the heading carries no count. The mock-up's intro line ("Every integration here connects…, with the evidence behind it.") is a promise and waits for Chris. It is not built.
  - **Filter panel (AECI-1720).** One Bone panel (`--accent-warm`), `rounded-card`, 16px padding, 16px between rows. Its name "Filters" is screen-reader only. The top row holds the search box (a search glyph 16px from its start, `ps-11`, the `.aec-field-control` edge, `--t-16`), the direction chips, and the "By category / One list" and "Cards / Table" toggles. Below `lg` the available controls stack. Then "Types of data" and "Partner category", each an overline label in an 8.5rem column from `md`, so wrapped chips line up after the label, not under it. Below `md` the label sits above, and each chip row is one line that scrolls sideways (`overflow-x: auto`, chips `shrink-0`), with 6px of padding so a focus ring is never clipped. The direction chips take mock-up 6's words (AECI-1720 restore C): "Any direction", "{product} sends", "{product} receives" and "Both ways", where {product} is this page's product. They keep their ruled behaviour: several may be pressed, each prints its count, and a 0 count disables it. **Only controls that can change the list (AECI-2021, ruled 2026-11-23).** A direction, data or category chip shows only while its count over the unfiltered rows is below the section's row total, so pressing it would hide at least one row. The test reads the unfiltered rows, so chips never come and go as the reader filters. A pressed chip always stays, so it can be released. A direction group with no such chip drops whole, "Any direction" with it. A data or category row with no chip left drops with its label. The search box shows from 5 rows (`INTEGRATION_SEARCH_MIN_ROWS`) and the Cards/Table toggle from 2 (`INTEGRATION_VIEW_TOGGLE_MIN_ROWS`, both in `integration-filter.ts`). The "By category / One list" toggle keeps its own rule. A panel with nothing left in it does not render, nor does the list row under it, so a one-row section is the heading and its card. From `lg` the top row is a flex row that wraps, the search filling the space from a 16rem basis and the toggles at the end, so it lays out the same whichever parts are left and long direction chips never crush the search box. With no search box, "Clear filters" hands focus to the panel's first enabled button. "Any direction" is pressed while no direction chip is, pressing it releases them, and its count is every row the other filters keep, rows with no direction on record included. "Any direction" follows mock-ups 6 and 7 (`#dirSeg`), and it is the one "any" chip: the data and category rows have none. It stays pending the owner's confirmation. Below `md` the four wrap two by two, as the mock-up's phone layout does, instead of the sideways-scrolling line the other chip rows use, which hid the third chip off the edge at 390px. The category label reads "Partner category", the mock-up's words. The search box is its own field. "Integrations it powers" uses the same one through `integration-filter-recipe.ts` (AECI-1788).
  - **List row (AECI-1720, AECI-2021).** Under the panel, the `role="status"` line at the end, and "Clear filters" while a filter is on. The list has no heading of its own: AECI-2021 (ruled 2026-11-23) removed the `<h3>` "All integrations on record" / "Integrations that match these filters", so the section `<h2>` heads the list. The status reads "Showing {n} of {N}" only while a filter is on and is empty otherwise, as on "Integrations it powers" (the Same-Behavior Rule). The mock-up's idle "Showing 14 of 14" is not built. Idle, the row holds only the empty status, so it is visually hidden (`sr-only`) and takes no room. It stays in the accessibility tree, so the first change is announced. Heading levels: on a one-lane page the cards are `<h3>`, and in a long list a category group's heading is `<h3>` and its cards `<h4>`. On a page with a Via lane each lane card's heading is the `<h3>` and its cards are `<h4>`. The one-lane cards list keeps "Integrations" as its accessible name (`aria-label`).
  - **Card body (AECI-1720).** A 48px mark (`aec-logo-or-initial size="md"`) beside the partner name (serif, `--t-24` since the card took the integration card recipe, §5 "The integration card"), with "by {vendor}" under it at `--t-13` (`partner_vendor_name`, the partner's primary vendor). Then three overline-labelled blocks: "Categories" with the category chips, "What moves" with the direction (`--t-14`, 600) and the types of data, and "How it connects" with the mechanism chips and name. The direction reads "Sends to {partner}", "Receives from {partner}" or "Syncs both ways" (mock-up 6, AECI-1720 restore C), the pair page's words. Each fact prints once (`mechanismDisplay`): a mechanism name that only repeats a kind chip ("Native" under "Native") is not printed, and a name that contains its row's one kind as a whole word ("REST API" for "API", "Native integration" for "Native") is the more specific value, so it takes the chip's place and the kind is not printed. A name that adds something the kind does not say keeps both. The table row uses the same rule. Then the agreement label, and any "Under review" lines. The agreement label shows every state except `unverified`: "Listed by AEC Integrations" does not render on the product page's cards or table rows (AECI-2021, ruled 2026-11-23), because it is the state of most rows and so says nothing about one. "Confirmed by both companies", "Confirmed by one company" and "Companies disagree" still render. The pair page keeps every label. The card carries no caption or correction line (AECI-1666 ruling 2): the pair page's "About this listing" has them. The pair-page link sits at the end of the name row (AECI-2021, ruled 2026-11-23), not at the foot, so each card is shorter. It is a short muted hint: "View details" at `--t-13` in `--text-secondary`, then a 16px chevron, with no round button. Its accessible name is "View details for the {partner} integration": the visible words start the name and the rest is visually hidden, so the name matches the label (WCAG 2.5.3) and still says which integration. The words are not "Click the card for more", because "click" is wrong for touch and keyboard readers and a link label names its action. The hint never wraps and never shrinks (`whitespace-nowrap`, `shrink-0`). The name block wraps its own text, and the hint stays on the name row while the mark and the name keep 12rem beside it. Below that, at any width, the hint drops under the name, so a narrow card (a phone, or a Via lane card in two columns at `md`) never squeezes the name to a sliver. The table row's chevron keeps its name, "View the {partner} integration". The glyph stays a chevron (the Arrow Rule). The link is stretched over the card, and the partner-name link sits above it (`relative z-10`). The data list keeps "Types of data" as its accessible name. The card's padding, gaps and chips are the integration card recipe's, and the hint is its head-row link (`MATCH_CARD_HEAD_LINK_CLASS`).
  - **Table (AECI-1720).** The table sits in a white `rounded-card` with the card shadow and a Bone header row. A "Categories" column shows from `lg`, the agreement label sits under the mechanism in the Connection cell, and the partner cell prints "by {vendor}". The 34rem floor below `lg` is unchanged. The mock-up's data-object matrix stays out (clash 24).
  - **Order line.** "A to Z by product name." under the heading (AECI-2021, ruled 2026-11-23; it read "A to Z by partner name." before). The rows sort by the partner product's name. It shows only on a page with no Via lane and two rows or more. A Via page orders its connector groups by row count (R1), so the line would not be true of the whole section, and it stays off there. One row has no order to state.
  - **Data chips.** "Types of data", A to Z by name, between Direction and Categories, each with its count. Data chips AND together. While one is pressed, "Integrations with no types of data on record are not shown while this filter is on." shows in a polite live region and describes the chip group (AECI-1816).
  - **Empty messages.** "No integrations match these filters." when any chip is pressed, with or without a search. "No integrations match that search." for a search alone.
  - **Card chip rows.** A small `--text-secondary` label, "Categories" or "Types of data", over each chip row. The list takes the same words as its accessible name, and the visible label is `aria-hidden`, so it is read once.
  - **One card** takes the full row (`data-columns` on the grid), in a short list, a group or the one-list view. Two or more take the integration card grid's two columns from `md` (`.aec-integration-grid`). No card is wider than its siblings.
- **The zero-integration state (AECI-1786).** Settled. It shows when the facts card shows its zero: the stored count is 0 and the page lists no row under Integrations or "Integrations it powers" (`integrations-on-record.ts`). The section also needs no reach line: a product with pairs reachable via connectors keeps the plain empty state and the reach line. The `<h2>` is the legal heading "We have no integrations for {product} on record", and the caveat sits under it. Both come from the `aec-figure` message ids, so the card and the section print one message. Under the caveat is one group, drawn as mock-up 8's `.know` card (AECI-1720 restore C): the page's one Forest feature card (§2 "A Forest feature card"), `rounded-card`, the card shadow, Forest fill, Bone ink, 32px padding (24px below `md`). It holds the `h3` "Know of one?" at `--t-24`, the line "Tell us where you saw it and we will look at it." in `--text-on-brand-secondary`, and "It does exist", the Bone main button (`on-brand`), full width below `md`, at the card's foot, on the existing correction request. The line is also the button's accessible description (`aria-describedby`). Chris added the heading and the line on 2026-11-16 (ruling 51, option A). They are the words `STAGE_1_5_SPEC.md` §11.4 approved for the empty pair page, and the copy the legal review relied on. Beside it is one card: the vendor's Integrations page and API documentation links when they exist, with the facts card's labels, then "An API is not an integration. We list an integration only when we can show that one exists.", then "We have not found an integrations page published by {vendor}." The last line shows only with a vendor and no integrations page link, and not while AECi has that link under review or removed. The state has no illustration (R5) and no category chart (R3, decision 19). Its one Forest feature card is the "Know of one?" card, and the product page carries no other, so the one-per-page rule holds. The vendor card beside it is white and stretches to the same height from `lg`. It has no filter panel, no Cards/Table toggle, no order line and no scope note. The API note shows on every zero state (ruling 52, 2026-11-16). The "It does exist" label is not in the `PLAN.md` §5.2 table, but `STAGE_1_5_SPEC.md` §11.4 fixes the same words for the empty pair page. AECI-1893 asks legal to confirm the group for the product page. Similar products "that have integrations" are not built (ruling 53, 2026-11-16, AECI-1895). A product with only reachable pairs keeps the facts card's zero sentence (ruling 58, 2026-11-16). **Mock-up 8's head and vendor card (AECI-1720).** The kicker "Integrations on record" sits over the `<h2>`. From `lg` the head splits in halves: the kicker and heading on the left, the caveat on the right. Under it, the "Know of one?" group on the left and the vendor card on the right. With a vendor, the card's heading reads "From {vendor}" (an overline) over the line "What the vendor publishes about connecting to {product}.". Without one it keeps "Resources". It also keeps "Resources", with no line, when AECi corrected a link the card shows. That link is AECi's value, so the card must not call it the vendor's. The "Corrected by" line itself is in "About this listing", not in the card (AECI-1666). The mock-up's third caveat sentence ("We list an integration once we can show it connects {product} to another product in our catalog.") waits for Chris and is not built. **The "No directory" line never ends in two full stops (AECI-1720).** Its message is legal-reviewed and unchanged. A vendor name that ends in a full stop ("SiteLedger Inc.") gives that stop to the sentence (`withoutTrailingFullStop` in `integrations-on-record.ts`), so it reads "…published by SiteLedger Inc.".
- **The long integration list (AECI-1783).** Settled by Chris on 2026-11-16 (rulings 62 to 68 and 70 to 72). `integration-category-groups.ts` holds every value.
  - **Threshold.** A single-lane section groups by partner category when it has 13 rows or more AND fewer groups than half its rows (the uncategorised group counts). So 13 rows in 13 one-card groups stays one flat list. The rule reads the row count and the partners' categories only, so the server and the browser agree. A list of 12 or fewer renders as before. A page with a Via lane keeps its lane cards and does not group.
  - **Placement and counts.** A row sits in every one of its partner categories and counts in each. The rail, the group heading and the category chip print one number, from one function (`rowsInCategory`). A chip counts the rows it would show with the reader's other filters on. Rows with no category form a last group, "No category on record" (confirmed 2026-11-16), shown only while no category chip is pressed. A pressed category chip shows only the chosen groups. So the group counts can add up to more than the "Showing n of m" line, which counts each row once, and no note says so (confirmed 2026-11-16).
  - **Order.** Groups run A to Z by category name, slug breaking a tie (R1). Rows keep the lane order inside a group.
  - **Show more.** A closed group shows its first 6 cards (ruled 2026-11-16, was 3). A group of 6 or fewer shows every card and has no button. **Below `md`, where the grid is one column, a closed group shows its first 3 (AECI-1720).** The 6 was ruled to avoid a lone card in a two- or three-column row, which one column cannot have, and 6 full cards a group made the phone list about 24,000px tall at 390px. Rows 4 to 6 carry `data-phone-extra` and a component style hides them under `md` only, so the server HTML and the hydrated DOM are the same at every width and nothing shifts. A group of 4 to 6 has a "Show more" button on a phone only (its wrapper is `md:hidden`). The button prints two labels and two logo stacks, one per width, each counting the cards it reveals there. Focus after "Show more" goes to the fourth card on a phone and the seventh from `md`. Find-in-page does not reach rows 4 to 6 on a phone until the group is open. The rest stay in the server HTML with `hidden="until-found"`, so find-in-page reaches them and opens the group. "Show more" reveals the rest of the group, moves focus to the first card it revealed, and becomes "Show fewer". "Show fewer" keeps focus on the button and scrolls it into view. A filter that leaves 12 rows or fewer opens every group. The grouped list has no `@defer` cut: every group renders and hydrates with the page, because a group still dehydrated when the reader filtered stayed in the DOM as a stale copy.
  - **Jump rail.** One `<nav>`. From `lg` it is a sticky panel in a 252px left column, held below the section bar by `--aec-sticky-offset`. Below `lg` the same links are a sideways-scrolling row of pills. A rail link jumps to its group and moves focus to the group heading. The current group takes the Forest fill and `aria-current="location"`. The group grid is 1 column, 2 from `md` and 3 from `xl`, because the rail takes the left column from `lg`.
  - **One list.** A "By category" and "One list" toggle sits beside Cards and Table on a long list in the card view, as mock-up 7 draws it (confirmed 2026-11-16). It takes the Cards/Table toggle's button recipe. One list shows 12 cards in lane order and a load bar: the stacked meter, a polite "Showing n of N" and a "Load more" secondary button. Every card is in the DOM. The server always renders the grouped arrangement.
  - **Table view** stays one flat table, as before. The three views stay (confirmed 2026-11-16). **Category icons** are not built, and none are wanted (confirmed 2026-11-16).
  - **Chip counts.** Every direction, data and category chip that shows prints its count, on every list length, so the panel behaves the same on a short list and a long one (confirmed 2026-11-16). Since AECI-2021 a chip that cannot hide a row does not show (the filter panel entry above). A chip whose count is 0 is disabled, unless it is pressed, so it can always be released (ruled 2026-11-16). The disabled chip keeps `--text-secondary` and takes a dashed edge, so the state is not colour alone. It is a native `disabled`, so it leaves the tab order. A released chip that turns disabled while it holds focus hands focus to its chip group, so a keyboard reader is not dropped to the page body.
  - **"Integrations it powers (N)"** keeps its number (confirmed 2026-11-16). Only the Integrations heading dropped its count.
- **"Integrations it powers" (AECI-1788).** Settled. No mock-up covers a connector product, so the section takes the Integrations section's parts as built. The `<h2>` keeps "Integrations it powers (N)" at the Integrations heading's size. Since 2026-11-17 (Chris: "align them", the Same-Behavior Rule) the head, panel and list row take the Integrations section's mock-up 6 recipe, from one set of class strings both sections bind (`integration-filter-recipe.ts`). Under the heading is the Bone filter panel with the search box and, since AECI-1924, the Cards and Table toggle, because this list has no chips. "Filters" names it for assistive tech only. Under the panel is the list row: "Showing n of m" and "Clear filters" at its end. It has no heading of its own, because each hub card carries one, as each Via lane card does. No kicker was added, because it would be new words. "Clear filters" shows only while the search holds text. It empties the search, restores every row, empties the status and moves focus to the search box, as on the Integrations panel. The panel is Bone but it is a panel inside the white body band, not a band, so the About band stays the page's one Bone band. Each hub keeps its Disclosure group card, and the hubless pairs keep theirs ("Other connections", or "Connections" alone). Inside each group card the pairs are nested `aecCard`s in the shared card grid, as the Via lane cards are: 24px padding, the 32px logo, the name at `--t-20`, the chevron at the top end, the direction line, then the mechanism and data-object chips. One or two cards share the row (`sharedRowColumns`). Each card is the row's one link to the pair page with the row's accessible name, so no heading and no partner link were added. Below `md` the row folded the mechanism and the object count into its meta line. A card shows its chips at every width instead. Direction names both products at every width, because the connector's page is read from neither of them (QA pass on AECI-2035, 2026-11-24, `pairFlowSentence`): "{hub} sends to {partner}", "{partner} sends to {hub}" or "{hub} and {partner} sync both ways" on a hub card, and the same with endpoint A first on a hubless card. The glyph beside the words stays framed on the hub, or on endpoint A. The direction is the row's claims-aware one (the public flows', else the stored), the rule the pair page and the endpoint rows use. The order, the counts, the empty states and the scope note are unchanged. There is no deferred cut. **Cards and Table (AECI-1924, 2026-11-18).** Chris asked to "toggle between cards and tables inside each hub". The toggle is the Integrations section's `aec-listing-view-toggle`, in the panel after the search box from `lg`, as on the Integrations panel. **One toggle for the whole section** (the builder's choice under the Same-Behavior Rule, 2026-11-18). Chris reviewed it on 2026-11-19 and raised no objection, so it stands. The Integrations section's one toggle sets every Via lane, so here one toggle sets every hub and the hubless group, and a page with 20 hubs has one control. Each group card then holds one table of its own pairs, named by the card's heading through `aria-labelledby`, as each Via lane's table is. The table takes the Integrations table's recipe (`integration-table-recipe.ts`) and its words. Since Chris's review of 2026-11-19 ("there's more info you can show") it has the columns the powered-pair payload supports: "Integrates with" (the partner; "Products" with both names in the hubless group), then from `md` "Direction" (the card's words, seen from the hub) and "How it connects" (each mechanism kind as a chip, then the mechanism's name through `mechanismDisplay`), and from `lg` "Types of data" (the card's count chip, because the payload carries data-object slugs and no names). Narrower, those columns drop and their content joins the meta line under the product, as the Integrations table folds its Connection column. The screen-reader "Details" heads the chevron, which is the row's one link with the card's accessible name, and a row click follows it. The payload carries no partner vendor, no partner categories and no agreement, so the table shows none of them. An agreement column needs an API change first. **No curved shape inside a square one** (Chris, 2026-11-19): a table inside a group card, a hub or a Via lane, has no frame radius or shadow of its own (`INTEGRATION_TABLE_NESTED_FRAME_CLASS`). Its Bone header runs edge to edge with square corners and the group card's radius clips the table's foot. The standalone Integrations table keeps its rounded frame. The table lists the same rows in the same order as the cards, under the same filter and "Showing n of m". Cards are the default, and the server always renders cards. The choice is component state, never the URL or a cookie.
- **The card view renders every row (AECI-1891).** The Integrations section's 20-row `@defer` cut now applies to the table view only. The server always renders cards with every card in its HTML, so the cut only deferred hydration. A filter typed before the reader scrolled left the still-dehydrated tail on the page, as AECI-1783 found for the grouped list.
- **The rule for public demand counts (no code issue).** Clash 43. Written before any count ships. See "Integration stat".

### Icons (clashes 33 and 34)

Chris ruled NEW on clash 34 and left no note: the new design wins, and "Lucide icons exclusively" is rewritten (ADR 0059 lists clash 34). The no-emoji rule stays. Clash 33 (NEW, no note) lets an arrow mean "go here", and the Arrow Rule in §4 holds that rule. The clash record's worry about density, that an icon on every element stops icons meaning anything, was set aside by the ruling. The ruling sets no icon quota, so the decorative-or-labelled rule below is the check. The code ships with the shell batch (`batch/redesign-shell`), not on `main` yet.

- **One custom set.** Icons are drawn for the site at 1.75 stroke in a 24 by 24 box, in `apps/web/src/app/shared/icon/icon-registry.ts` (AECI-1753, clash 34). A few paths are Lucide-derived, and Lucide's licence ships at `/third-party-notices.txt`.
- **One component.** Every UI icon on a public page renders through `aec-icon`. A glyph is never an inline `<svg>`.
- **Stroke only.** A filled glyph is not part of the set.
- **Colour is `currentColor` only.** Set the colour with a text colour on the parent. An icon never carries a colour of its own.
- **Four sizes.** The default is 20px.

| Size           | Tailwind step |
| -------------- | ------------- |
| 14px           | 3.5           |
| 16px           | 4             |
| 20px (default) | 5             |
| 24px           | 6             |

- **Every icon is decorative or labelled.** The selector is `aec-icon[label], aec-icon[decorative]`, so an icon with neither fails to compile. An icon beside text that says the same thing is decorative and hidden from assistive technology. An icon that is the only carrier of meaning takes a localised label. An icon-only button still needs its own accessible name.
- **Direction.** Arrows and chevrons that point along the line mirror in right-to-left text.
- **Named for what they show,** not where they are used. Typed maps tie data-object slugs and facets to icons. 12 of the 27 data-object slugs have no icon yet (AECI-1753).
- **No icon implies endorsement.** The no-verification-iconography rule in "Badges" stands.
- **Portal and admin page bodies draw every icon with `aec-icon`** since the redesign slices (AECI-1729, the last one AECI-2132), which removed every inline icon AECI-1756 and AECI-1757 covered. The shared header, footer and button render there with `aec-icon`. Since AECI-2083 the registry has a glyph for every shape those bodies draw inline (it added `check-circle` and `exclamation`). Two portal shapes map to a near match, not the same drawing, so the swap is visible: Lucide's arrow-right-left becomes `two-way` and its pencil-line becomes `pen`. Also, `src/inline-icon-svg.guard.spec.ts` holds their inline `<svg>` at zero with no allowlist since AECI-2132, when the last one moved to `aec-icon`. `admin/charts/` is data, not icons, and is not counted.

### Overlays (AECI-1770)

Three shared classes in `apps/web/src/styles.css` style every overlay surface. They sit in `@layer components`, so a caller's utility overrides them (`max-sm:rounded-none`). Layout (width, padding, height, position) stays with the caller. A template adds the class and drops its own radius, fill, border and shadow utilities.

| Class          | Used for                                                                                                                                                 | Radius                    | Shadow           | Fill             |
| -------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------- | ---------------- | ---------------- |
| `.aec-dialog`  | Modal dialogs: the claim dialog, the home feedback dialog, the account delete and review sign-in dialogs                                                 | 24px (`--radius-card`)    | `--shadow-modal` | `--surface-base` |
| `.aec-drawer`  | The request drawer. A bottom sheet below `sm`, so the drawer adds `max-sm:rounded-b-none`                                                                | 24px, top only below `sm` | `--shadow-modal` | `--surface-base` |
| `.aec-popover` | Menu panes, listboxes, the docs search popup, the product combobox popup, info hints and tooltip bodies with content, claim provenance, the account menu | 12px (`--radius-control`) | `--shadow-menu`  | `--surface-base` |

- **Radius was chosen by the orchestrator, not ruled, and is flagged for Chris.** This file names no overlay radius. The two modal surfaces take the card radius, as the Browse panel does. Every popover takes the control radius, 12px. It is on the ruled scale, matches the 12px triggers and avoids near-pill tooltips.
- **Fill is white for every overlay.** The old popovers sat on `--surface-raised`.
- **The edge is a real 1px hairline.** Its colour is the `@layer base` border default (`--border-default`), so the overlay class in `@layer components` sets none. A shadow is never the only edge. Forced-colours mode drops `box-shadow` and keeps the border, so the surface still has an edge. `e2e/overlays.spec.ts` pins it for a dialog and a popover.
- **Two scrims.** `--scrim` is `rgb(0 0 0 / 0.4)` and dims the page behind the open Browse menu (ruled, see §5 "Navigation"). `--scrim-modal` is `rgb(0 0 0 / 0.5)` and is the CDK backdrop behind a dialog. The modal backdrop fades in on `--duration-slow` and `--ease-standard`. The request drawer keeps a transparent backdrop (AECI-128), so the page stays visible behind it. The orchestrator kept it undimmed, and whether it should dim is flagged for Chris.
- **Motion.** The drawer slides up 12px on `--duration-slow` and `--ease-standard`. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` the global block makes the slide and the scrim fade instant. Dialogs and popovers have no enter or exit animation today, and none was added.
- **The consent banner and the update banner are not overlays.** They have no scrim, no focus trap and no Escape. They are fixed notices. They take the `.aec-popover` surface so that no off-vocabulary shadow is left. Their buttons are the shared `aecButton`, unchanged. While the consent banner shows, it sets `data-consent-reserve` and its height on `<html>`. The unlayered rule in `styles.css` turns that into a `scroll-margin-bottom` on every focusable element, so a control reached by Tab scrolls clear of the banner (WCAG 2.4.11, AECI-1818). The rule skips the open mobile menu and the CDK overlay container. They paint above the banner, and the margin there scrolled the locked page behind the menu.
- **Tooltips (WCAG 1.4.13).** `aec-info-hint` and `aec-relative-time` are hoverable (a 120ms grace lets the pointer cross), dismissible with Escape, and persistent while hovered or focused. Their text is also the trigger's accessible name, so the tooltip is never the only source. The home page "Source" tooltip in `home/home-why.ts` is a CSS-only span that is not hoverable and still adds to the scroll width (AECI-1846). It is not on the shared recipe and is left for that issue.
- **Z-index has no documented scale.** The values in use are listed below and were left alone. The CDK overlay container sits at 1000 and is above all of them.

| Value  | Where                                                                                                                      |
| ------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `z-10` | Section nav, the Browse panel, a few absolute chips and chart tooltips                                                     |
| `z-30` | The page dimmer behind the open header menu, the vendor integration detail bar                                             |
| `z-40` | The header, sticky preview bars, the plan call to action bar                                                               |
| `z-50` | The consent banner, the request drawer, the skip link on focus, listbox panels, the admin nav dropdown, four admin dialogs |
| `z-60` | The update banner                                                                                                          |

**Not moved yet.** AECI-1770 names the vendor seat invite dialog and the search autocomplete popup, and its line "every overlay uses one shadow token and one radius token" is not met yet. The remainder:

- **Portal and admin overlays.** They wait for the portal and admin page work, after Chris verifies the public pages (AECI-1813). They keep `shadow-lg`, `rounded-md` and `--surface-raised`. The four admin dialogs and the vendor facet editor keep the inline modal shadow. The vendor seat invite dialog moved to `aec-dialog` with AECI-1812. Each moves by adding `aec-dialog` or `aec-popover`. The list is on AECI-1770 and AECI-1813.
- **The mobile menu panel** (`layout/nav-menu.ts`, AECI-1775). AECI-1775 rebuilt it like the Browse panel: `--surface-base`, `--shadow-menu`, 24px bottom corners and a 1px edge on three sides, so it keeps an edge in forced colours. It does not take `.aec-popover`, whose 12px radius is on all four corners. Its dimmer is `--scrim`. The search list inside it takes whatever the search autocomplete popup takes.
- ~~The search autocomplete popup~~ (`search/search-autocomplete.ts`). Moved to `aec-popover` by AECI-1796, and its spec pins the class. The compact search trigger panel (`layout/search-trigger.ts`) went with PR #1312.

### Motion (clashes 36, 37 and 38)

Chris ruled BLEND on clash 36 and NEW on clash 37, both with no note. He ruled NEW on clash 38 with the note in the smooth-scroll rule below. The record is `records/design-rulings.md`, and ADR 0059 lists clash 38 only. The tokens ship with the shell batch (AECI-1751).

**Motion gives feedback. It never decorates.**

| Token             | Tailwind        | Value                      | Use                                                  |
| ----------------- | --------------- | -------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |
| `--duration-fast` | `duration-fast` | 120ms                      | Press, and row hover                                 |
| `--duration-base` | `duration-base` | 180ms                      | Hover, focus and disclosure                          |
| `--duration-slow` | `duration-slow` | 280ms                      | Menu open, the nav ink bar, the scrim and the drawer |
| `--ease-standard` | `ease-standard` | `cubic-bezier(.2,.7,.2,1)` | Every eased move                                     |

- **Three durations and one easing** (clash 36). The mock-up's 200, 250, 300, 420, 450 and 600ms values are dropped. The easing is the mock-up's own `--ease` (mock-up 6, line 15). The press takes the fast step (line 88), the card lift the base step (line 293), and the Browse menu (line 632) and the nav ink bar (line 182) the slow step.
- **No bounce, no elastic easing.**
- **Reduced motion.** Under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` every transition and animation resolves to an instant change. The global block in `apps/web/src/styles.css` does this today.
- **Motion never carries a state change alone.** A state is also shown by text, `aria-expanded`, `aria-current` or a colour change.
- **No decorative loop, and nothing moves for more than 5 seconds without a pause control. Nothing parallaxes and nothing flashes.** The second is WCAG 2.2.2. The last is 2.3.1.
- **Skeleton `animate-pulse` is the only loop kept.** It signals that content is loading, it runs for 2 seconds, and it stops under reduced motion. The mock-up's endless halo pulse (line 110), its per-card stagger (`rise`, line 296) and its counter bounce are removed (clash 36).
- **One hero entrance is kept** (clash 36). See "The hero diagram" below. Its timing is open.
- **Behaviour timings are not durations.** The Browse menu's 70ms open delay and 260ms close grace are set in `NavDisclosure` and recorded in §5 "Navigation" (AECI-1774).
- **Portal and admin page bodies** keep Tailwind's 150ms default until their migrations run (ruling R2). The shared header and button render there with the new durations.

#### Hover and press (clash 37, new)

Chris ruled NEW: the new design wins. The old "no transform, no scale, no shadow on hover" rules for buttons and cards are rewritten.

- **A card that is interactive lifts.** It moves `translateY(-4px)` on hover and `translateY(-1px)` on press, over the base duration (mock-up 6, lines 293 to 295).
- **A button presses.** It moves `translateY(1px) scale(.98)` on press, over the fast duration (lines 88 and 89).
- **No shadow joins either move** (clash 11). Index rows (§5 "Entity cards") do not lift or scale.
- **Both moves are off under reduced motion,** and neither is the only sign of hover or press.

#### Smooth scrolling (clash 38, new)

Chris: "Make sure we know how to adopt the old way if needed." ADR 0059 lists clash 38. AECI-1752 built it (#1300).

- **In-page section links only.** Smooth scrolling applies to a link marked `data-smooth-scroll`: on public pages, the product section nav, the product page's reviews link, the docs "On this page" rail and the `/account` section nav. In the vendor portal, the integration page's section nav. The skip link never opts in.
- **Never a global setting,** and off under reduced motion. A `routerLink` cannot opt in. The skip link never animates.
- **Router scroll restoration is untouched** (`CLAUDE.md`, "Router scroll restoration is already solved"). `ScrollBehaviorManager`, `InitialFragmentScroller` and `ScrollMarginViewportScroller` are not changed.
- **To restore the old global behaviour,** add `class="aeci-smooth-scroll"` to `<html>` in `apps/web/src/index.html`. `ANGULAR_STYLE_GUIDE.md` §3 documents it. `styles.css` sets no global `scroll-behavior`.

### Logos and initial tiles (clash 34 and counsel's ruling 9)

`PLAN.md` §4.5 states the rule as counsel's ruling 9 "as the legal agent read it". The record is `records/legal-review-2026-11-12.md`.

- **A small sourced logo may appear on an unclaimed product's own page** (counsel's ruling 9, see "Unclaimed note"). A claimed product's logo and brand colour may appear (`PLAN.md` §4.5).
- **An unclaimed product gets no brand colour anywhere** (ruling R7, AECI-1738, 2026-11-14).
- **Open with counsel: whether the product hero may show a large logo** (AECI-1826). Until it is answered the existing size rule stands (§5 "Unclaimed note").
- **Everywhere else an unclaimed product shows an initial tile.** Lists, cards, rows and search all show it. The Unclaimed note stays word for word (§5 "Unclaimed note").
- **The one exception: the integrations wheel (ruling 64, Chris, 2026-11-24, AECI-2018).** A wheel tile shows the product's logo whenever we hold one, claimed or not, and its initial tile only when we hold none. Nothing marks, badges, dims or outlines an unclaimed tile. Every logo sits inset on the same neutral tile, so no vendor colour reaches the ground, ring, border or card (ruling 34 stands). The wheel's card names an unclaimed listing with the plain-text label "Unclaimed". Two lines under every tile slide say "Shown A to Z. Position is not a ranking." and "Logos identify products only and imply no endorsement or affiliation. Listings may be unclaimed." The logo comes from the wheel's own `ProductDetail.wheel_marks` (`docs/API_CONTRACTS.md`), never from a list payload, so every other surface keeps the rule above. The tag passes `claimStatus` `'wheel'`, which `logo-or-initial.callers.spec.ts` allows in `products/product-integration-wheel.ts` only. "Whenever we hold one" means a logo we may show: the stored logo while the listing is claimed, the vendor's upload included, and once it is unclaimed only a logo we sourced ourselves (`sourced_logo_url` when the stored one is the vendor's upload), never an upload left over from an ended claim (Vendor Terms 1.3, ADR 0051). `publicLogoOf` in the API decides it and fails closed. The centre of the wheel is the page's own product by the same rule. A logo removed on an objection comes down on the wheel too: the removal clears `products.logo_url` and purges `product:{slug}`, which every product page carries for each of its partners (§5 "The hero diagram").
- **"Its own page" is the one slot where the note sits beside the mark (AECI-1769).** Counsel's ruling 9 keeps a small sourced logo "with the Unclaimed and not-affiliated line beside them". So the logo shows on the product page, the vendor page and each subject of a pair page (ADR 0051 decision 4), and only beside the note. It is a logo we sourced: once a claim ends the API never returns the vendor's upload there, even before the removal job runs (`publicLogoOf`, AECI-2057). Any other place on those pages, and every other route, shows the tile. The review form's rail at `/products/:slug/review` is not the product's own page: it shows the tile.
- **One component decides (AECI-1769).** `aec-logo-or-initial` takes `claimStatus` and `ownPage`. `claimStatus` is required, so every caller must choose (AECI-1851). A page-subject payload keeps the logo and binds its `claim_status`. A public list, link or search payload passes `'server-filtered'`, because the API already sends `logo_url: null` for an unclaimed listing (AECI-1618). A portal or admin surface, and the account avatar, pass `'stored'`: ADR 0051 keeps the real value there. The integrations wheel passes `'wheel'` (ruling 64, above). Any other value, including a missing `claim_status`, shows the tile. `integration-group-card` takes its mark as one `logo` object whose `claimStatus` is required, and forwards it. `ownPage` is set only on the slot beside `aec-unclaimed-note`. `logo-or-initial.callers.spec.ts` classifies every caller, checks the value each kind passes, and fails on a new one.
- **The initial tile must not look lesser.** It carries no label and no muted styling. A logo and its tile share the box, the 1px `--border-default` edge, the `--surface-raised` fill and the radius (AECI-1769). The initial is `--text-primary` on `--surface-raised`, 18.97:1. At 32px the initial is the body face at 14px and 600, as mock-ups 10 to 12 draw it. Larger tiles keep the serif at 20px and up (§3 "The Serif-Floor Rule", AECI-1928). The tile is `aria-hidden`, because the name is adjacent text at every caller. The person avatar (`shape="circle"`) keeps its unframed photo.
- **The 48px mark (AECI-1720).** `size="md"` is the product page's integration card mark (mock-up 6, `.card .mark`): 48px, the initial at `--t-20`, `rounded-mark`. An unclaimed partner's logo is already withheld by the API, so the card shows its tile.
- **Radius: `rounded-mark`, 8px, for the 32px and 64px marks alike (AECI-1769).** Logo and tile take the same radius, and the scale never inverts. The large-mark radius (64px, and the 104px hero mark) is still an open value for Chris (AECI-1750).
- **The hero size (AECI-1778).** `size="hero"` fills the box its caller draws, the band's 104px mark (68px below `md`). The image reserves 104px. The tile's initial is `--t-36`. It keeps `rounded-mark` until Chris rules the large-mark radius.
- **An unclaimed listing's logo never renders above 32px (AECI-1769).** The component ignores `ownPage` at any larger size, whatever the caller passes, until counsel answers AECI-1826. So the hero's 104px `heroMark` slot (AECI-1766) shows the tile for an unclaimed product. The wheel's tiles (24px to 48px) and its card's 48px mark are ruling 64's exception, above.
- **Never a vendor logo or vendor colour in the sticky bar,** on any listing. A vendor logo where our header has scrolled away would read as the vendor's own site. An unclaimed listing's bar shows only the name, as a claimed one does. The Unclaimed note stays in the hero (AECI-2012).
- **The reversed wordmark.** On a Forest ground the wordmark is `aec-brand-logo` with `tone="on-dark"`: Bone type, the Clay connector and a transparent field (AECI-1754, clash 42). Clay never flips. Bone on Forest is 11.03:1. §5 "Navigation" says where it sits.
- **Open: the large-mark radius** (AECI-1750), for the 64px mark and the 104px hero mark. The 64px mark uses 8px until Chris rules. The mock-up's mark is `border-radius: 22%`, with a shadow that §4 drops.

### The hero diagram (clash 35)

Chris ruled NEW on clash 35 and left no note. No rule existed. The product page's hero visual was first ruled by R3 decision 1 (AECI-1734, 2026-11-14): a category hub with a pair finder under it (AECI-1787). **The integrations wheel replaced both** (AECI-2018). Chris ruled the design in chat on 2026-11-23 and said "build it" on 2026-11-24. The mock-up is `docs/design/mockups/redesign-2026-11/mockups/9-product-hero-wheel.html`, its rulings are in `mockups/src/9w-notes.html`, and the later rulings are on the issue. Any diagram in a hero follows the clash recommendation:

- **Real records only.** No placeholder product and no invented count. The mock-up's counts are never copied.
- **Static in the server HTML.** It reads without script.
- **A text equivalent** for a screen reader and for a reader with no script.
- **Position implies no ranking.** Any products it shows are ordered A to Z by name, with the id as tie-break (ruling R1). The firewall covers it.
- **An honest empty state.** At zero there is no diagram, and the facts card says "We have no integrations for {product} on record".
- **Logos** follow "Logos and initial tiles", where the wheel is ruling 64's one exception.
- **One entrance animation, once.** It must not replay on hydration, and under reduced motion it shows its final state.

**The integrations wheel (AECI-2018, built).** `aec-product-integration-wheel` (`products/product-integration-wheel.ts`) in the hero band's panel. The model and the geometry are pure functions in `products/integration-wheel.ts`. The CSS is `.aec-wheel` in `styles.css`.

- **Who is on it.** Every product this one has an integration with on record: the partner of each row the Integrations section lists (`splitIntegrationLanes`, direct and via rows). The wording says "products" and "integrations", never "partners".
- **On a connector** (QA pass on AECI-2035, 2026-11-24). A `connector` shows the products its pairs reach instead: every endpoint of `integrations_as_connector` but itself, the set the hero's reach line counts (`connectedProducts`), so the line and the wheel's count line are one number. One slide, titled "Products it syncs" on a Sync Service and "Products it connects" otherwise (`connectorWheelCaption`), A to Z by name, id on a tie. A tile links to the product's own page, because the connector is not one of the pair's two products. A dot is one product, with the line "Too many to show as tiles. Each dot is one product." With no pair a connector falls back to its endpoint rows. A `hybrid` or an `application` shows its endpoint rows and falls back to the products it reaches. A `connector` with neither gets the panel with the powered section's empty sentence ("No integrations are recorded for this Sync Service yet."), so the hero never has a blank right half. `heroWheelSource` in `integration-wheel.ts` decides.
- **Slides.** "All integrations" first, then one slide per category, A to Z by category name through `compareText`, the slug breaking a tie. A category slide shows the products that carry that category, so a product with two categories is on both. A category with no product for this page is not offered. Left and right arrows step and wrap. Nothing moves on its own. The arrows are named for their destination ("Previous category: {name}", "Next category: {name}"). A product with one slide has no arrows and a static title.
- **The title.** The slide's name, the serif at 20px, over a count line ("54 products", "1 product"). It is a button that opens a flat A to Z list of every slide with its product count, a popover under the title. Up, Down, Home and End move in it, Enter picks, Escape and Tab close it, and focus returns to the title.
- **Tiles.** One per product, all the same size, A to Z by name ignoring capitals (`compareText`), then id, clockwise from 12 o'clock. A short Clay stroke marks 12 o'clock. Each tile is a link to the pair page (on a connector's wheel, to the product's page). Names show under the tiles on a slide of 8 or fewer, and not on a stage under 260px, where they run into the centre. A tile's link is named by `aria-label`. The visible name under it is `aria-hidden` and is not rendered at all where names are off, because a clipped, visually hidden name is still found by find-in-page and by a `#:~:text=` link, which then landed on the hero instead of the product's card (CI run 45347285193, `products-long-list.spec.ts`).
- **The geometry** (the mock-up's build notes, 2026-11-24). The stage is the panel's inner width, at most 476px: 476 from 1280px, 336 at 1024, and the viewport less 96px on a phone (294 at 390, 279 at 375, 224 at 320). Tiles keep 8px between them, 12px between rings, and 6px at the edge for the focus ring. The outer radius is stage/2 − 6 − tile/2, the inner is outer − tile − 12, and a ring holds floor(2π × radius / (tile + 8)).
- **One tile size per product.** It comes from the product's largest slide that can be tiled at the smallest tile: 28px beside the text, 24px on a phone, which is a stage under 336px (every stage narrower than the 1024 side-by-side panel is a stacked hero on a phone). If that slide is a one-ring slide (below), the tile is the largest size up to 48px that fits it on one ring. Otherwise it is the largest up to 40px that fits it on two rings. On a phone it is the largest up to 40px on one ring.
- **The ring count is per slide, and depends only on the slide's product count and the width** (`wheelMode`). Beside the text: one ring up to `oneRingMax`, which is 20 or the number of 40px tiles one ring holds, whichever is less; then two rings while they fit at 28px; then dots. On a phone: one ring while it fits at 24px, then dots. Any tile size the product rule picks fits that mode, because capacity only falls as the tile grows: a one-ring slide is at most `oneRingMax`, which fits one ring at 40px, and a two-ring product's tile is at most 40px. A property spec checks it for every band and every count from 1 to 120, whatever the product's largest slide. A one-ring slide sits on the outer circle. On two rings A to Z fills the inner ring and continues on the outer, split by circumference. The inner guide circle fades out on a one-ring slide (280ms).
- **What that gives, per server band** (stage px: one ring, two rings, dots from). 476 (1280 and up, and 768 to 1023): 1 to 20, 21 to 69, 70. 400: 1 to 20, 21 to 55, 56. 336 (1024): 1 to 18, 19 to 43, 44. Phones, one ring only (Chris: "Phones: keep the circle, no second ring"): 320 (a stacked hero at 416 to 431px, such as 428 and 430px iPhones) 1 to 27, 294 (390) 1 to 25, 279 (375) 1 to 23, 260 1 to 21, 224 (320) 1 to 18, then dots. `integration-wheel.spec.ts` derives these from the maths.
- **Dots.** A slide whose products do not fit as tiles shows no product. It shows one small dot per integration and the count in the centre ("212 integrations in 14 categories", "120 integrations in Construction Management", or "25 integrations" for a product whose partners carry no category). The dots are 6px on rings 10px apart, and 5px on rings 8px apart under a 400px stage, filled from the outer ring in. They are not links and identify no product. The title of a dots slide counts products, the centre counts integrations. With no script a dots slide links to the Integrations section ("See all {n} in Integrations"). Each ring of dots is one SVG circle with a zero-length round dash and a `pathLength` equal to its dot count. The dot count always equals the integration count: past what fits clear of the centre (about 1,400 on a 224px stage) the pitch tightens and the rings reach in, up to the whole disc at a 2px pitch (`dotsMax`, about 8,800 on a 224px stage, far above any product in the catalog).
- **The centre** of a tile slide is the page's own product with the logo we may show, by ruling 64's rule (its own entry in `wheel_marks`): the stored logo while claimed, the logo we sourced once unclaimed, else the initial tile. Chris confirmed the size is fine for an unclaimed product. A dots slide shows no logo there.
- **The lines under the wheel** are 13px, weight 400, `--text-on-brand-secondary` on `--surface-hero-panel` (6.62:1). A tile slide always shows both: "Shown A to Z. Position is not a ranking." and "Logos identify products only and imply no endorsement or affiliation. Listings may be unclaimed." (ruling 64, wording corrected on 2026-11-24). A dots slide shows only "Too many to show as tiles. Each dot is one integration." The block widens into the panel's padding (12px each side, 16px from 768px), and each phrase keeps together.
- **The card.** Hover, keyboard focus and a 450ms touch hold open it. The hold does not follow the link. It shows the logo or initial at 48px, the name in the serif at 20px, and "by {vendor}". An unclaimed listing adds the plain-text label "Unclaimed" beside the name, read with it, which wraps under the name with no indent when it does not fit. The card opens toward the centre: beside a side tile on the centre's side, below a top tile, above a bottom one. It is held inside the screen sideways and inside the stage from top to bottom, so it never covers the title or the lines. It is a CDK connected overlay (`role="tooltip"`, the tile's `aria-describedby` while open), so the hero never clips it. It stays open while hovered, closes 120ms after the pointer leaves, and Escape closes it. A press anywhere outside the card and its tile closes it, and a scroll closes a card a touch hold opened (WCAG 1.4.13).
- **Keyboard and announcements.** The ring is one tab stop after hydration. Left and Up go back, Right and Down forward, Home and End to the ends, and it wraps. The ring is labelled "{name}, {n} products, A to Z" and described by the hint "Arrow keys move between products." and the lines. A slide change is announced in a polite live region: "{name}, {n} products. {i} of {total}."
- **Rendering.** The first slide is in the cached server HTML, the same for every visitor, as a list of links. The server cannot know the width, so it lays the slide out at eight stage widths (`WHEEL_BANDS`: 224, 260, 279, 294, 320, 336, 400 and 476px). It writes each band's positions as custom properties on the tiles, its mode as `data-m{k}` and its names as `data-n{k}` on the wheel, and a container query per band picks one. A stage takes the band at or below its width. Positions are percentages, so a tile never renders below the size it was laid out at: a phone with no script never gets a tile under 24px. The browser uses the same bands and lays nothing out again at hydration, so the names, the ring count, tiles or dots and the space under the stage never change when the script arrives. 260 (names) and 336 (the phone rule) are band edges for that reason. Stepping lays the new slide out at every band too. Before hydration every tile is a tab stop, so a reader with no script reaches them all; after it (`data-live`) the ring is one tab stop. The no-script "See all" link is `{path}#integrations`, never a bare fragment (`injectInPageHref`, AECI-1839). With no script (`@media (scripting: none)`) the arrows are hidden and the static title shows.
- **The entrance.** The art (guides, tiles, dots) fades in and settles over twice `--duration-slow` with `--ease-standard`, from a 0.96 scale. Opacity and scale only, with no turn, because the ranking page says the wheel never moves on its own. It fills `backwards`, never forwards: once it ends nothing of it stays applied, so the wheel paints exactly as the first paint did (a forwards fill kept the art on its own compositor layer and failed the AECI-1854 first-paint guard on the half-pixel Clay mark). It runs only under `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference` and never after a step, and hydration reuses the animated wrapper, so it does not replay.
- **Order and the firewall.** No input a paid plan can change decides inclusion, order or size. Inclusion reads the rows, order reads the product name and id, the slides read the partner's categories (a Free edit), and size reads only the product counts and the stage width. A logo and a claim state reach the tile's image and the card's label only. The case is `[surface:product-page-hero-wheel]` in `on-page-ordering-firewall.spec.ts`.
- **Cache.** The wheel adds no `Cache-Tag`. The page already carries `product:{slug}` for every partner, which covers a rename, a logo change, a logo removal and a claim end. AECI-2025, the page's 500 above about 160 integrations because `Cache-Tag` passes 16,384 bytes, is open and is not this issue's.
- **Not in this issue:** Data mode (AECI-2019).
- **Where the build departs from the mock-up.** The mock-up's stand-in logos are not built: a tile shows the real logo or the initial tile. The server writes eight band layouts where the mock-up's server HTML carried two, and the browser keeps them rather than laying the slide out again at the measured width, so nothing shifts at hydration. Between two band widths a slide is the lower band's layout scaled up, so its cut-offs are the lower band's. With no script the title is centred with "{n} products", where the mock-up set it left with the category count too.

The category hub and the product page's pair finder are removed (`product-category-hub.ts`, `category-hub.ts`, `product-pair-finder.ts`). The home page keeps its own pair finder, and `shared/pair-finder/pair-finder-route.ts` stays for it. The hero band's `holdColumns()` and `releaseColumns()` (item 82) went with the hub, because no caller grows the panel in place now.

### Data visualization — operator console only (AECI-578)

_(Clash 19, 2026-11-13: public pages get one chart-like thing, the bar meter above. Everything below stays operator console only.)_

The admin panel (`/admin/*`, `docs/ADMIN_PANEL_SPEC.md` §8) is the one surface in
AECi that plots multi-series data. Its primitives live in
`apps/web/src/app/admin/charts/` and are hand-rolled SVG + HTML — there is no
charting dependency and none is to be added (§13 D3). The method comes from the
`dataviz` skill: pick the form, then assign colour by the job it does, then
validate the palette with a script rather than by eye.

**The series palette is scoped, and it is not a brand colour.** Eight categorical
hues are declared under `.aec-charts` in `apps/web/src/styles.css`, deliberately
**outside `@theme inline`** so they never become Tailwind colour utilities and
cannot drift onto a public surface. They encode _data-series identity_ on an
operator screen. They are also the reason `styles.css` is one of the two files
allow-listed from the AECI-597 colour-literal lint rule — it is the token
definition site, so it is the one place allowed to spell a hex value. Declare a
new series hue **there**; writing one into a component will fail `pnpm lint`. **Forest remains the sole brand primary** under the
Forest-Anchor Rule above; nothing here is a second primary, and none of these
hues may appear outside `/admin`.

| Slot | Hue     | Light     | Reserved for     |
| ---- | ------- | --------- | ---------------- |
| 1    | blue    | `#2a78d6` | human page views; Google on `/admin/seo` |
| 2    | orange  | `#eb6834` | bot page views; Bing on `/admin/seo` |
| 3    | aqua    | `#1baf7a` | —                |
| 4    | yellow  | `#eda100` | —                |
| 5    | magenta | `#e87ba4` | —                |
| 6    | green   | `#008300` | —                |
| 7    | violet  | `#4a3aa7` | —                |
| 8    | red     | `#e34948` | —                |

Adopted verbatim from the `dataviz` skill's validated reference palette rather
than derived from the AECi hues, because the brand system has exactly one
meaning-bearing hue (Forest) plus Clay deep and Error red — not a categorical
set, and a hand-derived one would need its own validation pass. Verified as a set
with the skill's `validate_palette.js`: lightness band PASS, chroma floor PASS,
CVD separation PASS (worst adjacent ΔE 9.1), normal-vision floor PASS (worst
adjacent ΔE 19.6).

Rules that ride with it:

- **Colour follows the entity, never its rank.** Slots are declared as part of a
  series' identity, not assigned by array index — a filter that changes the series
  count must not repaint the survivors. Human is always slot 1, bot always slot 2.
- **The relief rule.** Slots 3, 4 and 5 measure below 3:1 against the light
  surface (2.74 / 2.11 / 2.62). Any chart reaching slot 3 must ship **visible**
  value labels. The visually-hidden data table does _not_ discharge this — the
  reader who needs the relief can see the chart.
- **Eight is the ceiling.** A ninth generated hue is indistinguishable under CVD.
  Past eight, fold the tail into `--chart-other` or facet.
- **Text never wears the data colour.** Values, labels, legends and axis text use
  `--text-primary` / `--text-secondary`; a coloured mark beside the text carries
  identity.
- **Marks:** 2px lines with round caps; area fills at ~10% opacity; ≤24px bars
  with a 4px rounded data end and a square baseline; a **2px surface gap** between
  every pair of touching fills, columns and stacked segments alike; hairline solid
  gridlines in `--chart-grid`. White does the separating — never a stroke around a
  mark.
- **A missing value is a gap, never a zero** (AECI-822). A line chart breaks
  its line where a value was not recorded. Where every series is missing, it
  draws a hatched band with a label such as "Not recorded". The hatch is
  `--border-field`, 3.55:1 on the surface, and on a narrow chart the label runs
  down the band rather than hiding (AECI-823). The hidden table prints the same
  label. An open period, whose value can still change, is a
  hollow point off the line.
- **Never a dual axis**, never a truncated bar baseline, never a rainbow ramp.
- **Legend for ≥2 series, none for one** (the title already names a lone series).
- **Light only.** The skill's dark column is recorded there for the Stage 2 dark
  reintroduction; Stage 1 ships no `dark:` variant.
- **Responsive via `viewBox`, never a JS resize handler.** Axis labels are HTML
  positioned by percentage over the SVG, because `viewBox` scaling would shrink
  SVG text to unreadable sizes in a narrow column.

### Navigation

The redesign rulings of 2026-11-13 and 2026-11-14 rewrite this part of §5. They are clashes 26 to 32 and 42, ruling R4 (AECI-1735), the ordering exception (AECI-1814 and AECI-1774) and ruling R2 (AECI-1733). A clash number beside a rule means that ruling set it. Where the record has Chris's own words, they are quoted. Where it says only NEW, BLEND or CURRENT, the rule follows the clash recommendation and `PLAN.md` §4.7 to §4.10. The record is `docs/design/mockups/redesign-2026-11/records/design-rulings.md` and `design-clashes.md`. ADR 0059 does not list clashes 26 to 32 or 42, so it records none of them. Colours are the tokens AECI-1747 set. They are on its branch and not yet on `main`. §2 "Forest grounds" holds every contrast floor this part cites.

**Scope (R2, 2026-11-14).** Chris: "Just do public pages for now". This part governs the public shell: the header, the Browse menu, the mobile menu, the section nav, the breadcrumb, the footer and the bands under the header. The vendor portal and the admin console page bodies take the new tokens and nothing more. Their tab rows and dropdowns are described under "Portal and admin rows" below. Since AECI-1809 the vendor portal shell uses the shared breadcrumb, and since AECI-1813 the admin shell does too (2026-11-24 rulings on AECI-1729). They wear the shared header and footer (the ruling below).

**Ruling on the shared shell (Chris, by Slack, 2026-11-14).** The site header, the legal notice band and the footer render in `app.ts` above and below the router outlet on every route, `/vendor/*` and `/admin/*` included. The question was which shell those two routes wear. Chris: "A. This is a temporary stopping point, we won't go live half built." Option A was: the portal and admin wear the shared shell. Only their page bodies, tab rows and breadcrumbs stay as they are. R2 is a staging point and not a permanent limit. AECI-1771 (header) and AECI-1776 (footer) are unblocked. Portal and admin page bodies wait until Chris has verified the public pages. A signed-in vendor or admin sees the account menu in place of Sign in, and the portal's own navigation sits below the header and is not restyled by this part.

**The header never varies by visitor in cached HTML.** The header sits inside URL-keyed cached SSR pages. It renders the signed-out state on the server, which is a "Sign in" link. `SessionStatus` swaps in the account menu after hydration. The role-gated doors read the browser-only role probe, which is `null` during SSR, so no `/admin` or `/vendor` href reaches cached HTML. The active-route marker depends on the URL, which is the cache key, so it is neutral. The Browse menu fetches its terms and counts in the browser (`TaxonomyNavStore` is idle on the server), so no taxonomy state is baked into a page. None of this changes.

#### The header on Forest (clashes 1, 26, 29 and 42)
