- **Link type.** Nav links are `--t-16` at weight 400, the ramp's "Body, nav links" role (§3). The label is Bone with no opacity change. The mock-up dims it to 92% opacity (line 42), and the `no-color-opacity-modifier` source-constraint rule (AECI-1747, on the batch branch) bars a `/NN` alpha on a colour utility. Clash 6 says every colour is a named token.
- **Link hover.** A `--surface-on-brand-wash` fill (Bone 10%) behind the link, text still Bone. A link hovered on `--surface-on-brand-wash-hover` (16%) over Forest has Bone text only (6.98:1). `--text-on-footer-secondary` is 4.46:1 on that wash and fails. It also fails on Forest hover (4.39:1), so it is never used in the header.
- **Link shape.** §4 hands the nav hover shape to this section. The mock-up's hover wash is a pill (line 42). It is a hover fill on a text link and not a button, so clash 12's "no pill buttons" does not reach it. Chris to confirm.
- **Wordmark size.** Starting value 32px high, and 28px below `md` (mock-up lines 497 and 611).
- **Every control has a spoken name.** The wordmark link's name is "AEC Integrations home". The toggle and the search field are named under their own headings.
- **Settled values** (mock-up 6, default state, settled by AECI-1771 and AECI-1774): header height `76px`, `64px` below `md` (lines 36 and 553). The row sits on the page wrap, `BAND_WRAP_CLASS`: 1328px wide, with 24px gutters, 32px from `md` and 48px from `lg` (ruling A2, 2026-11-16). Gap between header parts `32px` (36), `16px` from `lg` to `xl`. The row links keep AECI-1771's `10px` side padding and `4px` gaps. The three-link row fits at `lg` with room to spare (see "Handover breakpoint"), but the contract's `16px` padding and `8px` gap were not adopted, so the row reads the same as AECI-1771 measured it. Below `md` the header is `64px` and the wordmark `28px`. From `md` the wordmark is `32px`. Sign in is `44px` high with `16px` side padding at `14px` and weight 600 (44). `button-on-brand` has 24px side padding. The header control is the compact form of the same button, like the ghost button's 16px, so the header row stays inside its width budget.

#### Active route and the Clay bar (clash 28, new)

Chris ruled NEW on clash 28. The new design wins over the clash recommendation, which had been a 2px underline everywhere.

- **In the header the Clay bar is the panel-open cue.** The mock-up draws it under "Browse" only while the Browse panel is open. It is `scaleX(0)` until the panel opens (lines 629 to 631). It is 3px high with rounded top corners (`3px 3px 0 0`), on the header's bottom edge, 14px in from each side of the label. Clay on Forest is 5.29:1, so it meets the 3:1 floor for a meaning-bearing graphic. On Forest hover it is 3.29:1.
- **The active route in the header is weight 600 plus `aria-current`.** The mock-up draws no active-route bar. Clash 28's ruled design is the mock-up's `.cm-trigger::after`, which is `scaleX(0)` at rest and shown only while the panel is open. Weight 600 plus `aria-current` is the floor carried over for the active route and needs no ruling. `PLAN.md` §4.7 says the same. AECI-1771's description says "the active route item has a Clay bar under it", and AECI-1771 follows this part. The label reserves its 600 width so the row does not shift when the active item changes.
- **`aria-current` is `page` on an exact match and `true` for a section.** Browse is a section: it is `true` on `/products`, `/categories`, `/audiences`, `/trades`, `/phases` and their detail pages. These are the routes that lit the old items, minus Home. How we rank and For vendors are `page` on their own routes. Colour alone never shows the active route.
- **The section nav's active bar is Clay (R3).** See "The section nav". There the weight and the text colour are the other cues, because Clay is 2.33:1 on white and 2.09:1 on Bone and can never carry the state alone.
- **Portal, admin and the public in-page router rows keep their rule.** See "Portal and admin rows" below.

#### Header search (clash 29, new)

Chris ruled NEW on clash 29 and left no note.

- **A pill with a shortcut hint.** Clash 29 drew "/". AECI-1772 built "Ctrl /" (see "The shortcut" below). The radius is the full pill, the one named exception to the 12px control radius (§4). Height 44px, `--t-16` text, a `kbd` chip on the end edge. It is a field and not a link, although the mock-up draws a link (line 38). The clash audit says a link styled as a field is not a field.
- **The edge.** A 10% Bone wash (`--surface-on-brand-wash`) alone measures 1.33:1 against Forest, which is not a visible boundary. The field takes a 1px edge in `--border-on-brand-strong`, 4.01:1 on Forest. The edge stays on hover. The mock-up has no edge, so this corrects it, on AECI-1747's measurements.
- **Ink.** The placeholder uses `--text-on-brand-secondary`, 6.00:1 on the 10% wash over Forest. Hover moves the wash to 16% (`--surface-on-brand-wash-hover`) and the text goes to Bone.
- **The `kbd` chip has no fill of its own.** The mock-up gives it a second 10% wash on top of the field's (line 40). Secondary ink on that stacked wash is 4.58:1 at rest and 3.92:1 when the field is hovered, which fails. The chip is a 1px `--border-on-brand` edge only, its text is secondary ink at rest and Bone when the field is hovered, and it is set at `--t-13`. §3 allows `--t-12` only for overlines, the two weight-500 chips and field captions.
- **The shortcut is Ctrl+/ or Cmd+/, not a bare "/" (AECI-1772).** It focuses the search from anywhere on the page and prevents the key from being typed. It does nothing in an input, textarea, select or contenteditable, with Alt held, or when both Ctrl and Cmd are held. It does nothing while any dialog is open: the mobile menu, the account menu, the claim dialog and every `BrnDialog` all carry `role="dialog"`. It does nothing while the pill is not laid out (below `lg`, where the mobile menu carries search). It does nothing inside the search field itself. The `kbd` cap reads "Ctrl /" and is `aria-hidden`. The field carries `aria-keyshortcuts="Control+/ Meta+/"`, which names it once for assistive technology. The listener is a `document:keydown` host listener on `aec-site-header`. Document listeners are not replayed, so it needs no replay guard. The guard is `layout/search-shortcut.ts`.
- **Requirement: WCAG 2.1.4 Character Key Shortcuts, met by a modifier.** A single-character shortcut must be turned off, be remappable to include a modifier key, or be active only while its component has focus. The issue asked for a bare "/". A bare "/" fails all three, because the site has no setting to turn it off and a shortcut that works across the whole page is not focus-bound. Ignoring the key in a field is necessary and is not sufficient. A shortcut that already needs Ctrl or Cmd is not a character key shortcut, so it meets the criterion with no setting. That is the route built. It also works with the combobox, whose own keys (Arrow, Home, End, Escape, Enter) never take a modifier. FLAGGED FOR CHRIS. The options are Ctrl or Cmd + "/" (built), Ctrl or Cmd + K, a bare "/" with a setting that turns it off, or no shortcut. If he wants a bare "/", it needs a per-visitor setting to turn shortcuts off (a footer control or an account preference), and that setting does not exist, so it is not built. The cap reads "Ctrl /" on every platform. The handler accepts Ctrl on a Mac too, and one label keeps the cached HTML the same for everyone. Safari may claim Cmd+/ for its status bar, so Ctrl is the form that always reaches the page.
- **Shape by width (settled by AECI-1772).** The pill shows from `lg` at every width above it, and the icon trigger (`aec-search-trigger`, `lg` to `xl`) is deleted. The pill is the one flexible part of the row: `flex-1`, `min-w-60` (240px) and `max-w-[460px]`. It sits after the wordmark. The links go to the end with `lg:ms-auto`. It measures 326px at 1024 and 460px from 1280. Below `lg` the mobile menu carries search. At 768 the inline row with an icon button is 2px over, so the menu toggle holds through `md` and the mobile menu carries search. The width table under "Handover breakpoint" has the measurements.
- **Placeholder copy.** The shipped copy is "Search directory". The mock-up reads "Search products, vendors, data objects". The suggestions are products and vendors, not data objects (§5 Header search autocomplete, AECI-985), so the mock-up's label would promise more than the box does. This overrides the mock-up. Keep "Search directory" until the suggestions include data objects. Chris to confirm. The ruling did not set the copy.
- **Settled values (AECI-1772).** The pill is `rounded-pill`, 44px high, `--t-16` text. The glyph is `aec-icon` search at 20px, `16px` from the start edge, in secondary ink. The field has `48px` start padding and `80px` end padding for the cap. The fill is `--surface-on-brand-wash` and `--surface-on-brand-wash-hover` on hover, with the strong edge from `.aec-on-brand-edge`. Typed text is Bone (`--text-on-brand`, 8.31:1 on the wash). The placeholder is `--text-on-brand-secondary` through the unlayered class `.aec-on-brand-placeholder`, because the global unlayered `::placeholder` rule (tertiary grey) beats a `placeholder:` utility (6.00:1 on the wash, 5.04:1 hovered). The cap is `--t-13`, a 1px `--border-on-brand` edge from `.aec-on-brand-keycap` (a hint, not a control), `rounded-mark`, no fill, secondary ink at rest and Bone while the field is hovered. The native clear button on `type="search"` is hidden in the pill because it would sit under the cap. The ring is the Bone ring, 3px at 3px, through `focusRingClass`, and the listbox panel stays a white panel with Forest rings. `SearchAutocomplete` takes `pill`, `shortcutHint`, `keyShortcuts`, `fieldClass`, `inputHeightClass` and `focusRingClass`, and every other mount keeps its defaults.

#### The Browse menu (clash 27, new, and ruling R4)

Chris ruled NEW on clash 27: one mega-menu replaces the four taxonomy dropdowns. The clash recommendation had been to drop the page dimmer. Chris chose the new design, so the page dims.

**Ruling R4 (AECI-1735, 2026-11-14).** Chris, by Slack: "A".

- No "How we rank" card in the menu.
- Four key categories in this order: AI, Project management, BIM authoring, Accounting and ERP.
- Seven items per column.
- No key items for trades, phases or audiences.

The card is dropped, not only switched off. The mock-up's card (lines 666 to 688) and its mobile twin are not built. A production check on 2026-11-14 (a comment on AECI-1735) found 69 AI, 62 project management, 15 BIM authoring and 92 accounting and ERP products. All four are above the floor of 5, so the ruling stands. If a key category ever has fewer than 5 products, it goes back to Chris. The mock-up's counts (29, 58, 64, 52 and 486) are placeholders and are never copied (`PLAN.md` §10.4). The menu prints each term's name as the site prints it everywhere else. Term names are data and not menu copy, so the Sentence-Case Rule does not reach them. R4's capitalisation is the list of categories and not a rename.

**Key categories are AECi's own pick, and the site must say so.** The pick is editorial. `/docs/trust/how-ranking-works` must say so, and the menu footer line says the same. This is a same-PR rule. AECI-1815 says each sentence on the help page must be true of the shipped code at merge, so the page cannot go first. The PR that ships the Browse menu carries the `how-ranking-works` sentences with it. AECI-1774 added them as the page's "The Browse menu" section. They cover the key categories being AECi's pick and each column's order, including the accepted count-order exception for audiences and trades. The gate covers the key-categories column and not only the footer line. The issue was filed with a `text` legal verdict. AECI-1774's plan check found no `/legal/*` sentence the menu makes false: the Vendor Terms promise "No plan changes where you rank or appear", and the menu names no listing. So the verdict under `docs/LEGAL_IMPACT_REVIEW.md` is `none`, and the help-page sentences are this same-PR rule, not a legal text change. *Superseded 2026-11-26 (AECI-2230):* the Vendor Terms now promise "Payment never changes the order in which products are listed or returned in search", and say a paid plan can put a product on more category pages (counsel ruling 65).

**The columns and their order.** Each order has a source. Nothing here is invented.

| Column     | Content                                                           | Order                                                                                         | Source                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ---------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Explore    | "All products" with the total count, then the four key categories | AECi's pick, not a count order: `ai`, `project-management`, `bim-authoring`, `accounting-erp` | Ruling R4. Held as one config list of slugs, not a table (AECI-1774). The slugs are from the AECI-1735 comment                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Categories | Seven categories, with any key category removed first             | Most products first (`product_count`, descending), then name with `compareText`, then id      | R4 says seven and `PLAN.md` §4.8 says "less any shown as a key category". Today's nav ranks by count with `topByCount` (`core/taxonomy/taxonomy-rank.ts`, top 10) and has no tie-break. The name tie-break is AECI-1774's. A Free seat can tag categories (`product.categories.edit`, `PLAN.md` §7.2), so no paid plan moves this order |
| Phases     | All 5                                                             | Project order: `taxonomy_phases.display_order` ascending, then name with `compareText`        | A curated order, `byDisplayOrder` in `taxonomy-rank.ts`. Not a count order                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Audiences  | Seven                                                             | Most products first, then name, then id                                                       | The accepted exception, below                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Trades     | Seven published trades                                            | Most products first, then name, then id                                                       | The accepted exception, below. The `TRADE_PUBLISH_MIN_PRODUCTS` floor still applies, as in the nav today                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

Phases have five terms, so seven does not apply to them. Each facet column ends with its own "See all" link to the facet index.

**The audience and trade order is an accepted exception to the no-pay-for-placement firewall.** Chris, by Slack, on AECI-1814 and AECI-1774: "A, create a ticket to address later". Audience and trade tags need `product.taxonomy.edit`, which is outside the Free set. A Managed vendor tagging a product can lift a term into the top seven. It moves a term and never a product. The firewall test records it as one named exception, and the permanent rule is AECI-1836. The menu orders audiences and trades by product count, as the nav does today. This is a known gap and not a finding that count order is right. AECI-1836 may change it, and this part follows its answer.

**The menu footer line.** One line under the columns: "Key categories are chosen by AEC Integrations. Other lists show most products first. Phases in project order." It stays true only while every non-phase list is count-ordered and the key list is the config list.

**Not in the menu.** No vendor or product names. No "Popular", "New" or "Featured" badges (`PLAN.md` §4.8).

**The behaviour contract** (`PLAN.md` §4.8, point for point):

1. "Browse" is a real link to the all-products page (`/products`). It also controls the panel, with `aria-expanded`, `aria-controls` and `aria-haspopup`. `PLAN.md` names `aria-haspopup`. This part drops it, below, so the trigger carries `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls` only.
2. Hover opens the panel. The down arrow opens it and moves focus to the first link.
3. Escape closes it and returns focus to "Browse". Mouse leave closes it after a short grace period.
4. A press inside the panel must not close it before the click lands.
5. The page behind is dimmed while the panel is open.
6. On mobile the menu shows "All products" and the key categories first, then four tap-to-expand sections.

Added by this part. All of it is CURRENT unless it says otherwise.

- **The pattern is disclosure navigation, not an ARIA `menu`.** "Browse" is one link that carries `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls`, over a `[hidden]` panel of links with no role. `layout/browse-menu.ts` builds it, and `browse-menu.component.spec.ts` pins the attributes, the down arrow and the Escape focus return. The panel needs no landmark role, and each column's list is named by its overline through `aria-labelledby`.
- **`aria-haspopup` is dropped.** The Browse panel is disclosure navigation of links (the WAI-ARIA Authoring Practices disclosure pattern). The trigger carries `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls`. `aria-haspopup="true"` announces a menu that is not there. AECI-1774 repointed the Escape selector in `NavDisclosure` to the first `[aria-expanded]` in the host, which is the trigger in both implementors, and the specs now assert that `aria-haspopup` is absent.
- **Built on `NavDisclosure`** (`layout/nav-disclosure.ts`), so Escape, focus leaving the host and the Safari press guard behave as they do today. A blur to nowhere during a pointer press inside the host does not count as focus leaving, because Safari never focuses a clicked link and closing on that blur loses the click. AECI-1774 deleted `nav-flyout-trigger.ts`. AECI-1775 deleted `nav-flyout-list.ts`: the mobile menu draws its sections with counts in `nav-menu-panel.ts`.
- **Open for the accessibility gate (AECI-1818):** whether a separate disclosure button beside the "Browse" link would serve screen reader users better than the down arrow on the link.
- **The keyboard reaches the panel by the down arrow.** Enter on "Browse" follows the link. The footer's Directory column is the Tab-reachable copy of the same destinations, and it renders in server HTML.
- **Angular Aria popups render inline in this repo, not in an overlay.** The Browse panel is plain inline markup under the header, which is what `NavDisclosure` expects. A panel in the browser's top layer is not a DOM descendant of the host, so every focus move into it would read as focus leaving.
- **The dimmer is not modal.** It is `aria-hidden` and decorative. It does not trap focus or make the page inert. Clicking it closes the panel. It sits under the header and over the page. Clash 6 does not allow a raw colour in a component, so the dimmer reads `--scrim`, `rgb(0 0 0 / 0.4)`, the mock-up's value (lines 15 and 690). Chris ruled NEW on clashes 27 and 30, so 40% is ruled for the menu dimmer. The CDK modal backdrop is a different token, `--scrim-modal` at 50%, because §4 pairs the modal shadow with a 50% backdrop (AECI-1770, §5 "Overlays"). The mobile menu has no dimmer today. The dimmer is a sibling of `<header>` at `z-30` under the header's `z-40`, so the Forest bar stays bright.
- **Timing (settled, AECI-1774).** Hover opens after 70ms and the pointer leaving closes after a 260ms grace (`src/catmenu.js` lines 19 and 15). The grace lives in the shared base: `NavDisclosure` has `openDelayMs` and `closeGraceMs`, and the Browse menu sets 70 and 260. They default to 0, so the admin console's row (`admin/admin-nav-dropdown.ts`) keeps its shipped instant open and close. The admin row keeps 0 for now (the orchestrator, 2026-11-14). Whether it takes the grace too is flagged for Chris under the Same-Behavior Rule. Escape anywhere on the page closes a hover-opened panel, so it is dismissible without moving the pointer even when focus is outside the menu.
- **WCAG 1.4.13 Content on Hover or Focus.** The hover-opened panel is additional content shown on pointer hover, so it must be dismissible, hoverable and persistent. Dismissible: Escape closes it without moving the pointer. Hoverable: the 260ms close grace lets the pointer cross from "Browse" into the panel. Persistent: it stays open until the pointer leaves, focus leaves, or Escape is pressed. A grace of zero would fail the second property.
- **Touch and the fallback at `lg` and above.** A tap on "Browse" follows the link to `/products`, because the trigger is a real link and hover does not exist. Touch visitors at `lg` and above reach the facets from the footer's Directory column and from `/products`. Below `lg` the mobile menu carries every column.
- **Reduced motion: no animated menu transitions.** Under `prefers-reduced-motion` the panel, its columns, the dimmer, the active bar and any chevron change state instantly (mock-up lines 187 and 720 to 723). Durations and easing belong to §5 "Motion".
- **The hover underline on a menu term is Clay,** as the mock-up has it (lines 653 and 658). A hover underline is not required information, and Clay as a stroke is allowed by clash 4. The term text stays `--text-primary` and the underline never carries the only cue for focus, which is the ring.
- **Counts are quiet tabular numbers** at `--t-13`, in `--text-tertiary` on the white panel (4.83:1). Never on a Bone or sunken surface. The count reads "1 product" or "N products" under the name. If the total is `undefined` (an older API Worker during a deploy), render "All products" without a count. Never print "undefined" (AECI-1773, AECI-1774). Phases show all five, so a phase with no products reads "0 products". The orchestrator kept that on 2026-11-14.
- **Edge cases.** A category, audience or trade with 0 products never appears (production has `robotics` at 0), and that includes a key category. Phases are the exception: all five always show. A configured key slug the API no longer returns is skipped, and the column shows the rest.
- **The focus ring inside the panel is Forest.** The panel is a white ground, although it sits inside the Forest header. Bone is invisible there. It is 3px at a 3px offset with an 8px radius (`rounded-mark`).
- **The panel body is lazy and still server-rendered (AECI-1774).** The initial bundle had under 2 kB of headroom. The body (`layout/browse-panel.ts`) sits in `@defer (on idle; hydrate on idle)`. Incremental hydration renders it on the server in full, so cached HTML carries "All products", every column label, every "See all" link and the footer line, hidden until opened. The browser fetches the chunk and hydrates the body as soon as the page is idle, not when the panel first opens, so a reader rarely meets it unhydrated. The terms and counts wait for the first browser render, so a body hydrated after the taxonomy fetch starts from the same empty lists the server sent.
- **A click never waits for the lazy body.** Every panel destination is a plain `<a href>` with no listener and no `RouterLink`, so the server HTML carries no event-replay marker inside the body. The eager panel box in `browse-menu.ts` catches the click, closes the menu and routes in the app. A modified or middle click is left to the browser. Before the app itself hydrates, a click follows the href as a full page load, as every link on the site does. If the chunk fails to load (a tab left open across a deploy), the server-rendered links stay and still route through the panel box. The terms simply never arrive. `e2e/nav-menu.spec.ts` pins a slowed chunk (first click and Enter both navigate) and a blocked chunk (links still navigate).
- **If `/api/taxonomy` fails,** the panel keeps what the server rendered: "All products", the labels and the "See all" links. Nothing in the header waits on the API. The menu adds no request: `TaxonomyNavStore` made the same one browser-side call for the old flyouts.
- **No JavaScript-only dead end.** "Browse" is a real link to `/products`. The hidden panel's index links are in the server HTML for crawlers. The footer's Directory column carries `/products`, `/categories`, `/audiences`, `/trades` and `/phases` visibly on every page. `e2e/nav-menu.spec.ts` pins both with JavaScript off.

**Panel type (clash 10, current, on the clash 9 ramp).** This replaces the old three-level panel hierarchy. Column labels are the overline (`.aec-overline`: 600, `--t-12`, uppercase, `--text-secondary`). The mock-up's 11px menu overline is dropped (§3). A destination is weight 400 at `--t-16` in `--text-primary`, pinned at the list component. The row's weight never reaches a panel. A key item is serif at `--t-24`, weight 400 (line 651). Each term's count sits under it at `--t-12` in `--text-tertiary` (2026-11-23). The old "column title, 600, 14px" level has no place in the Browse panel. Any other nav panel keeps the pin: destinations at 400, because 500 belongs to two chips only (§3). AECI-1774 moved the account menu's destinations (Account, Admin portal, Vendor portal, Sign out) and the mobile menu's "See all" links to 400. The identity block's name line is weight 600 (AECI-1774, the orchestrator's ruling of 2026-11-14). It labels the account and is not a destination, so the 400 pin does not reach it.

**Settled values** (AECI-1774, from mock-up 6, default state, line numbers in `6-product-detail.html`. The mock-up's 760px maps to `md` and its 1040px to `lg`):

| Value            | Starting value                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Line     |
| ---------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------- |
| Panel            | Full width under the header edge, `--surface-base` fill, radius `0 0 24px 24px`, `--shadow-menu`                                                                                                                                                                                    | 632      |
| Panel padding    | `48px` top, `32px` bottom. The sides use the header's page wrap (`BAND_WRAP_CLASS`: 1328px, 48px gutters from `lg`, ruling A2), so the Explore column lines up with the wordmark. The mobile panel uses the same wrap (24px below `md`, 32px at `md`) and drops to 16px below 320px | 634      |
| Grid             | Five equal columns (`grid-cols-5`), column gap `24px` from `lg` and `32px` from `xl` (1280px). The mock-up's 1200px step maps to `xl`. Columns do not share rows, so a wrapped term pushes only its own column down                                                                 | 644, 697 |
| Overline         | `12px`, `16px` below                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | 640      |
| Key item         | Serif `24px`, line-height `1.1`, weight 400, tracking `-0.01em`, padding `2px 0 12px`                                                                                                                                                                                               | 650, 651 |
| Term row         | `16px`, weight 400, line-height `1.25`, padding `8px 0`                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | 655, 656 |
| Count            | `13px`, tabular figures                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | 657      |
| Menu footer line | `13px`, `24px` above, `16px` padding over a hairline                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | 664      |

The mock-up's columns need about 1,130px of its 1,216px row (comment above line 667). AECI-1774 met the floor with five equal columns that let terms wrap. Measured on 2026-11-14 at 1024px: the panel is 1024px wide, its scroll width equals its client width (no sideways scroll) and the document does not overflow. Long names such as "Construction Management" wrap to two lines. The same holds at 1280 and 1440px.

#### The mobile menu (clash 30, new)

Chris ruled NEW on clash 30. Two floors stay whatever the ruling (`design-rulings.md`, `PLAN.md` §9.2, ADR 0059 floor 2): the toggle has an accessible name, and the open menu traps focus. AECI-1775 built it. The code is `layout/nav-menu.ts` (toggle, open state, trap, panel box), `layout/nav-menu-panel.ts` (the lazy body) and `layout/nav-menu-account.ts` (the lazy account block).

- **The toggle.** 44 by 44px, `rounded-control` (12px), a `--surface-on-brand-wash` fill, a Bone glyph (11.03:1), and the 1px `.aec-on-brand-edge` (4.01:1) AECI-1771 gave it. It sits at the end edge of the header (mock-up line 46). The glyph is `menu`, and `x` while open (lines 694 and 695). The wash is decoration. The glyph and the edge identify the control.
- **A spoken name.** The toggle's name is "Menu", as the mock-up has it (line 1196). `aria-expanded` carries the state, so the name stays true while the glyph is a close mark. The toggle also carries `aria-controls`. It has no `aria-haspopup`.
- **The open menu is a modal dialog that holds its own toggle.** While open, the `aec-nav-menu` host is `role="dialog"`, `aria-modal="true"` and named "Menu". The host holds the toggle and the panel, so the close control is inside the dialog. A screen reader on a phone has no Escape key, and it can always reach the close mark. Closed, the host has no role.
- **The focus trap.** Tab from the last control goes to the toggle. Shift+Tab from the toggle goes to the last control. Focus moves to "All products" on open.
- **The page behind is inert.** Every sibling of the host and of each of its ancestors up to `<body>` takes `inert` on open: the wordmark and the rest of the header row, the skip link, `main` and the footer. A `MutationObserver` watches those same parents while open, so an element added later beside the path (a toast, a banner) goes inert too. Close removes exactly what open set, late additions included. The page dimmer (`data-nav-scrim`) and the CDK overlay container are left alone.
- **The header's right-hand group hides while open (decision C).** The Sign in button or the account trigger is inert behind the open menu, and between `md` and `lg` it would show a second, dead Sign in beside the menu's own. It takes `invisible` while the menu is open, so its space is kept and the toggle does not shift, and it returns on close. The orchestrator chose this on 2026-11-14. Chris to confirm.
- **Escape closes it and returns focus to the toggle.** So does a click on the dimmer. An open search suggestion list takes the first Escape. A route change closes the menu.
- **Growing to `lg` closes the menu (decision B).** At `lg` the host is hidden, so a menu left open would trap focus in a dialog no one can see and keep the page inert. The orchestrator chose this on 2026-11-14. Chris to confirm.
- **Focus after the menu closes by navigation (AECI-1855).** A tap on a menu link closes the menu and the router takes over. Focus then moves to the new page's `h1`, or to `<main>` when the page has none, without scrolling, as after any other link on the site. That is the site-wide rule (`ANGULAR_STYLE_GUIDE.md` §18 "Focus after a navigation", `core/route-focus.ts`), and the Browse menu follows it too. A link to the page already showing lands focus on its `h1` the same way. Growing to `lg` hides the focused control and the inline row has no single counterpart to the toggle, so focus that was in the menu goes to the page's `h1` there too. The exception is a persistent section row (the admin row, the vendor portal row, marked `data-route-focus-keep`): a pressed link there keeps focus, because the row and the heading above it stay put.
- **Background scroll is locked while open.** `overflow: hidden` on the root element, set on open and removed on close. Setting and removing the lock never moves the page and never touches the router's scroll restoration. Focus can move it: opening focuses "All products" and closing focuses the toggle, and the browser scrolls a focused element into view when it is off screen. Where the page shows a classic scrollbar, `scrollbar-gutter: stable` keeps the header from shifting. The panel scrolls inside itself with `overscroll-behavior: contain`.
- **The inline dialog replaces `BrnPopover` (decision A).** An earlier version of this part said to keep the shipped `BrnPopover`. That line was replaced. The popover's dialog had no name (axe `aria-dialog-name`), its focus trap left out the toggle, and its content mounted on the first click, so it had to sit in the initial bundle. The inline panel fixes all three. The orchestrator chose this on 2026-11-14. Chris to confirm.
- **Order.** "All products" and the key categories first. Then four tap-to-expand sections in the desktop column order: Categories, Phases, Audiences, Trades. Then How we rank and For vendors, then the footer line, then search, then the account block (the identity block below, then Account, the role-gated doors and Sign out) or the Sign in button. The mock-up has no search and no account block.
- **The same data and the same orders as the desktop menu.** Every list comes from `TaxonomyNavStore.browse` (`browse-menu-model.ts`), with the same seven-item cap. The mobile body sorts and filters nothing, so there is one ordering path and the firewall test covers it. Phases show all five. The key categories label and list render only when the list is not empty.
- **Sections.** Each section is a button with `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls`. Its region is `[hidden]` when collapsed, and its list is named by the button through `aria-labelledby`. Each region ends with its own "See all" link. The chevron turns on expand over the base duration. It sits on a wrapper because `aec-icon`'s host is `display: contents`. Under reduced motion it does not animate.
- **Counts** are the desktop counts: "1 product" or "N products" at `--t-13`, tabular, in `--text-tertiary` on the white panel (4.83:1), at the end of the row. One function writes them for both menus (`browseCountText` in `taxonomy-nav-copy.ts`). If the total is `undefined`, "All products" shows no count.
- **The footer line** is the desktop line, word for word, at `--t-13` in `--text-tertiary` under For vendors. The `how-ranking-works` sentences shipped with AECI-1774, so the same-PR rule is met.
- **The panel is white, not Forest.** The panel is `--surface-base` with ink text, radius `0 0 24px 24px`, `--shadow-menu` and a 1px edge on three sides, under the Forest header strip (mock-up line 701 and the archived screenshot `browse-menu-mobile.jpg`). The issue's "Forest ground, to match the header" reads as the header strip. The focus ring inside is Forest, 3px at a 3px offset. The two buttons take the white-ground variants: "Sign in" primary and "Account" secondary, both full width.
- **Destinations are plain links.** Every body destination is a plain `<a href>` with no listener and no `RouterLink`. The eager host catches the click, closes the menu and routes in the app, as the Browse panel box does. A modified or middle click is left to the browser. Sign in and the account block use `RouterLink`, because they exist only after hydration.
- **The body is lazy and server-rendered.** `nav-menu-panel.ts` sits in `@defer (on idle; hydrate on idle)`. Cached HTML carries "All products", the section buttons, every "See all" link, How we rank, For vendors and the footer line, hidden until opened. Terms and counts wait for the first browser render. A tap on a link before the body hydrates, or after its chunk failed, still navigates. A tap on a section button before the body hydrates is queued by event replay and opens the section once the body hydrates. `e2e/nav-menu.spec.ts` pins a slowed chunk and a blocked chunk.
- **Search is mounted only while open,** so the server HTML keeps one search input, the header's. Its field is 44px high, as the header pill is.
- **The suggestion list fits the screen.** The field sits low in a panel that scrolls while the page is locked, so a list that only opens downward ran off a phone screen (one of ten suggestions visible at 390 by 844). The menu sets `SearchAutocomplete`'s `fitToViewport` input. The list opens below the field when there are at least 240px of room there, and otherwise on whichever side has more room. Its height is capped to that room less an 8px margin, never above the shipped 24rem, and it scrolls inside. The room is measured against the visible viewport, so a phone keyboard counts as taken space. The panel is a `cdkScrollable`, and the list uses the reposition scroll strategy, so it stays attached to the field when the panel scrolls. The header pill, the home hero and the claim dialog leave the input off and keep their shipped placement.
- **Cached HTML is visitor-neutral.** The server renders the closed menu, the hidden panel and the "Sign in" link. The account block is lazy (`@defer (on immediate)`) and renders only after the browser-side session probe. The portal doors and the badge read the browser-only role probe.
- **Settled values** (mock-up 6, lines 701 to 718, default state): `max-height: calc(100dvh - 64px)` below `md` and `calc(100dvh - 76px)` from `md`, matching the header heights, with `overflow-y: auto` and `overscroll-behavior: contain`. Side padding is the header's `32px` gutter, so the menu lines up with the wordmark. The mock-up's 24px phone gutter was not adopted. Top-level rows are serif 600 at `20px` and 58px high. "All products" and the key categories are serif 400 at `24px` and 48px high. Section items are `16px` at 400 and 44px high, with the count on the same line. "See all" is 44px high. A hairline separates the top-level rows. Search and the account block sit 24px apart below the footer line.
- **Reflow.** The menu works at 320px, at 200% zoom on a 320px screen and on a landscape phone. Nothing in the panel is cut off sideways, and the panel scrolls down inside the viewport. `e2e/nav-menu.spec.ts` pins all three.

#### The section nav and the sticky bar (`PLAN.md` §4.9, R3 and clash 13)

The section nav is the in-page jump bar on the product page (`shared/section-nav/section-nav.ts`). AECI-1768 built it on 2026-11-14. The product page is its only caller. The vendor portal's integration page draws its own side rail and does not use it.

- **It leads with our wordmark.** When the bar sticks it reads "AEC Integrations › Bluebeam Revu": the AEC Integrations wordmark (`aec-brand-logo`, linked home and named "AEC Integrations home"), then the product name. Never a vendor logo, a product mark or a vendor colour, on any listing. A vendor logo where our header has scrolled away would read as the vendor's own site. On an unclaimed listing that contradicts "not affiliated with or endorsed by this vendor" (`PLAN.md` §3.9 and §8.5). The component has no input that could carry a logo. A component spec and an e2e check that every image in the bar is one of ours.
- **The brand shows only while the bar is stuck.** At rest the header's own home link is on screen, so the bar adds none. The server render is never stuck, so cached HTML carries neither the wordmark nor the name. The cluster overlays the start of the row and the links slide clear of it by a `transform`, so sticking moves nothing in layout (CLS 0 at 1280 and 390px, pinned in `e2e/section-nav.spec.ts`).
- **The product name stays in the stuck bar,** as text beside our wordmark. Counsel's answer to Q61 (2026-11-17, `docs/LEGAL_COUNSEL_RECORD.md`) allows it without the not-affiliated line, so long as the bar carries no "official", no vendor colour or styling and no claim language.
- **The stuck name goes back to the top (AECI-2020).** It is a link to `#top`, which the browser scrolls to the document start with no element. It carries `data-smooth-scroll`, so it animates like the section links and is instant under reduced motion. Its accessible name is "{name}, back to top", which starts with the visible name (WCAG 2.5.3). It has no `data-section-id`, so a click sets no highlight. The bar returns to rest at the top, and the first link is current again. Same `--text-secondary` ink, hover to `--text-primary`, the Forest focus ring.
- **No "Unclaimed" tag in the bar (AECI-2012).** Ruling 41 put a one-word "Unclaimed" chip after the name on an unclaimed listing as a trial (AECI-1931). Chris removed it on 2026-11-23, at every width: "let's remove it from the sticky bar for now, but still show." The bar takes no claim status. An unclaimed product keeps the Unclaimed note in the hero, under its name. Counsel called the tag optional (Q61), and ruling 41 says removing it needs no counsel question. `e2e/section-nav.spec.ts` checks the stuck bar on an unclaimed and a claimed product at 1440, 768, 390 and 320px.
- **Items are plain text links,** not a button group. Chris disliked the nav looking like one (`PLAN.md` §3.9). `--t-16` at 400 in `--text-secondary` (7.73:1 on white). Hover goes to `--text-primary`. Each item is 44px high with 12px side padding.
- **The current item** is weight 600 in `--text-primary` with a 3px bar on the bar's bottom edge. The bar colour is **Clay**. Ruling R3 (AECI-1734, item 14, 2026-11-14): Chris, by Slack, "A", accepting all six recommendations. Item 14 reads "The section nav active bar is Clay."
- **The bar is never the only cue.** Clay is 2.33:1 on white and 2.09:1 on Bone. The weight and the text colour also mark the current item, and the link carries `aria-current="location"` (clash 28 and R3). Each label reserves its 600 width, so the current item never shifts its neighbours. The first link is current until a click or the scroll-spy names another, and SSR renders that first link for every visitor. When the bar returns to rest, the first link is current again. Under forced colours the bar draws in `CanvasText`.
- **The bar grows in place; it does not slide.** The current item's bar scales in over the slow duration (§5 "Motion") and is instant under reduced motion. The mock-up's one bar that slides between items (line 182) needs a layout read in script on every change. AECI-1768 built the in-place form, with no script. Chosen by the orchestrator (2026-11-14) and flagged for Chris. A slide is a follow-up if he wants it.
- **Counts are quiet tabular numbers,** not badges. `--t-13` at 400 in `--text-secondary`, with a 6px gap after the label (§3 puts counts at `--t-13`). The mock-up sets them at 14px and in tertiary ink (line 181), which fails on Bone. The product page passes counts for Extensions and Reviews. Integrations carries none, and since 2026-11-16 the section heading carries none either, so the page never shows two figures that disagree. A count is a fact about the section, never an order or a paid-plan input.
- **The surface is solid. No blur, no shadow.** Chris, clash 13: "Drop the frosted-glass blur on the sticky bar." The bar is `--surface-base` with a 1px `--border-default` hairline under it, at rest and stuck (mock-up lines 167 and 732). The hairline is a separator and not a control edge. AECI-1747's `--surface-sticky` (white at 94%) is therefore unused. The mock-up's stuck-bar shadow (line 145) is dropped under the one-shadow rule (§4). On the product band shell the bar is clear at rest and solid only once stuck (`rest="clear"`, AECI-1778, see §4). The default, `rest="solid"`, keeps the shipped look.
- **Size and place.** `position: sticky; top: 0`, `z-index: 20`. 72px high, and 64px below `md`, plus the 1px hairline. The header is not sticky, so the bar is the only bar at the top. The stuck wordmark is 24px high. The product name is `--t-16` in `--text-secondary`, capped at 200px with an ellipsis, and it gives way before the links do. Below `md` the stuck bar shows our monogram at 32px and no name (mock-up lines 576 and 577). The monogram is its own home link inside the section nav, so the header's `aec-brand-logo` (initial bundle) is unchanged. CSS displays one of the two links at any width. At rest the first label lines up with the page column.
- **It never hides a focused control (WCAG 2.4.11).** Two rules in `apps/web/src/styles.css`, both pure CSS, so the server HTML carries them. Every focusable element after the bar in its container takes `scroll-margin-top` of the bar's height plus 8px, so a link or button reached by Tab scrolls clear. A section's own `scroll-mt-20` (80px) still covers the anchor jump. `--aec-sticky-offset` holds the bar's height for anything else that sticks below it: the product page's metadata aside sticks 32px under the bar, and only on viewports at least 800px tall. The tallest seeded aside is 669px, and below that height a sticky aside lost its foot. Below 800px it scrolls with the page. The unlayered focus rule beats any `scroll-mt-*` utility on a focusable element after the bar. It is not root `scroll-padding-top`. Root padding adds to the section margin on a native jump and in `InitialFragmentScroller`, but not in `ScrollMarginViewportScroller`, so the three would land on different pixels. The router scroll stack is unchanged. An e2e test fails if the margin is removed.
- **It does not clip between 900 and 1300px** (`PLAN.md` §10.2). Overflow scrolls sideways inside the row, with a 40px fade at each edge that has more links past it. The row hides its scrollbar, as the mock-up does (chosen by the orchestrator, 2026-11-14, flagged for Chris). Tab and a phone swipe reach every link. The current link, and any link that takes focus, is scrolled fully into the row, clear of the fades and the brand cluster (the row only, never the page). Keyboard focus wins: while a link has focus, a later scroll-spy update or the stuck cluster arriving reveals the focused link, never the current one (AECI-1778 fixed a stuck bar at 320 and 390px that scrolled the row back to Reviews after Tab reached About). That scroll reads layout, so it waits while a smooth jump or a router navigation is in flight. The page still reflows at 320px. The e2e suite checks 320, 390, 640 (200% zoom), 900, 1100 and 1300px.
- **The focus ring is Forest,** 2px, drawn inside the item (radius 8px) because the scroller clips an outline outside it (mock-up line 173). Forest is 12.34:1 on white.
- **Links keep the query (AECI-1843).** Each href is the current path and query plus `#id`, from `injectInPageHrefFor()` in `shared/in-page-href.ts`. A click on a URL with `utm_*` is a same-document jump, never a page load. The server render keeps only the params the edge cache key keeps, so every request on one cache key gets byte-identical nav markup. The docs "On this page" rail and the vendor integration page use the same helper.
- **"Write a review" is not on the bar.** The mock-up has a button there above `lg` (line 1334). No ruling covers it, and the hero already carries the action. AECI-1768 left it out, chosen by the orchestrator (2026-11-14) and flagged for Chris.
- **Other in-page navs** (the account page's section nav, the docs "On this page" rail and the vendor integration page's rail) keep their own look and are not restyled here.

#### The breadcrumb (clash 31, current)

Chris ruled CURRENT on clash 31 with no note. The separator is `›`, marked `aria-hidden`. The mock-up's slash is dropped.

- **One breadcrumb (AECI-1777, built).** `aec-breadcrumb` (`shared/breadcrumb/breadcrumb.ts`) renders a `nav` named "Breadcrumb", ancestors as links, the last item as text with `aria-current="page"`. Callers pass `BreadcrumbItem[]` (`label`, optional `link` and `fragment`) and start with `homeCrumb()`. It serves `products-index.ts`, `taxonomy-index.ts`, `taxonomy-browse.ts`, `search-page.ts`, `product-detail.ts`, `vendor-detail.ts`, `products-pair.ts` and the docs shell. `DetailLayout`'s `breadcrumbs` slot is now a plain wrapper, so the component owns the one landmark. A last item that carries a link is a link with no `aria-current`: the docs top bar ends an article's trail on its section.
- **Inputs.** `tone="on-brand"` is for the hero band's `heroCrumbs` slot. The product hero uses it (AECI-1778). `truncate` keeps one line with an ellipsis, for the docs top bar. The default wraps.
- **On white** the links are `--text-secondary`, the separator is `--text-tertiary`, and hover goes to `--text-primary` and underlines, so colour is never the only cue. Ancestor crumbs are always secondary ink.
- **A visible change in AECI-1777: ancestor links are grey now.** On `/products`, `/search`, the taxonomy index and browse pages, and the pair page, the ancestor links were Forest green. They had no colour class and picked up the base-layer link colour. They are `--text-secondary` like the product and vendor pages, because one component serves all of them. Hover underlines.
- **On the Forest hero** the links and the separator are `--text-on-brand-secondary` (7.96:1 on Forest). The tertiary separator the code uses today is never allowed on Forest. Hover underlines the link and goes to Bone (`--text-on-brand`). `--t-14`, an `8px` gap, wrapping, and the current item is text and not a link (mock-up lines 54 to 57 and 1251).
- **The docs top bar draws `›` now** (it drew a slash until AECI-1777), and its current crumb is regular weight and no longer semibold, like every other trail (decided by the orchestrator, flagged for Chris). The waitlist welcome keeps the strong edge through `.aec-notice-neutral`, matching the legal band (also flagged for Chris). The `/taxonomy` trail is sentence case now and no longer uppercase.
- **Portal and admin breadcrumbs keep their rule** (R2). They already use `›`.

#### The shell bands and the skip link

The app shell (`app.ts`) renders, in order: the skip link, the header, the legal notice band, the waitlist welcome, `main`, the footer, the consent banner and the update banner. AECI-1777 restyles each to the new tokens and `aecButton`. No band's wording or behaviour changes.

- **The skip link is the first focusable element in the document** (WCAG 2.4.1). It appears above the Forest header on focus. The mock-ups have none and the app keeps it (`PLAN.md` §10.2). Its shipped Forest fill and Forest ring are invisible against the header. On focus it takes a Bone fill with Forest text (11.03:1) and a Bone ring (`--focus-ring-on-brand`), 3px at 3px, which leaves a Forest gap. It is 44px high, `--t-16` at 600, with a 12px radius (AECI-1777, settled).
- **What AECI-1777 changed in the bands.** Almost nothing visible, because AECI-1760 had already moved their buttons onto `aecButton` and the bands already used role tokens. Text sizes moved from `text-sm` to `text-t-14`. The waitlist welcome edge moved from a `border-default` utility that never applied to `.aec-notice-neutral`, the legal band's strong edge. The consent and update banners take `.aec-popover` since AECI-1770: white fill, a 1px hairline, a 12px radius and the menu shadow. AECI-1777 left their surface alone and changed only their text size. Widths and gutters are untouched.
- **What stays visitor-neutral.** The legal band renders in SSR from the date and never from the visitor. The waitlist welcome, the consent banner and the update banner render nothing in SSR and reveal after hydration.
- **The legal notice band is shell chrome.** It is a site-wide, full-width Bone band under the header, with a strong bottom border (§5 Vendor portal, AECI-1592). It does not count as the page's one Bone band. This is the orchestrator's reading of 2026-11-14, and Chris is to confirm. Its wording and its dismiss behaviour do not change. It relates to the legal page versions in `docs/STAGE_1_SPEC.md` §27.
- **The stack is a Forest header over a Bone band** on every page while the notice is live. The band is not part of the header.
- **Every control on a Forest ground,** in a banner or elsewhere, takes the Bone ring and Bone ink. A Forest ring is invisible.

#### Portal and admin rows (R2, unchanged)

These rows are outside the public shell and keep the current rule.

- **Active route.** Colour `accent-primary` with a 2px underline in `accent-primary`, drawn on the element and not as a side stripe. In the admin console's category row (AECI-694) the form is `-mb-px border-b-2` over the row's `border-b`, coloured by the unlayered `.aec-nav-tab` class. Since AECI-2088 a `border-(--accent-primary)` utility applies too. The vendor portal's section row moved to a drawn `::after` bar at weight 600 with AECI-1809 (see "Vendor portal (Stage 2)"). Narrow viewports scroll the row (`overflow-x-auto whitespace-nowrap`).
- **The `/search` tabs keep the Forest underline (ruling 9 of 2026-11-16).** Clay is 2.3:1 on white, so it cannot be the only state cue. The selected tab also takes weight 600 (AECI-1796). The docs guide toggle uses the same underline. The section nav uses a Clay bar, and the header marks the active route by weight and `aria-current`. Whether the docs guide toggle stays on the underline is still Chris's call under the Same-Behavior Rule.
- **The admin row is the one exception to the scroll rule, and it is a consequence and not a preference.** With three items it fits a 320px viewport, and `overflow-x-auto` computes `overflow-y` to `auto` too, which would clip its in-flow dropdown panels. It wraps. Any row that both scrolls and drops down has to portal its panels.
- **A child route level replaces its parent's row.** The vendor portal tried a segmented secondary row under the underlined primary row (AECI-959) and retired it (§6.11). Show one underlined row for the level the page is at, and let a breadcrumb carry the way back up.
- **Dropdown base.** `NavDisclosure` is a shared base. A new dropdown in the public primary nav extends it. The trigger shape is the implementor's. The admin console's row (`admin/admin-nav-dropdown.ts`, AECI-694) keeps a `button` trigger with no arrow icon. It is deliberately not `@angular/aria/menu`, because a navigation row of router links is not an application menu and `role="menu"` is the wrong semantic. `ANGULAR_STYLE_GUIDE.md` §19 and ADR 0010 carry that clarification. The arrow stays on form controls and on the mobile menu's expand rows.
- **A category with exactly one destination is a plain link,** not a one-item dropdown. Label it with the group name. Keep the rule structural (`items.length`).
- **A dropdown whose panel contains a text field does not hover-open.** It opens on click, focus goes into the field, Escape or Tab closes it and returns focus to the trigger, and an outside click closes it. It cannot use `NavDisclosure` mechanically, because a panel in the top layer is not a DOM descendant of the host.
- **Grouped nav lists are separated by vertical rhythm, never by indentation.** A destination sits on the same left rail as its group label. Roughly 12px between items and 26px at a group boundary tells a reader where a group ends. The live instances are the mobile menu's account block and the admin console's panels. Do not indent items under their label, because an indent reads as tree depth.

#### Handover breakpoint and the width budget

The inline row appears from `lg`. Below `lg` the toggle carries navigation (`aec-nav-menu` is `lg:hidden`). Clash 29 asks for `md`: the mock-up shows its inline row from 760px, which maps to `md`, and its search pill from 1040px, which maps to `lg`. The row is held at `lg`. AECI-1771 measured the shipped six-item row, and AECI-1774 measures the three-link row when it lands.

- **The old measurement is retired.** AECI-669 measured a six-item row at 768px and found it 127px over. The row now has three links, so that table no longer applies. The mock-up's own handover has not been measured in code.
- **A new primary destination is a re-measure at 1024px,** not an insert. Three classes move together if the handover moves: the row's `lg:flex`, `aec-nav-menu`'s `lg:hidden` and the search pill's `lg:block`. A mismatch renders two Primary landmarks, or none, or leaves a band with no search.
- **Re-measured for ruling A2, 2026-11-16,** signed out (the wider case), on Forest, after the header moved to the page wrap (24, 32 and 48px gutters, 1328px wide). AECI-1772 measured it first on the 7xl canvas with 32px gutters. The row is the wordmark (209px), the pill, the three links (300px: Browse 73, How we rank 113, For vendors 106, 4px gaps) and Sign in (77px). The pill takes what is left, between 240px and 460px. The handover stays at `lg`. The content box is the window minus both gutters. Chromium in the test run draws a 15px classic scrollbar, so its measured box is 15px narrower. The table gives both.

  | Width                           | Content box              | Fixed parts                                                    | Pill                   | Result                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
  | ------------------------------- | ------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
  | 768px                           | 704px (689px measured)   | wordmark 209, Sign in 77, menu toggle 44, two 32px gaps: 394px | none                   | Unchanged: the gutter is 32px at `md` before and after. Fits with 310px spare in the menu-toggle layout, which carries search. AECI-1774 measured the inline row at `md` at 650px with no search. An icon button makes it 706px, 2px over, so the menu toggle holds through `md` |
  | 1024px                          | 928px (913px measured)   | wordmark 209, links 300, Sign in 77, three 16px gaps: 634px    | 294px (279px measured) | Fits on one row. The pill is `flex-1`, so it takes what is left. It is 39px above its 240px floor with the scrollbar, 54px without. Every part shares one top edge                                                                                                               |
  | 1280px                          | 1184px (1169px measured) | wordmark 209, links 300, Sign in 77, three 32px gaps: 682px    | 460px (the cap)        | Fits with 42px spare (27px measured), which sits between the pill and the links                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
  | 1440px (the row caps at 1328px) | 1232px                   | as 1280px                                                      | 460px                  | Fits with 90px spare                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |

  Signed in, the account trigger (44px) replaces Sign in (77px), so the pill is 33px wider at 1024 (327px) and the spare at 1280 is 75px. That is an estimate from the signed-out measurement, because a session cannot be minted in the dev stack. The pill is the flexible part, so the row cannot overflow at either state. The header was measured at 768, 1024, 1280 and 1440 on 2026-11-16: one row, with no horizontal overflow of the row or the document. At 390 the header is 64px with no pill, and 76px from `md`. AECI-1771's six-item row needed 505px for the links alone.

- **The link row "Browse, How we rank, For vendors" shipped with AECI-1774.** Home and Products left the row: the wordmark goes home and "All products" is in the Browse menu. The four facet flyouts (`nav-flyout-trigger.ts`) were deleted. Their indexes are the Browse menu's "See all" links and the footer's Directory column.
- **The focus ring is a plain utility.** Since AECI-1845 the global `:focus-visible` colour sits in `@layer base`, so `focus-visible:outline-(--focus-ring-on-brand)` works with no `!` and no component CSS. The class sets are in `layout/header-link-classes.ts`. The header search field takes that set through `SearchAutocomplete`'s `focusRingClass` input, so it carries one ring set only.
- **Reflow.** Nothing clips at 320px or at 200% zoom, and a text label never wraps inside the header row.

**The Overflow Rule (rewritten by clash 26).** The primary row is width-budgeted, public-only and closed. It now carries three things: Browse, How we rank and For vendors.

- **A new secondary destination goes to the footer,** not into the row and not into a header menu. The footer is server-rendered on every page, so a link there is as reachable and as crawlable as one in the header. See "The footer" below. How we rank and For vendors are now also header links, and they stay in the footer.
- **A new primary destination is a re-measure at 1024px,** not an insert.
- **A role-gated surface gets one door in the account menu** (`layout/user-menu.ts` at `lg` and up, the mobile menu's account block below). Never a row item.

**Why the doors are in the account menu, not the row.** The row is the site and the avatar is you and what you can operate. The row renders on cached, URL-keyed, indexable pages and must look the same to everyone. A private, `noindex`, role-gated door in it would make the row's width depend on who is looking, against the closed width budget. The account menu is already the viewer-dependent region. It only mounts when signed in, and it already held the Vendor portal door. Putting Admin beside it makes the two portals symmetric.

**Both doors are one link each**: "Admin portal" to `/admin` and "Vendor portal" to `/vendor`, in sentence case. User-facing copy says portal, never dashboard (`STAGE_2_VENDOR_PORTAL_SPEC.md` §6.3). Neither restates its portal's sections. The pending-review badge sits on the account-menu trigger, following the Admin door. AECI-1771 measured its pairing on Forest: white text on the error fill, with a 2px Bone ring (`--text-on-brand`, 11.03:1 against Forest) as its edge.

**Nothing role-gated may reach server-rendered HTML.** Both doors are gated on the shared, browser-only role probe (`auth/role-status.ts`), which reports `null` during SSR. This is the header-side complement to `/admin/*` being non-cacheable.
