### The account menu's identity block (AECI-850)

The account menu opens with **who you are**, then the things you can do. Source:
`apps/web/src/app/layout/account-identity.ts`, one component rendered by **both**
the desktop dropdown (`user-menu.ts`) and the mobile menu (`nav-menu-account.ts`) — an
identity block composes three sources and gets the wrong answer _quietly_ when
duplicated, which a link list does not.

**Anchor: Laravel Cloud**, with the role pill from **Hootsuite**. Both are
departures from the Stripe site-chrome anchor (see "The footer"), recorded here
because the Anchor-Site Rule requires the anchor to be recorded with the surface.
The departure is deliberate and narrow: Stripe's own account dropdown puts name +
role in a **dim footer** below Sign out, which reads as metadata rather than as an
answer to "which account is this?". Laravel Cloud's top block — avatar, name,
email, hairline, destinations — states it first. Nothing else on the surface moves
to Laravel; composition, density and atmosphere elsewhere stay Stripe's.

| Row       | Content                                                                  | Rule                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| --------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Avatar    | 32px **circle** (`LogoOrInitial` `size="sm" shape="circle"`)             | A person is a circle; every company mark on the same page is a rectangle. That is the only thing distinguishing them at 32px                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Primary   | `profiles.display_name` → provider `full_name` → the email → "Signed in" | Never empty: an empty line collapses the reserved box and the menu jumps under the cursor                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Secondary | The email, at `text-secondary`                                           | **Dropped entirely** when the email is already the primary line, so the block never prints one string twice                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Pill      | Site admin / Vendor admin / Reviewer                                     | A dense `aec-chip` (AECI-1763): 13px at weight 600. Site admin and Vendor admin take the Forest-soft fill with Forest text and a Forest edge. Reviewer takes the sunken fill with secondary text and a default edge. Rendered for **every** role including the `reviewer` default. On a review platform "Reviewer" is a standing, not an empty state, and a staff-only pill would make the block's height depend on who is looking |

**Rules.**

- **The initial letter is the primary design; the photo is the enhancement.** Only
  Google sign-ins carry `user_metadata.avatar_url`, and magic-link is the majority
  path — so "no photo" is the common case. Design the block with the letter first
  and check the photo second, not the reverse.
- **The avatar stays neutral, never an accent.** It inherits `LogoOrInitial`'s own
  tokens verbatim — `text-primary` on `surface-raised` inside a `border-default`
  ring — so it reads as quiet chrome rather than as a highlight. Only the pill may take
  colour, and only Forest (`accent-primary-soft` fill, `accent-primary` text), and
  only for the two roles that open a portal door. Colour is never the sole signal
  — the label beside it names the role.
- **`referrerpolicy="no-referrer"` on every provider photo.** Hotlinking
  `lh3.googleusercontent.com` otherwise tells Google which AECi page a signed-in
  visitor is on, on every page load. Trust-first positioning does not permit that
  for a decorative 32px image. `img-src 'self' data: https:` already allows the
  load, so no CSP change was needed.
- **The trigger keeps its neutral glyph until a photo exists.** It does _not_ swap
  in an initial letter: the role is cached in `sessionStorage` but the email is
  not, so a letter would flicker in on every single page load. The photo lands
  once, with the async session snapshot, and only for Google accounts.
- **A photo that fails to load returns to the glyph, in both places.** The panel's
  avatar gets this from `LogoOrInitial`; the trigger has its own `(error)` handler.
  `user_metadata` only refreshes at re-authentication, so a long-lived session
  outlives the Google CDN URL it carries, and an empty `alt` makes a browser
  collapse a broken image to nothing — an empty circle where the account control
  should be. The failure is per-URL, so a fresh snapshot gets a fresh attempt.
- **The block is not focusable and carries no `role`.** It labels the menu that
  follows it. A link here would add a tab stop in front of "Account" for no new
  destination.
- **Nothing here reaches cached HTML**, by the same guarantee as the portal doors:
  `SessionStatus` is neutral through SSR and `RoleStatus.role()` is `null` until
  the browser-only probe resolves.
- **The panel is `w-64`, up from `w-56`.** The email is the widest string it
  carries; at 14rem it truncated to roughly fourteen characters and told the
  reader nothing.

### The footer

It was never specified until the Overflow Rule made it load-bearing. It is the home for every secondary destination and the only site-wide entry point for several of them. Source: `apps/web/src/app/layout/site-footer.ts`. The columns are pinned by `site-footer.component.spec.ts`, because a link quietly dropping out is a regression and not a tidy-up.

**The ground is Forest (clashes 1 and 32).** Chris ruled BLEND on clash 32 with no note. The blend is the clash recommendation: accept the Forest ground, and keep the four shipped columns and their overline labels. This rewrites the old "white `surface-base` on every page" rule. The mock-up's own columns (Browse, How we work, Company, Legal, with Vendors, Data objects, Attestations and Cookies added and the Help column gone) are not adopted. `PLAN.md` §4.10 says "The footer sits on a dark Forest ground. It keeps the four shipped columns, Directory, Help, Legal and Company, with overline labels."

**Anchor:** the redesign's mock-ups, which draw the footer (clash 44, §2 "The redesign's anchor"). The shipped layout stays: a brand region (the wordmark and a one-line tagline) beside a nav group of four labelled `<nav>` columns, over a bottom strip carrying the copyright, separated by a hairline. The layout is unchanged. The nav group is its own responsive grid (2 columns below `md`, 4 from `md`) so the columns stay balanced. Three columns at `sm` would orphan the fourth. From `lg` the brand sits left and the nav group right. The nav group does not shrink (`shrink-0`). From `md` its four columns size to their content (`repeat(4,auto)`), not equal fourths. At 16px links, equal fourths measured 181px each at 1024px and crushed the brand to 110px. Content-sized columns total about 604px, so no link label wraps and the brand keeps its width (AECI-1776, measured at 375, 768, 1024 and 1280px). The mock-up's grid steps (five columns above 1040px, two below) are not adopted, because clash 32 does not rule on layout.

**The wrap (ruling A2, Chris, 2026-11-16).** Both footer rows sit on the page wrap (`BAND_WRAP_CLASS`): 1328px wide, with 24, 32 and 48px gutters, the same edge as the header and the bands. At 1024px that leaves a 928px row, 32px less than the old 7xl canvas. The four columns still sit on one row there, and no link label wraps (every link box 24px high, measured at 768, 1024, 1280 and 1440px). The brand's tagline gives up the width.

**Colour on the footer ground** (`--surface-footer`, `#15291F`):

| Part                                      | Colour                                                    | Contrast |
| ----------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------- | -------- |
| Ground                                    | `--surface-footer`                                        |          |
| Links and the copyright                   | Bone, `--text-on-brand`                                   | 13.71:1  |
| Column labels (overlines) and the tagline | `--text-on-footer-secondary`                              | 8.76:1   |
| Hairline above the bottom strip           | `--border-on-brand-subtle` (Bone 14%), decorative, 1.51:1 |          |
| Focus ring                                | Bone, `--focus-ring-on-brand`                             | 13.71:1  |
| Wordmark                                  | The light-on-dark variant (AECI-1754)                     |          |

- **Link hover is an underline in Bone,** 3px offset (mock-up line 474). Colour never carries it. `--text-on-footer-secondary` fails on Forest hover (4.39:1), so the footer has no hover fill. The mock-up's links are Bone at rest (line 473), and the shipped footer's `text-secondary` rising to `text-primary` is retired.
- **Tertiary text is never on the footer ground** (§2).
- **Column labels are overlines** (`.aec-overline`, §3), in `--text-on-footer-secondary`. The mock-up's labels are `h4` and skip heading levels (`PLAN.md` §10.2). The shipped labels are `p` inside `nav` landmarks that carry an `aria-label`, so the footer skips no level. Settled by AECI-1776: the labels stay `p` with the overline style, and the footer has no headings.
- **Wording does not change.** The mock-up's pitch ("Integrations in architecture, engineering and construction, reviewed by the people who use them.") and its base line ("Rankings are algorithmic. No vendor pays for a place.") are invented copy and are not adopted. The shipped tagline and copyright stay. A trust sentence in the footer needs its own check against the legal and trust pages first.
- **Values, settled by AECI-1776** (mock-up lines 467 to 476, 497 and 498, measured at 375, 768, 1024 and 1280px): padding `64px` top and `48px` below the grid, label margin `16px` below, `12px` between links, links at `16px` on a `24px` line box (`text-base leading-6`, so a link is 24px high), the bottom strip `24px` above its copyright and `32px` below, copyright at `14px`, wordmark `28px` high. The nav columns are `repeat(4,auto)` from `md`: at 1024px and above they measure 76, 132, 181 and 91px wide, at 768px 102, 158, 207 and 117px. Below `md` it is a 2 by 2 grid. No link wraps from `md` up. Below `md` a long label wraps inside its column, with no overflow and every link at least 24px tall. The hairline is `aec-on-brand-rule` with `border-t`. The focus ring on a footer link is `focus-visible:outline-3 focus-visible:outline-offset-3 focus-visible:outline-(--focus-ring-on-brand)`, Bone, plain utilities.
- **Targets** are at least 24 by 24 CSS px (WCAG 2.5.8). Link rows with a 12px gap clear it at 16px text.

**The four columns, and why each holds what it does:**

| Column        | Holds                                                                                                          | Why                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Directory** | Home, Products, Categories, Audiences, Trades, Phases                                                          | The primary surfaces, in **server-rendered HTML**. The header's facet values render client-side and its mobile menu never reaches SSR, so this is where a crawler meets the taxonomy. With Home and Products gone from the header (clash 26), it is also the Tab-reachable list of the Browse destinations.                                                                                                 |
| **Help**      | Help center, Getting started, How ranking works, Vendor guide                                                  | The footer-only entry into `/docs` (AECI-1252, `docs/STAGE_2_PRODUCT_DOCS_SPEC.md` §7 question 3). "Vendor guide" links `/docs/vendors/overview`, the vendor guide's first page (AECI-1253). It is not called "For vendors", because that is the Company column's link to `/vendor-program`. "How ranking works" is also reached from the header's "How we rank". Four items, inside the four-to-six range. |
| **Legal**     | Terms, Privacy, Review guidelines, Listing accuracy, Vendor terms, Managed plan agreement, Third-party notices | Trust-first positioning means the legal set is one click from every page, not buried.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| **Company**   | About, Methodology, For vendors, Contact, Updates, Roadmap                                                     | Who we are and where we are going. Methodology sits next to About because it is the same kind of destination (AECI-804). "For vendors" is the public vendor landing page `/vendor-program`, after Methodology (AECI-1283, `VENDOR_PROGRAM_PAGE_SPEC.md` §2.4). The header now links For vendors too (clash 26). Methodology, Updates and Roadmap stay footer-only. Six items is the column's ceiling.       |

Nothing in the footer's links changes (AECI-1776: "keep the four columns and every link exactly"), with one addition: "Third-party notices", a plain `href` (same tab, not a route) to the static `/third-party-notices.txt`, last in the Legal column. It carries Lucide's licence text (AECI-1753) and changes no `/legal/*` page.

**Rules.**

- **Every column is a `<nav>` with an `aria-label`.** Four unlabelled navs in one landmark are indistinguishable in a screen reader's landmark list.
- **The footer is visitor-neutral, absolutely.** It sits inside URL-keyed cached HTML and holds no session state: no portal door, no account link, no badge. That is what lets it render identically for everyone. Role-gated affordances live in the account menu (the Overflow Rule). The vendor portal and admin console show this same footer under their own pages (Chris, 2026-11-14, see "Scope" above).
- **Vendors and Integrations are deliberately absent** (AECI-160, PO decision. AECI-165 removed the index pages, which now 301 to `/products`). Their detail pages stay reachable via product links, `sitemap.xml` and search.
- **i18n ids are shared, not duplicated.** Several links reuse `@@app.nav.*` and `@@app.footer.*` ids that other surfaces also emit. Keep the tight `<ng-container i18n>` wrap so the extracted source string stays byte-identical. An identical source under a shared id is one translation unit, and a differing one is a collision. If the header and the mobile menu drop their Home and Products links, the footer is the only emitter of those ids.
- **Adding a column** is a re-measure at every breakpoint. Prefer growing an existing column. Four to six items read fine. The Help column (AECI-1252) was the exception because the help center is a destination of its own kind. It moved the grid from 2 to 3 to 2 to 4, measured at 375, 768, 1024 and 1280px.

### Layout shells

Three reusable Angular shells (`apps/web/src/app/layouts/`) — every Phase 2 page projects body content into one of them via named slots (shadcn-style composition). No state, no inputs; structure only. Anchor inherited from the AECi site chrome (Stripe — see Named Rules).

Until clash 16, all three shared the same outer container. DetailLayout now has its own band shell (below, and §4). The Browse shell moved to the 1328px page wrap in AECI-1793 (ruling 3 of 2026-11-16, below). The form pattern keeps the **standard canvas** `mx-auto w-full max-w-7xl px-6 md:px-8` with `py-8 md:py-12` (the Page-Width Rule, §3): `max-w-7xl` centered, `surface-base` background, `text-primary` text, 24px horizontal / 32px vertical padding on phones, 32px / 48px on `md` and up. Section dividers use `border-default` (1px on the redesigned components, 0.5px on shipped surfaces not yet rebuilt). The canvas itself takes no shadow.

- **DetailLayout** (`<aec-detail-layout>`) — for the product and vendor detail pages. **Rewritten by clash 16 as a band shell for the product page** (new). AECI-1778 builds it. AECI-1797 moved the vendor page onto it. §4 "The detail page band shell" holds the rule, the band order and the starting values. This bullet keeps the slot contract.
  - **Slots:** `breadcrumbs`, `hero` (required), `nav`, `body-lead`, `metadata`, `body` (required). All six survive. On the band shell (`shell="bands"`, AECI-1778) `breadcrumbs` is not rendered, `metadata` is the facts card position, and `body-lead` is the About band. Each slot is declared once in a template and placed by an outlet in the active shell.
  - **Grid, product and vendor pages:** no sidebar column. The hero band is Forest, the facts card overlaps its lower edge, and the body is full-width bands (§4). It keeps one column at every width, so DOM order is the reading order.
  - **The sticky in-page nav gets its own slot.** `nav` is projected **bare** (no wrapper element) as a direct child of the page container. `position: sticky` is bounded by the parent's box, so a nav inside a body column unpins as soon as that column scrolls past. On the column shell its bottom margin therefore lives on the nav element itself. On the band shell the bands carry the spacing, and the layout gives the nav host the band wrap's gutters as padding (AECI-1778). Never wrap that slot. The nav always marks one link `aria-current="location"`: the first section until a click or the scroll-spy names another. SSR renders that same first link for every visitor, as the docs "On this page" rail does (AECI-831). A clicked link stays current until the reader next scrolls or types, even when its section is shorter than the scroll-spy's band (AECI-1856). In print the nav and the metadata rail are `static`, never `sticky`, so neither prints over the page (AECI-1856).
  - **Tables in a band** are checked against the content column, the viewport minus both gutters (§4). The old 608px check and the 38rem floor came from the sidebar split (AECI-853) and no longer apply on the product or vendor page.
  - **Vendor page (AECI-1797):** on the band shell with no `body-lead`, so the About band does not render and the facts ground is its one Bone band. §4 "The vendor page on the band shell" holds the parts. The old column layout is in git history at commit `5acfeda50`.
  - **Internal rhythm:** bands carry their own padding and separate by a hairline between two white bands (§4). The standard canvas above describes the Browse and form shells, which the rulings did not change.

- **The integration card** (`products/match-card-recipe.ts`, AECI-1835 and AECI-1720) is one roomy card for one integration, from mock-ups 5 and 6 (`.card`) with more room after the owner's reviews of 2026-11-18. It is a class set, like `integration-filter-recipe.ts`, because its users' contents differ. Two users: the empty pair page's closest-match card, and the product page's integration card (`product-integration-card.ts`) in the Integrations section, its category groups, its one list and its Via lane cards. The connector page's hub cards and "Other connections" cards take its compact variant. The card (`aecCard`): 24px padding on a phone, 32px from `md`, 20px between blocks (`MATCH_CARD_BODY_CLASS`). The head: the 48px mark, the name at the Title role (`--t-24`, 600, on a 1.1 line, one step above the mock-ups' 20px; on the product card the size sits on the partner link, because a heading's unlayered rule beats a size utility on the heading), "by {vendor}" at `--t-13` under it. Each labelled block ("Shares with {swap}" and "Moves with {kept}" on the match card; "Categories", "What moves" and "How it connects" on the product card) is an overline over its content, with 4px more above it than the card gap. Chips: 31px, 13px at 600 on a 1.6 line, the sunken fill, a 14px glyph where the icon map has one (`tag` for a category, the data-object map for data; mechanism kinds have no glyph), wrapping with 8px gaps; a picked data object takes Forest soft with an inset Forest ring. The foot is the pair link, its stretched target the whole card, 4px above it, ending in a 34px round sunken button with a chevron, never an arrow (the Arrow Rule). The product card replaces the foot with a short muted hint at the end of its name row (`MATCH_CARD_HEAD_LINK_CLASS`, AECI-2021, ruled 2026-11-23): "View details" at `--t-13` in `--text-secondary` and a 16px chevron, no round button, named "View details for the {partner} integration" with the rest visually hidden, so the card is shorter. The match card keeps the foot. The match card adds the mock-up's `.rates` score box: `--surface-raised`, the default edge, `rounded-control`, 16px padding, 8px rows, each score at `--t-16` with its stars, the review count under them. A label never wraps. Where the box is too narrow for a label, its stars and its score on one line (below `sm`, and from `md` to `lg` in two columns), every row stacks the stars under its label, so no row is two lines beside a one-line row. "Confirmed by both companies" is a Forest-soft pill with a dot. The product card keeps its own parts: the Via rows, the "Under review" markers, the agreement label (`aec-agreement-badge`, every state except "Listed by AEC Integrations" since AECI-2021), the direction words, and its block order from mock-ups 6 and 7. **The compact variant** (`MATCH_CARD_COMPACT_BODY_CLASS`): 24px padding at every width and 16px between blocks, the name at the Card title role (`--t-20`), the same chips and the 34px chevron button at its top end. It is for a card nested inside a group card on the connector page, which keeps its three-column `.aec-card-grid`, so a nested card is neither cramped nor larger than its group. **The grid** (`.aec-integration-grid`, `styles.css`): one column below `md`, two from `md`. A third column would need every card at least 400px wide, a 1,248px list, and the page wrap caps a list at 1,232px, so the grid stops at two. Mock-up 6 draws three 395px cards at 1440, and mock-up 7 three 300px cards beside its jump rail; both are tighter than this rule, so the rule wins. One card takes the row (`data-columns="1"`). The product card grids (`.aec-card-grid`) are a different pattern and keep three columns.
- **Hero band** (`<aec-hero-band>`, `shared/hero-band/hero-band.ts`, AECI-1766) — the shared frame of a detail page's Forest hero. The product page fills it through `aec-product-hero` (`products/product-hero.ts`, AECI-1778), which `DetailLayout`'s `hero` slot places full bleed. It is one component for every surface and fills no content of its own. Slots, as attributes on projected elements: `heroCrumbs` (above the two-column grid, full width, as mock-ups 6 and 12 draw `.crumbs` before `.hero-grid`, so a tall panel never pushes it down: AECI-1720 restore C measured it at 170px on the product page and 210px on the pair page against the mock-ups' 100px; with no panel it sits right above the kicker, where it sat before), `heroKicker` (the chip row), `heroMark`, `heroTitle`, `heroByline`, default content (lede, ratings), `heroActions` and `heroPanel`. `heroMark` is a logo left of the name in a 104px box, 68px below `md` (mock-up 6, `.hero-mark`), and the projected element fills it. `heroByline` sits under the name, in the same row as the mark. `heroTitle` takes inline content. The band renders the `h1`, which holds only the title, and sets Display (`--t-display`, weight 400, tracking -0.02em) on an inner `span`, so the unlayered `h1` rule cannot beat it. **A page that uses the hero band renders no other `h1`** (AECI-1778 removes the product page's current one). The panel is `--surface-hero-panel`, `rounded-card`, 24px padding and 32px from `md`. At `lg` it is a 400px right column and from `xl` a 540px one, and below `lg` it stacks under the text. The 400px step is AECI-1787's: at 1024px a 540px panel left the product name about 230px beside the mark, and "Fixture Acme Connector" broke mid-word. With nothing projected it is not rendered and the column does not exist. **Spacing follows mock-up 6** (`.namerow`, `.hacts`, lines 63, 87 and 556): 24px above the name row at every width, 16px under it (12px below `md`), 24px above the actions and a 12px gap. From `md` the actions are a wrapping row at their own widths. From 480px to `md` they are equal columns of one row (`grid-flow-col auto-cols-fr`, AECI-1778), because `flex: 1` did not equalise them: measured at 320px, "Visit website" was 148px and "Write a review" 98px and wrapped. Below 480px they stack in one full-width column (AECI-1780). Two columns there wrapped "Sign in to review" to two lines at 390px, 60px tall beside a 44px "Visit website", and the swap from the server's "Write a review" shifted the page on hydration. A projected wrapper must let its button fill it, as `aec-review-cta` does. The mock-up's mobile name row keeps 24px above, so the brief's 16px above the name below `md` was not followed. DESIGN.md has no other rule for it. **Inside the band** a plain link is Bone (`--text-on-brand`) and hover underlines it with a 3px offset, so colour is never the only cue. The base-layer link colour is Forest (1.00:1 on Forest), and the band overrides it in the base layer, so a caller's utility still wins. Every focus ring inside the band is Bone, 3px at 3px, set once in the base layer, and a chip in the band gets the same ring through an unlayered chip rule, because the chip's own ring is unlayered and Forest. A white surface inside the band, such as a card, sets its own Forest ring with the plain utility. **Aligning the columns (AECI-1720 restore C).** From `lg` the text column is centred against a taller panel by default, as mock-up 12 draws the pair page beside its two product cards. The `align` input set to `start` pins it to the top instead, as mock-up 6 draws the product page: the kicker and the name sit just under the breadcrumb, however tall the panel is. Only the product hero sets it. Pages with no panel (the vendor page) have one column, so neither setting moves them. **Holding the columns** (item 82) is retired: `holdColumns()` and `releaseColumns()` served the category hub's "Show all", and went with the hub (AECI-2018). The page wrap, padding and glow are the "Open values" entries above. Not adopted: `plan-cta.ts` is a Forest feature card, not a detail page's hero (clash 1), so it stays as it is (see §2 "A Forest feature card"). `home-trust-pillars.ts` was a mid-page Forest band. AECI-1800 took it off its full-bleed Forest band, because the hero band renders the page's `h1` and Forest may not fill a content page's body as a band. Its three commitments are now `/about`'s one Forest feature card (ruling 2026-11-17). `panelFrom="wide"` (AECI-1835) gives a panel drawn at a fixed 540px its own column from 1040px, as mock-up 5 does, top-aligned, and stacks it under the text below that. The default `lg` is unchanged. `titleSize="statement-wide"` is mock-up 5's statement beside that 540px column (`--t-statement-wide`, `clamp(2.35rem, 3vw + 0.75rem, 3.5rem)`: 47.4px at 1180px and 55.2px at 1440px as the mock-up draws it, with a 37.6px floor instead of its 34px so it doubles by 300% zoom, worst case 3.0). `bottom="band"` ends a hero that nothing overlaps with a plain band's 64px, 96px from `md`, instead of the default 128px kept for a card that pulls up over the band.
- **Product hero** (`<aec-product-hero>`, `products/product-hero.ts`, AECI-1778): the product page's hero, in its own file so the other product-page issues do not collide. Built values:
  - **Kicker row:** the role chip, for a connector or hybrid only. The maintenance chip with its date (R3 decision 12) is retired by AECI-1666: its words open "About this listing". Chips keep their light fill on Forest. There is no "Product" eyebrow. The breadcrumb already says Products, mock-up 6 drops it, and it cost a line at 320px.
  - **Name row:** the mark (`aec-logo-or-initial size="hero"`) and the name at Display, 72px at 1440px. Below `md` the band steps the name to `--t-statement` (37px at 390px), and below 360px to `--t-fig` (28px), so a long word such as "Management" or "Construction" fits beside the 68px mark (AECI-1720 restore, mock-up 7 at 390px). The `h1` keeps `overflow-wrap: break-word`, so a word breaks only when it cannot fit on a line of its own. Checked with "Construction Management Platform Enterprise" at 320, 360, 390, 768, 1024 and 1440px (`e2e/products-detail.spec.ts`). The vendor page's name takes the same steps. The mark carries mock-up 6's soft ring: a 4px `--border-on-brand-subtle` ring and the card shadow. Its radius is still open (AECI-1750). The band's `h1` is the page's only `h1`, and its text is the product name, as before.
  - **Byline:** "by {vendor}" at `--t-16`. The vendor link is Bone with a permanent underline (`--border-on-brand-strong`), weight 400, as an in-text link. With no vendor there is no byline. The unclaimed note sits under it. The logo correction is in "About this listing" (AECI-1666).
  - **Ratings:** with reviews, one panel on `--surface-hero-panel` with a `--border-on-brand-subtle` edge and `rounded-card`. It holds Product quality, then Onboarding experience (mock-up 6's names, AECI-1720 restore C; the stars' accessible names say the same words), each at `--t-fig` serif 600 with its stars (mock-up 6 `.hr-n`), then the review count in its own cell: a link to `#reviews` with a 16px down arrow, at least 44px tall. From `sm` the three cells sit in a row with hairlines between them. Below `sm` the two scores share the first row with a hairline between them, and the count takes its own full row under a hairline (mock-up 6 at 760px), so it never floats beside a score (AECI-1720 restore). With 1 to 4 reviews it holds "Not yet rated" and the count. With none it is one plain line, "No reviews yet", because a lone bordered box read as a button. Numbers, one-decimal rounding and the five-review rule are unchanged. The 48px score figure belongs to the reviews band (AECI-1784).
  - **Contrast on the panel:** Bone 9.18:1, `--text-on-brand-secondary` 6.62:1, the gold star 4.59:1, the empty star 6.95:1.
  - **Actions:** from 480px to `md` the band lays them out as equal columns of one row, so both buttons are the same width. Below 480px they stack full width (AECI-1780). "Visit website" is `on-brand`, the page's one filled button. "Write a review" is `on-brand-secondary`. Its signed-in "already reviewed" state takes Bone text and the Bone ring on Forest. The website's under-review line sits just above the buttons. Its correction line, and the `min-h-10` reserve it kept against a font-swap shift (0.0675 on `revit` at 390px), left with AECI-1666: the correction is in "About this listing".
  - **On-brand markers:** `aec-under-review` takes `tone="on-brand"`: `--text-on-brand-secondary` text. `aec-field-caption` keeps the same tone input, with a source link on a `--border-on-brand-strong` underline and the Bone ring, but since AECI-1666 no hero mounts it.
  - **Lede (AECI-1720 restore C, mock-up 6 `.lede`; summary AECI-2198).** Three states. **A summary** (`products.summary`, one line of at most 155 characters) is the lede: printed whole, never clamped, at `--t-20` / 1.5 in `--text-on-brand-secondary`, 32em wide at most, stepping down to `--t-16` at its 1.6 line height below 640px, as the vendor hero lede does (ruling 77). It is text no other part of the page carries, so it is **not** `aria-hidden`. **A summary AECi hid while it checks it** shows its "Under review" line in the lede's slot (§11d.7), with no fallback text under it. **No summary:** the lede is the first paragraph of the stored description, split as About splits it, its lines joined by a space. It sits under the name row at `--t-20` / 1.5 in `--text-on-brand-secondary`, 32em wide at most, clamped to two lines (`line-clamp-2`). The clamp is CSS on server-rendered text, so nothing moves on hydration. It never adds words: a description that runs past two lines ends in an ellipsis, and About, about one screen below, prints it whole. Because About reads it in full, the lede is `aria-hidden`, so a screen reader hears the description once. No description, or one AECi hid while it checks it, prints no lede and no gap.
  - **Font-swap shift (fixed by AECI-1892).** On an unclaimed product at 1280px the web font's swap re-wrapped a facts-card row above the fold, and the page's layout shift rose from 0.0096 to 0.0327. The size-matched fallback faces (§3 "How the fonts load") took it to 0.000 with the fonts delayed 1.5 s.
  - **The panel holds the integrations wheel** (AECI-2018, which replaced R3 decision 1's category hub and pair finder). On a connector it shows the products its pairs reach. A connector with nothing on record gets the panel with the powered section's empty sentence. Any other product with no integration on record has no panel. §5 "The hero diagram" has the built values.
- **Bone band** (`<aec-bone-band>`, `shared/bone-band/bone-band.ts`, AECI-1766) — the page's one Bone band, a frame with projected content and no named slots. Values from mock-ups 6 to 8 (`.band`, lines 203 to 206 and 579 to 580): the 1328px wrap with 24, 32 and 48px gutters, 96px of padding above and below, 64px below `md`. The `first` input is the mock-up's `.band.first`: 48px above, 32px below `md`. The `flushEnd` input drops the bottom padding, for content that closes the band itself: the home hero ends on its credibility row, 24px above the band's edge (mock-up 9, AECI-1792). The ground is `--accent-warm`. It has no border in normal rendering, since Bone against white is 1.12:1 and the mock-up draws none. Under forced colours it gets a 1px `CanvasText` edge above and below (`.aec-bone-band`, unlayered). It does not move and reads no visitor state.
  - **Ink and controls on Bone, measured:** `--text-primary` 17.70:1, `--text-secondary` 6.91:1, Forest 11.03:1 (a ghost button's hover text and the default Forest focus ring), error 5.84:1, `--text-tertiary` 4.32:1 (never on Bone). A form control's edge `--border-field` is 3.31:1, and its `--surface-base` fill is 1.12:1 against Bone and 3.71:1 against that edge. The primary button is Forest on Forest hover and unchanged. A secondary button brings its own `--surface-raised` fill and hover `--surface-sunken`, so its label is 18.97:1 and 18.01:1 on its own fill. Its edge `--border-strong` is only 1.32:1 on Bone and its fill is 1.07:1. That edge is as weak on white, and the label names the control (WCAG 1.4.11), so it is flagged for Chris and not changed here. A ghost button has no fill and no edge. `--surface-on-brand-hover` is the hover of the Bone button on Forest, so it is never used on Bone.
  - **Seam with the Forest footer:** a flat edge, no gap and no hairline. Bone against the footer ground is 13.71:1, so the step is itself the boundary. The specimen sits last on `/_demo/spartan` to show it.
  - **One per page is a usage rule.** In dev mode a second `aec-bone-band` in the document logs an error after the first browser render. Production never logs, and the check does not run on the server.
  - **Live Bone-ground surfaces, not moved by AECI-1766.** Page grounds, which a later issue moves onto the band or onto white:
    - The home page moved in AECI-1792: the hero is on `aec-bone-band` and the closing call to action is a white card on a white band.
    - `shared/mailing-list-signup/mailing-list-signup.ts`: on the band shell it is white (§4 item 6), and on `/roadmap` since AECI-1801, whose hero is the Bone band. Elsewhere it is the page's Bone band, drawn with the `aec-bone-band` class and the same wrap and rhythm, not the element, so the dev check counts only page bands. Since AECI-1799 its card is the shared `aecCard`. Since the AECI-1720 restore the card splits from `lg`: the overline, heading and body on the left, the form on the right with a 48px gap, so the card never leaves its right half blank. The two halves are equal at `lg`, and from `xl` the form takes a 32rem column. A 32rem form at 1024px left the words 240px and broke the headline over three lines. The headline is `text-balance`, so its last word never sits alone. The Subscribe button stays enabled until a submit is in flight or done. A submit with an empty or invalid email shows the inline error and posts nothing. Focus moves to the email field, so a screen reader hears the error through `aria-describedby` on every try. Every mount takes the same layout: `/products`, the term pages, `/search`, the product, vendor and pair pages, and `/roadmap`. The mock-up 6 heading "New integrations, once a month" and its two promise lines are not built: they promise a cadence and an unsubscribe path and wait for the owner.
    - `/about` and `/methodology` moved in AECI-1800: the hero is on `aec-bone-band` and the body is white on the page wrap.
    - `/updates`, `/roadmap` and `/contact` moved in AECI-1801: the hero is on `aec-bone-band`. `/updates` is all hero, its form in a white `aecCard` on Bone. `/roadmap` and `/contact` have a white body on the page wrap, and `/roadmap`'s mailing list signup is `ground="white"`, so the page has one Bone band.
    - `/vendor-program` and `/vendor-program/list-your-product` moved in AECI-1802: the hero is on `aec-bone-band` and the body is white on the page wrap. The "How listings are sorted" panel left its Bone fill for the shared white card.
    - `legal/legal-page.ts:139` and `accessibility/accessibility.ts:31`: AECI-1804.
    - `/unsubscribe`, `/notifications/mute` and `/claims/confirm` moved in AECI-1808: each is a white focused flow with one shared card and no Bone band.
    - `legal/legal-notice-band.ts:90`: shell chrome, **not counted** as the page's one Bone band. This is an earlier reading, flagged for Chris to confirm (§4 "The detail page band shell").
    - `products/product-card-grid.ts:39`: the `/products` lead card, a Bone card and not a band. Ruling 2 of 2026-11-16 keeps it.
    - `products/products-pair.ts:1017`: a nested Bone note on the pair page. AECI-1798.
    - `preview/unified-home/unified-home-preview.ts:209, 236, 416 and 446`: a preview route and not a public page. No issue moves it.
    - Three small public Bone notices, which are notices and not bands: `requests/claimant-fields.ts:156`, `requests/claim-routed-notice.ts:21` and `home/home-feedback-form.ts:64`. They are nested fills and never count against "one Bone band per page".
    - Portal and admin Bone fills (`vendor/components/*-banner.ts`, `vendor-plan-panel.ts:333`, `admin/admin-notes.ts:59`, `admin/reindex/reindex-history.ts:262`) are badges and notices, not bands. R2 leaves them for the portal and admin issues.

- **BrowseLayout** (`<aec-browse-layout>`) — for `/products`, the four taxonomy term pages, the four taxonomy index pages and `/search` (mock-up 10, AECI-1793, AECI-1795).
  - **Slots:** `header` (required), `filters`, `grid` (required). The `filters` input (default true) drops the rail and its landmark for a page with nothing to filter, such as a term with no products yet, so both states of a page keep one header and one wrap.
  - **Wrap:** `BAND_WRAP_CLASS`, the 1328px wrap with 24, 32 and 48px gutters, on the header and on the body (ruling 3 of 2026-11-16). Content lines up with the header and footer.
  - **Header:** white, no hero. 24px above and 32px below, 32px and 40px from `md`. A hairline runs the full width below it. The `/search` tablist sits on that hairline by a negative margin equal to the bottom padding, so change both together.
  - **Grid:** `md:` two columns (1fr filters / 3fr results) with a 48px gap, 24px stacked below `md`, where the filters collapse above the results into the "Filters" disclosure. The body is 32px above and 64px below, 40px and 96px from `md`.
  - **Shared pieces inside it:** the filter panel (§5 "Search & discovery"), the listing toolbar and the product card grid (§5 "Product card grid"), `aec-product-table` (§5 "Entity cards"), `aec-pagination-footer`, and `aec-empty-state` (`shared/empty-state/`), a sunken panel at the card radius with no dashed edge.
  - **Page header (AECI-1794, AECI-1795).** The breadcrumb, then the `h1` 16px below it, then the lede 12px below that. The `h1` carries no size class and takes the Headline role from the global `h1` rule. The lede takes the Lede role: 20px sans at 1.5, secondary, 42rem measure. A term page adds its product count 12px under the lede at 14px. One constant, `layouts/browse-header.ts`, spells it on `/products`, the term pages and the taxonomy index pages. The `/products` lede carries no count (ruling 8 of 2026-11-16): "Showing X of Y" in the footer is the one count.
  - **Empty term:** no filter rail. The empty state holds a lead line at 16px and 600 in primary ink, the sentence under it, and "Browse all products" as the standalone link with no padding and a 44px target.
  - **Taxonomy index pages** (`/categories`, `/audiences`, `/phases`, `/trades`, AECI-1795) sit in the same shell with no filter rail. The terms are `aecCard interactive` cards on `aec-card-grid`, three columns at most, uniform. The card's title is an `h2` holding the one link, stretched over the card. The description is clamped at three lines and the counts sit at 13px below it. Sorting uses `aec-listing-sort-select` (ruling 7 of 2026-11-16), not a row of buttons: "Name (A–Z)" and "Products", with "Project order" first on `/phases`. Each facet keeps its default, and the choice stays out of the URL.
    - The label "Most products" is proposed for the "Products" option and waits on the owner. Until he rules, the option keeps the old button's word and message id (`@@taxonomy.index.sort.products`).

- **Form + context rail** (AECI-987; a pattern, not a shell component): for single-step forms, namely the review form, the routed claim and correction pages, `/welcome`. Anchor: Stripe (the AECi chrome anchor), whose settings and checkout forms pair a capped form with a quiet summary column.
  - **Grid:** the standard canvas, then from `lg` a 12-column grid with `gap-x-12`. The header and the form take columns 1 to 7, the controls capped at `max-w-2xl`. The `aside` takes columns 9 to 12 (`lg:col-start-9 lg:col-span-4`), spans the header and form rows, and is `lg:sticky lg:top-8 lg:self-start`.
  - **Sticky goes on the tall column's child.** The sticky classes sit on the `aside` itself, a direct grid child whose grid area is the full row height. Never on a `nav` or `aside` inside a short host box: sticky travels only within its containing block, so it would not stick.
  - **Below `lg`** the rail stacks between the page header and the form as a contained panel: `surface-raised` fill, `border-default` hairline, `rounded-card`, `p-6`. Docked at `lg` it is bare on the page background. _(2026-11-14: the card radius is now 24px, `rounded-card` and `--radius-card` (clash 12). `rounded-lg` still renders 8px. `p-5` is 20px, off the spacing scale, so it is `p-6`. The rail's content is short copy, so nothing nests in it today, though clash 14 would allow it. Whether it takes the card shadow is open, with the rest of that set in §4 "Open values" (AECI-1765). Until then it takes none.)_
  - **Content:** only data the page already loads (no new API call) and short static copy: what you are acting on, what happens next, one docs link that opens a new tab so a half-filled form survives. All copy is `i18n` / `$localize`.

Every visible string and every ARIA label is i18n-wrapped (`@@app.layouts.{detail|browse}.{slot}.aria`). Concrete pages add their own i18n keys for projected content.

> **`IndexLayout` and `EntityTable` (§11.2) — neither ships.** The Phase 2 spec listed a generic sortable / paginated `EntityTable` primitive; in implementation its whole responsibility (the semantic `<table>`, the scroll container, the `table-header` / `table-body` / `pagination` slots) was folded into a third shell, `IndexLayout` (`<aec-index-layout>`), so no separate `EntityTable` class was ever written. `IndexLayout` then lost its own consumers: AECI-165 deleted the `/vendors` + `/integrations` index pages, and AECI-190 rebuilt `/products` on **`BrowseLayout`** (it needs the facet rail, which `IndexLayout` has no slot for). The shell was deleted in AECI-657. Listing pages now compose `BrowseLayout` + `aec-product-table` (AECI-1793) or `ProductCardGrid`.

## 6. Do's and Don'ts

The strategic anti-references in `PRODUCT.md` carry through here as concrete visual prohibitions. Every PRODUCT.md anti-reference appears below as a "Don't" with the same language. The redesign rulings of 2026-11-13 and 2026-11-14 rewrote several of them. A clash number beside a rule means that ruling set it, and the rule it replaced is named.

### Floors that no ruling overrides

These are accessibility and legal rules, not style. `PLAN.md` §9.2 lists the first four, and ADR 0059 records them. Reviews added the rest. None of them is a lint check unless a line says so.

1. **Inputs keep a 3:1 boundary** (clash 23, WCAG 1.4.11). Every form control has a visible edge at 3:1 against its ground (§5 "Inputs / Fields").
2. **The mobile menu toggle has an accessible name, and the open menu traps focus** (clash 30, §5 "The mobile menu").
3. **An unclaimed product shows an initial tile everywhere except its own page** (counsel's ruling 9, §5 "Logos and initial tiles").
4. **Smooth scrolling must not break router scroll restoration, and it must respect reduced motion** (clash 38, §5 "Motion").
5. **The focus ring is an outline, not a box-shadow,** because forced-colours mode drops shadows. On Forest, the hero panel and the footer ground it is Bone (`--focus-ring-on-brand`, AECI-1747, 11.03:1 on Forest). A Forest ring on the hero panel is 1.20:1 and is never used (§4 "Shadow Vocabulary"). Use the plain utility for the ring. Use the shared unlayered `aec-on-brand-rule` class for a `--border-on-brand-subtle` hairline on Forest, and `aec-on-brand-edge` for the 3:1 `--border-on-brand-strong` control edge. These classes predate AECI-2088, when a `border-(--x)` utility lost to the unlayered `*` border rule. Never write component CSS or a `!` for any of these (AECI-1845). Inside the hero band the ring colour is set once in the base layer, so a white surface inside the band (a card) sets the Forest ring with the plain utility `focus-visible:outline-(--accent-primary)` (AECI-1766).
6. **Focus is never hidden under the sticky bar or the consent banner** (WCAG 2.4.11, §4 "The detail page band shell", §5 "The section nav" and §5 "Overlays"). AECI-1818 found the banner hiding up to 27 focus stops at 320px.
7. **State is never shown by colour alone** (WCAG 1.4.1). A selected chip, a pressed segment, a side stripe, an agreement state and an error each carry a second cue.
8. **A target is at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels** (WCAG 2.5.8). Buttons are 44px high (§5 "Buttons").
9. **Fluid type respects zoom.** Each fluid bound is in rem, and the tokens are checked at 200% zoom (WCAG 1.4.4, §3).
10. **No summary count of agreement states ships** (clash 41, `PLAN.md` §7.7, §5 "Badges").
11. **No pay-for-placement.** A new list is A to Z by name with the id as tie-break (ruling R1). No order, default filter or featured subset reads an input a paid plan can change (`PLAN.md` §7.8, tested by AECI-1814). "Confirmed by both companies" is never a filter or a sort (§5 "Order (R1, 2026-11-14)" and "Badges").
12. **Public counts wait for the Privacy Policy text and hide below 10** (ruling R7, AECI-1825, §5 "Integration stat").
13. **Legal-reviewed strings are used verbatim** (§5 "Unclaimed note" and "Integration stat").
14. **Cached SSR is visitor-state neutral** (§5 "Navigation", "The header never varies by visitor in cached HTML").
15. **Light theme only.** No dark theme, no `dark:` variant and no toggle (§1 Key characteristics, §2).
16. **Text meets AA contrast, and the `--text-tertiary` limits hold** (§2).
17. **A control on Forest or on the hero panel takes a filled background** (§2 "Forest grounds", §5 "Inputs / Fields").
18. **The "/" shortcut follows WCAG 2.1.4** (§5 "Header search").
19. **Hover content follows WCAG 1.4.13** (§5 "The Browse menu").
20. **Pages reflow at 320px and at 200% zoom** (§5 "Handover breakpoint and the width budget").

### Do:

- **Do** use OKLCH in CSS (`oklch(...)`) for color tokens. Hex values are documented as fallbacks but the canonical source is OKLCH (the front matter, plus `apps/web/src/styles.css`).
- **Do** pair Source Serif 4 (display) with Atkinson Hyperlegible Next (body) — the chosen system pair. Use them in their assigned roles (display for headings, body for prose, label for buttons / badges / table headers, overline for eyebrows / kickers / sidebar microheadings).
- **Do** use sentence case everywhere. Headings, buttons, labels, navigation, table headers, page titles, section titles. The overline role is the single uppercase exception (§3).
- **Do** use a 1px border on controls and chips (`border-default`, with `border-strong` for emphasis and 2px for featured and error states). This rewrites "0.5px default borders" (clashes 20, 22 and 23). Some shipped surfaces still draw 0.5px and change as each is rebuilt.
- **Do** keep Clay rare (≤5% per screen, decorative/fill only — fills carry `text-primary`, never white). Clay-colored text and icons use Clay deep (`#A14D22`). Star-rating glyphs use Goldenrod (`#DAA520` / `--accent-rating`). The main button is Forest with white text (the Forest-Anchor Rule). On a Forest ground it is Bone with Forest text (clash 3). It is never Clay.
- **Do** use Bone as a _warm-tinted accent surface_: one band per page (clash 2), never a page background. On a Forest ground Bone is the ink (clashes 3 and 4).
- **Do** vary spacing for visual rhythm (4pt scale: 4 / 8 / 12 / 16 / 24 / 32 / 48 / 64 / 96). Tight groupings, generous separations.
- **Do** use container queries (`@container`) for component-level responsiveness; viewport queries for page layout.
- **Do** respect `prefers-reduced-motion` on every transition. Use the three durations: fast 120ms for press and row hover, base 180ms for hover, focus and disclosure, slow 280ms for menu open, the nav ink bar and the scrim (clash 36, §5 "Motion").
- **Do** draw every icon on a public page through `aec-icon`, in `currentColor`, decorative or labelled (clash 34, §5 "Icons").
- **Do** show a visible focus-ring on every interactive element (`:focus-visible`), as an outline. Never `outline: none` without a replacement.

### Don't:

- **Don't** let a side stripe be the only cue. Coloured side stripes are allowed on a card or tile (clash 15, new), always beside a text label, in an existing token, never as a gradient (§4 "Coloured side stripes are allowed"). This rewrites "Side-stripe borders are banned". The ruling names cards and tiles. Whether a callout or an alert may take one is open (Chris). Three shipped stripes already exist: the prose blockquote (`styles.css`, `.aec-prose blockquote`), the pair diff rows (`products-pair.ts`, `DIFF_ADDED_ROW` and `DIFF_REMOVED_ROW`) and the vendor history reason (`vendor-history-page.ts`, `data-history-reason`).
- **Don't** use gradient text (any combination of `background-clip` text fill plus a gradient `background`). **Gradient text is banned.** Solid colors only; emphasis via weight or size.
- **Don't** use the hero-metric template (big number + small label + supporting stats + gradient accent). It's the SaaS cliché; the score-display component is the editorial alternative. One large figure per band (clash 8).
- **Don't** use sparklines as decoration. Tiny charts that look sophisticated and convey nothing meaningful are noise.
- **Don't** put a shadow on a nested card. Cards may nest (clash 14, new): only the outer card carries `--shadow-card`, and the nested panel is a tint or a hairline (§4 "Cards may nest"). This rewrites "Don't nest cards inside cards".
- **Don't** build a card grid wider than three columns, or one with no break (clash 18, blend). Cap the grid at three columns and break it with a group header (§4 "Card grids cap at three columns"). Ruling R3 dropped the wide lead card on the integrations list. This rewrites "Don't use identical card grids". Cards with an icon, a heading and eight words each are still a cliché.
- **Don't** use any of the reflex-reject fonts: Inter, DM Sans, Plus Jakarta Sans, Geist, Mona Sans, Space Grotesk, IBM Plex Sans, Outfit, Roboto, Open Sans, Arial, Fraunces, Newsreader, Lora, Crimson Pro, Playfair Display, Cormorant, Cormorant Garamond, DM Serif Display, DM Serif Text, Instrument Sans, Instrument Serif, Syne. The chosen pair is Source Serif 4 + Atkinson Hyperlegible.
- **Don't** use `#000` or `#fff` directly. Use `text-primary` (`#0A0A0A`) and `surface-base` (`#FFFFFF`) tokens.
- **Don't** use bounce or elastic easing on motion. Every eased move uses `ease-standard`, `cubic-bezier(.2,.7,.2,1)` (clash 36). Real objects decelerate smoothly.
- **Don't** use glassmorphism (blur, glass cards, glow borders) decoratively. Rare and purposeful, or nothing. The sticky bar has no blur (clash 13).
- **Don't** put a box-shadow on a button, chip, input or tab (clash 11). Top-level floating cards take `--shadow-card`, and menus take `--shadow-menu`. This rewrites "no shadows on cards". A tinted shadow is never allowed.
- **Don't** use the AI color palette: cyan-on-dark, purple-to-blue gradients, neon accents on dark backgrounds, gradient backgrounds for impact. The one gradient allowed is the hero's soft single-colour glow (clash 13).
- **Don't** use stock photography of construction sites, hard hats, or blueprints. The AEC visual cliché — the brand is editorial about AEC, not a costume of AEC.
- **Don't** use emoji in UI chrome. Use the custom icon set (clash 34, §5 "Icons"). Emoji rendering is inconsistent across platforms and clashes with the editorial brand. This rewrites "Lucide icons exclusively".
- **Don't** use em dashes in UI copy (also not `--`). Use commas, colons, semicolons, periods, or parentheses.
- **Don't** make every button primary. One filled button per surface (clash 21). Use ghost and secondary variants — hierarchy matters.
- **Don't** use modals as the first thought. Exhaust inline / progressive disclosure / drawer alternatives first.
